✗ Wrong options: PT · early WB · early ROM · Ca/vit D

**PM step-off ≥ 2 mm post-op** — **independent risk for arthritis** (fragment size not clearly linked)

**Overall** — \~90% success; final function at **\~2 yr**; 90% mild / no pain at 1 yr

**Good outcome predictors** — **age < 40**, male, **ASA 1–2**, no diabetes

**Adverse** — older age, osteoporosis, **diabetes**, PVD, female, ↑ ASA, **smoking**, **alcohol**, ↓ education, **medial malleolar fracture**

**Age** — **no effect on outcomes / complications of unstable fractures** (EXCEPT / LEAST answer)\
✗ Wrong options: osteoporosis · function · smoking · diabetes · skin

### Fibular stress fracture

**Who** — athletes, **military recruits**; female, training change, older age

**X-ray** — negative or **periosteal reaction along outer distal fibular shaft**\
✗ Wrong options: avulsion = sprain · syndesmotic HO = old high sprain · calcified lesion = enchondroma · anterior spur = impingement

**Treatment** — immobilise + rest → heals 6–12 wk, return \~9 wk

### Paediatric transitional fractures (TO)

| Fracture (TO) | Salter-Harris | Key fact |
|---|---|---|
| **Tillaux** | **SH III** of distal tibia | **AITFL avulses anterolateral epiphysis (Chaput)**; age **12–14**; forced **lateral rotation of foot / medial rotation of leg on fixed foot** (SER); ORIF if **> 2 mm**; \~5% of paediatric ankle fractures |
| **Triplane** | **SH IV** | Age **10–17**; **sagittal** epiphysis (AP view) + **coronal** posterior metaphysis (lateral view) + **axial** physis; lateral commonest (AITFL, weakest lateral physis); medial rare (adduction) |

**Both** — occur during **partial physeal closure** (medial closes first)

## X vs Y

| | **SER** | **PER** | **PAB** | **SAD** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fibula | Short oblique **AI → PS** at syndesmosis | Spiral **AS → PI** above syndesmosis | **Transverse comminuted** above | **Low transverse** |
| Medial | Transverse MM / deltoid (last) | Transverse MM / deltoid (first) | Transverse MM / deltoid (first) | **Vertical MM** |
| Special | Commonest | **Maisonneuve** | Butterfly; elderly | **Plafond impaction**, talar OCD |

| | **Tillaux–Chaput** | **LeFort–Wagstaffe** |
|---|---|---|
| AITFL avulses | **Tibia** (anterolateral) | **Fibula** (anterior tubercle) |

| | **Bosworth** | **Log-splitter** |
|---|---|---|
| Lesion | Fibula locked **behind posterolateral tibial ridge** | Talus driven into **syndesmosis** |
| Key | Irreducible → posterolateral open reduction | **PTOA 54–84%** |

## How it's asked

**"Vertical MM + fibular tip fracture — mechanism?"** → **supination–adduction** (TO)

**"PAB fibular pattern?"** → **comminuted, at / above syndesmosis**

**"Fracture from abduction of foot?"** → **PAB image**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated diastasis = ER · medial subtalar = inversion

**"Medial buttress plate parallel screws implies?"** → **SAD**

**"Pattern most likely with talar injury?"** → **SAD**

**"Unique feature to recognise in SAD?"** → **anteromedial plafond marginal impaction**

**"Plating technique for SAD medial malleolus?"** → **antiglide**

**"SAD bimalleolar treatment?"** → **fibular plate + elevate impaction + MM antiglide plate**

**"Mortise view taken in?"** → **15° internal rotation** (TO)

**"Talocrural angle second line?"** → **between malleolar tips**

**"Indicates anatomic reduction?"** → **TCA 83°**

**"Post-op finding needing CT?"** → **tibiofibular overlap 2 mm on AP**

**"Best test of deltoid competence?"** → **ER stress radiograph**

**"ORIF indication in isolated fibula fracture?"** → **MCS widening on gravity stress**

**"Most predictive of medial injury?"** → **stress MCS 6 mm vs superior space 3 mm**

**"Stable Weber B — treatment?"** → **splint 2–4 wk → WBAT boot**

**"Axial CT fragment = ?"** → **Chaput tubercle — AITFL** (TO)

**"Fragment attached to PITFL?"** → **posterolateral (Volkmann)**

**"Cotton test evaluates?"** → **syndesmosis**

**"Direction of greatest syndesmotic instability?"** → **anterior–posterior**

**"Most important for outcome after syndesmotic injury?"** → **anatomic syndesmotic reduction**

**"Lowest malreduction rate?"** → **suture button**

**"Clamp technique EXCEPT?"** → **lateral tine posterior to ridge**

**"Most accurate syndesmotic reduction check?"** → **direct view of anterolateral joint**

**"Iatrogenic fibular shortening first causes?"** → **syndesmotic malreduction**

**"Restores strongest syndesmotic portion?"** → **reduce + fix posterior malleolus**

**"PM fixation EXCEPT?"** → **biomechanically inferior to syndesmotic screw**

**"Fixation contributing most to syndesmotic stability (Weber C + PM)?"** → **posterior malleolus fixation**

**"Intact vs broken syndesmotic screw?"** → **intact may be worse than loosened**

**"Maisonneuve mechanism?"** → **pronation–external rotation** (TO)

**"Isolated posterior malleolus fracture, swollen leg — next?"** → **tib-fib radiographs**

**"Large PM (45%) — treatment?"** → **ORIF with absolute stability**

**"Posterolateral approach interval?"** → **SPN (peroneals) ↔ tibial (FHL)**

**"Spur sign + large PM?"** → **fibula ORIF + PM buttress via posterolateral approach**

**"Irreducible fracture-dislocation — block?"** → **posterolateral tibial ridge (Bosworth)**

**"Lost dorsiflexion after Bosworth fixation?"** → **posterior fixation of fibula + PM**

**"Lateral plate risk vs posterior plate?"** → **intra-articular screw**

**"Antiglide plate injures?"** → **peroneus brevis**

**"Peroneal pathology risk?"** → **low plate + prominent distal screw**

**"Most rigid fibular fixation?"** → **locking plate**

**"Plate doing neutralisation + buttress?"** → **under-contoured plate protecting lag screw**

**"Nerve crossing proximal lateral fibular dissection?"** → **SPN — two terminal branches**

**"Neuropathy is independent risk for complications of?"** → **ankle fractures**

**"Most important predictor of complications in diabetic?"** → **peripheral neuropathy**

**"Diabetic neuropathic ankle ORIF — next after plating?"** → **syndesmotic screws + NWB 8–12 wk**

**"Diabetic ORIF — weight bearing?"** → **protected WB in CAM at 10 wk**

**"Adjunct improving stability in neuropathic diabetic?"** → **retrograde hindfoot pin / nail**

**"Best candidate for primary TTC nail?"** → **complicated diabetes with neuropathy**

**"TTC fusion position?"** → **neutral DF, 5–10° ER, 5° valgus, 0–5 mm posterior talus**

**"Diabetic nonop, lost to follow-up 12 wk?"** → **fracture displacement**

**"Age effect on outcome / complications?"** → **none**

**"Factor affecting wound complications?"** → **smoking**

**"Braking time after right ankle ORIF?"** → **\~9 wk (6–9 wk)**

**"Taxi driver 8 wk after ORIF — braking normal when?"** → **one week from now** (9 wk rule)

**"Athlete with on-field fracture-dislocation?"** → **splint in situ** (TO)

**"Pulseless after fracture-dislocation reduced?"** → **re-evaluate pulses**

**"Blisters over incisions after failed reduction?"** → **spanning ex-fix (relative stability)**

**"Allgöwer-Donati benefit?"** → **↑ incisional perfusion**

**"Clean type I open ankle — NOT true?"** → **VAC + delayed closure ↓ infection**

**"Log-splitter PTOA rate?"** → **50–85%**

**"PM fixed with 2 mm step-off?"** → **step-off is risk factor for arthritis**

**"Ball sign indicates?"** → **malreduced (shortened) fibula** (TO)

**"Shortened fibular malunion — treatment?"** → **lengthening osteotomy + syndesmosis reduction**

**"Fusion vs osteotomy decided by?"** → **degree of tibiotalar arthritis**

**"Missed SAD impaction, anteromedial pain?"** → **surgical correction of malunion**

**"Pain 9 mo after nonop fibula?"** → **CT for nonunion**

**"Symptomatic MM nonunion?"** → **ORIF + autograft**

**"Military recruit lateral ankle pain 7 cm above joint?"** → **periosteal reaction (stress fracture)**

**"Tillaux fracture?"** → **SH III distal tibia** (TO)

**"Triplane fracture?"** → **SH IV** (TO)

**"Fleck sign at lateral malleolus?"** → **superior peroneal retinaculum injury** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Brachial Plexus Injuries

*Trauma · 5 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

| Traumatic BPI pattern | Share |
|---|---|
| **Complete (all roots, supraclavicular)** | **75–80%** — commonest |
| **C5–C6 upper trunk (Erb)** | **20–25%** |
| **C8–T1 (Klumpke)** | **0.6–3%** |

**Cause** — **high-speed vehicle, mostly motorcycle (83%)**

**Spectrum** — traumatic · obstetric (Erb, Klumpke) · burners / stingers · Parsonage-Turner

### Plexus anatomy – cord / trunk innervation & lesion localisation

![Brachial plexus motor and sensory innervation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1008-brachial-plexus-fixed.jpg)

| Level | Branches |
|---|---|
| **Roots** | **Dorsal scapular (rhomboids, C5)** · **long thoracic (serratus anterior, C5–7)** · phrenic contribution (C3–5) |
| **Upper trunk (C5–6)** | **Suprascapular (SS, IS)** · nerve to subclavius |
| **Lateral cord** | **Musculocutaneous** · **lateral root of median** (FCR, PT) · lateral pectoral (clavicular pec major) |
| **Medial cord** | Medial pectoral · medial brachial + antebrachial cutaneous · **ulnar** · medial root of median |
| **Posterior cord** | **Upper + lower subscapular** (subscapularis, teres major) · **thoracodorsal (latissimus)** · **axillary (deltoid, teres minor)** · **radial** (triceps, BR, wrist + finger extensors) |

**Erb's point (TO)** — **C5 + C6 roots + suprascapular nerve + nerve to subclavius** converge\
✗ Wrong options: **long thoracic** (does not meet there)

**Upper trunk lesion (tackle / stinger)** — **axillary + musculocutaneous + suprascapular** deficits: lateral shoulder + radial forearm numbness, weak abduction, ER, elbow flexion, ↓ biceps reflex → **normal 2nd + 5th finger abduction** (ulnar, inferior trunk)\
✗ Wrong options: C4 · middle trunk · lateral cord · axillary nerve

**Posterior cord lesion** — spares **supraspinatus** (suprascapular, upper trunk) (EXCEPT answer) · clavicle fragment on posterior cord → radial numbness + weak abduction, IR, wrist extension

**Subscapularis** — upper subscapular nerve consistently from **posterior cord** (lower subscapular variably from axillary)\
✗ Wrong options: biceps = lateral cord · pec major = medial + lateral · rhomboid = C5 root · supraspinatus = upper trunk

**Axillary nerve path (quadrilateral space syndrome)** — **C5–6 → upper trunk → posterior division → posterior cord**\
✗ Wrong options: C5–7 / lateral cord = musculocutaneous · C5–6 / upper trunk = suprascapular · C5–7 roots = long thoracic · C8–T1 / medial cord = ulnar

**Cord-level lesion (TO)** — **Tinel in infraclavicular fossa**; long thoracic, suprascapular, pectoral spared

| Cord lesion (TO) | Deficit |
|---|---|
| **Lateral** | Musculocutaneous (biceps), lateral median (FCR, PT), lateral pectoral; **small anterolateral forearm sensory zone**; **GH subluxation** |
| **Posterior** | Subscapular, thoracodorsal, axillary, radial → can't IR, elevate, extend; sensory loss over deltoid |
| **Medial** | Combined ulnar + median (except FCR, PT) + medial arm / hand sensory loss |

**Can't flex fingers + no ulnar-side sensation (TO)** — **C8**

### Root values (TO)

| Root (TO) | Key motor | Key sensory |
|---|---|---|
| **C5** | Shoulder abduction, ER | Skin over deltoid |
| **C5–C6** | Shoulder abduction, **elbow flexion**, supination | — |
| **C6** | — | **Thumb + index** |
| **C7** | **Elbow + wrist extension** (not specific to one muscle) | **Middle finger** |
| **C8–T1** | **Finger flexion, hand intrinsics** | C8 ring + little; T1 medial forearm |
| T2 | — | Medial arm |
| **C5–T1** | **Flail limb** | — |

## 2 Mechanism

**Caudally forced shoulder / head to opposite side** — **upper plexus** (high energy → all roots)

**Forced abduction (clutching while falling)** — **lower roots (C8–T1)**

## 3 Presentation

**Horner syndrome** — **ptosis, miosis, anhidrosis**; appears **\~3 days** after injury → **C8 / T1 root avulsion** (sympathetic chain)

**Severe pain in anaesthetic limb** — **root avulsion**

**Serratus anterior + rhomboids working** — C5 injury more likely **postganglionic**

**Pulses** — check; arterial injury common with complete BPI

### Upper vs lower vs total palsy

| | **Erb (C5–6)** | **Klumpke (C8–T1)** | **Total (C5–T1)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cause | Head to opposite side + shoulder depression; difficult delivery; fall on shoulder | **Excess abduction** (falling, clutching); cervical rib, lung mets | High energy |
| Posture | **"Waiter's tip"**: **adducted + IR shoulder, extended elbow, pronated forearm**, flexed wrist | **Claw hand**: wrist extended, MCP hyperextended, IP flexed | **Flaccid arm** |
| Nerves | C5 → axillary, suprascapular, musculocutaneous · C6 → radial (BR, supinator) | Ulnar + median (all intrinsics) | All |
| Extras | C7 also in **50%** (TO) | **Preganglionic + Horner** | — |
| Prognosis | **Best** | **Poor** | **Worst** |

**Erb (TO)** — weak deltoid, biceps, brachialis, SS, IS, serratus, rhomboids, levator scapulae, supinator; sensory loss lateral arm / forearm / radial hand

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Pre- vs post-ganglionic lesions

| | **Preganglionic (avulsion, proximal to DRG)** | **Postganglionic** |
|---|---|---|
| Regeneration | **CNS — none** → **worst prognosis** | PNS — can regenerate |
| **Horner** | **Yes** | No |
| **Scapular winging** | **Medial winging** (serratus) · rhomboid palsy → lateral winging | No |
| Hemidiaphragm | **Elevated** (phrenic) | Normal |
| Tinel in neck | **Absent** | Present; **advancing Tinel** = regeneration |
| **SNAP** | **Normal / intact** (DRG cell body intact) despite anaesthesia | **Absent** |
| Motor NCV | Absent | Absent |
| **EMG paraspinals** | **Denervated** | **Normal** |
| **Histamine test** | **Normal triple response** (red, wheal, **flare**) | **Abnormal — no flare** |
| CT myelogram | **Pseudomeningocele** (> 95% predictive) | Roots intact |
| Wallerian degeneration of sensory fibres | **No** | Yes |
| Flail arm | **Yes** | **Yes** (both) |

**SNAP normal + insensate ulnar zone** → **preganglionic C8–T1** · **SNAP normal + insensate median zone** → **preganglionic C5–C6**

**TO NOT-seen trap (TO)** — preganglionic shows **absent motor but intact sensory conduction** → **"absent sensory conduction" is NOT seen** · if both absent → postganglionic

**C8–T1 avulsion vs plexus injury** — **ptosis (Horner)**\
✗ Wrong options: weak radial pulse · SSSC double break · elevated hemidiaphragm = C5 · musculocutaneous deficit

**Child, fall clutching ladder, Horner + hand weakness** — **root level (preganglionic)**

**Paraspinals normal on EMG** — **postganglionic**\
✗ Wrong options: avulsion · Horner · normal histamine

**Postganglionic injury spares** — **rhomboid major** (dorsal scapular from root)\
✗ Wrong options: ECRL, biceps, deltoid, BR

**Most suggestive of postganglionic** — **advancing Tinel in supraclavicular region**\
✗ Wrong options: rhomboid / serratus involved · pseudomeningocele · diaphragm palsy · Horner

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| **CXR (PA + lat)** | **1st / 2nd rib fracture** → plexus injury · old rib fractures matter for **intercostal transfer** · inspiration / expiration → **paralysed diaphragm** (upper root) |
| **C-spine AP + lateral** | **Transverse process fracture** → root avulsion |
| Scapula / shoulder (AP + axillary) | **Scapulothoracic dissociation** → root avulsion + major vascular injury |
| Clavicle | Fracture may signal BPI |
| **CT myelography** | **Gold standard for root level**; avulsion → **meningocele**; do at **3–4 wk** (clot dissipates, meningocele forms) |
| **MRI** | Lesions **distal to roots**; **pseudomeningocele (T2)**, **empty root sleeves (T1)**, **cord shift away from midline (T1)**, neuromas |

### Electrodiagnostics

| Test | Fact |
|---|---|
| **EMG** | Fibrillations **10–14 d** proximal muscles, **3–6 wk** distal; test **rhomboids, serratus, cervical paraspinals** for pre vs post |
| **NCV / SNAP** | SNAP **preserved proximal to DRG** → preganglionic |
| **NAP (intra-op)** | Across lesion; detects reinnervation **months before EMG**; NAP negative = **neurapraxic** · NAP positive = **axonotmetic** (Orthobullets wording) |
| **SSEP / MEP** | More sensitive than EMG / NCV for root–cord continuity; at **4–6 wk** (Wallerian); stimulate **Erb's point**, record scalp |

## 5 Management

```
Traumatic brachial plexus injury
├─ Sharp / open / iatrogenic / vascular / expanding haematoma → explore < 1 wk
├─ Near-total, high energy (avulsion likely) → early surgery 3–6 wk
└─ Partial upper plexus, low energy / GSW → observe
       └─ plateau at 3–6 mo → delayed surgery (not > 6 mo)
```

### Operative criteria (timing)

| Timing | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Immediate (< 1 wk)** | **Sharp penetrating** (not GSW), iatrogenic, open, progressive deficit, **vascular injury / expanding haematoma** |
| **Early (3–6 wk)** | **Near-total plexus, high energy** · preganglionic → explore + reconstruct **within 6 wk** (OB) |
| **Delayed (3–6 mo)** | **Partial upper plexus, low energy**, plateaued recovery · postganglionic: wait 6 wk–6 mo |
| **Observe** | Most; **GSW** without vascular injury → **3 months** |

**Motor end-plate deadline** — irreversible loss **\~1 yr** (OB) · **20–24 months** (another OB explanation) → nerve transfer > 20–24 mo relatively contraindicated → **free functioning muscle transfer** — read the options

**Best clinical sign of regeneration** — **advancing Tinel**

### Techniques

| Technique | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Direct repair** | Rare — acute sharp injuries only |
| **Nerve graft** | Common for **postganglionic traction**; upper + middle trunk best; donors **sural**, medial brachial cutaneous, MABC; **vascularised ulnar** (superior ulnar collateral a.) if C8–T1 avulsed |
| **Neurotisation (nerve transfer)** | Expendable motor nerve → important denervated muscle; **reduces high injury to low injury**, faster reinnervation |
| Extraplexal donors | **Spinal accessory (CN XI)**, **intercostals**, **contralateral C7**, hypoglossal (CN XII) |
| Intraplexal donors | Phrenic, median / ulnar fascicles, pectoral |
| **Muscle / tendon transfer** | **Isolated adult C8–T1** (too far to reinnervate intrinsics) · late presentation · **gracilis** = commonest free muscle |

### Nerve transfers (Oberlin, intercostal)

**Oberlin / double fascicular transfer** — **ulnar fascicle → biceps branch** + **median fascicle → brachialis branch** → elbow flexion

**Indication** — **acute (< 3–6 mo) C5–C6 avulsion / upper trunk** · restores **musculocutaneous (lateral cord)** function\
✗ Wrong options: C8–T1 avulsion (ulnar dead) · 22 mo old palsy · medial + posterior cord GSW · C6 ASIA A

**Other C5–6 options** — **SA → suprascapular** · SA or intercostal → musculocutaneous

**Pan-plexus avulsion (C5–T1, pseudomeningoceles)** — ulnar / median useless → **intercostal → musculocutaneous**: donor is **expendable + functional**\
✗ Wrong options: near end plate · synergistic · near sensory target · pure motor (intercostals carry sensation)

**Contralateral C7 (TO)** — harvested in total palsy with minimal donor deficit

### Reconstruction priorities

| Priority | Nerve |
|---|---|
| **1 Elbow flexion** | **Musculocutaneous** |
| **2 Shoulder stability / abduction** | **Suprascapular** |
| 3 Brachial–thoracic pinch | Pectoral |
| 4 C6–C7 sensation | Lateral cord |
| 5 Wrist extension / finger flexion | Lateral + posterior cords |
| 6 Wrist flexion / finger extension | — |
| 7 Intrinsics | — |

**Least important (TO)** — **elbow extension**\
✗ Wrong options: shoulder abduction · elbow flexion · medial-hand sensation

**Medial-hand sensation (TO)** — restore to prevent ulcers, burns, self-mutilation → median fascicles → ulnar sensory / dorsal ulnar branch; LABC → dorsal ulnar via 15 cm sural graft

### Obstetric brachial plexus palsy

**Erb** — **commonest obstetric palsy** (1:1000) + **best prognosis** → **shoulder IR + forearm pronation** (adducted, elbow extended, wrist flexed)

**Good prognosis** — **biceps return before 3 months** + **full passive shoulder ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: breech (preganglionic) · no clavicle fracture · Horner · absent Moro

**Poor signs** — **breech** (preganglionic), **Horner**, **absent Moro**

### Deafferentation pain after plexus injury (TO)

**Stem (TO)** — severe chronic whole-limb pain weeks after BPI → options: baclofen pump, duroplasty, dorsal nerve ablation, **DREZ (dorsal root entry zone) procedure** — **answer key not provided by the source**

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis** — recovery up to **3 yr**; regeneration **1 mm/day**

**Better** — **infraclavicular > supraclavicular** · **upper plexus** (hand preserved) · postganglionic

**Worst** — **root avulsion (preganglionic)**: not repairable → arthrodesis, tendon transfer

## X vs Y

| | **Oberlin transfer** | **Intercostal → MC** |
|---|---|---|
| Use | **Upper trunk (C5–6)**, ulnar + median working | **Pan-plexus avulsion** |
| Donor | Intraplexal, synergistic, near target | Extraplexal, expendable, distant |

| | **NAP negative** | **NAP positive** |
|---|---|---|
| Lesion | Neurapraxic (Orthobullets) | Axonotmetic / regenerating |

## How it's asked

**"Nerve that does NOT meet at Erb's point?"** → **long thoracic** (TO)

**"Can't flex fingers, ulnar-side numbness — root?"** → **C8** (TO)

**"Infraclavicular Tinel, anterolateral forearm numbness, GH subluxation?"** → **lateral cord** (TO)

**"Posterior cord transection spares?"** → **supraspinatus**

**"Superior trunk injury — what stays normal?"** → **2nd + 5th finger abduction**

**"Tackle: lateral shoulder + radial forearm numbness, weak abduction / ER / elbow flexion?"** → **upper trunk**

**"Muscle innervated by posterior cord?"** → **subscapularis**

**"Quadrilateral space nerve pathway?"** → **C5–6, upper trunk, posterior division, posterior cord**

**"Clavicle fragment, radial numbness, weak abduction + IR + wrist extension?"** → **posterior cord**

**"NOT seen in preganglionic palsy?"** → **absent sensory conduction** (TO)

**"Supports preganglionic injury?"** → **intact SNAPs**\
✗ Wrong options: abnormal histamine · flail arm · intact roots on myelogram · normal paraspinals

**"Normal paraspinal EMG means?"** → **postganglionic**

**"Postganglionic — muscle NOT paralysed?"** → **rhomboid major**

**"Suggests C8–T1 root avulsion?"** → **eyelid droop (Horner)**

**"Most suggestive of postganglionic?"** → **advancing Tinel**

**"Gold standard for root level?"** → **CT myelography at 3–4 wk**

**"Ulnar → biceps + median → brachialis transfer indication?"** → **C5–6 avulsion 2 months ago**

**"Ulnar / median fascicle transfers treat injury to?"** → **lateral cord**

**"Intercostal → MC in pan-plexus avulsion — principle?"** → **expendable, functional donor**

**"Least important to reconstruct?"** → **elbow extension** (TO)

**"Top reconstruction priority?"** → **elbow flexion (musculocutaneous)**

**"Commonest obstetric palsy — posture?"** → **shoulder IR + forearm pronation**

**"Obstetric palsy — good prognosis?"** → **biceps return before 3 mo + full passive shoulder ROM**

**"GSW plexus injury without vascular injury?"** → **observe 3 months**

**"Sharp penetrating plexus injury?"** → **explore < 1 week**

**"Isolated adult C8–T1?"** → **tendon / muscle transfer**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Calcaneus Fractures

*Trauma · 6 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest tarsal fracture** — \~65% of tarsal fractures (OB)

**Intra-articular** — **60–75%** (Orthobullets) · **75%** (OB) · tuberosity fractures **1–3%** · avulsion + tongue-type **< 15%**

**Open** — **17%** (15–20%) → no ↑ infection, ↑ wound complications

**Tuberosity fractures** — peak in **women, 7th decade**

### Associated injuries

| Association | Rate |
|---|---|
| **Calcaneocuboid joint extension** | **63%** |
| **Vertebral fracture** | **10%** |
| **Contralateral calcaneus** | **10%** |
| **Peroneal tendon instability (SPR tear)** | **\~20%** — **commonest acute concurrent pathology** |
| **Foot compartment syndrome** | **10%** (Orthobullets) · **3–10%** (TO) |

### Fracture anatomy – constant fragment, sustentaculum & FHL

**Facets** — **posterior = largest, main weight-bearing** · **middle on sustentaculum (anteromedial)** · anterior often confluent with middle · **sinus tarsi** = calcaneal interosseous sulcus + talar sulcus

**Constant (superomedial / sustentacular) fragment** — **sustentaculum tali**; stays attached to talus by **interosseous + medial talocalcaneal ligaments** (+ deltoid); lateral fragments + tuberosity are reduced **to it**\
✗ Wrong options: held by Achilles · by navicular · displaces superolateral

**FHL** — wraps **inferiorly around sustentaculum** in a groove · **medial to posterior facet, inferior to middle facet**; origin **inferior ⅔ posterior fibula + interosseous membrane**

| FHL problem | Cause | Sign |
|---|---|---|
| **Iatrogenic** | **Lateral → medial drill / screws too long** into constant fragment | **Fixed flexed hallux** post-op |
| **Stenosis / tear** | **Sustentaculum tali fracture** | **Pain / popping with great-toe flexion** |
| **Entrapment** | **Marked posterior facet displacement** | **Limited hallux motion** |

**✗ FHL traps** — beak / tuberosity (Achilles) · lateral wall · FDL runs **superior** to sustentaculum (lesser-toe flexion) · plantar paraesthesia = medial plantar nerve · spring ligament

**Bifurcate ligament** — dorsal **anterior process → cuboid + navicular**; resists **plantarflexion–inversion**; EDB also originates there

### Blood supply

**Extensile lateral flap** — **lateral calcaneal branch of peroneal artery** (± lateral malleolar + lateral tarsal)\
✗ Wrong options: branch of ATA · lateral malleolar a.

**Heel pad** — **medial calcaneal branch of posterior tibial artery**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral calcaneal · sinus tarsi a. · tarsal canal a. (those feed talus)

## 2 Mechanism

**Intra-articular** — **axial load** (fall from height, MVC)

**Primary fracture line** — **oblique shear** → **superomedial (constant) + superolateral (posterior facet)** fragments · secondary line decides **tongue vs joint depression**

**Tuberosity (avulsion)** — osteoporosis + violent **triceps surae** contraction with forced dorsiflexion / knee extended · tight gastroc · neuropathy

**Anterior process** — **inversion + plantarflexion** → **bifurcate ligament avulsion**

**Stress** — ↑ activity with relative energy deficiency; runners, **military recruits**, female athlete triad, obesity, **after hip / knee arthroplasty**

## 3 Presentation

**Heel** — swollen, **shortened, widened**, apparent varus

**Posterior heel skin** — **tenting, blanching, ecchymosis** with tuberosity / tongue fractures → **urgent fixation**

**Fracture blisters** — must epithelialise before surgery · **wrinkle sign (Langer lines)** = skin ready

**Check** — compartments, nerves, pulses (severe PVD may preclude surgery), Achilles continuity, plantarflexion strength

![Posterior heel skin compromise over a displaced tuberosity fragment](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1051-clinical-photograph-with-large-hematoma-and-posterior-skin-c.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View / study | Use |
|---|---|
| AP, lateral, oblique foot | Standard |
| **Broden** | **Posterior facet**; ankle neutral, **\~45° IR**, beam **40, 30, 20, 10° cephalad**; intra-op reduction check |
| **Harris (axial)** | Tuberosity **widening, shortening, varus**; max dorsiflexion, beam 45° |
| AP ankle | **Lateral wall extrusion → fibular impingement** |
| **CT (2–3 mm)** | **Gold standard** · **30° semicoronal** = posterior + middle facets (Sanders) · **axial** = **CC joint** · **sagittal** = tuberosity · axial CT shows peroneal dislocation |
| MRI | **Only for stress fracture** with normal X-ray: **T2 oedema first**, later **T1 low-signal fracture line** |

| Sign / angle | Normal | Fracture |
|---|---|---|
| **Böhler angle** | **20–40°** | **↓** (posterior facet collapse) |
| **Crucial angle of Gissane** | **120–145°** | **↑** |
| **Double-density sign** | — | **Lateral portion of posterior facet** depressed / rotated \~90° into body, separated from sustentaculum → subtalar incongruity |
| Calcaneal shortening, varus tuberosity | — | Present |

**Böhler angle** — line anterior process top → posterior facet top **vs** line along superior tuberosity (lateral view)

**Gissane** — line along lateral posterior facet **vs** line anterior to calcaneal beak

![Böhler angle and crucial angle of Gissane](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1051-shows-bohler-and-gissane.jpg)

### Essex-Lopresti classification

| Type | Secondary line |
|---|---|
| **Tongue-type** | **Axial plane beneath facet, exiting posteriorly** — posterior facet stays attached to tuberosity → Achilles pulls fragment up |
| **Joint depression** | **Behind posterior facet** |

### Sanders classification (coronal CT, widest posterior facet)

| Type | Posterior facet | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Non-displaced** (any number of lines) | **Nonop** |
| **II** | **1 line → 2 fragments** | **ORIF** |
| **III** | **2 lines → 3 fragments** | **ORIF** |
| **IV** | **> 3 lines, comminuted (≥ 4 fragments)** | **ORIF + primary subtalar arthrodesis** |

**Sanders III vs II** — **6.5× PTOA**, **4× subtalar fusion**

![Sanders classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1051-diagram-of-sanders-classification.jpg)

### Beavis classification (tuberosity)

| Type | Fracture |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Sleeve** — small cortical shell avulsed |
| **2** | **Beak** — oblique line from superior posterior facet running posteriorly |
| **3** | **Infrabursal** — from mid-tuberosity |

### Anterior process fracture types

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| I | Non-displaced avulsion |
| II | Displaced, **no** CC joint |
| III | Displaced, **involves CC joint** |

**Extra-articular (25%)** — anterior process (bifurcate), sustentaculum, tuberosity (Achilles avulsion)

## 5 Management

```
Calcaneus fracture
├─ Tongue-type / tuberosity + skin threatened → splint 30° PF → urgent fixation
├─ Sanders I, small extra-articular, comorbid → NWB cast 10–12 wk
├─ Sanders II–III → ORIF at 10–14 d (wrinkle sign)
└─ Sanders IV → ORIF + primary subtalar fusion
```

### Operative criteria

| Nonoperative (NWB cast 10–12 wk) | Operative |
|---|---|
| **Small extra-articular < 1 cm**, Achilles intact, < 2 mm | **Displaced tongue-type > 1 cm**, **threatened skin**, open |
| **Sanders I** | **Sanders II + III**: facet **> 2–3 mm**, flattened Böhler, varus tuberosity |
| **Near-normal Böhler (20–40°)** | **Large extra-articular, > 10° varus / valgus** |
| **Anterior process < 25% CC joint** | **Anterior process > 25% CC joint** |
| **Smoker, diabetes, PVD** (avoid wound complications) | **Displaced sustentaculum** |
| Elderly low-demand tuberosity < 1 cm, skin OK | **Sanders IV → primary subtalar arthrodesis** |

**Stress fracture** — **NWB short-leg cast 6 wk** (well-padded heel) / CAM boot + NWB

**Cast technique (10–12 wk)** — mild equinus, **posterior heel window**, **weekly cast changes** (skin complications)

**Timing of ORIF** — **10–14 days** (swelling + blisters resolve, wrinkle sign) · no benefit to early surgery · **except displaced tuberosity / tongue with skin compromise → urgent**

**Goals (TO)** — **posterior facet congruity** · restore **height, width, length, Böhler angle** · **correct hindfoot varus** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: correct **talar declination** (that's talar neck)

### Tongue-type / tuberosity fracture – urgent fixation

**Why urgent** — Achilles pulls fragment **proximal + posterior** → **posterior skin necrosis** (> 20%)\
✗ Wrong options: subtalar arthrosis · nonunion · AVN · compartment syndrome · better reduction

**Splint** — **30° plantarflexion** (max PF) to relax skin

**Fixation** — **immediate**, minimally invasive / percutaneous **lag screws posterosuperior tuberosity → inferior + distal** (perpendicular to fracture) · ORIF bone-to-bone if large · ± **gastrocnemius recession (Strayer)**

**✗ Tuberosity traps** — cast in equinus · **excise fragment + reattach Achilles** (large fragment, tension lost) · **percutaneous K-wires** (too weak vs triceps) · **extensile lateral approach** with swelling · delayed ORIF · primary arthrodesis (beak is extra-articular)

**Posterior approach (tuberosity)** — prone, midline; lag screws / tension band / Krackow through tunnels; restricted WB 6 wk + 6 wk progression

### Approaches & techniques

| Technique | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Closed reduction + percutaneous pinning** | **Minimally displaced tongue-type**, severe PVD / soft-tissue compromise; Essex-Lopresti manoeuvre; pins out 8–10 wk; ± distracting ex-fix (tuberosity, cuboid, tibia) |
| **Extensile lateral (L-shaped)** | Vertical limb between fibula + Achilles, horizontal toward 5th MT base; full-thickness flap (**lateral calcaneal a.**); **high wound complications**; sural nerve + peroneals in flap; **bone graft adds nothing** |
| **Sinus tarsi** | **Tip of fibula → 4th MT base**, 2–4 cm; EDB up, peroneals back; sees **posterior facet, anterolateral fragment, lateral wall**; **↓ wound complications, ↓ infection, ↓ op time, better Maryland foot scores**; same Böhler restoration; **↑ radiation**; **sural nerve at greatest risk** (identify inferiorly) |
| **Arthroscopic-assisted** | Anterolateral + posterolateral portals; ↓ dissection; ↑ swelling, demanding |
| **Primary subtalar arthrodesis** | **Sanders IV**; restore height + fuse; avoids second surgery |

**Post-op (sinus tarsi)** — NWB 6–8 wk; ankle ROM from 2 wk

### Operative vs nonoperative – prognostic factors

| Better outcome with ORIF | Worse outcome with ORIF |
|---|---|
| **Female** | **Male** (men do worse with surgery) |
| **Age < 29–30** | **Age > 50** (op ≈ nonop) |
| **No workers' compensation** | **Workers' comp**, litigation |
| **Sedentary / light job** | **Heavy manual labour** |
| **Sanders II** (less comminution) | Higher Sanders, **Böhler < 0°** |
| Böhler **0–14°** (moderately flat, OB) · **> 15°** (another OB) | **Smoking**, obesity, open, **bilateral**, polytrauma, vasculopathy |
| — | **Previous calcaneus fracture** (no benefit, EXCEPT answer) |

**Most likely eventual subtalar fusion** — **male + WC + heavy labour + Böhler < 0°**

**Highest PTOA risk** — **Sanders III treated with ORIF** (articular damage drives arthritis, not sex / job)

**Best predictor of PTOA** — **initial energy / fracture severity (Sanders)** > reduction quality

**Positive prognostic factor (Sanders II, 2 fragments)** — **Sanders classification**\
✗ Wrong options: sex · age 40 · labourer job · approach

**Only undisputed op vs nonop difference** — **ORIF ↑ wound complications + reoperation** (nonop → ↓ wound complications) · function, satisfaction, walking, shoe fit **equal at 5 yr**

**Surgery reduces** — **PTOA / subtalar fusion \~6× (5.5–6×)**\
✗ Wrong options: faster RTW · ↑ subtalar ROM · ↓ nonunion (nonunion is rare)

**Sanders II, young healthy non-smoker** — **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · fusion · percutaneous (tongue only) · ex-fix

**68-yo healthy, blisters** — **delayed ORIF** (age > 50 no longer absolute contraindication; comorbidities matter more)

### Open calcaneus fractures (TO)

**Medial wound < 4 cm, closeable, stable (TO)** — **serial I&D → standard ORIF** when swelling settles (Sanders III)\
✗ Wrong options: percutaneous · ex-fix

**Lateral wounds / medial > 4 cm / not closeable / unstable after 7–10 d (TO)** — **open reduction through wound + percutaneous fixation**

**Grade 1–2 medial open, clean at 3 wk** — **infection ≈ closed fracture** with extensile ORIF; IV antibiotics 2–3 d\
✗ Wrong options: 3–5× infection · ex-fix only

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Wound complications** | **10–25%** (Orthobullets) · **10–35%** (OB) · **delayed wound healing / dehiscence = commonest** (up to 25%) · deep infection 1–4% closed |
| **Subtalar arthritis** | ↑ with nonop; **commonest complication of nonop** |
| **Sural neuroma** | Extensile lateral / sinus tarsi |
| **FHL injury** | Long lateral→medial screws at sustentaculum |
| **Compartment syndrome** | **10%** → **claw toes** |
| **Peroneal tendinitis / impingement** | Lateral wall blowout; **typical after nonop** |
| **Malunion** | Loss of height, widening, varus, impingement |
| Overall | **\~40% complication rate** |

### Wound complications, approach & sural nerve

**Risk factors (TO)** — **smoking** · **diabetes**, **open fracture**, single-layer closure, higher Sanders, long op, ↑ EBL, many people in OR, delay to surgery, ↑ BMI, **less surgeon experience**\
✗ Wrong options: RA · steroids · alcohol

**Not wound risk factors (OB)** — workers' comp, allograft, contralateral fracture, male sex

**Prevention (TO)** — tourniquet, no smoking, full-thickness flap, **drain**, **two-layer closure ends → middle**, sutures **2–3 wk**, no ROM during that time, keep hardware away from incision corner

**Superficial dehiscence after sinus tarsi in smoker** — **stop PT, immobilise, local wound care** (± oral antibiotics) · deep / purulent → serial debridement\
✗ Wrong options: I&D · hardware removal

**Sural neuroma (burning lateral pain, 7 cm above heel)** — **excise + bury sural nerve in muscle or vein**\
✗ Wrong options: SPN neuroplasty · subtalar fusion · hardware removal at 6 mo

**Nerve by approach** — sinus tarsi / extensile = **sural** · direct lateral fibula + anterolateral ankle = **SPN** · posteromedial = tibial nerve + PTA

### Associated injuries – peroneal tendon dislocation

**SPR tear + peroneal instability** — **\~20%**, from **lateral wall blowout** (joint depression); ↑ with higher Sanders + fracture-dislocation

**Fleck / cortical avulsion off distal fibula on CT** — **SPR disrupted → anterior peroneal subluxation** confirmed intra-op\
✗ Wrong options: intact SPR · FHL entrapment · skin necrosis

**CT** — limited sensitivity → **test SPR intra-operatively**

**TO stem (TO)** — calcaneum X-ray, "which movement is weak?" (plantarflexion / dorsiflexion / inversion / eversion) — **answer key not provided by the source**

### Compartment syndrome – claw toes

**Strongest association (TO)** — **Sanders IV**\
✗ Wrong options: soft-tissue compromise · delay · crush

**Lesser-toe clawing at 3 mo** — **missed foot compartment syndrome** (intrinsic contracture)\
✗ Wrong options: FHL tethering · sural · medial plantar neuropathy · tib post

### Malunion sequelae & subtalar distraction bone-block arthrodesis

**Malunion triad** — **↓ height (talus dorsiflexed, talar declination < 20°)**, **↑ width (lateral wall exostosis)**, **varus**

| Sequel | Cause |
|---|---|
| **Anterior tibiotalar impingement / ↓ dorsiflexion** | **Loss of posterior facet height** → dorsiflexed talus (**talar declination < 20°**) |
| **Subfibular / peroneal impingement, peroneal tendinitis** | **Lateral wall blowout + widening** (✗ Wrong options: hindfoot valgus (uncommon)) |
| Shoe-wear problems | ↑ width |
| **Subtalar arthritis** | Articular incongruity (most cases; often radiographic only) |
| **Varus** hindfoot | Tuberosity pulled into varus |

**Nonop DIACF frequently** — **peroneal tendinitis**\
✗ Wrong options: return to normal · narrowing · lengthening · plantarflexed talus

![Calcaneal malunion classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1051-illustration-of-calcaneal-malnunion-classification.jpg)

| Malunion type (Stephens–Sanders) | Features | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | Lateral exostosis, **no** subtalar arthritis | **Lateral wall resection (exostectomy)** |
| **II** | Exostosis **+ subtalar arthritis** | **Lateral wall resection + subtalar fusion** |
| **III** | + **varus malunion** | Wall resection + subtalar fusion **± valgus osteotomy** (controversial) |

**Distraction bone-block subtalar arthrodesis** — indicated by **loss of height with anterior ankle impingement / ↓ dorsiflexion** (+ subtalar arthritis); restores height (\~8 mm), talocalcaneal angle (\~9°), **talar declination** (\~11°); corrects varus, TN subluxation

**Bone block not for** — **full dorsiflexion, no tibiotalar impingement** (EXCEPT) · **peroneal impingement alone** (needs **lateral wall exostectomy**) · isolated sinus tarsi pain (→ **in situ** fusion) · + tibiotalar arthritis (→ triple / larger fusion)

**Ankle + lateral pain, subfibular impingement + arthritis** — **distraction subtalar arthrodesis + lateral wall ostectomy**

**Early post-traumatic subtalar arthritis, ankle pain-free** — **Arizona / gauntlet brace** (limits inversion–eversion) before surgery\
✗ Wrong options: ASO lace-up · UCBL · medial wedge

**Subtalar fusion after prior ORIF** — better function + fewer wound problems than after nonop malunion (Radnay)

### Stress & insufficiency fractures

**Clinical** — **heel squeeze (medial–lateral compression) tender**; pain all day, worse with WB; walls of calcaneus

**X-ray** — **sclerotic line at 2–3 wk** (dense condensation in elderly insufficiency, e.g. after TKA)

**MRI** — most sensitive: **marrow oedema (T2 / STIR) + linear low-signal fracture line on T1**\
✗ Wrong options: plantar fascia thickening · Achilles insertion signal · OCD · serpiginous AVN line

**Next step if suspected** — **restricted WB + MRI**\
✗ Wrong options: heel-pad steroid (fat-pad atrophy) · PRP · nerve release · Graston · EMG

**Treatment** — **boot / cast + NWB**; 4–6 months cast excessive\
✗ Wrong options: fascia stretching · eccentric Achilles · CRPP · activity as tolerated

### Anterior process fracture – bifurcate ligament

**Mechanism** — **inversion + plantarflexion → bifurcate (Y) ligament avulsion**\
✗ Wrong options: CFL (inversion + dorsiflexion) · ATFL (talus) · tibiocalcaneal (deltoid)

**Often missed** as "ankle sprain"; best seen on **oblique foot X-ray or CT**; prone to nonunion

**Treatment** — acute non-displaced → immobilise NWB · **> 25% CC joint → ORIF**

## X vs Y

| | **Tongue-type** | **Joint depression** |
|---|---|---|
| Secondary line | Exits **posteriorly** through tuberosity | **Behind** posterior facet |
| Facet | Attached to tuberosity | Depressed separately |
| Urgency | **Skin necrosis → urgent** | Delay 10–14 d |
| Option | **Percutaneous** reduction / screws | ORIF |

| | **Extensile lateral** | **Sinus tarsi** |
|---|---|---|
| Wounds / infection | **Higher** | **Lower** |
| Op time | Longer | **Shorter** |
| Radiation | Less | **More** |
| Böhler restoration | Same | Same |

| | **In situ subtalar fusion** | **Distraction bone-block fusion** |
|---|---|---|
| Indication | Isolated subtalar arthritis | **+ loss of height, anterior ankle impingement** |

## How it's asked

**"Displaced tongue-type — feared sequel?"** → **posterior skin necrosis**

**"Immediate fixation of tuberosity avulsion prevents?"** → **skin necrosis**

**"Splint position before tongue-type fixation?"** → **30° plantarflexion**

**"Tuberosity fracture with blanching skin?"** → **immediate percutaneous reduction + screws**

**"Displaced tuberosity, threatened skin — definitive?"** → **urgent ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · excision · K-wires

**"FHL at risk with lateral→medial drill in?"** → **intra-articular calcaneus fracture**

**"Superomedial fragment — true?"** → **FHL wraps inferiorly around it**

**"Fixed flexed hallux after ORIF?"** → **screws too long into constant fragment**

**"Pain / popping with great-toe flexion months later — initial injury?"** → **sustentaculum tali fracture**

**"Constant fragment is?"** → **sustentaculum tali**

**"Double density on lateral?"** → **lateral portion of posterior facet**

**"Tendon entrapped by posterior facet — origin?"** → **inferior ⅔ posterior fibula (FHL)**

**"NOT an aim of calcaneus management?"** → **correct talar declination** (TO)

**"Superior ORIF outcome predictors EXCEPT?"** → **male sex**

**"ORIF better than nonop EXCEPT?"** → **previous calcaneus fracture**

**"Most likely eventual subtalar fusion?"** → **male, WC, heavy labour, Böhler < 0°**

**"Highest PTOA risk?"** → **male, Sanders III, ORIF**

**"Surgical advantage over nonop?"** → **↓ post-traumatic arthritis**

**"Only undisputed difference op vs nonop?"** → **nonop ↓ wound complications**

**"Long-term difference ORIF vs nonop?"** → **ORIF ↑ additional surgery**

**"Favourable predictor after ORIF?"** → **age < 30**

**"Sanders III — counselling?"** → **initial energy / severity best correlates with PTOA**

**"Positive prognostic factor in Sanders II?"** → **Sanders classification**

**"Infection risk factor after lateral approach?"** → **smoking** (TO)

**"Sanders III with 3 cm closeable medial wound?"** → **serial I&D → ORIF** (TO)

**"Wound complication risk factor?"** → **open injury**

**"Commonest complication after extensile ORIF?"** → **delayed wound healing / dehiscence**

**"Grade 1–2 medial open, ORIF via lateral approach — infection?"** → **same as closed**

**"Burning lateral pain, injection helps — treatment?"** → **excise + bury sural nerve**

**"Superficial dehiscence after sinus tarsi in smoker?"** → **stop PT, immobilise, local wound care**

**"Approach riskiest for sural nerve?"** → **sinus tarsi**

**"Sinus tarsi vs extensile?"** → **↓ operative time**

**"Wound complications correlate with surgeon experience — fracture?"** → **calcaneus**

**"Extensile flap artery?"** → **lateral calcaneal branch of peroneal**

**"Heel pad supply?"** → **medial calcaneal branch of PTA**

**"Commonest acute concurrent pathology?"** → **SPR tear / peroneal instability**

**"Fibular fleck + lateral wall blowout?"** → **SPR disrupted, peroneals sublux anteriorly**

**"Compartment syndrome most associated with?"** → **Sanders IV** (TO)

**"Lesser-toe clawing at 3 months?"** → **missed foot compartment syndrome**

**"Bone-block distraction fusion improves?"** → **anterior ankle impingement**

**"Sanders IV nonop, block to dorsiflexion, height loss?"** → **distraction bone-block subtalar arthrodesis**

**"Bone-block indications EXCEPT?"** → **full dorsiflexion without impingement**

**"Bone block alone won't fix?"** → **peroneal impingement** (add lateral wall exostectomy)

**"Subtalar arthritis + subfibular impingement + ↓ dorsiflexion?"** → **distraction fusion + lateral wall ostectomy**

**"Factor deciding distraction fusion?"** → **anterior impingement with ↓ dorsiflexion**

**"Commonest complication of nonop DIACF?"** → **subtalar arthritis**

**"NOT seen after malunion?"** → **hindfoot valgus with subfibular impingement**

**"Failure to restore posterior facet height causes?"** → **tibiotalar impingement**

**"Talar declination < 20° causes?"** → **limited dorsiflexion**

**"Early subtalar arthritis, injection helps?"** → **Arizona / gauntlet brace**

**"Runner, + squeeze test — next?"** → **MRI** (restricted WB)

**"Recruit, heel pain after march — treatment?"** → **boot + NWB**

**"Elderly heel pain after starting to walk post-TKA?"** → **calcaneal insufficiency fracture**

**"Expected MRI in calcaneal stress fracture?"** → **marrow oedema + T1 linear fracture line**

**"Lateral foot pain, anterior process avulsion?"** → **bifurcate ligament (inversion + plantarflexion)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Capitellum Fractures

*Trauma · 7 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **1% of elbow fractures** · **6% of distal humerus fractures**

**Associated** — **radial head and / or LUCL injury in up to 60%**

### Anatomy

**Radiocapitellar joint** — key static stabiliser: **longitudinal + valgus stability**; capitellar excision → coronal instability if medial structures are torn

**LUCL relationship** — integral to posterolateral complex → **don't destabilise LUCL** during fixation

**Blood supply** — enters **posterolateral** → preserve during exposure

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — low-energy **FOOSH**; **axial compression through radial head with elbow semi-flexed** → **shear** of anterior capitellum

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — elbow pain, swelling, ± wrist pain

**Exam** — **mechanical block to flexion / extension and / or rotation**; instability; NV exam

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Best view** — **lateral elbow** (+ AP, radiocapitellar view)

**Double-arc sign** — **pathognomonic** on lateral: **subchondral bone of displaced capitellum + lateral trochlear ridge** → **coronal shear fracture extending into trochlea** · also called "double bubble"\
✗ Wrong options: sagittal lateral epicondyle · coronal medial condyle · olecranon · trans-olecranon dislocation

**CT** — low threshold; delineates anatomy, trochlear involvement, posterior comminution

### Bryan & Morrey classification (McKee modification)

| Type | Description | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I — Hahn-Steinthal** | **Large osseous capitellar fragment**, may involve trochlea | Displaced > 2 mm → **ORIF** |
| **II — Kocher-Lorenz** | **Articular cartilage shear** with **little subchondral bone** | Displaced → **excision** |
| **III — Broberg-Morrey** | **Comminuted / multifragmentary** | Displaced → **excision** (or TEA elderly) |
| **IV — McKee** | **Coronal shear including capitellum + trochlea** | **ORIF** |

![Type I (Hahn-Steinthal) capitellum fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1023-hahn-steinthal-1a.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Capitellum fracture
├─ Non-displaced (< 2 mm) type I / II → posterior splint < 3 wk
├─ Displaced type I or IV → ORIF, headless screws
│      └─ posterior comminution / medial injury → posterior ± olecranon osteotomy
├─ Displaced type II / III → fragment excision
└─ Elderly, unreconstructable + medial column instability → TEA
```

### Treatment choice – ORIF vs arthroplasty

| Indication | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Non-displaced I + II (< 2 mm)** | **Posterior splint < 3 wk** |
| **Displaced type I (> 2 mm)**, **type IV** | **ORIF** |
| Isolated type I with good bone (experienced arthroscopist) | **Arthroscopic-assisted ORIF** (70° scope; proximal anterolateral portal trocar reduces; flex past 90° to lock; screws P→A) |
| **Displaced type II, III** | **Fragment excision** |
| **Elderly, unreconstructable + medial column instability** | **TEA** |

**Healthy 33-yo coronal shear (double bubble)** — **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: excision (large fragment excision → arthritis / instability with MCL tear) · sling · capitellar replacement

**Displaced capitellum, no dislocation (diabetic 41-yo)** — **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI for ligaments (unlikely injured without dislocation) · cast · splint + motion · radial head replacement

**88-yo comminuted low distal humerus (capitellum + trochlea), poor bone** — **TEA** · TEA = choice for comminuted DHF > 70 yr; lifting limit **10–15 lb**; interposition arthroplasty for young who can't comply\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF · nonop · excise capitellum + fix trochlea

### Surgical approach & fixation

| Approach | Use | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| **Lateral column / lateral extensile (Kaplan or Kocher)** | **Isolated capitellum + capitellum into lateral trochlear ridge (type I, IV)** | **Supine**; incision over lateral epicondyle → 2 cm past radial head; strip **ECRL + distal BR** off lateral column (wrist-extensor origin), elevate brachialis off anterior capsule; triceps posterior / BR + ECRL anterior; distal ECU–anconeus (Kocher) |
| **Kaplan interval** | — | **EDC ↔ ECRB** → **PIN at risk** → **keep forearm pronated** |
| **Posterior ± olecranon osteotomy** | **Capitellum + trochlea with posterior comminution, medial injuries, distal humerus / olecranon fractures** | **Lateral decubitus**; radial + ulnar flaps |

**Isolated capitellar shear, no trochlear / posterior comminution** — **supine + Kaplan (lateral column)**\
✗ Wrong options: prone / lateral + osteotomy · triceps elevation · medial epicondylectomy

**Capitellum + small piece of lateral trochlea** — **lateral column approach**\
✗ Wrong options: Bryan-Morrey · Hotchkiss (medial) · posterior ± osteotomy (too morbid)

**PIN injury from Kaplan approach** — **can't extend thumb**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar = medial approach · AIN = can't flex thumb / index IP · brachial artery = anterior · EIP

**Fixation** — **headless compression screws** (buried beneath cartilage — avoid radial-head damage) **A→P** or **P→A**; mini-fragment screws P→A; capitellar / mini plates for proximal extension

**Headless screw most indicated** — **capitellar coronal shear (double arc)**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral condyle (pins / headed screw) · medial epicondyle (headed ± washer) · olecranon (plate / TBW) · supracondylar (plate / pins)

**Lateral approach best for** — **complete coronal shear capitellum**\
✗ Wrong options: extra-articular DHF (posterior) · proximal radial shaft (Henry / Thompson) · undisplaced radial head (nonop) · trans-olecranon (posterior)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Elbow contracture / stiffness** | **Most common**; **commonest reason for reoperation** (reoperation up to **48%**) |
| **Nonunion** | **1–11%** after ORIF |
| **Heterotopic ossification** | **4%** after ORIF |
| AVN of capitellum | Less common |
| Ulnar nerve injury, tardy ulnar palsy | — |
| Olecranon osteotomy nonunion | — |
| Instability, PTOA, cubital valgus, infection | — |

**Prognosis** — functional ROM but **residual stiffness** common; ORIF results generally favourable

**Worst outcome predictor** — **posterior comminution** (needs osteotomy / TEA)\
✗ Wrong options: single fragment > 2 cm · coronal shear 1 cm · 5 mm post-reduction displacement · lateral trochlear extension

## X vs Y

| | **Type I Hahn-Steinthal** | **Type II Kocher-Lorenz** | **Type IV McKee** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fragment | Large bony capitellum | Cartilage shell | Capitellum + trochlea |
| Sign | — | — | **Double arc** |
| Displaced → | ORIF | Excision | ORIF |

| | **Lateral column (Kaplan)** | **Posterior + olecranon osteotomy** |
|---|---|---|
| Pattern | Isolated / lateral trochlear ridge | Posterior comminution, both columns, medial injury |
| Position | **Supine** | **Lateral decubitus** |
| Risk | **PIN** (pronate forearm) | Osteotomy nonunion, ulnar nerve |

## How it's asked

**"Double-arc sign means?"** → **coronal shear fracture of capitellum (into trochlea)**

**"Bicycle fall, double arc on lateral — describe?"** → **capitellum fracture extending into trochlea**

**"Young adult with displaced capitellar shear?"** → **ORIF**

**"Elderly, comminuted distal articular fracture, poor bone?"** → **TEA**

**"Lateral approach elevating extensors off lateral column best for?"** → **complete coronal shear capitellum fracture**

**"Ideal position + approach for isolated capitellar shear?"** → **supine, Kaplan**

**"Capitellum + small lateral trochlea, headless screws — exposure?"** → **lateral column approach**

**"Complication of the approach?"** → **can't extend thumb (PIN)**

**"Headless screw most indicated for?"** → **capitellar coronal shear**

**"Most common complication after fixation?"** → **elbow flexion contracture / stiffness**

**"Most common complication requiring reoperation?"** → **elbow contracture**

**"Finding predicting worst outcome?"** → **posterior comminution**

**"Non-displaced type I or II?"** → **posterior splint < 3 wk**

**"Displaced type II (cartilage shell)?"** → **fragment excision**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Clavicle Fractures - Distal

*Trauma · 8 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Clavicle fractures** — **2.6–4%** of adult fractures · **distal third = 10–25%** of clavicle fractures

**Who** — older / osteoporotic adults; less common in children (intact periosteal sleeve)

**Associated (rare)** — floating shoulder · **scapulothoracic dissociation** (if widely distracted) · rib fracture · PTX · NV injury

### Neer classification & coracoclavicular ligament anatomy

| Stabiliser | Function | Insertion from distal clavicle |
|---|---|---|
| **AC ligament** (sup, inf, ant, post) | **Anterior–posterior** stability; **superior strongest**, then posterior (posterosuperior most important) | **\~6 mm** |
| **Trapezoid (lateral)** | Vertical | **2–3 cm** (3 cm, OB); **trapezoid tubercle — anterolateral** |
| **Conoid (medial)** | **Most important for vertical stability** | **4–4.5 cm** (4.5 cm, OB); **conoid tubercle — posteromedial** |
| Dynamic | Deltoid + trapezius | — |

**✗ Conoid traps** — at 2–3 cm · anterolateral to trapezoid · trapezoid as main vertical restraint

**Displacement depends on** — **location** (extra- vs intra-articular), **pattern** (simple vs comminuted), **CC ligament integrity**, **age**

**CC ligaments** — conoid + trapezoid = primary resistance to **superior displacement of medial fragment**

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **direct compressive blow / fall onto point of shoulder** (as for midshaft)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — anterior shoulder pain, swelling, AC deformity, **skin tenting** (impending open)

**Suprascapular nerve** — at risk → **weak ER with arm adducted**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Use |
|---|---|
| **Upright AP of both shoulders** | Displacement vs normal side |
| Axillary lateral | AP displacement |
| **Zanca — 15° cephalic tilt** | Superior / inferior displacement; ± **5–10 lb weight** in hand |
| CT (3D) | Displacement, shortening, comminution, articular extension, nonunion |

**Superior displacement of medial clavicle relative to coracoid** — **CC (conoid) disruption**

### Neer classification

| Type | Fracture | CC ligaments | Stability | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Extra-articular, lateral** to CC | **Intact** | **Stable**, minimal displacement | Nonop |
| **IIA** | **Medial** to CC | **Both intact** (on distal fragment) | **Unstable**; **nonunion up to 56%** nonop | **ORIF** |
| **IIB** | (1) **Between** CC — **conoid torn, trapezoid intact** · (2) **lateral** to CC — **both torn** (proposed "IIC") | Conoid torn | **Unstable**; **nonunion 30–45%** | **ORIF** |
| **III** | **Intra-articular** into AC joint, lateral to CC | Intact | Stable → later **AC arthritis** | Nonop |
| **IV** | **Physeal** (skeletally immature); clavicle pops out of periosteal sleeve superiorly | Intact | Stable | Nonop |
| **V** | **Comminuted**; inferior fragment stays on CC | Intact | Usually **unstable** | **ORIF** |

**Stable** — **I, III, IV** · **Unstable** — **IIA, IIB, V**

**Conoid injury on X-ray** — **lateral fracture with superiorly displaced medial fragment**\
✗ Wrong options: conoid still on butterfly fragment · medial to CC · undisplaced

![Neer type IIB – fracture between conoid and trapezoid](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12770-2b.jpg)

### Alternative classification

| Type | Definition | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | Non-displaced, CC intact (A1 extra-, A2 intra-articular) | Nonop |
| **B** | Displaced, CC intact (B1 extra-articular, B2 comminuted) | Nonop vs op |
| **C** | **Displaced, CC torn** (C1 extra-, C2 intra-articular) | **Op** |

### Differential

**Mimics** — clavicle shaft fracture · paediatric medial / distal physeal injury · **AC separation**

## 5 Management

```
Distal clavicle fracture
├─ Stable (Neer I, III, IV), minimally displaced, children → sling, ROM 2–4 wk
└─ Unstable (IIA, IIB, V) / displaced in athlete → ORIF
       └─ tiny distal fragment → hook plate or CC reconstruction
```

### Treatment indications – sling vs ORIF / CC reconstruction

| Absolute | Relative |
|---|---|
| **Open / impending open (skin tenting)** | **Unstable pattern (IIA, IIB, V)** |
| **Subclavian artery / vein injury** | Brachial plexus injury (66% recover spontaneously) |
| **Floating shoulder** (+ scapular neck > 10 mm displaced) | Closed head injury, seizures, polytrauma |
| **Symptomatic nonunion** | — |

**Minimally displaced, 56-yo golfer** — **sling → gentle ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: IM pin · bone stimulator · ORIF · distal clavicle resection

**Displaced, professional motocross athlete** — **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: sling · Kenny-Howard (skin breakdown) · smooth K-wires (migration) · Weaver-Dunn (unnecessary with good bone)

**Displaced, wants to avoid second operation** — **small-fragment plate ± CC reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: distal clavicle resection (instability) · transacromial wires (removal / migration) · CC screw (removal) · hook plate (removal for acromial osteolysis)

### Sling immobilisation

**Technique** — sling or figure-of-8 (**no difference** in function / cosmesis); **no reduction attempt**; ROM at **2–4 wk**; strengthening **6–10 wk**

**Outcomes** — nonunion **\~15%** (Neer II up to 56%); poorer cosmesis

### Open reduction internal fixation

| Construct | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Locking / precontoured / LC-DCP plate** | Needs **large enough distal fragment** for multiple locking screws; **≥ 3–4 bicortical screws medially** |
| **Hook plate** | When **distal fragment too small**; hook **posterior to AC joint, far lateral**; correct depth; **needs removal (2nd surgery)** — acromial osteolysis |
| Others | AC-spanning fixation, transacromial wires, TBW, IM screw, **CC repair / reconstruction**, modified Weaver-Dunn (excisable fragment + CA ligament) |

**ORIF advantages** — ↑ union, faster union, less overhead pain, ↓ symptomatic malunion, better cosmesis

**ORIF disadvantages** — further procedures, symptomatic hardware, infection

**Rehab** — sling 7–10 d → active motion; strengthen \~6 wk; sport \~3 mo; hardware removal after 3 mo

## 6 Complications

| Nonoperative | Operative |
|---|---|
| **Nonunion \~15%** — comminution, displacement, Z deformity, **female, older, smoker**, distal > middle third → asymptomatic: leave · symptomatic: **ORIF + graft** | **Hardware prominence** (\~30% request removal; superior plates worst) |
| **AC arthritis** — **Neer I + III** → **distal clavicle resection** | **Hardware removal** — commonest with **hook plates** |
| — | **NV injury 3%** (superior plates → subclavian penetration), **subclavian thrombosis** |
| — | Nonunion **1–5%** · infection \~4.8% · mechanical failure \~1.4% · PTX |
| — | **Adhesive capsulitis 4%** needing surgery |

## X vs Y

| | **Neer IIA** | **Neer IIB** |
|---|---|---|
| Fracture vs CC | **Medial** to CC | **Between** (or lateral to) CC |
| Conoid | **Intact** | **Torn** |
| Trapezoid | Intact | Intact (or torn) |
| Nonunion nonop | **Up to 56%** | **30–45%** |

| | **Neer IIB distal clavicle fracture** | **AC separation (type III)** |
|---|---|---|
| CC injury | **Conoid only** | **Conoid + trapezoid** |

| | **Conoid** | **Trapezoid** |
|---|---|---|
| Distance | **4–4.5 cm** | **2–3 cm** |
| Tubercle | **Posteromedial** | Anterolateral |
| Role | **Main vertical stabiliser** | Vertical |

## How it's asked

**"X-ray indicating conoid injury?"** → **lateral fracture with superiorly displaced medial fragment**

**"Conoid ligament — true?"** → **4–4.5 cm medial, posteromedial to trapezoid tubercle, main vertical stabiliser**

**"Neer IIB?"** → **between CC ligaments, conoid torn, trapezoid intact**

**"Fracture with ligament violation → vertical instability?"** → **Neer IIB**

**"Wants to avoid a second operation?"** → **small-fragment plate ± CC reconstruction**

**"Minimally displaced, golfer?"** → **sling + gentle ROM**

**"Displaced in professional athlete?"** → **ORIF**

**"RELATIVE indication for surgery?"** → **unstable fracture pattern**\
✗ Wrong options: tenting · subclavian vein injury · symptomatic nonunion · floating shoulder = absolute

**"Hook plate downside?"** → **removal needed (acromial osteolysis)**

**"Nonunion risk in IIA nonop?"** → **up to 56%**

**"AC arthritis after Neer I / III?"** → **distal clavicle resection**

**"Nerve at risk, weak ER in adduction?"** → **suprascapular**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Clavicle Fractures - Midshaft

*Trauma · 9 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1 / 1000 / yr** · **2.6–4%** of adult fractures

**Location** — **middle third = 75–80%** (80–85%, OB) of clavicle fractures

**Who** — young active **males < 30**

### Anatomy

**Shape** — S-shaped; flat laterally, tubular centrally, prismatic medially

**Weakest point** — **junction of outer + middle thirds**: thinnest, transition in curvature + cross-section, **only part without muscle / ligament reinforcement**

**SC joint stabilisers** — posterior capsular ligament (key), anterior SC ligament, costoclavicular ligament, intra-articular disc · **AC joint** — CC + AC ligaments

**CC insertions** — trapezoid **3 cm**, conoid **4.5 cm** from distal end

**Subclavian vessels** — behind + below clavicle at **medial–middle junction**; **vein closest**, anterior to artery + plexus

**Supraclavicular nerves** — vertical cutaneous nerves over clavicle → injured by plating

### Deforming forces

| Fragment | Pulled | By |
|---|---|---|
| **Medial** | **Posterosuperior** | **Sternocleidomastoid** |
| **Lateral** | **Inferomedial** | **Pectoralis major + weight of arm** (± deltoid) |

**Shortening** — pec major pulls medially; shortening ↓ deltoid lever arm

**Open fracture** — medial fragment **buttonholes through platysma**

![Weight of the arm pulls the lateral fragment down](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1011-weight-down.jpg)

### Associated injuries

**Medical** — **pneumothorax**, **closed head injury**

**Orthopaedic** — **rib fracture = commonest associated fracture (TO)** · floating shoulder · **scapulothoracic dissociation** (wide distraction, widened scapula–spine interval, plexus / vascular injury) · NV injury

**Brachial plexus injury (TO)** — commonest with **medial-third** fractures

**Open clavicle fracture** — **closed head injury 65%**, pulmonary 75%, spine 35%\
✗ Wrong options: STD · calcaneus · pelvis · open tibia

**Ipsilateral 1st–4th rib fractures** — ↑ odds of **late displacement > 100%** of nonop fracture (SSSC instability)\
✗ Wrong options: plexus palsy · contralateral scapula · deltoid atony · head injury

**Weak pulses after fall (arteriogram)** — **axillary artery** (3rd part, distal to subscapular) — collaterals keep hand perfused

## 2 Mechanism

**Fall onto lateral shoulder** — **85%** (Orthobullets) · **87%** (TO) · direct blow 7% · **FOOSH only 6% (TO)**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — pop / crack, anterior shoulder pain

**Exam** — tenderness, crepitus, deformity, **skin tenting** (impending open) · check subclavian vessels + plexus

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View / study | Use |
|---|---|
| **Upright AP clavicle** | Supine **under-estimates** displacement |
| **15° cephalic tilt (Zanca)** | Removes scapular overlap |
| Shoulder series | Proximal humerus, scapula injuries |
| **Upright CXR** | **Shortening vs contralateral**; PTX |
| CT (± contrast) | Comminution, shortening, nonunion; **vascular injury**, medial fracture, **SC dislocation** |

**Findings** — medial fragment up, lateral fragment down, shortening

**Measures** — **shortening > 2 cm** → ↓ strength + endurance · **> 100% displacement** → nonunion risk

### Classification

| Displacement | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **< 100%** (non-displaced) | Nonop |
| **> 100%** (displaced, no cortical contact) | Op considered |

| AO type | Subtypes | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **A — simple** | A1 spiral, A2 oblique, A3 transverse | Nonop vs op |
| **B — wedge** | B1 spiral, B2 bending, B3 fragmented | Nonop vs op |
| **C — complex** | C1 complex spiral, **C2 segmental**, C3 irregular | Op |

**Allman** — medial / diaphyseal / lateral

### Differential

| Mimic | Clue |
|---|---|
| Distal-third fracture | Older, osteoporotic; ↑ CC distance |
| **SC dislocation** | High energy; dysphagia, stridor, asymmetric pulses → **serendipity view / CT** |
| AC injury | Pain + prominence over AC; Zanca / axillary |

## 5 Management

```
Midshaft clavicle fracture
├─ < 100% displaced, < 2 cm short, closed, NV intact → sling (≡ figure-of-8)
├─ Open / tenting / subclavian injury / floating shoulder → ORIF (absolute)
└─ > 100% displaced, > 2 cm short, comminuted / Z, segmental, polytrauma → ORIF (relative)
```

### Operative vs nonoperative – indications

| Absolute | Relative / fracture-specific |
|---|---|
| **Open** | **Shortening > 2 cm** (> 15 mm, OB) |
| **Displaced with skin tenting** | **Displacement > 100% / > 2 cm (TO)** |
| **Subclavian artery / vein injury** | **Increasing comminution (> 3 fragments) (TO)** |
| **Floating shoulder** | **Segmental** |
| — | Scapular malposition / winging, obvious deformity (TO) |
| — | **Bilateral**, **polytrauma**, head injury, seizures, brachial plexus injury (66% recover) |

**Comminuted displaced clavicle + bilateral plateau fractures** — **ORIF plate**\
✗ Wrong options: figure-of-8 · pins · sling

**TO true statement (TO)** — **increasing comminution is an indication for surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: fixation rarely required · figure-of-8 treatment of choice · > 2 cm shortening remodels

### Operative vs nonoperative – outcomes

| Outcome | ORIF vs nonop |
|---|---|
| **Nonunion** | **↓** (op 1–5% / \~2–2.5%; union > 94%) vs nonop **\~15%** |
| **Symptomatic malunion** | **↓** |
| Time to union | **16.4 vs 28.4 wk** (faster) |
| **Constant + DASH** | **Better at all time points to 1 yr** (COTS) |
| **Strength / endurance** | Nonop ↓ to \~**80%** of other side |
| **Shoulder ROM** | **Same** |
| Cosmesis / satisfaction | Better with ORIF |
| Cost | **↑ with ORIF** |
| Secondary surgery | ↑ with ORIF (hardware removal commonest) |

**Pooled recent literature** — ORIF **↓ nonunion + symptomatic malunion**; **no clinically significant differences** in strength, ROM, return to activity, or unplanned surgery (contrasts with COTS 2007) — read the options

**Primary goal of ORIF** — **↓ nonunion**\
✗ Wrong options: faster pain-free union · no cosmetic deformity · elite strength · fewer complications

**Nonop, 1 yr outcome** — **↓ strength + endurance**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ motion · instability

**3 cm shortening — similar between op + nonop** — **shoulder ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion · strength · endurance

**Lowest nonunion + malunion (2.6 cm short)** — **ORIF with plate**\
✗ Wrong options: pins · ex-fix · sling

### Sling immobilisation

**Indications** — < 2 cm shortening / displacement, < 1 cm SSSC displacement, closed, NV intact, low demand

**Sling ≡ figure-of-8** — **no difference** in healing time, rate, alignment, function, cosmesis; **figure-of-8 → more pain, shortening, ↓ compliance**\
✗ Wrong options: figure-of-8 advantages

**No reduction attempt** · passive ROM 2 wk · strengthening 6 wk · sport **4–6 mo**

### Plating technique

| Plate | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|
| **Superior** | **Higher load to failure**, stronger with inferior comminution, less deltoid detachment | **Prominent**, **↑ subclavian / neurovascular risk** |
| **Anteroinferior** | **Less prominent**, **longer screws** (wider front-to-back laterally), ↓ subclavian risk | **Detach part of deltoid origin** |
| **Dual mini-fragment (2.7 sup + 2.4 ant)** | **↓ hardware removal (0–3.7%)**; equivalent (not superior) biomechanics | Cost; union + reduction same |

**Single 3.5 mm superior vs dual** — **single has more hardware removal / reoperation**\
✗ Wrong options: dual 10× stronger · dual more reoperation · union time differs

**Hardware removal risk** — **low BMI**, **female**\
✗ Wrong options: age · male · dual plates

**Plates** — LC-DCP 3.5 mm, reconstruction 3.5 mm, 2.0–2.7 mm for dual

**Rehab** — sling 7–10 d → active; strengthen \~6 wk; sport \~3 mo

### Intramedullary fixation

**Pros** — small incision, less stripping, **spares supraclavicular nerves**, **best for simple patterns**

**Cons** — **can't lock → no length / rotation control**, biomechanically inferior, migration, irritation, removal at \~6 mo

**Contraindicated** — **substantial comminution, segmental**

**TO EXCEPT trap (TO)** — "**IM nailing gives better union than plates in comminuted fractures**" = false

**Implant** — nail ≈ **30–40% of midshaft diameter**; cannulated screws, TENS, Hagie pin

### Nonunion management

**Symptomatic atrophic nonunion** — **ORIF compression plate + bone graft**\
✗ Wrong options: stimulator · figure-of-8 · CRPP · IM nail

**Graft** — **local autograft ≈ iliac crest union rates** (✓ true)

**After failed plating** — biology (soft tissue, approaches, infection) vs mechanics (malreduction, weak / short plate, wrong stiffness) → dual mini-fragment revision

**Commonest reoperation after ORIF** — **implant removal**\
✗ Wrong options: infection

## 6 Complications

### Nonunion risk factors

| Factor | Note |
|---|---|
| **Displacement > 100%** | **Strongest predictor** (Jørgensen) |
| **Comminution / Z-deformity** | ✓ |
| **Shortening > 2 cm** (> 15–20 mm) | ✓ |
| **Female** | ✓ (highest in options) |
| **Advanced age** | ✓ |
| Smoking | ✓ |
| Not risk factors | Sling vs figure-of-8, early ROM, male, dominant arm, young age, simple pattern, > 50% displacement, 1 cm shortening, diaphyseal location |

**Nonunion rates** — nonop displaced **\~15%** (OB, Orthobullets) · **10–15%** · historic **1%** · 7–15% vs op 2% · overall 5–6% · **0.1–13% with 85% of nonunions in middle third (TO)** — read the options

**Highest nonunion by location** — **middle third** (TO, most nonunions) · **lateral third** has highest *rate* vs diaphysis (OB)

**6-wk predictors** — **fracture-site motion, no callus, DASH > 40** → 0 factors: 3% · 2–3 factors: **60% nonunion**

**Elderly comminuted displaced** — highest nonop nonunion risk = **comminution + displacement + age**

**Nonop displaced, shortened — commonest complication** — **nonunion**\
✗ Wrong options: skin necrosis · CRPS 2% · SC / AC arthrosis 4–6%

| Nonoperative | Operative |
|---|---|
| **Nonunion \~15%** → symptomatic: **plate + graft** | **Supraclavicular nerve numbness — most common** (83% at 2 wk, \~50% at 1 yr) |
| **Malunion \~20%**: shortening > 3 cm, angulation > 30°, translation > 1 cm → overhead fatigue, **thoracic outlet syndrome**, strap problems → **osteotomy + graft** | **Hardware prominence** — **8–30%** request removal (superior plates) |
| ↓ strength + endurance (> 2 cm short; Lazarides > 18 mm men, > 14 mm women) | Subclavian injury / thrombosis (superior plates) |
| Poor cosmesis | Nonunion 1–5%, infection \~4.8% (drug use, diabetes, prior surgery), failure \~1.4%, PTX, adhesive capsulitis 4% |

## X vs Y

| | **Superior plate** | **Anteroinferior plate** |
|---|---|---|
| Strength | **Higher load to failure** | — |
| Prominence | **More** | Less |
| Screws | Shorter | **Longer** |
| Subclavian risk | **Higher** | Lower |
| Deltoid | Less detachment | **Detach some deltoid** |

| | **Plate** | **IM device** |
|---|---|---|
| Comminuted / segmental | **Yes** | **No** |
| Length / rotation control | Yes | **No (unlocked)** |
| Supraclavicular nerves | At risk | Spared |

## How it's asked

**"Nonunion risk factors with nonop?"** → **displacement + comminution**

**"Highest nonunion rate factor?"** → **female gender**

**"73-yo highest nonunion risk?"** → **comminution + displacement + advanced age**

**"Predisposes young male to nonunion?"** → **fracture displacement**

**"NOT a nonunion risk factor?"** → **sling vs figure-of-8**

**"Best estimate of nonunion, displaced, nonop?"** → **15%**

**"Risk factor in mountain biker?"** → **Z-type pattern**

**"FALSE except (TO)?"** → **highest incidence of nonunion in middle third**\
✗ Wrong options: FOOSH commonest · plexus with middle third · humerus commonest associated

**"True about clavicle fracture (TO)?"** → **increasing comminution indicates surgery**

**"Comminuted displaced + bilateral plateau fractures?"** → **ORIF plate**

**"Nonop at 1 yr?"** → **↓ strength + endurance**

**"Figure-of-8 vs sling?"** → **no advantage**

**"2.6 cm shortening — lowest nonunion + malunion?"** → **plate ORIF**

**"Op vs nonop — true?"** → **nonop ↑ symptomatic malunion / nonunion**

**"COTS — true?"** → **better Constant + DASH at all time points**

**"ORIF outcome at 1 yr?"** → **↑ functional scores**

**"ORIF vs sling — nonunion + cost?"** → **↓ nonunion, ↑ cost**

**"Similar between op + nonop?"** → **shoulder ROM**

**"ORIF statement most appropriate (22-yo, > 100%)?"** → **improves functional outcome**\
✗ Wrong options: union > 95% nonop

**"Primary goal of ORIF?"** → **reduce nonunion**

**"Pooled literature — plating gives?"** → **↓ nonunion**

**"FALSE about surgical fixation (TO)?"** → **IM nail better union in comminuted**

**"Anteroinferior plate disadvantage?"** → **detach deltoid origin**

**"Risk for reoperation after dual plating in thin runner?"** → **low BMI**

**"Single 3.5 superior vs dual mini?"** → **single → more hardware removal**

**"Painful nonunion at 9 months?"** → **compression plating** (+ graft)

**"Nonunion repair graft?"** → **local ≈ iliac crest**

**"Commonest association with open clavicle fracture?"** → **closed head injury**

**"Predicts late displacement of nonop fracture?"** → **ipsilateral 1st–4th rib fractures**

**"Arteriogram after clavicle fracture, weak pulses — vessel?"** → **axillary artery**

**"Most common complication of plating?"** → **supraclavicular nerve numbness**
