# Coronoid Fractures

*Trauma · 10 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **10–15% of elbow injuries**

**Meaning** — essentially **pathognomonic of an elbow instability episode**; isolated fractures less common than thought; usually with dislocation → recurrent instability

### Anatomy & biomechanics

**Coronoid** — **anterior buttress of greater sigmoid notch** → resists **posterior subluxation**; primary resistor of subluxation / dislocation

**Tip** — **intra-articular**; **nothing inserts on it** · **anterior capsule 6 mm** distal to tip\
✗ Wrong options: avulsion

**Anteromedial facet** — **varus stability**; critical weight-bearing ulnohumeral surface

**Sublime tubercle** — **anterior bundle of MUCL**, **18 mm** distal to tip → basal fractures amplify instability

**LUCL insertion** — **crista supinatoris** of proximal ulna

**MUCL origin** — anteroinferior **medial epicondyle**

## 2 Mechanism

**Shear** — distal humerus driven against coronoid during **severe varus stress or posterior subluxation**

| Mechanism | Fracture | Associated | Instability |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Varus + posteromedial rotation** (pronation, axial load) | **Anteromedial facet** | **LCL avulsed from humerus**; **radial head + MCL spared** | **Varus PMRI** |
| **Valgus + supination (posterolateral)** | **Small tip (transverse)** | **Radial head fracture + LCL**, posterolateral dislocation | **PLRI / terrible triad** |
| Olecranon fracture-dislocation | **Large basal** fragment | Trans-olecranon / trans-ulnar basal | — |

**Anteromedial facet sequence** — **LCL fails first**, then medial coronoid compressed against / under medial trochlea

**Isolated coronoid fracture (no radial head fracture)** — think **varus PMRI**\
✗ Wrong options: PLRI · valgus anterolateral · posterior · anterior

**Trans-ulnar basal coronoid fracture-dislocation** — basal coronoid displaced from olecranon + metaphysis → **worse ROM, PROMs, more complications + reoperation**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain, swelling, deformity; forearm / wrist pain → associated injury

**PMRI clue** — **crepitus / "crispy rice cereal" + pain moving the elbow with arm abducted** (varus stress); history of dislocation, clunk, spontaneous reduction; LCL tenderness

**Gravity-assisted varus stress test** — arm **abducted 90°**, neutral rotation, flex + extend → pain, grinding, instability → **most sensitive / specific for PMRI**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral pivot shift · milking · chair rise · posterior drawer (PLRI / valgus tests)

**Exam** — varus / valgus stress (difficult but key), document ROM + crepitus, NV

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — AP + lateral; overlap makes reading hard

**PMRI signs** — **lateral joint widening, medial joint collapse** on AP · **crescent sign** on lateral (depressed anteromedial facet)

**CT** — **next step for anteromedial facet fracture** (size, comminution, articular extent)\
✗ Wrong options: EUA · traction views · immobilise 3 wk · immediate terrible-triad surgery

![Anteromedial facet fracture on 3D CT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1020-anteromedial-facet-fracture-3d-ct.jpg)

### Regan & Morrey classification (height)

| Type | Fragment |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Tip** |
| **II** | **≤ 50%** of height |
| **III** | **> 50%** of height |

![Regan and Morrey classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1020-shows-the-regan-and-morrey-classification-of-coronoid-fractu.jpg)

### O'Driscoll classification (location)

| Type | Location | Association (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Tip** | **Terrible triad** (commonest) |
| **II** | **Anteromedial facet** | **Varus PMRI** |
| **III** | **Base** | **Olecranon / proximal ulna fracture-dislocation** |

| Anteromedial subtype (TO) | Involves |
|---|---|
| **1** | Anteromedial **rim** only |
| **2** | Rim + **tip** |
| **3** | Rim + **sublime tubercle** (± tip) → also **valgus instability** (MCL insertion) |

## 5 Management

```
Coronoid fracture
├─ Minimally displaced + stable elbow (any type) → brief splint, early ROM
├─ Anteromedial facet (PMRI) → medial approach ORIF + LCL repair
│      └─ still unstable → hinged ex-fix
└─ Terrible triad / olecranon fracture-dislocation → fix coronoid (lateral / posterior)
```

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Approach / fixation |
|---|---|
| **Minimally displaced, stable elbow** | **Nonop** — brief immobilisation, early ROM |
| **Type I–III with persistent instability** | **Medial ORIF** |
| **PMRI (anteromedial facet)** — most need surgery; residual subluxation / crepitus = dynamic incongruity | **Medial buttress plate / screws + LCL repair** (± sublime tubercle / MCL fix) |
| **Olecranon fracture-dislocation, terrible triad** | **Posterior approach** |
| Large fragments, poor bone, revisions | **Hinged external fixator** |

### Anteromedial facet – varus PMRI treatment

**Medial approach** — between **two heads of FCU**, or more anteriorly through **flexor-pronator split**

**Fixation by type** — **type I tip → cerclage / No. 5 suture through ulnar drill holes** · **II / III → retrograde cannulated screws or plate** · **anteromedial facet → buttress plate / pins + lateral ligament repair**

**Still unstable after coronoid ORIF** — repair **LUCL at crista supinatoris** · LUCL injured in **83% (15/18)**\
✗ Wrong options: MUCL origin · radial tuberosity = biceps · sublime tubercle (MCL rarely torn) · coronoid tip = capsule

**Splinting** — **LUCL torn (+ pivot shift) → forearm neutral** · **LUCL intact → full supination** (tightens medial side)

**Untreated / nonunion** — **varus laxity** persists → severe progressive arthritis\
✗ Wrong options: valgus laxity (MUCL intact) · milking · rotation block (radial head fine) · ulnar paraesthesia

**Rehab** — thermoplastic splint 90°, forearm neutral; **block terminal 30° extension 2–4 wk**; **avoid shoulder abduction 4–6 wk** (varus moment); early active motion (dynamic compression of joint)

### Terrible triad – coronoid tip & LCL

**Terrible triad** — **posterior / posterolateral dislocation + radial head fracture + coronoid (tip, transverse) fracture**; valgus + supination; MCL fails last if at all

**Coronoid tip fracture associated with** — **radial head fracture + posterolateral ulnohumeral dislocation**\
✗ Wrong options: PMRI · capitellum · trans-olecranon · MCL rupture

**Terrible triad, 50% coronoid, radial head replaced + LCL repaired → redislocates** — **coronoid not fixed (from lateral approach)** · fix via **ulnar bone-tunnel sutures or dorsal → volar screws**\
✗ Wrong options: immobilisation length / position (flexion + pronation stabilises lateral side) · medial buttress plate · MCL repair

### Olecranon fracture-dislocation

**Posterior approach** — mobilise olecranon fragment to reach coronoid → **fix coronoid first**, then ulna with dorsal plate

## 6 Complications

**Recurrent instability** (esp. medial) · **stiffness** · **PTOA** · **HO** · **early failure** = unrecognised / unrepaired instability

**Prognosis** — high complication + reoperation rates

## X vs Y

| | **Anteromedial facet (PMRI)** | **Tip (terrible triad, PLRI)** |
|---|---|---|
| Force | **Varus + posteromedial** | **Valgus + supination** |
| Radial head | **Intact** | **Fractured** |
| Ligament | **LCL (humeral avulsion)** | LCL ± MCL |
| Test | **Gravity-assisted varus stress** | Lateral pivot shift, chair rise, posterior drawer |
| Approach | **Medial** (buttress) + LCL repair | Lateral (± posterior) |

| | **Regan-Morrey** | **O'Driscoll** |
|---|---|---|
| Basis | **Height** (tip, ≤ 50%, > 50%) | **Location** (tip, anteromedial, base) |
| Strength | Simple | **Recognises anteromedial facet** |

## How it's asked

**"Dislocation, subtle coronoid, intact radial head, CT anteromedial — association?"** → **varus PMRI** (TO)

**"Anteromedial facet fracture, intact radial head — exam finding?"** → **varus PMRI**

**"Varus + posteromedial mechanism causes?"** → **anteromedial coronoid fracture**

**"Posteromedial instability 2 months after dislocation — fracture?"** → **anteromedial facet**

**"Isolated coronoid fracture — instability?"** → **varus posteromedial**

**"Mechanism for anteromedial facet?"** → **varus + posteromedial rotation**

**"Provocative test likely positive?"** → **gravity-assisted varus stress**

**"Valgus stress + milking positive, pivot shift negative, splinted supinated?"** → **PMRI with intact LUCL**

**"Injury most leading to PMRI?"** → **anteromedial facet fracture**

**"PMRI — fracture needing bony + soft-tissue fixation?"** → **anteromedial facet**

**"Nonunion of anteromedial facet — persistent sign?"** → **varus laxity**

**"Coronoid type in terrible triad?"** → **tip** (TO)

**"Terrible triad redislocates after RHA + LCL repair?"** → **coronoid not fixed (lateral approach)**

**"Coronoid tip on CT after valgus-supination fall — associated?"** → **radial head fracture + posterolateral dislocation**

**"Unstable after anteromedial facet ORIF — next insertion to address?"** → **crista supinatoris (LUCL)**

**"Anteromedial facet on X-ray — next step?"** → **CT**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Distal Femur Fractures

*Trauma · 11 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **3–6% of femur fractures** · < 1% of all fractures

**Bimodal** — **young males, high energy** (displaced) · **elderly osteopenic females, low-energy fall**

**Open** — **5–10%** of supracondylar fractures

### Anatomy

**Anatomic axis** — **6–11° valgus**; medial condyle extends further distal

**Trapezoidal cross-section** — **lateral cortex slopes \~10°**, **medial cortex \~25°** → straight AP hides long medial screws → check with **AP in 30° internal rotation**

**Condyles** — posterior halves lie **behind** posterior shaft cortex

![Trapezoidal distal femur](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1041-distal-femur-anatomy.jpg)

### Deforming forces

| Muscle | Deformity |
|---|---|
| **Quadriceps + hamstrings** | **Shortening** |
| **Adductor magnus** | **Varus or valgus** (depends on comminution vs adductor tubercle) |
| **Gastrocnemius** | **Extension / apex-posterior** (recurvatum) + condylar rotation with intercondylar split |

## 2 Mechanism

**Young** — high energy, displaced · **Elderly** — fall from standing, osteoporotic, less displaced

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — distal thigh tenderness, swelling, varus / valgus deformity, effusion if intra-articular, open wounds

**Vascular** — **popliteal artery** at risk with displacement; femoral vessels tethered at **adductor hiatus**

### Vascular assessment (ABI)

| ABI | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **> 0.9** | **NPV 99%** (Orthobullets, OB) · **100%** (one OB explanation) → serial exams |
| **< 0.9** | **95% sensitive, 97% specific** for major arterial injury → **angiography / CTA** |
| 0.9–1.3 | Normal |

**Pulse returns after traction but "feels different"** — **ABI** next\
✗ Wrong options: serial exams (inaccurate) · angiography first (time / dye risk) · duplex (operator / availability) · compartment pressures

**GSW, ABI 0.81** — **CTA**

**Angiography also for hard signs** — pulselessness, expanding haematoma, massive bleeding

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| AP + lateral | Standard |
| Traction views | Characterise (painful) |
| Whole femur, contralateral femur | Associated injuries; templating |
| **CT** | **Intra-articular extension**, **Hoffa**, notch osteochondral fragments; after ex-fix |
| Elderly | Look for **pre-existing knee OA** |

**Commonest intra-articular split** — **sagittal**

### Hoffa (coronal-plane) fractures

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Incidence in supra- / intercondylar fractures | **38%** (X-ray, Orthobullets) · **\~40%** (CT, OB) |
| Condyle | **Lateral 80%** |
| Missed on X-ray | **up to 31%** (\~30%) |
| Best seen | **Lateral view / CT** (✗ Wrong options: AP) |
| Association | **Open fractures \~3× more likely**; high energy (✗ Wrong options: ground-level fall) |
| OTA | **33B3** |
| Fixation | **Anterior → posterior lag screws across lateral condyle** (interfragmentary) |
| Implant to avoid | **95° blade plate / DCS contraindicated** |

**CT after motorcycle supracondylar fracture — likely finding** — **coronal fracture of lateral femoral condyle**\
✗ Wrong options: tibial eminence · sagittal MFC · Schatzker I · axial MFC

![Hoffa fracture on lateral radiograph](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1041-hoffa-fracture-2.jpg)

### AO / OTA 33 classification

| Type | Definition |
|---|---|
| **33A** | **Extra-articular** |
| **33B** | **Partial articular** — part of joint still attached to shaft; **B3 = coronal (Hoffa)** |
| **33C** | **Complete articular** — articular block separated from shaft |

![AO/OTA classification of distal femur fractures](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1041-ao-ota-classification-of-distal-femur-fractures.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Distal femur fracture
├─ Unstable polytrauma / bad soft tissue / contamination → spanning ex-fix → staged
├─ Extra-articular or simple articular → retrograde nail or lateral locked plate
│      └─ intra-articular → reduce + lag articular block first
├─ Partial articular (33B) → lag screws + buttress plate (medial approach for B2/B3)
└─ Elderly + OA / unreconstructable → distal femoral replacement (immediate WB)
```

### Treatment options

| Option | Indications | Key facts |
|---|---|---|
| **Hinged knee brace** (rare) | Stable non-displaced, non-ambulatory, prohibitive risk | 6–8 wk; ROM 3–4 wk; stiffness, wounds |
| **Ex-fix** | Temporising (polytrauma, soft tissue, contamination); definitive for severe open / comminuted | Definitive union **92–100% at 4–6 mo**; keep pins out of plate zone; narrow anterior safe zone |
| **ORIF** | Displaced, intra-articular, **osteoporotic periprosthetic**, nonunion | **Fixed-angle for metaphyseal comminution** (non-fixed → **varus collapse**); nonunion up to 18% |
| **Retrograde IMN** | **Extra-articular**, **simple intra-articular**, periprosthetic with **open-box** femoral component | **≥ 4 cm intact distal femur** (traditional); more symmetric callus, ↓ malunion, ↑ satisfaction |
| **TKA / DFR** | **Pre-existing OA**, **unreconstructable**, **loose TKA**, low demand | **Immediate WB**, better ambulation, ↓ nonunion; same complications + reoperations; ↓ longevity |

**Up to 20% wheelchair-bound** at 1 yr after internal fixation (elderly)

### Locked plating principles & implant choice

**Locking plates** — screw–plate interface (angular stability), no bone–plate contact needed → **comminuted metaphyseal / diaphyseal**, **short segments**, **osteoporotic periprosthetic**\
✗ Wrong options: simple diaphyseal · displaced articular unless lagged first

**Lag first, then lock** — locked screws can't reduce or compress → **reduce + lag articular block, then attach to shaft**

**Osteoporotic 68-yo, metaphyseal comminution + sagittal split** — **lag screws then locking plate**\
✗ Wrong options: non-locking plate · retrograde nail · ex-fix

**Lateral locking plate NOT for** — **33B partial articular (isolated medial condyle)** → **medial approach, lag + buttress plate**

| Implant | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Locking compression plate** | **Best control of coronal-plane + small distal segments**; multiplanar screws for Hoffa; may be **too stiff → nonunion / failure** |
| **95° angled blade plate** | Precise insertion needed; **greater torsional strength** than LCP but **more subsidence, lower load to failure**; not C3 or Hoffa; poor in osteoporosis |
| **Dynamic condylar screw** | Sagittal alignment forgiving (rotates on barrel); **removes most bone** |
| **Dual plating (lateral + medial)** | **Greatest axial + torsional stiffness** — best for **medial comminution in osteoporosis** |
| **Nail-plate combination** | **↓ reoperation** vs lateral plate alone (LLP nonunion \~20%); same infection; allows immediate WB |

**Plate failure risk** — **short working length**, plate-screw density **> 0.5**, short plates, non-locked diaphyseal screws, **stainless steel may be worse than titanium**, improper bridging\
✗ Wrong options: early ROM · bridging · density < 0.5

**Percutaneous submuscular precontoured locking plate** — ↓ infection + nonunion, preserves perfusion, **but ↑ malreduction (rotational)**

**Intercondylar screw length** — **AP in 30° internal rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: ER view · full extension AP

### Approaches

| Approach | Use |
|---|---|
| **Lateral** | All types; arthrotomy for joint |
| **Swashbuckler (modified anterior)** | Extra-articular / lateral; submuscular plate |
| **Lateral parapatellar** | **Complex articular extension**, Hoffa; evert patella |
| **Medial parapatellar** | Complex **medial condyle** |
| **Medial** | **B2, B3**; medial plate augment in C3 |
| **Posterior (prone, between gastrocnemius heads)** | Very posterior Hoffa |

### Retrograde nail technique

**Start** — **superior margin of Blumensaat line (lateral)**, **centre of notch (AP)**; knee flexion **≥ 70°**

**Approach** — patellar tendon split or medial parapatellar (extend 2–8 cm if articular)

**Rules** — fix articular fragments first (lag screws out of nail path); **long nail to lesser trochanter** (short nails rarely; vascular risk)

**Blocking (Poller) screws** — narrow the metaphysis; place in **proximal part of distal segment** on **concave side**: **varus → AP screw medial to nail** · **recurvatum → ML screw anterior to nail** · valgus → lateral · procurvatum → posterior

**Gastrocnemius** — recurvatum + posterior translation; wide metaphysis → varus / valgus

### Elderly & periprosthetic

**Retrograde nail contraindicated** — **distal femoral replacement** (long stem)\
✗ Wrong options: PS knee (technically harder) · CR · spiral · **flexion** contracture helps; **extension** contracture blocks access

**Periprosthetic nail** — box needs **posterior start** → **recurvatum** risk

**Elderly intra-articular + pre-existing OA (cane user)** — **DFR**\
✗ Wrong options: nail-plate · dual plates · lateral plate · stemmed TKA

**Lateral locked plate, extra-articular elderly** — **WBAT** immediately (no ↑ failure / reoperation)\
✗ Wrong options: NWB · TDWB · partial

**Plate vs nail, elderly extra-articular + immediate WB** — **no difference in function**; no shorter op / stay

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Knee pain / stiffness** | Early ROM, PT; **nail → anterior knee pain** |
| **Symptomatic hardware** | **Lateral plate → IT band** pain with flexion / extension · **long medial screws** |
| **Malunion** | After plating: **rotation, recurvatum, coronal**; **"golf-club" deformity = plate too posterior distally → medial translation of articular block**; percutaneous plate → **rotational**; **malalignment commoner with nails**; acceptable < 5° any plane |
| **Nonunion** | **Up to 19%** (metaphyseal; articular part heals) → **revision ORIF + autograft** ± change fixation |
| Infection | Diabetics with foot ulcers → debride, antibiotics |
| **Implant failure** | Up to 9% |
| **Loss of fixation** | **Varus collapse (commonest)** — toggling non-fixed-angle screws; proximal screw failure with short plates / non-locked diaphysis |

**Oligotrophic supracondylar nonunion** — **open reduction + plating + autograft**\
✗ Wrong options: nail + BMP · ex-fix + BMP · percutaneous plate + BMP · calcium phosphate

### External fixator pin placement

**Anterior femoral half-pin safe zone** — narrow: **femoral nerve branches** up to **58 ± 36 mm (0–136)** below lesser trochanter (174 ± 43 mm from ASIS) · **suprapatellar pouch** reaches **46 ± 13 mm (20–74)** above patella (up to 7.4 cm) → avoid **intra-articular pins (infection)**

## X vs Y

| | **Lateral locked plate** | **Retrograde nail** | **DFR** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Best for | Comminuted metaphyseal, periprosthetic, articular (after lagging) | Extra-articular, simple articular, open-box TKA | Elderly + OA, unreconstructable, loose TKA |
| Problem | Varus collapse, rotation malunion, IT band, nonunion \~20% | Malalignment, knee pain | Longevity, mechanical failure |
| WB | WBAT OK | WBAT OK | **Immediate** |

| | **Blade plate** | **DCS** | **LCP** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Insertion | Precise 3-plane | Forgiving sagittal | Multiplanar |
| Bone removed | — | **Most** | — |
| Coronal / Hoffa control | No | No | **Yes** |

## How it's asked

**"Pre-op CT likely shows?"** → **coronal (Hoffa) fracture of lateral condyle**

**"Commonest Hoffa — screw orientation?"** → **A→P across lateral condyle**

**"Hoffa — true?"** → **commoner in open fractures**

**"Implant controlling coronal fractures best?"** → **locking plate**

**"Most appropriate use of locking plates?"** → **osteoporotic periprosthetic distal femur**

**"Lateral locking plate NOT appropriate for?"** → **partial articular (33B) medial condyle**

**"Osteoporotic comminuted metaphyseal + sagittal split?"** → **lag screws then locking plate**

**"Check intercondylar screw length?"** → **AP with leg 30° internally rotated**

**"Submuscular locking plate ↑ risk of?"** → **malreduction (rotational)**

**"Lateral plate failure risk factor?"** → **short working length**

**"Varus + recurvatum during nailing — blocking screws?"** → **AP medial to nail + ML anterior to nail in distal segment**

**"Highest axial stability, medial comminution, osteoporosis?"** → **lateral locked + medial plate**

**"Nail-plate vs lateral plate?"** → **↓ reoperation**

**"Contraindication to retrograde nail around TKA?"** → **distal femoral replacement**

**"WB after lateral locked plate, 73-yo?"** → **WBAT**

**"Elderly intra-articular + OA?"** → **DFR**

**"Plate + immediate WB vs nail in elderly?"** → **no difference in function**

**"Oligotrophic nonunion?"** → **plate + autograft**

**"Pulse back after traction but different?"** → **ABI**

**"ABI requiring CTA?"** → **0.81**

**"Safe anterior ex-fix pin site?"** → **mid-femur, clear of femoral nerve branches + suprapatellar pouch**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Distal Humerus Fractures

*Trauma · 12 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **2% of all fractures** · **30% of elbow fractures** · rising

**Who** — young males (high energy) · older females (low-energy falls)

**Commonest pattern** — **distal intercondylar (bicolumnar)**

**Associated** — elbow dislocation, terrible triad, floating elbow, **Volkmann contracture** (missed forearm compartment syndrome)

### Anatomy

**Articular orientation** — **6° valgus, 5° ER, 30° flexion** (anterior)

**Trochlea** ↔ sigmoid notch (flex / ext) · **capitellum** ↔ radial head (rotation)

**Origins** — **medial epicondyle = flexor-pronators** (PT, FCR, PL, FDS, FCU) · **lateral = extensors** (anconeus, ECRL, ECRB, EDC, EDM, ECU)

**MCL anterior bundle** — medial epicondyle → **sublime tubercle**; primary valgus restraint **30–120°**; tight in pronation

**LCL** — lateral epicondyle → **crista supinatoris**; vs PLRI; tight in supination

**Ulnar nerve** — subcutaneous in cubital tunnel behind medial condyle

### Radial nerve landmarks (posterior approach)

| Landmark | Distance |
|---|---|
| Spiral groove | **15 cm** proximal to distal articular surface |
| From medial olecranon edge | **10 cm** |
| From lateral olecranon edge | **7.5 cm** |
| **Proximal to triceps aponeurosis** | **3.9 cm (≈ 2 finger-breadths)** |
| Pierces lateral intermuscular septum | **\~14 cm** above lateral epicondyle (OB) |

**Course** — between **BR + brachialis** above elbow → divides into **PIN + superficial radial** at radial head

**PABCN** — radial sensory branch **superficial to lateral intermuscular septum** → **posterior distal arm + forearm sensation**; trace it proximally to find radial nerve\
✗ Wrong options: LABCN = musculocutaneous · MABCN = medial cord · SBRN = dorsoradial hand

## 2 Mechanism

**Elbow flexed < 90° + axial load** — transcolumnar fracture · direct posterior blow → olecranon ± distal humerus

**Elbow flexed > 90°** — **intercondylar** fracture

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — open wounds (**posterior**), gross instability (**avoid ROM**), radial / ulnar / median function, **brachial artery** (↓ pulse → non-invasive studies → vascular surgery), **forearm compartment syndrome**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Use |
|---|---|
| **AP (≈ 40° flexion)** + lateral | Standard |
| Humerus, forearm, **wrist** | Associated injuries |
| Oblique | Trochlea continuity with medial epicondyle → implant choice |
| **Traction radiographs** | Comminuted, telescoped / flexed fragments → approach, **ORIF vs TEA**, 3rd plate, tricortical graft (✗ Wrong options: oblique · flexion / extension films) |
| **CT** | Planning; shear fractures of capitellum + trochlea |
| MRI | Not indicated acutely |

### OTA classification

| Type | Definition |
|---|---|
| **A** | **Extra-articular** — supracondylar (80% extension type), epicondyle |
| **B** | **Partial articular** — single column, coronal shear, epicondyle with articular extension |
| **C** | **Complete articular** — both columns, no joint contiguous with shaft |

![OTA classification of distal humerus fractures](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1017-ota-distal-humerus.jpg)

### Milch classification (single column — 5%, lateral > medial)

| Type | Lateral trochlear ridge | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Intact** (stable) | Non-displaced → **cast** · displaced → **CRPP** |
| **II** | **Fractured** through ridge (unstable) | **ORIF** |

**Casting** — **supination for lateral condyle**, **pronation for medial condyle**

### Jupiter classification (bicolumnar)

| Pattern | Feature |
|---|---|
| **High-T** | Transverse at / above olecranon fossa |
| **Low-T** | Just above trochlea (**common**) |
| **Y** | Oblique through both columns + vertical distal line |
| **H** | **Trochlea free fragment → AVN risk** |
| Medial / lateral lambda | Proximal line medial / lateral |
| Multiplane T | + coronal fracture |

**5 articular fragments** — capitellum / lateral trochlea, lateral epicondyle, posterolateral epicondyle, posterior trochlea, medial trochlea / epicondyle

![Jupiter classification of bicolumnar fractures](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1017-comment-attachment-image-637385494004503339.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Distal humerus fracture
├─ Non-displaced Milch I → cast · displaced Milch I → CRPP
├─ Supracondylar / bicolumnar / Milch II, adult → ORIF, dual plates
│      └─ complex articular / coronal split → olecranon osteotomy
├─ Elderly, comminuted, osteoporotic / RA, low demand → semiconstrained TEA
└─ Unfit / can't comply → "bag of bones"
```

### Exposure – approaches

| Approach | Use / facts |
|---|---|
| **Triceps-splitting (Campbell)** | Midline split to olecranon; open fractures → use the wound; exposes **35%** of joint |
| **Triceps-sparing (paratricipital, Alonso-Llames)** | **Extra-articular + simple articular split**; medial + lateral windows; convertible to osteotomy |
| **Olecranon osteotomy** | **Complex intra-articular, coronal split**; **most exposure (57%)** |
| **Triceps-reflecting (Bryan-Morrey)** | Medial → lateral sleeve; transosseous repair, protect 4–6 wk; **46%** exposure; good medial, poor lateral view |
| **TRAP (O'Driscoll)** | Triceps + anconeus V-flap; repair via drill holes; no early mobilisation |
| Lateral muscle interval | Lift ECRB + part ECRL off supracondylar ridge |

**Young, complex articular (motorcycle)** — **olecranon osteotomy + parallel plating** (✗ Wrong options: Bryan-Morrey · TRAP) · or posterior extensile + **chevron** osteotomy (✗ Wrong options: Henry · Kocher · medial column · CRPP)

### Olecranon osteotomy

**Technique** — **chevron, apex distal**, \~**2 cm from tip**, through **bare area**; fix with **TBW, screws, plate**, or **intramedullary cancellous screw angled slightly medially** (proximal ulna varus bow)

**TEA after osteotomy** — **not contraindicated**; triceps-sparing preferred if TEA possible, but **all posterior approaches** (paratricipital, split, tongue, osteotomy) allow TEA

| Complication | Rate |
|---|---|
| **Symptomatic hardware** — **most common** | **6–30%** (Orthobullets) · **8–33%** (OB) · removal up to **70%** in 90/90 + osteotomy series |
| Osteotomy nonunion | **0–9%** (Orthobullets) · **0–2%** (OB); healing 95–100% |
| Fixation failure | \~5% |
| **AIN injury** (K-wires through anterior ulnar cortex) | **Can't flex thumb IP** — check in recovery (✗ Wrong options: wrist flexion · thumb sensation (AIN has none) · dorsal web · index abduction) |

**Osteotomy + TBW vs other fixation** — **↑ AIN injury**; **not** ↑ ulnar neuritis

### Fixation constructs – parallel vs orthogonal plating

**O'Driscoll principles** — **every distal screw through a plate**; **screws as long as possible**, engaging many articular fragments; **interdigitate** screws from both plates; **compress both columns** at supracondylar level

**Sequence** — provisional K-wires → articular fragments with **countersunk / headless screws** (use **positional, not lag, screws across trochlea** to avoid narrowing) → plates to shaft

| Construct | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Parallel (medial + lateral, 180°)** | **Greatest rigidity** — **medial + lateral LC-DCP** stiffest |
| **Orthogonal (90-90)** | **Posterolateral + medial**; clinical outcomes **= parallel** |
| **1/3 tubular plates** | **Too weak** → failure + nonunion |
| Single plate (Y, medial) | Weaker than any dual construct |
| **Locking plates** | Poor bone, comminution |

**50-yo displaced intra-articular** — **dual column plates**\
✗ Wrong options: TEA · brace · cast · K-wires

**Adult bicolumnar (any age except frail)** — dual plating (90° or 180°)

**Post-op** — splint **70° flexion** → ROM by 7–10 d · osteotomy: active / active-assisted, **no active extension against gravity / resistance for 6 wk** · strengthening 6 wk, full 3 mo

### Ulnar nerve dysfunction

**Transposition at ORIF** — **no benefit**; **↑ ulnar neuritis** (33% vs 9%) **(TO: no effect or increased)**\
✗ Wrong options: decreased

**In situ release vs transposition** — **in situ → less ulnar neuritis**; PROMs equal

**Transpose only if** — nerve **on stretch or touching implants**, subluxing, recurrent

**Pre-existing cubital tunnel symptoms + H-type fracture** — **parallel plating (all screws through plate) + in situ cubital tunnel release**\
✗ Wrong options: orthogonal + submuscular · observe neuropathy · lag screws outside plate + subcutaneous

**Persistent ulnar symptoms after in situ release, no stretch / contact** — **observe + anti-claw splint**\
✗ Wrong options: EMG acutely · revision transposition · Guyon release

**Post-op ulnar palsy** — mostly **traction** during ORIF

### Elderly / osteoporotic – TEA vs ORIF

**TEA indication** — **elderly (> 65–70), low demand, osteoporotic, comminuted bicolumnar / distal articular fractures not amenable to stable fixation**; **RA**, **chronic steroids**; must accept **lifting limits**

**Implant** — **semiconstrained (linked)** — collaterals need not heal; radial head not needed\
✗ Wrong options: unconstrained in RA

**Lifting limit** — **10 lb** (Orthobullets) · **5 lb** (OB) · **5–10 lb lifelong** (OB) — read the options

| ORIF vs TEA (elderly) | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Reoperation** | **ORIF higher (27% vs 12%)** (OB) · similar frequency (OB) · some studies higher with TEA (OB) — read the options |
| **Early PROMs / short-term function** | **TEA better** |
| ROM | TEA greater (Orthobullets, OB) · similar (OB) |
| Complications | TEA fewer (OB) · TEA more frequent (OB) |
| Operative time | **TEA shorter** |
| **Nonunion** | **Not a TEA complication** |
| Ulnar nerve dysfunction | Both |

**TEA facts** — **> 75% survival at 10 yr**; ROM **26–125°**; commonest complication **loosening / bushing wear (10–15%)**; **men** revised more; **salvage TEA after failed ORIF ≈ primary TEA**; complications: loosening, poly wear, periprosthetic fracture

**Failed ORIF in 83-yo (screws cut out at 5 wk)** — **elbow arthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: revision ORIF · bag of bones · arthrodesis

**Not TEA candidates** — young labourer open T · transolecranon fracture-dislocation · coronal shear · nonunion in 50-yo

**"Bag of bones"** — brief immobilisation then ROM for unfit / non-compliant elderly → painless pseudarthrosis, fair function, high nonunion\
✗ Wrong options: **immediate** ROM

### Extra-articular distal-third shaft – functional bracing

**Brace vs surgery** — **similar small loss of elbow motion** (6° vs 3°, NS); 100% union; malalignment without functional limits; shoulder motion within 10°

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Stiffness** | **Most common** (3–42%); mean arc 90–106°; static-progressive splint |
| **HO** | **8%** (Orthobullets) · \~4% (OB); no routine indomethacin (↑ nonunion); risk: **head injury**, floating elbow, type A + B, delayed fixation |
| **Nonunion** | **0–11%** (2–10%, OB); stripping, open, comminution, **low transcondylar**; → revision ORIF + graft |
| Malunion | Cubitus valgus (lateral column) / varus (medial column) |
| **AIN injury** | Olecranon osteotomy |
| **Ulnar nerve** | **10–38%** (Orthobullets) · **0–12%** (OB) |
| Wound | Up to 16% |
| Infection | 0–14% |
| PTOA | — |

### Stiffness, contracture release & HO

**Most common complication after ORIF** — **↓ ROM / stiffness** → subsequent **open release of capsule + posterior bundle of MUCL**\
✗ Wrong options: revision for malunion / nonunion · hardware removal · HO removal alone

**Limited flexion (30–90° or 10–80°)** — **open release of posterior bundle of MCL + posterior capsule + excise osteophytes / HO** · consider ulnar transposition if flexion < 90°\
✗ Wrong options: release **anterior** bundle → valgus instability · complete MCL + LCL release · anterior arthroscopic release (that limits extension) · dome osteotomy · indomethacin / radiation (prophylaxis only)

**Expected after ORIF** — **\~25% loss of flexion strength**; full ROM rare; most regain \~75% motion + strength; goal **30–130°**

**Unsatisfactory** — up to 25% (low fracture lines, metaphyseal fragmentation, articular comminution, poor bone)

### Radial nerve & branches

**Neurapraxia on NCV / EMG at 6 wk** — **normal amplitude + velocity in superficial radial sensory nerve** (no Wallerian degeneration)\
✗ Wrong options: LABCN · FCU · EDC fibrillations = worse injury · triceps MUPs

**Recovery order** — **proximal → distal**: **BR first … EIP last**

**Laceration 2 finger-breadths above triceps aponeurosis** — radial nerve → **can't extend index finger**\
✗ Wrong options: thumb adduction = ulnar · small DIP = ulnar · thumb IP = AIN · pronation = median

## X vs Y

| | **Parallel plating** | **Orthogonal (90-90)** |
|---|---|---|
| Plates | Medial + lateral | Medial + posterolateral |
| Rigidity | **Higher** | Lower |
| Clinical outcome | Same | Same |

| | **ORIF** | **TEA (elderly)** |
|---|---|---|
| Who | Adults, good bone | Elderly, osteoporotic, comminuted, RA |
| Nonunion risk | Yes | **None** |
| Early function | Lower | **Better** |
| Restriction | — | **5–10 lb lifelong** |

| | **In situ ulnar release** | **Anterior transposition** |
|---|---|---|
| Post-op ulnar neuritis | **Lower** | **Higher / no benefit** (TO) |

## How it's asked

**"85-yo osteoporotic comminuted, low-level ambulator?"** → **TEA**

**"92-yo comminuted, non-dominant?"** → **TEA**

**"Most appropriate TEA candidate?"** → **86-yo comminuted bicolumnar**

**"71-yo on steroids, comminuted intra-articular?"** → **TEA**

**"ORIF vs TEA in 79-yo?"** → **ORIF ↑ reoperation**

**"TEA in RA patient — true?"** → **lifelong 5–10 lb lifting limit**

**"TEA vs ORIF — true?"** → **TEA better early PROMs**

**"Treatment with loosening risk — true?"** → **no risk of nonunion**

**"TEA — true?"** → **> 75% survival at 10 yr**

**"RA + steroids, comminuted — best?"** → **semiconstrained TEA**

**"Failed ORIF at 5 wk in 83-yo?"** → **elbow arthroplasty**

**"TEA vs ORIF in 86-yo — true?"** → **TEA better short-term function**

**"Approaches allowing TEA if ORIF abandoned?"** → **all posterior approaches**

**"50-yo intra-articular displaced?"** → **dual column plates**

**"Stiffest construct?"** → **parallel medial + lateral LC-DCP**

**"H-type + chronic ulnar symptoms?"** → **parallel plating + in situ release**

**"Osteotomy + TBW vs other — true?"** → **↑ AIN injury**

**"AIN deficit after osteotomy — exam?"** → **can't flex thumb IP**

**"Best visualisation + early ROM, young complex?"** → **olecranon osteotomy + parallel plating**

**"Osteotomy characteristics?"** → **chevron apex distal, 2 cm from tip, screw angled medially**

**"Most common complication with 90/90 + osteotomy?"** → **pain from olecranon fixation**

**"Most common complication of osteotomy exposure?"** → **symptomatic hardware**

**"Intra-articular fracture — approach?"** → **posterior extensile + chevron osteotomy**

**"Most common complication after ORIF?"** → **↓ elbow ROM**

**"Arc 30–90° with posteromedial HO — improve flexion?"** → **HO excision + release posterior band of UCL**

**"Arc 10–80° after nonunion repair?"** → **release posterior bundle MCL + osteophytes**

**"Expected outcome after ORIF?"** → **\~25% loss of flexion strength**

**"Subsequent surgery for commonest complication?"** → **capsule + posterior MUCL release**

**"Neurapraxia finding at 6 wk?"** → **normal superficial radial sensory conduction**

**"Radial palsy after posterior approach — last to recover?"** → **EIP**

**"Nerve 2 finger-breadths above triceps aponeurosis cut?"** → **can't extend index finger**

**"Branch superficial to lateral septum, 14 cm above epicondyle?"** → **PABCN — posterior arm + forearm sensation**

**"Anterior transposition effect?"** → **no effect or ↑ ulnar neuritis** (TO)

**"Ulnar symptoms persist after in situ release, no stretch?"** → **observe + anti-claw splint**

**"Extra radiographs that realign fragments?"** → **traction views**

**"Bracing extra-articular distal-third shaft vs surgery?"** → **similar loss of elbow motion**

# Distal Radial Ulnar Joint (DRUJ) Injuries

*Trauma · 13 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Overview

**Why it matters** — DRUJ injury with distal radius fractures is a common, **under-appreciated cause of pain + lost rotation** → assess it **independently** after fixing the radius

**Associated** — **ulnar styloid / distal ulna fractures**, **TFCC tears**, **ulnar impaction**, **Essex-Lopresti**, **Galeazzi**

### Anatomy

**Joint** — ulnar head in **sigmoid notch** (shallow); motion = rotation + translation

**Most stable** — **supination**

**Primary stabilisers** — **volar + dorsal radioulnar ligaments** + **TFCC** (inserts at **fovea** at ulnar styloid base)

**Secondary / back-up primary** — **distal oblique bundle (DOB) of interosseous membrane** → can keep forearm stable when TFCC torn\
✗ Wrong options: annular ligament = PRUJ · central band (not DRUJ) · meniscal homologue · proximal oblique cord

**TFCC components** — central articular disc · meniscal homologue · volar + dorsal radioulnar ligaments · ulnolunate + ulnotriquetral origins · **ECU sheath floor**

**Ligament roles** — **pronation: volar ligaments block dorsal subluxation** · **supination: dorsal ligaments block volar subluxation**

![TFCC anatomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1028-shows-the-anatomy-of-the-tfcc.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**TFCC tear** — wrist **extension + forearm pronation**

## 3 Presentation

**Acute dislocation** — pain + instability (often with open distal radius fractures)

**Post-traumatic arthritis** — dorsal wrist pain, **↓ pronosupination**, snapping, crepitus, ↓ grip; pain on rotating with ulna compressed against radius

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **AP** | **DRUJ widening** |
| **Lateral** | **Dorsal displacement** of ulnar head; instability = subluxed **full width** of sigmoid notch in neutral |
| **Dynamic CT** (neutral, full supination, full pronation, vs other side) | **> 50% translation = abnormal**; subtle chronic instability |
| **MRI** | **TFCC** tears |

![DRUJ instability on radiographs](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1028-druj-instability.jpg)

### Palmer TFCC classification

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Traumatic** |
| **II — degenerative (ulnocarpal impaction)** | **IIA** thinning · **IIB** + lunate / ulnar chondromalacia · **IIC** + perforation · **IID** + LT ligament tear · **IIE** + ulnocarpal + DRUJ arthritis |

## 5 Management

```
DRUJ after distal radius / radial shaft fixation
├─ Stable → no extra treatment (ulnar styloid ignored)
├─ Unstable, ligamentous → reduce + splint (dorsal → supination, volar → pronation) 4 wk
│      └─ highly unstable → pin 0.062 K-wires ± ligament repair
├─ Unstable + displaced styloid base / sigmoid notch / TFCC avulsion → ORIF / repair
└─ Irreducible after anatomic radius → ECU interposed → open removal
```

### Ligamentous instability

**Closed reduction + immobilise 4 wk** — **dorsal instability → supination** · **volar instability → pronation**

**Highly unstable** — **pin DRUJ with 0.062-inch K-wires** ± radioulnar ligament repair

**ECU interposition** — blocks reduction

### DRUJ instability after radius fixation – ulnar fixation & ECU

**Incongruent DRUJ after anatomic radial shaft fixation (Galeazzi)** — **interposed ECU** → extricate via dorsal approach\
✗ Wrong options: revise plating · IM nail · pronator quadratus · FCU

**Indication to fix distal ulna fracture** — **intra-operative DRUJ instability**\
✗ Wrong options: angulation, comminution, location, size alone

**Galeazzi** — distal-third radius fracture + DRUJ injury → **radial ORIF + DRUJ pinning** often; nonop (splint in supination) rarely

### Ulnar styloid fractures

**Meaning** — marker of **high initial displacement**; **base fractures → TFCC rupture + instability**

**Nonunion without instability** — **does not worsen outcome** (least likely to affect function)\
✗ Wrong options: DRUJ instability · step-off > 3 mm · radial shortening · workers' comp (all worsen)

**Operative** — **displaced base fracture + instability**, sigmoid notch fracture, Galeazzi, TFCC avulsion with unstable DRUJ → ORIF or excision **preserving TFCC ulnar attachment**

**Non-displaced, proximal** — cast

### TFCC tears

**Acute traumatic** — immobilisation + NSAIDs first → arthroscopic / open debridement or repair if persistent

**Degenerative (type II)** — debride TFCC + treat impaction: **ulnar shortening osteotomy** (no DRUJ arthrosis) or **wafer resection**

### Ulnar impaction syndrome

**Cause** — radial shortening → **positive ulnar variance** → **lunate chondromalacia + degenerative TFCC tear**

**Surgery** — TFCC debridement, radial osteotomy, **ulnar shortening**, **wafer (preserve TFCC attachment)**

### Forearm stabilisers – Essex-Lopresti

**Essex-Lopresti (longitudinal radioulnar dissociation)** — **radial head fracture + interosseous membrane tear + DRUJ disruption** → **proximal radial migration** → DRUJ pathology + ulnocarpal abutment

**Acute** — fix / replace radial head (restore radiocapitellar contact + length) + **pin DRUJ 6 wk in neutral**; failed pinning / missed → radial head replacement

**Chronic** — restore radial length, level DRUJ, **reconstruct central band of IOM**

**Ipsilateral radial head / neck + Galeazzi (functional Essex-Lopresti)** — treatment causing chronic instability = **radial head excision**\
✗ Wrong options: temporary ex-fix · radial head ORIF · arthroplasty · DR ORIF + cross-pinning

## 6 Complications

### DRUJ arthrosis – salvage

| Procedure | Key fact |
|---|---|
| **Darrach** (distal ulna excision) | **Low-demand elderly**; risk **unstable painful ulnar stump** |
| **Hemiresection-interposition** (Bowers) | Preserves **TFCC ulnar insertion**; soft tissue prevents impingement |
| **Sauvé-Kapandji** | **DRUJ fusion + proximal ulnar neck pseudarthrosis**; younger patients; keeps rotation |
| Ulnar head prosthesis | — |
| **One-bone forearm** | **Ultimate salvage**; **eliminates rotation** |

**Prevention** — **anatomic reduction of the distal radius** usually reduces the DRUJ

![Sauvé-Kapandji procedure](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1028-sauve-kapandji-pa-and-lateral.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Darrach** | **Sauvé-Kapandji** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | Elderly, low demand | Younger |
| What | Distal ulna excised | DRUJ fused + proximal pseudarthrosis |
| Risk | **Unstable ulnar stump** | Painful proximal stump |

| | **Dorsal DRUJ instability** | **Volar DRUJ instability** |
|---|---|---|
| Stable position | **Supination** | **Pronation** |

## How it's asked

**"DRUJ incongruent after anatomic radius fixation?"** → **reduce interposed ECU**

**"Indication for distal ulna fixation?"** → **intra-operative DRUJ instability**

**"Least likely to affect outcome after DR ORIF?"** → **ulnar styloid nonunion**

**"Treatment causing chronic instability in radial head + Galeazzi?"** → **radial head excision**

**"Gross forearm stability after radius fixation despite DRUJ injury — structure?"** → **distal oblique bundle**

**"Dynamic CT translation abnormal?"** → **> 50% vs contralateral**

**"Dorsal DRUJ dislocation — immobilise in?"** → **supination**

**"Essex-Lopresti — DRUJ?"** → **pin 6 wk in neutral**

**"Ultimate salvage for DRUJ?"** → **one-bone forearm**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Distal Radius Fractures

*Trauma · 14 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest orthopaedic injury** — **17.5% of adult fractures**; lifetime risk **15% women, 2% men**; **F:M 2–3:1**

**Bimodal** — young high energy (MVC) · elderly low energy (FOOSH) = **fragility fracture → DEXA**

**Pattern** — **65% extra-articular**, 10% partial articular, 25% complete (Orthobullets) · **\~50% intra-articular** (OB)

**Predictor of later fractures** — osteoporosis, women > 50

**Associated soft-tissue injury (70%)** — **TFCC 40%**, **SL 30%**, **LT 15%** · DRUJ injury · radial styloid fracture (higher energy)

### Abbreviations

**Tendons / nerves** — EPL = extensor pollicis longus · EIP = extensor indicis proprius · FPL = flexor pollicis longus · EDC = extensor digitorum communis · PIN = posterior interosseous nerve · CTR = open carpal tunnel release

### Anatomy

**Load** — radius carries **80%** of axial load

| Column | Contents | Roles |
|---|---|---|
| **Radial** | Styloid (**volar** structure) + scaphoid fossa | BR insertion, **long radiolunate** + **radioscaphocapitate** (blocks ulnar carpal translation); buttress; ulnar-deviation load |
| **Intermediate** | **Lunate fossa** | Load transfer; **short radiolunate** origin (radiocarpal stability) |
| **Ulnar** | TFCC + distal ulna | DRUJ stability, rotation |

**Lister tubercle** — dorsal, \~1 cm above joint; **EPL just ulnar** → long volar screws rupture EPL

**Watershed line** — **most volar prominent rim** (lunate portion highest; short radiolunate) → **keep volar plate proximal**

**Volar-ulnar ("critical") corner / volar lunate facet** — radiolunate ligaments → loss → **volar carpal subluxation**

## 2 Mechanism

**FOOSH** (elderly) · MVC (young)

**Chauffeur (Hutchinson) (TO)** — **radial styloid** fracture from **scaphoid impacting styloid**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar styloid

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — pain, swelling, **dinner-fork deformity** if displaced; median nerve check

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Measure | Normal | Acceptable (post-reduction) |
|---|---|---|
| **Radial height (PA)** | **13 mm** (Orthobullets) · **11 mm** (TO) | **< 5 mm shortening** |
| **Radial inclination (PA)** | **23°** (22° TO text) | **< 5° change** |
| **Articular step-off** | Congruous | **< 2 mm** |
| **Volar tilt (lateral)** | **11°** | **Dorsal angulation < 5° or within 20° of contralateral** (Orthobullets, TO) · **dorsal tilt < 10°** in healthy < 65 (OB) |
| Ulnar variance | Slightly negative | — |

**TO EXCEPT trap (TO)** — "**dorsal tilt up to 7°**" is **not** acceptable

**Radial length (TO)** — radial styloid \~1 cm distal to ulnar head

**CT** — articular involvement, planning · **MRI** — TFCC, SL (DISI), LT (VISI)

### Classifications & eponyms

**Systems** — **Fernandez** (mechanism) · **Frykman** (radiocarpal / DRUJ ± ulnar styloid) · **Melone** (4 intra-articular types by displacement) · **AO** (comprehensive, cumbersome)

| Eponym | Description |
|---|---|
| **Colles** | **Dorsally angulated, extra-articular** |
| **Smith** | **Volarly angulated, extra-articular** |
| **Volar Barton** | Radiocarpal fracture-dislocation with **volar lip** fragment |
| **Dorsal Barton** | Radiocarpal fracture-dislocation with **dorsal lip** fragment |
| **Chauffeur (Hutchinson)** | **Radial styloid** (transverse / oblique) |
| **Die-punch** | **Depressed lunate fossa** |

![Normal radiographic relationships of the distal radius](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1027-illustration-radiographic-relations-emedicine-moved.gif)

**Colles complications (TO)** — TFCC tear, DRUJ instability, CRPS, stiffness, **malunion (dinner fork)**, **EPL tear**, nonunion\
✗ Wrong options: **gunstock deformity** (supracondylar humerus)

## 5 Management

```
Distal radius fracture
├─ Reduce + rigid splint → meets criteria → cast
├─ Unstable / > criteria, young → ORIF (volar locked plate)
│      └─ volar-ulnar corner fragment → fragment-specific fixation
├─ Elderly > 60–65 → closed treatment acceptable (function equal)
└─ Open / highly comminuted / unwell → ex-fix ± pins, or dorsal bridge plate
```

### Instability & indications for fixation

**LaFontaine instability predictors** — **radial shortening (most predictive)**, then **dorsal comminution**; dorsal comminution > 50%, palmar / intra-articular comminution, **dorsal angulation > 20°**, **initial displacement > 1 cm**, **shortening > 5 mm**, severe osteoporosis, associated ulnar fracture; **≥ 3 criteria → ↑ loss of reduction**

**Redisplacement after closed reduction** — **severity of initial displacement** (esp. shortening), age, metaphyseal comminution\
✗ Wrong options: TFCC tear · open · radial head · time to reduction

**Best stability predictor** — **pre-reduction radiographs**

**Repeat closed reduction** — **< 50%** satisfactory

| ORIF indications |
|---|
| **Dorsal angulation > 5° / > 20° vs contralateral** |
| **Intra-articular displacement > 2 mm** |
| **Radial shortening > 5 mm** |
| Volar or dorsal comminution · associated ulnar fracture (not styloid) · severe osteoporosis |
| **Barton (articular margin) fractures** · **Smith** (comminuted) · **die-punch** |
| Progressive loss of tilt / length in cast |

**Young, large dorsal articular fragment** — **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: casts · ex-fix · CRPP

**Young high-energy metaphyseal comminution** — **ORIF of radius** (locking resists shortening + dorsal angulation)\
✗ Wrong options: cast · ex-fix + pins · styloid repair (only if DRUJ unstable / base displaced)

### Closed reduction & casting

**Technique** — anaesthesia, traction + volar / dorsal pressure; below-elbow cast / splint (**rigid / sugar tong**)

**Avoid Cotton-Loder position** — **extreme flexion + ulnar deviation** → **CTS**

**Within criteria (65-yo)** — **rigid splint + clinic follow-up**\
✗ Wrong options: admit · ORIF · soft splint

**75-yo dorsally angulated extra-articular — initial treatment** — **closed reduction + splint**

**Complications** — **acute CTS**, **EPL rupture**

### Elderly / osteoporotic – closed vs operative

**Meta-analyses** — **no functional difference** closed vs operative in **> 65** (Orthobullets) / **≥ 60** (OB); **radiographic** results better with surgery

**Osteoporotic, failed closed treatment** — **peri-articular locked plate**

**Volar comminution in 65-yo — contraindicated** — **Kapandji intrafocal pinning alone**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · pinning + ex-fix · dorsal bridge plate · volar plate

**Dorsal bridge plate** — ligamentotaxis for comminuted fractures; **polytrauma / elderly needing walker** → **immediate platform (front-wheel) walker** WB (\~22% BW) · must be removed\
✗ Wrong options: crutches \~50% BW · NWB wheelchair · no device

### CRPP

**Indication** — extra-articular with **stable volar cortex**; 82–90% good

**Techniques** — **Kapandji intrafocal** (pins lever fracture) · Rayhack (arthroscopic)

**Complications** — **radial sensory nerve injury**, **pin-tract infection**

### Volar plating & volar (Henry) approach

**Volar plating preferred** over dorsal; locked plates support subchondral bone

**Classic Henry** — between **BR and radial artery** (radial to artery) · **modified (FCR) Henry** — **ulnar (medial) to radial artery**, through FCR sheath (TO)

**Distal third (TO)** — FCR medial + radial artery lateral → **sweep FPL ulnarward with a finger, protecting palmar cutaneous branch** → **PQ incised along its RADIAL border** and elevated with periosteum

**TO FALSE statement (TO)** — "**PQ elevated from ulnar side**"\
✗ Wrong options: true: FPL swept ulnarly, modified Henry medial to artery, Thompson more cosmetic for middle / distal-third shaft

**Proximal third (TO)** — **full supination** moves PIN away; incise supinator medially · **middle third** — pronate, partially detach pronator teres

**Palmar cutaneous branch of median nerve** — leaves median **4–6 cm** above wrist crease, between median nerve + FCR → **thenar base hyperaesthesia** from retraction\
✗ Wrong options: CRPS · Wartenberg · CTS · C7

**Bone void** — **calcium phosphate** incorporates by \~1 yr\
✗ Wrong options: calcium sulfate (resorbs 4–12 wk) · TCP (osteolysis) · OP-1 · freeze-dried allograft (not synthetic)

### Volar lunate facet / volar-ulnar corner

**Volar-ulnar corner fragment** — → **volar carpal translation**\
✗ Wrong options: die-punch · radial styloid (ulnar translation) · extra-articular

**Early failure 2 wk after volar plate** — **lunate facet fragment not supported** → **fragment-specific fixation** (small plates, TBW, K-wires)\
✗ Wrong options: non-locking plate · volar tilt · styloid column · 3 shaft screws

**Region risking radiocarpal instability if missed** — **volar lunate facet**

### Intra-operative fluoroscopic views

| View | Shows |
|---|---|
| **Tilted lateral / radial inclination view (22–23° elevated)** | **Radiocarpal (intra-articular) screw penetration** |
| **Dorsal tangential (skyline)** — wrist max flexed, beam \~15° volar to radial axis | **Dorsal cortex screw penetration** |
| PA anatomic tilt (\~11°) | Articular congruity |
| Extended wrist tangential ("sigmoid notch") | DRUJ penetration |
| Standard lateral | Plate vs watershed |

**TO answer (TO)** — **dorsal tangential + radial incline view**

**Avoid extensor injury** — **pegs** for articular block, **capture \~75%** of volar–dorsal width

### External fixation

**Indications** — open, highly comminuted, unwell · **cannot reliably restore 10° palmar tilt alone** → add pins / plate

**Spanning** (small articular fragments) vs **non-spanning** (large fragments)

**Ligamentotaxis** — traction restores length + inclination; **volar translation of lunate restores volar tilt** (multiplanar, Agee)

**Rules** — open placement of radial pins (**superficial radial nerve** at "bare area" \~4 finger-breadths above styloid, dorsal to BR — **commonest nerve injured**); **avoid over-distraction (< 5 mm)**, flexion, ulnar deviation; **≤ 8 wk**; aggressive finger OT

**Pin care** — **daily showers + dry dressings**; chlorhexidine / peroxide **no better**; complication rate \~20%

**Ex-fix + K-wires vs volar plate** — **both give satisfactory results**; pin / wound complications **higher with ex-fix**\
✗ Wrong options: tip styloid needs fixation · plate proximal to watershed ↑ flexor injury · pillar pain

### DRUJ instability & ulnar styloid fractures

**Stable DRUJ after radius fixation + ulnar styloid fracture** — **no adverse effect without fixation**

**Shuck test positive after fixation** — **radioulnar ligaments of TFCC**\
✗ Wrong options: thumb UCL · ulnolunate / ulnotriquetral

**Shuck test** — elbow 90°, translate ulna sagittally in neutral, pronation, supination

**SL disruption seen intra-op** — **separate dorsal incision → repair dorsal SL ligament + K-wires** · results better than isolated SL injuries\
✗ Wrong options: immobilise · pins alone · volar extension · arthroscopic

### Rehabilitation & outcomes

**Formal therapy vs home programme** — **no long-term difference** (Wakefield-McQueen); **better DASH + ROM early (6 wk)** in women > 40 — improves early, not late PROMs

**DASH variability (nonop)** — **depression**\
✗ Wrong options: neuroticism · occupation · handedness

**Poor outcome** — workers' comp, ↓ SES, ↓ education, ↓ BMD

**Good outcome** — **articular reduction accuracy**, restored anatomy, early finger + wrist motion

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Median neuropathy / CTS** | **Commonest neurological**; **1–12% low energy, 30% high energy** (Orthobullets) · acute CTS **5.4–8.6%** (OB) |
| **EPL rupture** | Nondisplaced / nonop **0.3–5% (3–5%)**; volar plate with **proud dorsal screw** → **EIP transfer** |
| **FPL rupture** | **Volar plate distal to watershed** |
| Radiocarpal arthrosis | 2–30%; **90% of young adults with step-off > 1–2 mm** |
| Malunion / nonunion | Radial shortening → worst function |
| ECU / EDM entrapment, ulnar neuropathy | DRUJ injury |
| Compartment syndrome | — |
| **CRPS** | Vitamin C prophylaxis (AAOS) — later analyses show no benefit |

### Acute carpal tunnel syndrome

**Urgent release indications** — **progressive paraesthesia**, **weak thumb opposition**, paraesthesia not improving after reduction **> 24–48 h**

**Worsening numbness after reduction / CRPP / ORIF** — **urgent carpal tunnel release** (+ ORIF / pinning)\
✗ Wrong options: bivalve + 1 wk · hand compartment pressures · NCS · EMG · plexus exploration · repeat closed reduction

**Pre-op mild paraesthesia, 2PD 6 → > 10 mm day after volar plate** — **immediate CTR**

**Child SH II, worsening after bivalving** — **CTR + pinning**

**TO nuance (TO)** — **immediately after reduction in palmar flexion / ulnar deviation → ORIF + CTR (TO)** · **delayed CTS in cast → release cast + neutral wrist first (TO)**; CTR if persists · no prophylactic CTR

**Urgent-surgery sign on rounds** — **can't oppose thumb**\
✗ Wrong options: can't extend thumb IP (EPL) · initial displacement · humeral fracture · dorsal comminution

**Mechanism** — **nerve compression**\
✗ Wrong options: laceration · arterial · RSD

### EPL rupture after nondisplaced fracture & EIP transfer

**Classic** — **non- / minimally displaced** fracture in cast → sudden painless loss of thumb extension at **7 wk–4 mo**\
✗ Wrong options: PIN palsy · intersection · malunion · EPB

**Cause** — **hypovascular zone in 3rd compartment**, attrition, callus / ischaemia\
✗ Wrong options: displaced fragments

**Most likely complication of nonop nondisplaced DRF** — **EPL rupture**\
✗ Wrong options: CTS · FPL · abutment · arthritis

**Treatment** — **EIP → EPL transfer** ± palmaris graft; pre-rupture → 3rd compartment release\
✗ Wrong options: primary repair (attritional) · EPB (too little excursion) · ECRL · splinting

**EIP** — **4th compartment** with EDC + **PIN** · EPL innervated by **PIN**

**Dorsal screw ulnar to Lister tubercle** — irritates **EDC** (4th)\
✗ Wrong options: ECRL / ECRB (radial to Lister) · ECU · EPB

### FPL rupture – volar plate past watershed

**Risk** — plate **volar / distal to watershed (Soong) line**, prominent screws

**Soong grade** — **0** dorsal to critical line · **1** volar to line, proximal to rim · **2** on / distal to volar rim

**Flexor injury** — up to **12%** of volar plates; **FPL 57%**, FDP index next; mean **\~9 mo**

**Plate distal to watershed, asymptomatic at 2 wk** — **plan removal after union** to protect FPL

**FPL** — origin volar radius + IOM → **base of thumb distal phalanx** → rupture = **can't flex thumb IP**

### CRPS – vitamin C

**Dose** — **500 mg daily × 50 days**\
✗ Wrong options: 200 mg · 1500 mg (no better) · alendronate

**Evidence** — AAOS 2010 / 2013 moderate support; subsequent analyses **no benefit** — read the options

### Malunion

**Nascent malunion at 2 mo in young drummer** — **acute corrective osteotomy** (easier, less structural graft)\
✗ Wrong options: wrist fusion · USO / Darrach alone

**Dorsal angulation > 10°** — stiffens IOM, ↓ rotation → opening-wedge osteotomy + graft

## X vs Y

| | **EPL rupture** | **FPL rupture** |
|---|---|---|
| Setting | **Nondisplaced, casted** · proud dorsal screw | **Volar plate distal to watershed** |
| Deficit | Can't extend thumb IP / "thumbs up" | **Can't flex thumb IP** |
| Fix | **EIP transfer** | Repair / graft / transfer + plate removal |

| | **Tilted lateral (22–23°)** | **Skyline** |
|---|---|---|
| Checks | **Joint penetration** | **Dorsal cortex penetration** |

| | **Classic Henry** | **Modified (FCR) Henry** |
|---|---|---|
| Interval | **BR ↔ radial artery** | **FCR ↔ radial artery** |

## How it's asked

**"3 months after minimally displaced DRF, can't extend thumb — next?"** → **EIP → EPL transfer** (TO)

**"Spontaneous EPL rupture most associated with?"** → **nondisplaced distal radius fracture**

**"Nerve supplying the ruptured tendon?"** → **PIN**

**"Commonly transferred tendon's compartment?"** → **4th**

**"Donor tendon description?"** → **in same compartment as PIN**

**"Risk in nonop nondisplaced DRF?"** → **EPL rupture**

**"Tendon at risk 12 months after volar plating (plate prominent)?"** → **FPL**

**"Distal plate, grip weakness at 6 months?"** → **can't flex thumb IP**

**"Plate past watershed at 2 wk?"** → **remove after union to protect FPL**

**"Tendon at risk — origin / insertion?"** → **volar radius + IOM → base of thumb distal phalanx**

**"Prominent screw ulnar to Lister?"** → **EDC**

**"Pain + paraesthesia in lateral digits after casting comminuted fracture?"** → **release cast, neutral position** (TO)

**"Numbness right after reduction in flexion / ulnar deviation?"** → **ORIF + CTR** (TO)

**"Worsening median signs post-op day 1?"** → **immediate CTR**

**"Worsening numbness 2 h after reduction?"** → **emergent ORIF + CTR**

**"Indication for urgent surgery on rounds?"** → **inability to oppose thumb**

**"Vitamin C prevents?"** → **CRPS**

**"Vitamin C dose?"** → **500 mg daily × 50 days**

**"Early failure — cause?"** → **unsupported volar lunate facet**

**"Fracture causing volar carpal translation?"** → **volar-ulnar corner fragment**

**"Radiocarpal screw penetration view?"** → **radial inclination (22–23° tilted lateral)**

**"Dorsal + articular screw check (TO)?"** → **dorsal tangential + radial incline views**

**"Stable DRUJ + ulnar styloid fracture untreated?"** → **no adverse effect**

**"Shuck test positive — structure?"** → **radioulnar ligaments of TFCC**

**"SL disruption on intra-op film?"** → **dorsal incision repair + K-wires**

**"Osteoporotic failed closed treatment — plate?"** → **peri-articular locked**

**"Within criteria, 65-yo?"** → **rigid splint + clinic**

**"Elderly ORIF vs closed — meta-analysis?"** → **no functional difference**

**"Volar comminution — contraindicated?"** → **Kapandji pinning alone**

**"Dorsal bridge plate — weight bearing?"** → **platform walker immediately**

**"Acceptability EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **dorsal tilt up to 7°**

**"Redisplacement predictor?"** → **severity of initial displacement**

**"Ex-fix — volar translation of lunate?"** → **restores volar tilt**

**"Nerve most injured placing ex-fix?"** → **superficial radial**

**"Ex-fix vs volar plate?"** → **both satisfactory**

**"Pin-site care?"** → **no special solution better than soap + water**

**"Formal therapy vs home exercise?"** → **no long-term difference** (early benefit only)

**"DASH variability after nonop?"** → **depression**

**"Thenar base hyperaesthesia after Henry approach?"** → **palmar cutaneous branch injury**

**"Void filler incorporated at 1 yr?"** → **calcium phosphate**

**"Henry approach — FALSE (TO)?"** → **PQ elevated from ulnar side**

**"NOT a Colles complication (TO)?"** → **gunstock deformity**

**"NOT true about chauffeur fracture (TO)?"** → **ulnar styloid fracture**

**"Young drummer, nascent malunion at 2 mo?"** → **corrective osteotomy**

# Distal Radius Malunion

*Trauma · 15 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Most common complication of distal radius fractures**

**Rates** — **17% overall** · **11% after operative** · **24% after definitive casting**

**Risk factors** — **post-reduction malalignment**, **osteoporosis**

### Anatomy – three columns

**Distal radius** — carries **80% of wrist axial load**

| Column | Contents | Role |
|---|---|---|
| **Radial** | Radial styloid, **scaphoid fossa** | Buttress vs radiocarpal translation; load in ulnar deviation; anchors **radioscaphocapitate** (vs ulnar translation) |
| **Intermediate** | **Lunate fossa** | Main load transfer carpus → forearm |
| **Ulnar** | TFCC + distal ulna | DRUJ stability |

### Biomechanics

| Deformity | Effect |
|---|---|
| **↓ volar tilt (dorsal tilt)** | **Limits flexion + supination** |
| **↑ volar tilt** | **Limits extension + pronation** |
| **↓ radial inclination** | ↓ flexor mechanical advantage → **hand weakness** |
| **Radial shortening** | **↑ positive ulnar variance**; load shifts to ulnocarpal joint; **largest change in DRUJ kinematics** |
| Volar tilt change | DRUJ incongruity → IOM tightens → ↓ rotation + flexion–extension arc |

**Volar-angulated malunion (OB stem)** — **↓ supination + extension**, **dorsal subluxation of radius in supination** (OB) vs ↑ volar tilt limits extension + pronation (Orthobullets) — read the options

**Dorsal malunion carpal instability** — (1) **adaptive midcarpal DISI** · (2) **dorsal radiocarpal subluxation** with maintained midcarpal alignment

## 2 Mechanism

**How** — initial displacement · **secondary loss of reduction in cast** · failed fixation

| Pattern | Note |
|---|---|
| **Dorsally angulated (apex volar)** | **Most common**; DISI-like collapse |
| Volarly angulated (apex dorsal) | — |
| Radial shortening | DRUJ kinematics |
| Intra-articular | With **initial intra-articular displacement > 2 mm** |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain (localise radial vs ulnar side → guides ulnar procedures), **grip weakness**, ↓ motion with **pronation / supination worst**

**Exam** — deformity, tenderness map, ROM all planes, **bilateral grip dynamometry**, **DRUJ shuck test** (pain with instability), Allen test, CTS, CRPS

**Late DRUJ instability in adult (longshoreman)** — from untreated **childhood distal radius fracture** malunion\
✗ Wrong options: supracondylar (cubitus varus / valgus) · medial epicondyle · both-bone forearm (DRUJ preserved) · elbow dislocation

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Measure | Normal |
|---|---|
| **Radial height (PA)** | **8–14 mm** |
| **Radial inclination (PA)** | **21–25°** |
| Articular step-off | Congruous |
| **Volar tilt (lateral)** | **7–15°** |
| **Ulnar variance** | Neutral (< 1 mm change) |

![Radiographic parameters of the distal radius](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob423135-radiographic-parameters-with-appropriate-measurements-obtain.jpg)

**Views** — **bilateral** PA, lateral, oblique · **anatomic (tilted) lateral** for lunate facet

**CT** — **intra-articular malunion**, axial rotation, **DRUJ instability (> 50% translation vs other side)**

**MRI** — TFCC

## 5 Management

```
Symptomatic distal radius malunion
├─ Low demand / CRPS / can't comply → therapy, NSAIDs, splint
├─ No arthritis → corrective osteotomy ± graft + plate
│      └─ ulnar positive with good radial alignment → ulnar procedure
├─ Arthritis → salvage (wrist fusion, etc.)
└─ Pain only, low demand, good function → PIN / AIN neurectomy
```

### Corrective osteotomy for symptomatic malunion

**Indications** — symptomatic after failed casting or failed fixation with **mechanical symptoms, ↓ grip, ↓ ROM, instability on stress**; **Fernandez**: active patient, extra-articular **> 25–30° angulation**, **no degenerative change**

**Ballet dancer, dorsal angulation, joint space preserved** — **corrective osteotomy** · arthritis → salvage\
✗ Wrong options: wrist arthrodesis · PRC · SLAC procedures · interposition

**Nonop outcomes** — **age 18–65 → more adverse outcomes** when untreated

| Approach | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|
| **Dorsal (2nd–4th compartments)** | Easy osteotomy; **tension-side** fixation; for dorsal subluxation | **Extensor tendon** irritation |
| **Volar (extended FCR)** | Align volar cortex → stability, **may avoid graft**; for volar subluxation; volar malunions | **Flexor tendon** irritation |

| Osteotomy | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Opening wedge** | **Needs bone graft** |
| **Closing wedge** | Bone contact + compression; **↑ ulnar variance → often needs ulnar shortening** |
| Intra-articular | Arthroscopy-aided; outcomes ≈ extra-articular osteotomy; **push-pull screw** restores height |

**Graft** — **structural bicortical ICBG** → union \~7 wk, better supination · **cancellous distal radius ≈ structural ICBG** · allograft, DBM, calcium phosphate options

**Complication rate** — extra-articular correction **\~50%**; **higher with distraction osteotomy**

### Denervation

**PIN ± AIN neurectomy** — debilitating pain + infection, low demand, preserved function; dorsal incision proximal to DRUJ; PIN on radial floor of **4th compartment**; AIN after dividing IOM (1 cm excised)

### Ulnar-sided procedures

**Indications** — **ulnar-positive with preserved radial alignment**, DRUJ instability, TFCC tear, styloid nonunion

| Procedure | Rule |
|---|---|
| **Ulnar shortening osteotomy (Milch)** | **Isolated radial shortening**; **not with DRUJ arthrosis** |
| **Wafer (Feldon)** | **Intact TFCC**; angular deformities |
| **Hemiresection-interposition (Bowers)** | **Intact TFCC**; DRUJ arthrosis / instability |
| **Sauvé-Kapandji** | **Younger**, incongruity + degeneration; keeps rotation |
| **Darrach** | Salvage; don't over-resect |
| Ulnar head / DRUJ arthroplasty | Salvage |
| **One-bone forearm** | **Ultimate salvage**; no rotation |

**TFCC** — central perforations usually settle; irreparable + unstable DRUJ → **Adams reconstruction (palmaris graft)** or BR wrap; confirm reparability arthroscopically

**Styloid nonunion** — small + stable → **excise** · large + unstable → **repair** (headless screw, TBW, or excise + foveal TFCC repair)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Tendon irritation / rupture** | **20%** of complications; **EPL commonest**; rupture \~**10 wk**; risk: **distraction osteotomy**, dorsal callus, volar plate, dorsal osteophyte, prominent hardware |
| Others | Nerve injury, CRPS, nonunion, infection, PTOA |

## X vs Y

| | **↓ volar tilt (dorsal malunion)** | **↑ volar tilt (volar malunion)** |
|---|---|---|
| Lost motion | **Flexion + supination** | **Extension + pronation** (Orthobullets) |
| Carpus | DISI / dorsal subluxation | — |

| | **Opening wedge** | **Closing wedge** |
|---|---|---|
| Graft | **Needed** | Not needed |
| Ulnar variance | Restores length | **↑ positive → ulnar shortening** |

## How it's asked

**"Dancer 11 mo after casting, dorsal angulation, no arthritis?"** → **distal radius corrective osteotomy**

**"Adult with DRUJ instability from childhood injury?"** → **distal radius fracture (malunion)**

**"Commonest malunion direction?"** → **dorsal angulation (apex volar)**

**"Deformity with biggest DRUJ kinematic change?"** → **radial shortening**

**"↓ radial inclination causes?"** → **grip weakness**

**"Ulnar shortening contraindicated with?"** → **DRUJ arthrosis**

**"Commonest tendon complication?"** → **EPL rupture (\~10 wk)**
