# Domestic and Elder Abuse

*Trauma · 16 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### IPV epidemiology

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Women experiencing domestic violence | **\~25%** (Orthobullets) · lifetime **\~30% / 33% (PRAISE)** (OB) |
| Women in ER with injuries due to DV | **up to 35%** |
| Past-year IPV in women at fracture clinics | **1 in 6** · **32%** within 1 yr before orthopaedic visit |
| Fracture-clinic visits directly from IPV | **1 in 50** |
| Ever asked about IPV in health care | Only **14%** |
| Men who are victims | 15% |
| Children abused in DV homes | **\~50%** |
| Surgeon-perceived vs actual prevalence | **\~10% vs up to 40% → 4× under-estimated** |

**DV = commonest cause of non-fatal injury to women**

### Elder abuse epidemiology

**Scale** — \~2 million Americans / yr; **1 in 25** over 65; widely **under-reported**; forms: physical, mental, neglect, financial, abandonment

## 2 Mechanism

### IPV risk factors

| Risk factor | Note |
|---|---|
| **Female** | — |
| **Age 19–29** (Orthobullets) · **16–49** (OB) · 20–29 (OB) | Young |
| **Pregnancy** | **Highest risk**; 40–60% of battered women abused in pregnancy |
| **Short-term relationship** | — |
| **Cohabitation** | — |
| **Low income / SES** (< $10,000 / yr) | (✗ Wrong options: "equal in all incomes") |
| Alcohol / drug dependence | — |
| ≥ 1 prior child | Higher prevalence with children |
| Not risk factors | **Prior divorce**, no children, older age, long relationship, living apart |

### Elder abuse risk factors

**Victim** — **↑ age**, **functional disability**, **cognitive impairment / dementia**, poor physical + emotional health, disruptive / aggressive behaviour, social isolation, poverty, race, prior violence toward abuser; regional child-abuse rates

**Caretaker** — **substance abuse**, mental illness, **financial dependence on elder**, burden perception, shared living, stress

**Gender** — **not** a risk factor for elder abuse

## 3 Presentation

### Red flags (injury pattern)

**Injuries** — **inconsistent with history**, **long delay to treatment**, **repeat injuries**, **multiple injuries of varying ages**, multiple providers / EDs, unusual patterns

**Most common IPV manifestation** — **head, neck, face injuries** (unwitnessed)

**Elder abuse** — **long-bone + rib fractures**, bruises **> 5 cm on face, neck, back**, burns, lacerations, poor hygiene, behaviour change

### Behaviour

**Victim** — changed affect, withdrawn, seeks partner's approval, **repeated ED visits**, wants to stay in hospital, **missed work / ↓ productivity**, chronic headaches, sleep disturbance, high-risk behaviour, GI / psychosomatic / pelvic symptoms

**Abuser** — won't leave, overly attentive, hostile, **answers for the patient**

**Repeated ED visits, vague pain, frightened, ecchymoses, negative imaging** — **IPV**\
✗ Wrong options: malingering · CRPS · anxiety · fibromyalgia

### Barriers to disclosure

**Barriers** — **fear of retaliation**, shame, embarrassment, **reluctance with male physicians**, custody fears, downplaying

**Emotional abuse** — **harder to detect** than physical

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**No skeletal survey** in competent adults (or pregnancy)

**Documentation** — exact quotes, abuser's name, body maps / drawings, **photographs with consent**, demeanour, opinion if injuries don't fit story

## 5 Management

```
Suspected abuse
├─ Child → report (all states)
├─ Elder → report (almost all states); in danger → admit / transfer to ED
└─ Adult IPV → interview alone, screen, document, social work, resources
       └─ report only per state law / with consent
```

### Screening & management

**First step in suspected IPV** — **speak with patient alone, privately, non-judgementally** → resources\
✗ Wrong options: confront partner · treat + discharge · mandatory psych referral · call police first

**Pregnant, inconsistent history, bruises** — after treating fracture → **social work consult**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopy · MRI · skeletal survey · urgent ORIF

**Shoulder dislocation + positive pregnancy test** — **ask privately about injury + home safety**\
✗ Wrong options: skeletal survey · nutritionist · anorexia admission · MRI

**Treat injuries promptly** regardless of social situation\
✗ Wrong options: "resume care once social situation assessed"

**Interdisciplinary collaboration** → better outcomes

### Reporting obligations

| Victim | Duty |
|---|---|
| **Child** | **Mandatory in all states** |
| **Elder** | **Mandatory in almost all states**; **hospitalise if in immediate danger** |
| **Adult IPV** | **Not standardised**; many states need consent; physician **has no authority to provide protection** → encourage victim to report |

**Federal law does NOT mandate photographs** (EXCEPT answer) — only with consent

**AAOS roles** — know state laws, be aware you may be first physician, keep accurate records, transfer endangered elder to ED + notify, advocate legislation

## X vs Y

| | **IPV** | **Elder abuse** |
|---|---|---|
| Gender risk | **Female** | **Not a risk factor** |
| Reporting | Varies by state | Mandatory (almost all) |
| Classic injury | Head / neck / face | Long bones, ribs, large bruises |

## How it's asked

**"DV management EXCEPT?"** → **federal law mandates photographs**

**"Orthopaedist's role EXCEPT?"** → **report all adult IPV in all states**

**"IPV — true?"** → **interdisciplinary collaboration helps**

**"Dislocation + pregnancy, inconsistent story?"** → **question privately about home safety**

**"AAOS statement EXCEPT?"** → **resume care once social situation assessed**

**"Pregnant, bruises, delay — next step?"** → **social worker consult**

**"Withdrawn, partner interrupts — first step?"** → **speak with patient alone**

**"IPV risk factors EXCEPT?"** → **prior divorce**

**"4th ED visit, vague pain, bruises?"** → **IPV**

**"IPV — true?"** → **4× more prevalent than surgeons perceive**

**"Risk factor for IPV?"** → **cohabitation**

**"IPV — true (facial bruises)?"** → **head + neck injuries commonest**

**"IPV — true (partner answers)?"** → **highest prevalence age 16–49**

**"Elder abuse risk factors EXCEPT?"** → **gender**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Elbow Dislocation

*Trauma · 17 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rank** — **2nd commonest major joint dislocation** (after shoulder); **commonest dislocation in children**

**Share** — **10–25%** of elbow injuries · **posterolateral 80%** · peak **age 10–20**

**Simple vs complex** — **simple (no fracture) 50–60%** · complex 40–50%

**Associated** — shoulder / wrist injuries **10–15%** · radial head fracture up to 10%

### Anatomy – stabilisers

| Type | Structures |
|---|---|
| **Primary static** | **Ulnohumeral joint**, **anterior bundle MCL** (key valgus), **LCL complex incl. LUCL** (primary varus + posterolateral rotatory) |
| **Secondary static** | **Radiocapitellar joint**, **capsule**, **common flexor + extensor origins** |
| **Dynamic** | **Anconeus, brachialis, triceps** (+ biceps) — compression |

**LCL (TO)** — radial collateral, annular, **lateral ulnar collateral** · **MCL (TO)** — **anterior bundle (valgus)**, posterior bundle (secondary valgus + ulnar rotation) · extensors resist varus, flexors resist valgus

## 2 Mechanism

**Posterolateral dislocation** — **axial load + forearm supination / ER + valgus posterolateral force** (O'Driscoll) · varus posteromedial mechanism also reported

**Circle of Horii (lateral → medial)** — **LCL fails first** → capsule → **MCL last** (energy dependent); some recent work suggests failure may start medially (TO)

**LUCL failure mode** — **avulsion from humeral (lateral epicondylar) origin** · McKee: proximal avulsion 32, midsubstance 18, bony humeral 5, ulnar 3 / 62\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar insertion · midsubstance (less common) · bony avulsion

**MRI of torn LCL** — avulsed off lateral epicondyle (MCL + flexor origin intact)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — pain, swelling, deformity; **skin (open?)**; **compartments**; **wrist + shoulder**; NV

**Commonest nerve injury (TO)** — **ulnar nerve** (stretch) · median rare (with brachial artery) · brachial artery (rare; open)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**AP + lateral** — congruency esp. post-reduction · **obliques** — periarticular fractures · **CT** — complex patterns, fractures, loose bodies

**"Drop sign" (TO)** — **↑ static ulnohumeral distance** on post-reduction lateral → residual posterolateral subluxation

### Classification

| Basis | Types |
|---|---|
| **Anatomic** (olecranon vs humerus) | **Posterolateral (commonest)** · posteromedial |
| **Simple** | No fracture |
| **Complex** | With fracture: **terrible triad** (dislocation + LUCL + radial head + coronoid tip) · **varus PMRI** (LCL + anteromedial coronoid; radial head spared) |

![Simple posterolateral elbow dislocation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1018-posterolateralelbowdislocation.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Elbow dislocation
├─ Simple, stable after reduction → splint 5–10 d → early active ROM
│      └─ residual subluxation (drop sign) → active ROM pronated, avoid varus
├─ Unstable (needs > 50–60° flexion) / irreducible → open, repair LCL ± MCL
├─ Complex → fix coronoid / radial head / olecranon + LCL ± MCL
└─ Chronic → open reduction, release, hinged ex-fix
```

### Simple dislocation – reduction & early motion

**Reduction** — analgesia + relaxation → **in-line traction** (coronal) + **forearm supination** (coronoid under trochlea) + **flex elbow with pressure on olecranon** → "clunk"

**Post-reduction test** — unstable in **extension** + **valgus**; test valgus with **forearm pronated** (locks lateral side)

**Splint** — posterior splint **≥ 90° flexion**, rotation of maximum stability: **LCL torn → pronation** · **MCL torn → supination** (O'Driscoll: reduce in supination, immobilise in pronation) · neutral acceptable

**Duration** — **5–10 days** (5–7 d, OB) → **early active ROM** through stable arc; hinged brace with extension block **3–4 wk**; light duty at 2 wk; full extension by **6–8 wk** · avoid varus / valgus loading until 12 wk (TO)

**Stable after reduction — start motion** — **\~1 week**\
✗ Wrong options: immediately · 3 wk · 6 wk · after LCL repair

**Best outcome predictor** — **mobilisation within 5–10 days** (> 10 days → arthrofibrosis, pain) · immobilisation **> 3 wk → poor final ROM**

**Avoid** — **immobilisation 4 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: vs active motion, 90° neutral splint, extension-limited early rehab, light duty (all OK)

**Simple dislocation, can't move elbow** — **closed reduction, splint, early ACTIVE ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: passive ROM · hinged ex-fix · acute LCL repair · open reduction

**Residual posterolateral subluxation (drop sign) (TO)** — **splint 1 wk → active motion with forearm PRONATED through full range**; elbow at side (no varus / shoulder abduction); supinate only at ≥ 90° flexion\
✗ Wrong options: LCL reconstruction · splint 1 month

**Recurrent instability after simple dislocation** — **rare (< 1–2%)**

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Irreducible** (soft tissue / osteochondral fragment) | Open reduction |
| **Persistent instability** — needs **> 50–60° flexion** to stay reduced (Orthobullets, OB) · **redislocates at < 30° flexion** (TO) | **Repair LCL ± MCL** |
| **Complex** (coronoid, radial head, olecranon) | ORIF / arthroplasty + LCL ± MCL |
| Open, vascular injury, recurrent | Surgery |
| Throwers | ± MCL repair |
| **Chronic dislocation** | **Open reduction + capsular release + dynamic hinged ex-fix** |
| Still unstable after all repairs | **Hinged external fixator** |

**Highly unstable after closed reduction** — **open reduction + LCL repair ± MCL repair**\
✗ Wrong options: 6 wk splint · ex-fix alone · internal joint stabiliser alone · reconstruction + 6 wk immobilisation

**Essential lesion in persistent instability** — **LUCL**\
✗ Wrong options: MCL (repaired only if still unstable) · coracohumeral · capsule

**Structure needing repair** — **LUCL**\
✗ Wrong options: radial collateral · MCL bands · accessory collateral

### Approaches

**Kocher (ECU–anconeus)** — LCL, extensor origin, coronoid, capitellum, radial head; incise **anterior to radial head midline** to spare posterior LCL fibres; careful retractors → **PIN**

**Medial** — MCL, flexor-pronator, comminuted coronoid; **protect ulnar nerve**

### Complex dislocation – fixation

**Coronoid** — tip fractures rarely need ORIF (proximal to brachialis); lateral or medial approach

**Radial head ORIF** — **safe zone ≈ 90° non-articulating arc** (between Lister tubercle + radial styloid lines) (Orthobullets, OB) · **\~110°** in original dissections · **69%** of head articulates with ulna

**Radial head arthroplasty** — if unreconstructable; **anatomic size / length** → restores varus + ER stability **only once LCL repaired** · **excision → varus / ER instability** without LCL

**Dislocation + comminuted radial head (55-yo)** — **open reduction, LCL repair, radial head ORIF or metallic replacement**\
✗ Wrong options: cast 4 wk · brace + immediate ROM · silastic implant (contraindicated) · excision

### Complex dislocation – coronoid & PMRI

**Varus PMRI** — **LCL rupture (humeral) + anteromedial coronoid facet fracture + ulnohumeral subluxation / dislocation**; radial head usually spared

**Dislocation + anteromedial coronoid fracture on AP** — highest risk **varus PMRI**\
✗ Wrong options: AIN / PIN palsy · PLRI · chair-rise instability

**Fixation preventing PMRI** — **anteromedial coronoid facet** · failure to fix / malunion → poor function + arthrosis (Doornberg & Ring)\
✗ Wrong options: radial head · coronoid tip · olecranon tip · medial capitellum

**Transolecranon fracture-dislocation (TO)** — **anterior dislocation with intact PRUJ**, mainly **bony**, usually **no ligament injury**, coronoid fracture common → **NOT a Monteggia variant** (EXCEPT) · type I simple / II complex · restore ulnar length, alignment, rotation + **sigmoid notch**; missed coronoid = failure

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Loss of terminal extension** | **Commonest** (simple + complex) → early active ROM; static progressive splint at 6–8 wk |
| Stiffness / contracture | **Immobilisation > 3 wk** |
| **HO** | May need excision |
| Varus PMRI | LCL + anteromedial facet |
| Nerve | **Ulnar** (stretch) · **AIN palsy** (weak pinch, can't flex thumb IP — misread as FPL rupture) |
| Brachial artery | Rare, open dislocations |
| Compartment syndrome, articular damage, recurrent instability | — |

### Post-traumatic stiffness – release & HO excision

**Functional arc** — **30–130°**

**Arc 30–95° (flexion-limited) + HO** — **excise HO + release posterior bundle of MCL + ulnar nerve decompression** (flexion < 100° → decompress)\
✗ Wrong options: supracondylar osteotomy + anterior MCL release · anterior capsule release (helps extension) · **LCL release → instability**

**MCL release order** — **posterior bundle → transverse → (posterior half of anterior bundle)**

**Extension loss 80° with mature anterolateral HO, no ulnar symptoms** — **open resection + capsulectomy via lateral approach** (access anterior + posterior)\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic (risky with tight capsule) · anterior approach (no posterior access) · indomethacin + radiation (too late once visible) · manipulation (contraindicated)

**HO maturation** — fluffy → sharp margins over **3–6 months**; labs + bone scan not prognostic; prophylaxis (NSAID, **700 cGy**) ineffective once visible

## X vs Y

| | **Terrible triad (PLRI)** | **Varus PMRI** |
|---|---|---|
| Force | Valgus, supination, axial | Varus, posteromedial, axial |
| Radial head | **Fractured** | **Spared** |
| Coronoid | **Tip** | **Anteromedial facet** |
| Ligament | LCL (± MCL last) | LCL humeral avulsion |

| | **LCL torn** | **MCL torn** |
|---|---|---|
| Most stable forearm position | **Pronation** | **Supination** |

| | **Transolecranon fracture-dislocation** | **Posterior Monteggia** |
|---|---|---|
| PRUJ | **Intact** | Disrupted |
| Ligaments | Usually intact | LCL injured in up to ⅔ |

## How it's asked

**"Commonest sequel of simple + complex dislocations?"** → **loss of terminal extension**

**"Rehab protocol to AVOID?"** → **immobilisation 4 wk then PT**

**"Simple dislocation — next step?"** → **closed reduction, splint, early active ROM**

**"Stable after reduction — when to start motion?"** → **1 week**

**"Best outcome strategy?"** → **mobilise within 5–10 days**

**"Post-reduction drop sign — management?"** → **splint 1 wk, then active motion forearm pronated** (TO)

**"LUCL failure mode?"** → **avulsion from humeral origin**

**"Essential lesion in recurrent instability?"** → **LUCL**

**"Torn structure on coronal MRI?"** → **LCL (avulsed from epicondyle)**

**"Highly unstable, won't stay reduced?"** → **open reduction + LCL repair ± MCL repair**

**"Structure most needing repair?"** → **LUCL**

**"Transolecranon fracture-dislocation EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **Monteggia variant**

**"Dislocation + anteromedial facet — risk?"** → **varus PMRI**

**"Dislocation + comminuted radial head?"** → **open reduction, LCL repair, radial head ORIF / metal replacement**

**"Long jumper, MCL attachment fracture — pattern?"** → **LCL disruption + anteromedial coronoid facet + dislocation**

**"Fixation preventing PMRI?"** → **anteromedial coronoid facet**

**"Stiff 30–95° with HO, ulnar symptoms?"** → **HO excision + posterior MCL bundle release + ulnar decompression**

**"Extension loss with mature anterior HO?"** → **open resection + capsulectomy via lateral approach**

**"Weak pinch, can't flex thumb IP after dislocation?"** → **AIN palsy**

**"Radial head articulating with ulna?"** → **69%** (safe zone \~90°)

**"Commonest nerve injured (TO)?"** → **ulnar**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Evaluation, Resuscitation & DCO

*Trauma · 18 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Trimodal death after trauma

| Peak | Share | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| **Minutes** | **50%** | Massive bleeding, neurological injury |
| **Hours** | **30%** | Shock, hypoxia, neurological injury |
| **Days–weeks** | **20%** | **MOF, infection** |

**Golden hour** — \~60% of preventable deaths

**Airbags (head-on)** — ↓ head injury, facial fractures, thoraco-abdominal injury (splenic rupture, flail chest), extraction · **pelvic ring injuries NOT reduced**

**Psychological** — **PTSD \~50%** after orthopaedic trauma (**females 4×**; LE / pelvic injuries 2× UE) · depression 33%

**PTSD risk factor** — **high ISS** (> 15 = polytrauma)\
✗ Wrong options: social history · timing of surgery · tobacco · rehab duration

**Female sex is linked to** — **PTSD**\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion · malunion · infection · fat embolism

### Genes & lab markers

**IL-6** — **most reliable marker of systemic inflammatory response**; correlates with **injury severity, complications, mortality** · **> 500 pg/dL → DCO**\
✗ Wrong options: IL-8, IL-10, CRP, TNF-α (too variable)

**Elevated IL-6** — ↑ **mortality** · also sensitive for **PJI**\
✗ Wrong options: osteomyelitis, rhabdo, compartment syndrome

**Lactate** — normal **< 2–2.5 mmol/L** (0.6–1.7 in one OB explanation); **most sensitive** marker of occult hypoperfusion; **last to normalise**; clearance within **12–24 h** predicts survival

**Base deficit** — normal **−2 to +2**; global hypoperfusion; **most predictive of mortality** (incl. normotensive elderly); confounded by saline (hyperchloraemia), renal failure, DKA, CO₂ retention

**Gastric mucosal pH** — normal **> 7.3 (7.30–7.35)**; regional endpoint

### SIRS criteria (≥ 2)

| Parameter | Threshold |
|---|---|
| Temperature | **> 38 °C or < 36 °C** |
| Heart rate | **> 90 / min** |
| Respiration | **RR > 20 or PaCO₂ < 32 mm Hg** |
| WBC | **> 12,000, < 4000, or > 10% bands** |

**Lactate is NOT a SIRS criterion**

**Adults vs children** — **adults: robust systemic response, lungs fail first (day 2–3)** · children: dampened systemic, robust local, all organs simultaneously

## 2 Mechanism

**Two-hit theory** — **1st hit** (hypoxia, hypotension, soft tissue, fractures) + **2nd hit** (surgery, ischaemia-reperfusion, compartment syndrome, infection) → SIRS → ARDS / MOF

**Compensated shock** — normal vitals with ongoing hypoperfusion (**up to 85%**) → **"primed" immune system → exaggerated SIRS** to a 2nd hit\
✗ Wrong options: "no risk" · ↑ nonunion / infection after staged fixation

**Lethal triad (TO)** — **hypothermia + acidosis + coagulopathy**\
✗ Wrong options: haemorrhage · hypoxia · hypotension

## 3 Presentation

### Primary / secondary / tertiary survey

**Primary (ABCDE)** — treat greatest threat first; **A** incl. C-spine · **B** · **C** incl. haemorrhage control (pelvis!) · **D** · **E**

**Pregnancy** — **left lateral decubitus** (or right hip wedge) → relieves aortocaval compression (supine ↓ CO 25%)

**Tertiary survey** — when mental status recovers → ↓ missed injuries

**Missed fractures** — commonest cause = **extremity never imaged**\
✗ Wrong options: X-ray read wrongly · MRI / CT not done

### Haemorrhagic shock classes (ATLS)

| Class | Blood loss | HR | BP | Pulse pressure | Urine | Mental state | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **< 15% (< 750 mL)** | Normal | Normal | Normal / widened | > 30 mL/h | Anxious | Fluid |
| **II** | **15–30% (750–1500)** | **> 100** | **Normal** (↑ diastolic) | **Narrowed** | 20–30 mL/h | Confused, irritable | Fluid |
| **III** | **30–40% (1500–2000)** | **> 120** | **↓ (first hypotension)** | Narrow | 5–15 mL/h | Lethargic | **Fluid + blood** |
| **IV** | **> 40% (> 2000)** | **> 140** | ↓↓ | **Very narrow** | Negligible | Coma | Fluid + blood |

**Blood volume** — adult **\~5 L (4.7–5 L, \~7% body weight)** · child 2–10 yr **75–80 mL/kg**

**First tachycardia + narrow pulse pressure** — **\~25% loss (class II)**

**2 L loss in 70 kg** — **class IV**: ↓ SBP + narrow pulse pressure\
✗ Wrong options: widened = class I · normal SBP with ↑ diastolic = class II

**HR 135, BP 85/58, low urine** — **30–40% (class III)**

**Each thigh** — holds **\~20%** of blood volume; isolated femur fracture can cause **class II** shock

### Non-haemorrhagic shock

| | **Hypovolaemic** | **Septic** | **Neurogenic** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **SVR** | **↑** | **↓ (key discriminator)** | ↓ (vasodilation) |
| Cardiac output | ↓ | **↑** (early; ↓ end-stage) | ↓ |
| PCWP | ↓ | ↓ | — |
| CVP | ↓ | ↓ | — |
| Mixed venous O₂ | ↓ | **↑** | — |
| HR | **Tachycardia** | Tachycardia | **Bradycardia** |
| Skin | Cool, clammy | — | **Warm, dry** |

**Haemorrhagic vs neurogenic** — **tachycardia** favours haemorrhagic\
✗ Wrong options: bradycardia · ↓ CO (both) · vasodilation · warm skin

**Septic shock** — **↓ SVR** · mortality 25–50%\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ SVR (EXCEPT answer)

**Swan-Ganz (TO)** — differentiates shock types: **PAWP ↑ in cardiogenic**, ↓ in hypovolaemic / distributive (TO stem: cardiogenic shock after spinal injury)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **AP chest** | Mediastinal widening, PTX |
| Lateral C-spine | Must see C7–T1; largely replaced by **CT** |
| **AP pelvis** | Ring, acetabulum, proximal femur; **CT after preliminary pelvic stabilisation** |
| **CT C-spine, chest, abdo, pelvis** | Rule out life-threatening injuries |
| **Image any limb** with pain, crepitus, ecchymosis, deformity | Avoid missed fractures |

### Resuscitation endpoints

| Endpoint | Adequate |
|---|---|
| **Lactate** | **< 2 mmol/L** (best, TO) · **< 2.5** (Orthobullets) |
| **Base deficit** | **−2 to +2** |
| Gastric mucosal pH | **> 7.3** |
| Urine output | **≥ 0.5–1 mL/kg/h** (\~30 mL/h) |
| Pulse pressure | > 15 mm Hg |

**Resuscitation complete** — **oxygen debt repaid, aerobic metabolism restored in ALL tissue beds**\
✗ Wrong options: MAP > 90 · pulse pressure · urine output · maximised O₂ delivery

**Normal BP, HR, urine after fluids** — resuscitation **cannot be determined** without lactate / BD / pHi

**Occult hypoperfusion** — **lactate 4.5–5 mmol/L**\
✗ Wrong options: HR 80 · SBP 120 · BD −1.8 · urine 40 mL/h

**Successfully resuscitated** — **lactate 1.9**\
✗ Wrong options: urine 0.25 mL/kg/h · BD 5.5 · gastric pH 6.5 · pulse pressure 15

**Alternative to lactate / BD** — **gastric intramucosal pH 7.4**

**Normal lactate + BE despite abnormal vitals** — **adequate tissue perfusion**\
✗ Wrong options: arterial line best · normal Hct = no shock (Hct lags)

**Variable best indicating readiness for definitive fixation** — **base deficit / lactate**\
✗ Wrong options: pulse pressure · HR · Hct · urine · SBP · platelets

**Most predictive of mortality** — **base deficit (e.g. BE −12)**\
✗ Wrong options: ETCO₂ · Hct · HR · BP

### Patient condition (Pape) — 3 of 4 criteria

| Criterion | Stable | Borderline | Unstable | In extremis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **SBP (mm Hg)** | ≥ 100 | 80–100 | **< 90** | **≤ 70** |
| Blood units / 2 h | 0–2 | 2–8 | 5–15 | > 15 |
| **Lactate** | Normal | ≈ 2.5 | **> 2.5** | Severe acidosis |
| Base deficit | Normal | — | — | > 6–8 |
| ATLS class | I | II–III | III–IV | IV |
| **Platelets** | > 110,000 | 90–110,000 | **70–90,000** | **< 70,000** |
| Fibrinogen (g/L) | > 1 | ≈ 1 | < 1 | DIC |
| **Temperature (°C)** | — | **33–35** | **30–32** | ≤ 30 |
| PaO₂/FiO₂ | 350–400 | 300–350 | 200–300 | < 200 |
| Chest AIS | 1–2 | ≥ 2 (2–3 rib fractures) | ≥ 3 | ≥ 3 (unstable chest) |
| Pelvic (AO) | A | B / C | C | C (crush) |

**OB unstable thresholds** — **SBP < 90**, **lactate > 2.5**, **platelets < 90,000**, fibrinogen < 1, **temp < 35 °C**, pulmonary contusion, severe liver / spleen, pelvic ring disruption · lactate **> 6 alone** → DCO

**TO DCO criteria (TO)** — **pH < 7.24**, **lactate > 2.5**, **temp < 35 °C**, **platelets < 90,000**, op time > 90 min, > 10 U PRBC · **SBP < 90 is NOT among these (TO)**

## 5 Management

```
Polytrauma with long-bone / pelvic fractures
├─ Unstable / extremis / ongoing acidosis / head or chest injury → DCO
│      └─ ex-fix long bones + pelvis, splint UE, I&D open → definitive later
├─ Stable, resuscitated → early definitive fixation (EAC, < 36 h)
└─ Borderline → DCO if any parameter fails
```

### Fluids & transfusion

**Resuscitate** — isotonic crystalloid → **O-negative / type-specific / cross-matched blood**

**Massive transfusion** — **1 : 1 : 1 (PRBC : platelets : plasma)** + **IV calcium** (≥ 4 U)

| Transfusion risk | Rate |
|---|---|
| **HBV — highest viral risk** | 1 in 205,000 (Orthobullets) |
| HCV | 1 in 1.8 million (Orthobullets) · 1 in 103,000 (OB) |
| HIV | 1 in 1.9 million (Orthobullets) · 1 in 500,000 (OB) |
| **Clerical error → haemolytic reaction — most prevalent adverse event** | **1 in 12,000–50,000** |
| Anaphylaxis | 1 in 150,000 |
| Bacterial sepsis | 1 in 1 million |

### Damage control vs early total care

**ETC (1980s)** — fix everything at once ("too sick not to operate") → **2nd hit** in unstable, head, chest injuries

**DCO (2000)** — temporary stabilisation (ex-fix, traction, splints, I&D) → definitive once physiology improves

**EAC (2013)** — definitive fixation of **spine, pelvis, acetabulum, femur within 36 h** once **any ONE** of: **lactate < 4.0 mmol/L**, **pH ≥ 7.25**, **base excess ≥ −5.5 (BD ≤ 5.5)** → ↓ delay, ↓ complications (ARDS, MOF, death), ↓ LOS, ↑ revenue; main delay = surgeon decision

### Operative criteria – DCO indications

| DCO indication | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **ISS** | **> 40** without chest trauma · **> 20** with chest trauma |
| **GCS** | **≤ 8** · **head injury AIS ≥ 3** |
| **Bilateral femur fractures** | Yes |
| **Pulmonary contusion on radiograph** | Yes (✗ Wrong options: on CT only · rib fractures alone) |
| **Hypothermia** | **< 35 °C** |
| **Severe pelvic / abdominal injury + haemorrhagic shock** | Yes (e.g. grade IV liver + SBP 85) |
| **IL-6** | **> 500 pg/dL** |
| Persistent acidosis | Lactate, BD, pH out of range |

**Not DCO triggers** — HR alone, SBP 90 alone, unilateral femur fracture, lactate 2.3–2.5, Hb < 9, rib fractures + PTX (borderline), pubic rami fracture (stable), temp 35.5 (borderline)

**Head injury** — **intra-operative hypotension ↑ mortality** → avoid long surgery; SDH needing CPP monitoring → **ex-fix femur**; maintain **CPP > 70 mm Hg** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: reamed / unreamed nail · traction · beads

### Inflammatory window & timing

**Acute inflammatory window** — **days 2–5** → avoid elective definitive surgery; operate only for life / limb threats: **unstable pelvis**, compartment syndrome, vascular injury, unreduced dislocations, amputations, unstable spine, cauda equina, open fractures

**Definitive conversion** — **pelvis 7–10 d** · **femur ex-fix → nail within 3 wk** · **tibia 7–10 d**

**Ex-fix → nail at 7 d** — **equal union + infection rates**\
✗ Wrong options: higher infection / nonunion / ARDS / mortality

### DCO scenarios

| Scenario | Answer |
|---|---|
| Unstable (MAP 51, BD 10, SDH) femur + open plateau | **I&D + ex-fix femur + ex-fix plateau** |
| BD 10, bilateral femurs, open tibia, forearm | **Ex-fix both femurs + tibia, I&D, splint forearm** (✗ Wrong options: ORIF forearm · nails) |
| Bilateral chest tubes, lactate 7, open femur + tibia | **I&D + bilateral ex-fix** (✗ Wrong options: nails · splints alone) |
| Lactate 10 unchanged after laparotomy, contusions | **Ex-fix tibia + femur** (✗ Wrong options: nails · slab + traction) |
| Lactate 8 → 7, femur + pelvis | **Ex-fix femur + pelvis** (✗ Wrong options: ORIF pelvis · splint femur) |
| High lactate, BD, lung injury — femur | **Ex-fix** (✗ Wrong options: nail · plate · traction (supine harmful)) |
| BD 10, oliguria, open foot, femur, forearm, head injury | **I&D, ex-fix ankle + foot + femur, splint forearm** |
| Rising lactate 3 → 5, core temp 34 °C | **Ex-fix femur** (✗ Wrong options: reamed nail · traction · plate · MAST) |
| pH 7.21, BD −11, pelvis + distal femur + humerus | **Ex-fix pelvis + femur, splint humerus** |
| pH 7.21, BE −6.5 | **DCO** (not EAC) |
| Vertical shear pelvis, lactate 9, laparotomy | **Skeletal traction** (✗ Wrong options: binder (worsens) · anterior ex-fix · ORIF · SI screws now) |
| SFA transection, unresuscitated, guarded prognosis | **Ex-fix femur + temporary arterial shunt** (+ fasciotomy) → definitive repair 24–48 h |
| **Stable, lactate 1.7**, femur + ankle + humerus | **IMN femur, splint ankle + humerus** |
| Stable, open ankle, femur + tibia | **IMN femur + tibia, I&D + ex-fix ankle** |
| Isolated floating knee, stable (ISS 27) | **IMN tibia + femur** (retrograde femur + antegrade tibia, one setup) |

**Upper-limb fractures in DCO** — **splint**

**Inadequately resuscitated for definitive care** — **BE −6.5**\
✗ Wrong options: lactate 2.0 · IL-6 300 · pH 7.28

**Supports early definitive care** — **BD 5.5**\
✗ Wrong options: IL-6 550 · pH 7.21 · lactate 4.4 · BE −5.8

**Femur fixation decision in polytrauma (TO)** — **lactate < 4.0** · **best resuscitation marker (TO)** — **lactate < 2 mmol/L**\
✗ Wrong options: BP · > 3 long bones · BE 5.5 as written

### Pelvic haemorrhage

**Order** — **reduce pelvic volume (sheet, binder, ex-fix, traction)** → stable → **CT chest / abdo / pelvis** · unstable → laparotomy and / or **angiography + embolisation** (venous bleeding commoner than arterial)

**Stabilised after sheet** — **CT** next\
✗ Wrong options: swap to binder · ER ex-fix · embolisation · ORIF

**APC II — avoid (TO)** — **log-rolling** (disturbs retroperitoneal clot) (✓ binder, ✓ perineal exam, ✓ early contrast CT)

**Pelvic packing** — **subumbilical midline incision** (6–8 cm up from symphysis) → split rectus fascia → **3 lap pads each side** below brim, SI joint → retropubic\
✗ Wrong options: ASIS–symphysis · ASIS–ASIS · pararectus

### Triage, transfer & battlefield

**Mass casualty triage** — immediate, delayed, minor, expectant

**ATLS transfer to trauma centre** — open pelvic fracture, major crush, multiple proximal long-bone fractures, solid-organ injury managed expectantly · **isolated clean open forearm fracture → may stay**

**Battlefield mangled leg bleeding (TO)** — **tourniquet first**\
✗ Wrong options: pressure dressing · haemostatic dressing · evacuate first

**Vascular reconstruction limit (TO)** — **6 h warm ischaemia** (TO) · ideally **< 8 h** from injury (OB)

### Head injury (TO)

| GCS (TO) | Eye | Verbal | Motor |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6 | — | — | Obeys |
| 5 | — | Oriented | **Localises** |
| 4 | **Spontaneous** | Confused | Withdraws |
| 3 | To voice | **Inappropriate words** | Flexion |
| 2 | To pain | Incomprehensible | Extension |
| 1 | None | None | None |

**Localises, eyes open spontaneously, words make no sense (TO)** — **E4 V3 M5**

| | **Extradural (TO)** | **Acute subdural (TO)** |
|---|---|---|
| Space | **Inner table ↔ dura** | **Under dura** |
| CT | **Biconvex** | **Crescentic** |
| Source | **Middle meningeal artery (85% arterial)**, pterion, temporoparietal fracture | Bridging veins / cortical laceration |
| Classic | **Lucid interval** (10–25%), contralateral hemiparesis, ipsilateral dilated pupil | More brain injury; worse prognosis |
| Surgery | **> 30 cm³** regardless of GCS; nonop if < 30 cm³ + < 15 mm + MLS < 5 mm + GCS > 8 + no deficit; **GCS < 9 + anisocoria → immediate** | **Thickness > 10 mm or MLS > 5 mm → evacuate regardless of GCS**; else if GCS ↓ ≥ 2, pupils abnormal, ICP > 20 |

**SDH age on CT (TO)** — acute 1–3 d **hyperdense** · subacute 4 d–3 wk **isodense** · chronic > 3 wk **hypodense** · anticoagulation ↑ ASDH 7× men, 26× women

**Uncal herniation (TO)** — **CN III palsy** (ipsilateral fixed dilated pupil, down + out)

**Kernohan notch (TO)** — midline shift pushes **contralateral cerebral peduncle** onto tentorium → **ipsilateral hemiplegia** (false localising)

**CSF rhinorrhoea after facial trauma (TO)** — **observe \~2 wk** (70% stop in 1 wk) · **β2-transferrin** confirms · **CT cisternography** localises · surgery if > 2 wk, delayed / spontaneous, recurrent meningitis; anosmia 78%\
✗ Wrong options: immediate dural repair · acetazolamide first · cautery

**Tension pneumocephalus (TO)** — **Mount Fuji sign**; trauma commonest; burr holes if symptomatic

**Carotico-cavernous fistula (TO)** — **pulsatile proptosis, chemosis, bruit, ↓ vision**, **CN VI palsy** commonest diplopia; direct type A (high flow, traumatic 0.2%) → embolisation

**Stems without answer key (TO)** — elderly on antiplatelets with confusion + gait disturbance (options EDH, chronic SDH, SAH, infarct) · 14-month-old enlarging parietal mass after injury at 8 mo (options growing skull fracture, hygroma, NCC, cephalhaematoma) · CT image identification — **answer key not provided by the source**

### Chest injuries (TO)

**Flail chest (TO)** — **≥ 2–3 adjacent ribs broken in ≥ 2 places** → **paradoxical movement**

**Stove-in chest (TO)** — flail segment **collapses inward**; **blunt** trauma\
✗ Wrong options: penetrating (NOT true)

## 6 Complications

**ARDS + MOF** — 2nd-hit during inflammatory window; ETC in unstable patients

**Fat embolism** — immediate nailing in unresuscitated chest-injured patients

**Hypothermia < 35 °C** — ↑ mortality

## X vs Y

| | **ETC** | **DCO** | **EAC** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Era | 1980s | 2000 | 2013 |
| Principle | Fix all at once | Stabilise → definitive later | Definitive < 36 h when resuscitated |
| Trigger | — | Unstable / extremis | Lactate < 4, pH ≥ 7.25, BE ≥ −5.5 (any one) |

| | **Class II** | **Class III** |
|---|---|---|
| Loss | 15–30% | 30–40% |
| BP | **Normal** | **↓** |
| HR | > 100 | > 120 |
| Blood needed | No | **Yes** |

## How it's asked

# Femoral Head Fractures

*Trauma · 19 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rare** — **12% of hip dislocations**; rising (more MVAs, better resuscitation)

**With posterior dislocation** — **5–15%** (Orthobullets) · **\~15%** (TO) · **95% of head fractures accompany a dislocation (TO)**

### Blood supply

| Vessel | Fact |
|---|---|
| **MFCA** (from profunda) | **Main supply to weight-bearing head**; runs **posteromedially** → endangered by posterior approach |
| Artery of ligamentum teres (obturator or MFCA) | **10–15%**; perifoveal area |

![Femoral head blood supply](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1036-blood-supply.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — impaction, avulsion, shear; **dashboard injury** (knee on dash, hip flexed + adducted + IR), falls, sport, industrial

**Posterior dislocation** — head **shears against posterior acetabular rim** → fracture (usually **anteromedial / anteroinferior** fragment)

**Anterior dislocation** — **impaction / indentation** fractures of head

**Associated** — femoral neck fracture, acetabular (posterior wall) fracture, **sciatic neurapraxia**, AVN, **ipsilateral knee ligament injury**

## 3 Presentation

**Posterior dislocation** — **flexed, adducted, IR**, shortened (little rotation with large wall fractures)

**Anterior dislocation** — flexed, **abducted, ER**

**Check** — **sciatic nerve**, ipsilateral knee stability

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| AP pelvis + hip series (pre + post reduction) | — |
| Judet | Acetabular fracture |
| Inlet / outlet | Pelvic ring |
| **Post-reduction CT** | **Loose bodies / incarcerated fragments**, fragment size, plane, comminution, impaction, acetabulum |

### Pipkin classification

| Type | Definition | Key fact |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Below fovea** (infrafoveal), **non-weight-bearing**, small | Nonop or **excision** (excision > ORIF) |
| **II** | **Above fovea** (suprafoveal), **weight-bearing**, larger; ligamentum stays on inferior fragment → may block reduction | ORIF if displaced (ORIF > excision) |
| **III** | I or II **+ femoral neck fracture** | **Highest AVN** → catastrophic ORIF failure |
| **IV** | I or II **+ acetabular fracture (usually posterior wall)** | Treat by acetabular injury |

![Pipkin classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1036-pipkin.jpg)

### Chiron classification (TO)

| Type (TO) | Extent |
|---|---|
| 1 | Osteochondral injury |
| 2 | ¼ of head |
| 3 | ⅓ of head |
| 4 | ½ of head |
| 5 | **Cartilage impaction of weight-bearing dome** (superior collapse) |

**Subtypes (TO)** — **A** pure head · **B** + acetabulum · **C** + neck · **Brumback** — more prognostic than Pipkin (TO)

## 5 Management

```
Femoral head fracture
├─ Dislocated → reduce < 6 h → post-reduction CT
├─ Pipkin I, or II with < 1 mm step-off, stable, no fragments → TDWB 4–6 wk
├─ Pipkin II > 1 mm / loose bodies / irreducible → ORIF (anterior)
│      └─ Pipkin IV → TFO surgical dislocation (head + posterior wall)
└─ Pipkin III or older / comminuted / osteoporotic → arthroplasty
```

### Operative criteria

| Nonoperative | Operative |
|---|---|
| **Pipkin I** | **Pipkin II with > 1 mm step-off** |
| **Pipkin II with < 1 mm step-off** (Orthobullets, OB) · **< 2 mm** (TO) | **Loose bodies / incarcerated fragments** |
| No interposed fragments | **Pipkin III, IV** |
| **Stable, concentric hip** | Irreducible fracture-dislocation, polytrauma |
| — | **Older patients**, comminution, osteoporosis → **arthroplasty** |

**1 mm, not 2 mm** — femoral head cut-off differs from other articular fractures (OB)

**Strongest nonop indication on post-reduction CT** — **infrafoveal (Pipkin I) with congruent joint**\
✗ Wrong options: suprafoveal displaced · neck fracture · displaced acetabulum · incarcerated posterior wall fragment

**TO nonop indication (TO)** — **Pipkin II with 1 mm displacement**\
✗ Wrong options: Pipkin I with 3 mm · Pipkin III · nonconcentric reduction

**Displaced Pipkin II on CT (TO)** — **ORIF with Herbert (headless) screw**\
✗ Wrong options: nonop 6 wk

### Hip reduction

**Timing** — **within 6 h** → AVN **5–40%**, ↑ with delay

**Technique** — full sedation + relaxation; in-line traction, slight adduction, counter-pressure on pelvis; **no forceful manoeuvres** → **post-reduction CT**

**Nonop regimen** — **TDWB 4–6 wk**, avoid **adduction + IR**; serial X-rays

### Surgical approach

| Approach | Facts |
|---|---|
| **Smith-Petersen (anterior)** | **Internervous: superior gluteal ↔ femoral**; best view of **anteromedial** fragment; shorter, less blood loss, better reduction; **spares MFCA → lower AVN** (KL **3.2× AVN**, TO); **↑ functionally significant HO** |
| Watson-Jones (anterolateral) | TFL ↔ gluteus medius (both superior gluteal → intermuscular) |
| **Surgical hip dislocation + trochanteric flip osteotomy (TFO)** | **Whole head + acetabulum through one approach** (head + posterior wall); **preserves MFCA**; ↓ AVN + **↓ PTOA**; **↑ HO**; same function; **lateral position** |
| **Kocher-Langenbeck ± digastric (trochanteric) osteotomy** | Posterior wall (Pipkin IV); gluteus max split (no internervous plane); **plain KL → worse outcomes, more AVN** |

**Exposure** — **periacetabular capsulotomy** to protect head supply

**Pipkin IV — preserving blood supply** — **greater trochanteric osteotomy (surgical dislocation)**\
✗ Wrong options: strip gluteals off crest = extended iliofemoral · ligate LFCA ascending branches · detach piriformis (not needed) · supine (lateral position)

**TFO primary advantage** — **address all injury sites through one approach**\
✗ Wrong options: minimally invasive · minimal stripping · better union

**Irreducible posterior fracture-dislocation — TFO true?** — **lower PTOA**\
✗ Wrong options: more AVN · less HO · incomplete view · worse function

**Polytrauma, posterior dislocation + head fracture — definitive approach** — **Smith-Petersen**\
✗ Wrong options: Southern / posterior (MFCA) · Stoppa · KL · iliofemoral

**Pipkin IV with small posterior wall** — wall may be left; suprafoveal head via anterior approach

### Fixation & outcomes

**Fixation** — **≥ 2 lag screws 2.7 / 3.5 mm (mini-fragment) countersunk**, headless screws, bioabsorbable pins\
✗ Wrong options: 3.0 mm cannulated screws with washers → poor results

**Pipkin II ORIF — true?** — **mini-fragment screws suffice**\
✗ Wrong options: HO uncommon (\~15–20%) · KL optimal · excision better · 2 mm cut-off

**Post-op** — immediate ROM, **delay WB 6–8 wk**, quads + abductor strengthening; X-ray at 6 mo for AVN / OA

**Outcome** — mirrors associated injury (dislocation, neck fracture)

**Pipkin III ORIF in 51-yo — 6-mo outcome** — **fixation failure + AVN → THA**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior HO revision · pain-free · core decompression · advanced OA at 6 mo

### Arthroplasty

**Indications** — **older patients** with Pipkin I–IV displaced, osteoporotic, comminuted

**Facts** — immediate WB; **higher dislocation risk** than THA for OA; hemiarthroplasty if no acetabular fracture; **posterior approach best for posterior wall**

**Arthroscopy** — loose body removal

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Rate / fact |
|---|---|
| **HO** | **6–64%**; **anterior approach > posterior**; \~15–20% after fixation; **radiation** prophylaxis (esp. head injury) |
| **AVN** | **0–23%**; ↑ with **delayed reduction**; anterior approach **not** ↑ AVN |
| **Sciatic neurapraxia** | **10–23%**; **peroneal division**; **60–70% recover** |
| **DJD** | **8–75%** (incongruity, cartilage damage) |
| ↓ Internal rotation | Often not disabling |

## X vs Y

| | **Smith-Petersen** | **Kocher-Langenbeck** | **TFO surgical dislocation** |
|---|---|---|---|
| View | Anteromedial head | Posterior wall | **Whole head + acetabulum** |
| AVN | **Lower** | **3.2× higher** (TO) | Lower |
| HO | **Higher** | Lower | **Higher** |
| Best for | Pipkin I / II | PW only | **Pipkin IV** |

| | **Pipkin I** | **Pipkin II** |
|---|---|---|
| Location | **Infrafoveal** | **Suprafoveal (weight-bearing)** |
| Displaced → | **Excise** | **ORIF** |

## How it's asked

**"Nonop indication (TO)?"** → **Pipkin II with 1 mm displacement**

**"CT: displaced suprafoveal fragment (TO)?"** → **Pipkin II — ORIF with Herbert screw**

**"Which image is Pipkin II?"** → **suprafoveal fragment without neck / acetabulum fracture**

**"Strongest indication for nonop?"** → **Pipkin I, congruent**

**"Preserve blood supply in Pipkin IV?"** → **greater trochanteric osteotomy**

**"TFO primary advantage?"** → **all injuries through one approach**

**"Best definitive approach after posterior dislocation + head fracture?"** → **Smith-Petersen**

**"TFO — true?"** → **lower post-traumatic OA**

**"Pipkin II ORIF — true?"** → **mini-fragment screws sufficient**

**"Pipkin III ORIF at 6 months?"** → **failure + AVN → THA**

**"Reduce dislocation within?"** → **6 hours**

**"Sciatic palsy — division?"** → **peroneal; 60–70% recover**

# Femoral Neck Fracture Nonunion

*Trauma · 20 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rate** — **10–30% after femoral neck ORIF**

| Risk factor | Note |
|---|---|
| **Vertical fracture (Pauwels III)** | Shear > compression |
| Significant initial displacement, **posterior comminution** | — |
| **Malreduction, esp. varus** | (✗ Wrong options: valgus) |
| **Older age** | — |
| **Delay to fixation > 24 h** | — |

![Pauwels classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12293-pauwels-classification.jpg)

### Why the neck fails to heal

**Intracapsular** — **no periosteum / extraosseous supply**, **bathed in synovial fluid**, **intra-osseous healing only (no callus)** → anatomic reduction essential

### Anatomy

**Neck-shaft angle** — **130 ± 7°** · **anteversion 10 ± 7°** · tensile + compressive trabeculae

**Blood supply** — extracapsular ring → **ascending cervical (retinacular) arteries**; **MFCA (lateral epiphyseal) = main adult supply**; LFCA anterior ring; gluteal small; **ligamentum teres insignificant**

**Load** — single-leg stance force **parallel to neck**, **4× body weight**

**Associated** — **AVN**, malunion, hardware failure, **septic nonunion**, low BMD

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — persistent groin / buttock pain, pain on WB + hip extension, referred to medial thigh / knee

**Exam** — **↓ internal rotation**, antalgic gait, **pain with log-roll**, pain on straight-leg raise; check old incision, sciatic + femoral nerves

**Nonunion signal** — **persistent pain + no radiographic healing at 3 months** → consider revision

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| AP pelvis + hip, **cross-table lateral**, full femur | Lucent line, **settling**, **hardware backing out**, head perforation, **> 10 mm change in position**, collapse, calcar comminution, **varus** |
| **CT** | **Most definitive diagnosis**; planning |
| MRI | **Head AVN** |
| Bone scan | Uptake at nonunion; helps exclude AVN |

**Infection work-up** — **CBC, ESR, CRP** → aspirate if raised → intra-op frozen section → **staged** if infected

### Classification (descriptive)

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| **1** | Inadequate fixation / non-anatomic reduction |
| **2** | Loss of fixation with displacement |
| **3** | Fibrous nonunion, no displacement, intact fixation |

## 5 Management

```
Femoral neck nonunion
├─ Young, viable head → valgus intertrochanteric osteotomy
│      └─ aligned but posterior bone loss → vascularised graft
├─ Older / active → THA (long stem past screw holes)
├─ Older / low demand / cognitive decline → hemiarthroplasty
└─ Non-ambulatory, comorbid → observe
```

### Treatment options

| Option | Indication | Fact |
|---|---|---|
| Observation | Non-ambulatory, comorbid | Poor function |
| **Revision ORIF** | **Not recommended** | Bone loss, LLD, high failure |
| **Valgus intertrochanteric osteotomy** | **Young**, viable head, varus collapse, nonunion > 3 mo | **Commonest salvage**; turns **vertical → \~30° (horizontal)** line → **compression**; restores length + abductor lever; **80–90% union**; not with bone loss, incongruity, **age > 65 (relative)** |
| **Vascularised graft** (quadratus femoris pedicle, free fibula) | Aligned nonunion with **posterior bone loss**, failed fixation | Up to 90% union; not if malalignment is the cause |
| **Hemiarthroplasty** | **Low demand, comorbid, cognitive decline** | **Lower dislocation than THA**; **cemented > uncemented**; bipolar ≈ unipolar; risk of acetabular erosion |
| **THA** | **Older, active, cognitively intact**; acetabular degeneration | **\~90% 10-yr, 75% 20-yr survival** |
| Arthrodesis | Young, non-viable head, heavy labourer | **20–35° flexion, 0–5° adduction, 5–10° ER**; arthroplasty now favoured |

![Valgus intertrochanteric osteotomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12293-valgus-it-osteotomy-xr.jpg)

**26-yo nonunion after screws at 3 mo** — **valgus intertrochanteric osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: graft + revision ORIF · hemiarthroplasty · DHS without correction · core decompression

**76-yo community ambulator** — **THA gives best long-term outcome**\
✗ Wrong options: valgus malreduction causes failure (varus does) · revision ORIF + inverted triangle · valgus osteotomy · hemi ↑ dislocation (hemi is lower)

**Healed fracture but AVN collapse + screw cut-out into acetabulum (62-yo)** — **THA with long cementless stem** to bypass screw-hole stress risers\
✗ Wrong options: primary tapered stem · hardware removal · valgus osteotomy · IM revision

### Technique points

**Valgus osteotomy** — lateral approach; **blade plate or sliding hip screw**; risks: malrotation, **over-medialisation of shaft → ↓ offset + abductor weakness** (longer blade avoids)

**Conversion THA** — **longer stems** past screw holes (intra-op fracture risk), gentle cup impaction ± screws, **dual-mobility / large heads** vs dislocation; loosening ↑ if **age < 65 + BMI > 30**

**Dual mobility vs fixed bearing (< 55 yr)** — **lower dislocation** (same HHS, loosening, infection, op time)

**Failed hemi → THA** — worse than primary THA

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **AVN** | **Up to 40%** with nonunion; \~15% symptomatic; risk: displacement, poor reduction → arthroplasty |
| Acetabular cartilage wear | → THA |
| **Trendelenburg gait** | Lost offset / abductor damage |
| **Prosthetic dislocation** | **10–20% in salvage THA** vs < 10% primary; abductor insufficiency, posterior approach |

## X vs Y

| | **Valgus IT osteotomy** | **THA** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | **Young**, viable head | **Older**, active |
| Mechanism | Shear → compression | Replace joint |
| Result | 80–90% union | \~90% 10-yr survival |

## How it's asked

**"26-yo, screws, 3-mo nonunion?"** → **valgus intertrochanteric osteotomy**

**"76-yo nonunion — true?"** → **THA gives best long-term outcome**

**"Healed but AVN collapse + screw cut-out?"** → **THA with long cementless stem**

**"Dual mobility vs fixed bearing?"** → **lower dislocation**

**"Most definitive imaging for nonunion?"** → **CT**

**"Malreduction most linked to failure?"** → **varus**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Femoral Neck Fractures

*Trauma · 21 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rising** — ageing population · **women > men**, Caucasians > African Americans · **USA highest incidence**

**Cost** — **most expensive fracture per person**

**Associated** — **ipsilateral femoral shaft fracture: 6–9% of shaft fractures** (\~10%, OB) → fix **neck first**

### Healing biology

**Intracapsular** — **bathed in synovial fluid, no periosteum, limited callus** → poor healing; anatomic reduction essential

### Anatomy

**Neck-shaft angle 130 ± 7°** · **anteversion 10 ± 7°**

**Head supply** — **MFCA (lateral epiphyseal artery) = main** · LFCA anterior / inferior head · inferior gluteal · ligamentum teres insignificant

**Displacement** — disrupts retinacular vessels + **intracapsular haematoma (tamponade, effect debated)**

**Lowest intracapsular pressure** — **flexion + abduction + ER** (patients lie this way) · highest = **extension + IR**\
✗ Wrong options: flexion + IR · extension + ER

![Blood supply to the femoral head](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1037-blood-supply.jpg)

### Genes & lab markers

**FRAX (WHO)** — uses **femoral neck BMD (DEXA)**, BMI, current smoking, parental hip fracture, prior fracture < 50, etc. → **spine T-score NOT used** · \~50% of fragility fractures occur without DEXA-defined osteoporosis

## 2 Mechanism

**Young** — high energy (vertical, Pauwels III) · **Elderly** — low-energy falls

**Cyclic load** — single-leg stance compression \~4× body weight → superior-neck tension fractures

## 3 Presentation

**Impacted / stress** — mild groin or medial thigh / knee pain, no deformity, pain at ROM extremes, **pain on percussion of greater trochanter**

**Displaced** — leg **shortened, externally rotated, abducted**; pain with log-roll

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| AP pelvis, cross-table lateral, full femur | Standard |
| **Traction-internal rotation AP (15° IR)** | **Best to define fracture type** (accounts for anteversion) |
| **AP in abduction + IR (TO)** / 15–20° IR | **Best position for impacted neck fracture (TO)** |
| Contralateral hip | Templating |
| CT | Displacement, comminution |
| **MRI** | **Occult fracture — imaging of choice** (< 24 h); **not** reliable for head viability |
| **Thin-slice MDCT (TO)** | **Second-line after X-ray for occult fracture (TO)** — sensitivity + specificity 100% with experienced reader |
| Bone scan | Occult fracture **after 72 h** if MRI contraindicated |
| Duplex | **DVT if delayed presentation (> 2 days)** |

**Occult fracture next step** — **MRI hip + pelvis** (Orthobullets, OB) · **thin-slice MDCT** (TO) — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: stress views · bone scan at 7 d · repeat CT at 48 h · WBAT

**Delayed presentation 3 days** — **bilateral lower-limb duplex** before surgery\
✗ Wrong options: inlet / outlet · CT head without head signs · CT / MRI for displaced fracture

### Garden classification

| Type | Description | Group |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Incomplete, valgus impacted** | Non-displaced |
| **II** | **Complete, non-displaced** | Non-displaced |
| **III** | **Complete, partially displaced** | Displaced |
| **IV** | **Complete, fully displaced** | Displaced |

![Garden IV femoral neck fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1037-illustration-garden-iv-emed-moved.jpg)

### Pauwels classification (angle from horizontal)

| Type | Angle | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **< 30°** | Compression |
| **II** | **30–50°** | — |
| **III** | **> 50°** | **Shear; most unstable; highest nonunion + AVN** |

## 5 Management

```
Femoral neck fracture
├─ Young (< 50), displaced → urgent anatomic reduction (open if needed) + fixation
│      └─ vertical (Pauwels III) / basicervical → sliding hip screw ± anti-rotation screw
├─ Elderly non-displaced (Garden I–II) → cannulated screws (inverted triangle)
├─ Elderly displaced, active, independent / OA → THA
└─ Elderly displaced, low demand, dementia, Parkinson, > 80 → cemented hemiarthroplasty
```

### Treatment options

| Option | Indication |
|---|---|
| Observation | Non-ambulatory, minimal pain, prohibitive risk |
| **Closed reduction + cannulated screws** | **Non-displaced**; Garden I–II in elderly; displaced in young if reduction anatomic |
| **ORIF** | **Displaced in young / physiologically young (< 50)** (female → ↑ reoperation) |
| **Sliding hip screw** | **Basicervical**, **vertical (Pauwels III) in young** ± anti-rotation screw |
| **Hemiarthroplasty** | Debilitated elderly, metabolic bone disease, low demand |
| **THA** | **Older active**, **pre-existing OA / inflammatory arthritis**, Garden III–IV < 85 yr |

### Young patients – urgent ORIF, reduction & osteonecrosis

**Displaced in young** — **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: THA (salvage only) · closed parallel screws ± Pauwels screw · cephalomedullary nail

**Most important for healing + outcome** — **quality of reduction**\
✗ Wrong options: surgery < 12 h · open reduction itself · capsular decompression · approach · comorbidities · implant

**Unacceptable closed reduction** — **> 5 mm displacement in any view** or **> 10° varus / valgus / version** → **open reduction + SHS**\
✗ Wrong options: intra-op CT · repeated closed attempts (↑ AVN) · accept 7 mm · acute THA

**Most prevalent complication after ORIF in young** — **osteonecrosis** (> nonunion) · AVN **15–30% in < 60 with displaced fracture**

**Highest AVN risk** — **45-yo woman, displaced** (young + displaced)

**Approaches** — **Smith-Petersen** (sartorius–TFL, protect LFCN) · **Watson-Jones** (TFL–gluteus medius; capsulotomy **anterior to lesser trochanter** to spare MFCA)

**Reduction** — joysticks (A→P K-wires), evacuate haematoma; **length-stable / compression** to avoid neck shortening (shortening → ↓ SF-36, QoL)

**Ipsilateral neck + shaft** — **lag screws for neck first, then retrograde femoral nail** (✗ Wrong options: shaft first · plate shaft · proximal plate) · in unresuscitated patient (lactate 6, BE −8) → **delay until resuscitated** (EAC), but still early (✗ Wrong options: ex-fix + 1 wk · immediately after IR · single implant proven better · THA)

### Fixation constructs

**Cannulated screws** — **3 screws, inverted triangle**, **inferior screw posterior + along calcar**, max spread; **start at / above lesser trochanter** (below → subtrochanteric stress riser); avoid multiple lateral perforations; 4th screw for posterior comminution (unproven)\
✗ Wrong options: central

**Union by screw–cortex contact (Lindequist)** — posterior + inferior contact **89%** · one site **59%** · none **0%**

**Improve success** — **screws abutting posterior + inferior neck cortex**\
✗ Wrong options: titanium · start below LT · DHS for transcervical · alignment guide for parallelism

**Vertical (Pauwels III)** — **SHS + side plate** mechanically optimal (± anti-rotation screw)\
✗ Wrong options: 2–4 parallel screws (cantilever)

**FAITH (SHS vs cannulated screws)** — overall **no difference in reoperation / union**; **SHS ↑ AVN (9% vs 5%, NS)**; **SHS ↓ reoperation in displaced, basicervical, current smokers**

**55-yo FNF fixation — true?** — **SHS ↑ AVN vs screws**\
✗ Wrong options: cut-out with TAD < 25 mm (> 25 mm) · fixation ↓ reoperation vs arthroplasty · timing > reduction for AVN · screws ↑ reoperation overall

### Active elderly – THA vs hemiarthroplasty

| Outcome | THA vs hemiarthroplasty |
|---|---|
| **Function / outcome scores** | **Better with THA** (more so long-term ≥ 4 yr) |
| Pain relief | Better, more predictable |
| **Reoperation** | **Lower** (hemi → acetabular erosion) |
| **Dislocation** | **Higher (\~10%; 5× [Orthobullets] · 7× [Orthobullets complications, OB])**; \~5% vs 2% (OB) |
| Peri-operative complications, blood loss, time | **Higher** |
| Mortality, infection, LOS, readmission, periprosthetic fracture | **Similar** |

**THA best for** — **active, independent community ambulator** (golf instructor, pickle-ball player), **pre-existing OA**, < 75–80 yr

**Hemi best for** — **> 80**, low demand / household ambulator, **dementia, Parkinson's**, hepatic encephalopathy, can't follow precautions, **dialysis / multiple comorbidities** (THA no better short-term; hemi more cost-effective)

**85-yo Parkinson's, household (TO)** — **hemiarthroplasty** (bipolar; if THA → dual mobility)\
✗ Wrong options: THR · DHS · bed rest

**65-yo active Garden IV (TO)** — options AMP, bipolar, cemented, uncemented hemi — **answer key not provided by the source**

**THA vs internal fixation (elderly displaced)** — **lower reoperation**, less pain, better function; mortality similar; ADL better short-term only\
✗ Wrong options: shorter OR · less blood · less infection · shorter stay

**ORIF in dialysis patient** — **higher complication rate than hemi** (2 / 13 united); mortality 45% at \~2 yr, same between groups

### Hemiarthroplasty – cement, dislocation & conversion

**Cemented > uncemented** — **lower aseptic revision + fewer intra- / post-op periprosthetic fractures**, **better QoL at 4 mo**, better short / medium-term mobility · collared stems ↓ periprosthetic fracture\
✗ Wrong options: in-hospital mortality difference · shorter OR (cement longer)

**Unipolar ≈ bipolar** outcomes · bipolar for instability risk (Parkinson, dementia)

**Dislocation risk** — **posterior approach** (✗ Wrong options: anterior, anterolateral, bipolar, unipolar) · **posterior wall insufficiency**, labral injury, poor capsular repair, oversized head (✗ Wrong options: 20° anteversion · ↑ offset · +1.5 cm length (tighten) · cementless stem (fracture not dislocation))

**Anterolateral approach** — ↑ abductor weakness

**Failed bipolar with acetabular wear + superior migration** — **convert to THA — add acetabular component only** (femoral stem well fixed)\
✗ Wrong options: remove wires · walking aid · PT · revise stem

**Elderly displaced insufficiency fracture (walker user)** — **hemiarthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: CMN · DHS (for extracapsular) · ORIF · resurfacing (FNF absolute contraindication)

**Failed screw fixation in 82-yo, comminuted at revision** — **remove hardware + arthroplasty**

**Internal fixation vs hemi at 3 mo–1 yr** — **higher failure with fixation** (most within 2 yr) · **46% vs 8% failure at 2 yr** (> 70 yr) · mortality same

### Perioperative care

**Timing** — **as soon as medically optimised, ideally < 24–48 h**; **< 24 h of admission ↓ 30-d, 90-d, 1-yr mortality**; **delay > 4 days ↑ 1-yr mortality**; delay > 48 h acceptable **only to optimise multiple comorbidities**

**> 3 comorbidities** — **pre-op medical optimisation** matters more than speed

**Delays surgery without changing treatment** — **pre-op echocardiogram**\
✗ Wrong options: fascia iliaca block · geriatric admission · Hb < 10 · aspirin

**DOAC (rivaroxaban 12 h ago)** — **proceed to surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: andexanet (life-threatening bleeding only) · FFP (ineffective) · delay 24 h · PCC

**Anaesthesia** — spinal vs general: **no difference** in delirium, mortality, ambulation at 60 d (Orthobullets) · **general ↑ delirium + mortality** (OB) — read the options

**↓ Mortality + complications** — **surgery < 24 h**, **multidisciplinary / ortho-geriatric co-management**, neuraxial anaesthesia, **TXA**, **early WBAT + mobilisation**, opioid-sparing analgesia\
✗ Wrong options: delayed WB · echo · general anaesthesia · PCA · traction · SNF discharge

**Post-op delirium (\~25%)** — **↑ length of stay**, nursing home placement · risk: age, dementia, pre-injury nursing home\
✗ Wrong options: ICU · in-hospital death

### Mortality & prognosis

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| **1-yr mortality** | **\~25–30%** (14–36%; 20% in 85-yo) |
| **In-hospital mortality** | **\~6%** |
| **CRF / dialysis** | **\~45% at 2 yr** — **highest-risk pre-op factor** |
| Loss of independence | **\~50%** at 1 yr |

**Strongest determinant of survival** — **pre-injury mobility**

**Poor pre-injury cognition** — ↑ mortality after **hip fracture**

**Poor outcome predictors (TO)** — older age, not at home, poverty, unmarried, ↓ pre-fracture function, **cognitive impairment**, comorbidities, **high ASA**, ↓ grip, depression, ↓ albumin / folate, visual impairment, **extracapsular** type, delay > 48 h, **NWB post-op**, long LOS · BMI unrelated\
✗ Wrong options: **intracapsular fracture** (NOT a poor predictor)

**Loss of independence after fixation** — **age > 80, ASA > 1, prior walking aid, smoking**, implant placement quality · **pre-op ambulation without aid → independent at 1 yr**

### Timed Up and Go (TUG)

**Normal** — **< 12 s** → predicts **walking without aid at 2 yr** · **> 26 s** → persistent walking aid\
✗ Wrong options: predicts fracture, life expectancy, independent living, falls (**UPST predicts falls**, ASA predicts mortality)

### Neglected fracture (TO)

**AIIMS box technique (TO)** — **neglected femoral neck fracture** with large neck defect: box from GT base, head anterior wall + **quadratus femoris pedicle graft** posteriorly, cancellous autograft + screws

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Osteonecrosis** | **10–45%**; ↑ displacement, **non-anatomic reduction**, **SHS (FAITH)**; can occur in undisplaced; time to reduction not proven → young > 50% head: FVFG vs THA · old: arthroplasty |
| **Nonunion** | **5–30%**; displaced; **varus malreduction** most linked to failure → **valgus IT osteotomy** (young), FVFG, arthroplasty |
| **Dislocation** | THA \~10% (5–7× hemi); hemi: posterior approach, labral / wall injury, oversized head |
| Failure | Fixation ≫ arthroplasty, mostly first 2 yr |
| Loss of independence | See above |

### Nonunion / malunion – valgus intertrochanteric osteotomy

**Concept** — turns **vertical shear** line into **horizontal compression**; corrects varus; works even with **non-collapsed AVN**

**Valgus osteotomy indication** — **young nonunion with viable head**\
✗ Wrong options: collapsed AVN → arthroplasty · retroversion / cross-over → PAO · high dysplasia → subtroch osteotomy + THA · metastases → cemented arthroplasty

**48-yo runner, varus collapse at 6 mo** — **valgus IT osteotomy + blade plate**\
✗ Wrong options: hemi · in-situ DHS · core decompression · revision screws

**22-yo vertical nonunion at 9 mo** — **valgus IT osteotomy + 130° blade plate**\
✗ Wrong options: convert to SHS · THA · perpendicular 7.0 screw · varus osteotomy

**Wedge + plate maths** — **side-plate angle = guide-pin angle + wedge angle** → pin 130° + **20° wedge → 150° plate**\
✗ Wrong options: 110° / 95° = varus · 140° = lateral gap · 35° + 165° (too short, non-standard)

## X vs Y

| | **THA** | **Hemiarthroplasty** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | Active, independent, OA | > 80, low demand, dementia, PD |
| Function | **Better** | Lower |
| Dislocation | **Higher** | Lower |
| Reoperation | Lower | Higher (acetabular wear) |

| | **Cemented stem** | **Uncemented stem** |
|---|---|---|
| Periprosthetic fracture | **Lower** | Higher |
| Aseptic revision | **Lower** | Higher |
| QoL at 4 mo | **Better** | Worse |
| OR time | Longer | Shorter |

| | **Cannulated screws** | **Sliding hip screw** |
|---|---|---|
| Best for | Non-displaced transcervical | **Basicervical, Pauwels III** |
| FAITH | Less AVN | ↓ reoperation in smokers / displaced / basicervical |

## How it's asked

**"Young — most likely complication at 2 yr?"** → **AVN**

**"Most prevalent complication after urgent ORIF in young?"** → **osteonecrosis**

**"Failed closed reduction in 46-yo cyclist?"** → **open reduction + SHS**

**"Highest AVN risk?"** → **45-yo woman, displaced**

**"Displaced FNF in 32-yo?"** → **ORIF**

**"Most important predictor of healing / outcome?"** → **quality of reduction**

**"Ipsilateral neck + shaft, unresuscitated?"** → **delay for resuscitation**

**"Optimal cannulated screw configuration?"** → **inverted triangle, inferior screw posterior along calcar**

**"Improve fixation success?"** → **screws abutting posterior + inferior cortex**

**"Vertical (Pauwels III) — best construct?"** → **SHS + side plate**

**"SHS vs screws in smoker?"** → **lower reoperation**

**"FAITH — true?"** → **SHS ↑ AVN**

**"Indication for valgus IT osteotomy?"** → **young FNF nonunion**

**"Varus collapse at 6 mo in 48-yo?"** → **valgus osteotomy + blade plate**

**"40° fracture line, pin at 130° — correction?"** → **20° wedge + 150° plate**

**"22-yo vertical nonunion?"** → **valgus osteotomy + 130° blade plate**

**"Best function at 7–10 yr (pickle-ball)?"** → **THA**

**"IF vs hemi to 1 yr?"** → **higher failure with IF**

**"Minimally displaced, IF — true?"** → **pre-op unaided walking → independence**

**"NOT poor-outcome predictor (TO)?"** → **intracapsular fracture**

**"Delay > 4 days?"** → **↑ 1-yr mortality**

**"Poor cognition ↑ mortality in?"** → **hip fracture**

**"Dialysis — 2-yr mortality?"** → **45%**

**"20% mortality + 50% dependence at 1 yr?"** → **femoral neck fracture**
