# Femoral Shaft Fractures

*Trauma · 22 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **37.1 / 100,000 / yr**

**Who** — high energy in young (MVC) · low energy in elderly · gunshot

### Associated injuries

| Association | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Ipsilateral femoral neck fracture** | **2–6%** (Orthobullets) · \~5% · 1–9% / 2.5–9% / 0–15% (OB) · 7.5% (Tornetta); **basicervical, vertical, non-displaced**; **missed 19–31% (\~30%)** |
| **Bilateral femur fractures** | ↑ **mortality** (5.6% vs 1.5%), initial hypotension, head + pelvic injuries (✗ Wrong options: **not** ↑ rib fractures); pulmonary complications |
| Ipsilateral tibia (floating knee), acetabulum | — |
| Pulmonary injury | Early fixation → ARDS \~2% if under-resuscitated |
| Head injury | Intra-op hypotension → ↓ cerebral perfusion |

**Blood loss** — **closed femur 1000–1500 mL** · closed tibia 500–1000 mL · **open ≈ double** · **bilateral closed femur 2000–3000 mL**

### Anatomy

**Femur** — largest, strongest; **anterior bow** (radius of curvature **120 ± 36 cm**); **linea aspera** = posterior compressive strut

**Compartments** — anterior (sartorius, quads) · posterior (hamstrings) · adductor (gracilis, adductors)

**Blood supply after fracture** — **periosteal + soft-tissue supply predominates**; reaming disrupts endosteal but stimulates periosteal

### Deforming forces

| Fragment | Deformity | Muscle |
|---|---|---|
| **Proximal** | **Abducted** | Gluteus medius + minimus |
| **Proximal** | **Flexed** (+ ER) | **Iliopsoas** (+ short rotators) |
| **Distal** | **Varus** | Adductors |
| **Distal** | **Extension** | Gastrocnemius |

![Deforming forces on femoral shaft fractures](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1040-biomechanics-of-femoral-shaft-fracture.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

| Force | Pattern |
|---|---|
| **Pure bending** | **Transverse** |
| Rotation | Spiral |
| Uneven bending | Oblique |
| **4-point bending** | Segmental |
| High-speed crush / torsion | Comminuted |

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — tense swollen thigh, shortened, tender; hip exam unreliable (neck fracture easily missed); **document distal NV status**

**Adequate resuscitation** — HR < 100, SBP > 100, DBP > 70, temp > 35 °C, urine 0.5–1 mL/kg/h, **lactate < 2.5**, **BD −2 to +2**, **IL-6 < 500 pg/dL**, gastric pH > 7.3

**Compensated shock clues** — normotensive, afebrile tachycardia, cool limbs, narrow pulse pressure, weak pulses, slow refill

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **AP + lateral whole femur**, ipsilateral **hip** + **knee** | Standard |
| **AP hip in \~10° internal rotation** | Neck in profile (✗ Wrong options: ER view) |
| **Fine-cut (2 mm) CT of hip** | **Best pre-op study for occult neck fracture**; **CT capsular sign** = **> 1 mm more capsular distension** than uninjured side |
| **Intra-operative fluoroscopy of neck** | **Still needed even after negative 2 mm CT** |
| **Post-op hip X-ray in OR** + follow-up **AP hip in 15–20° IR** | **New groin pain after nailing → hip X-ray first** |

**Neck protocol** — 10° IR AP hip + 2 mm CT + intra-op fluoro + post-op film\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan · traction views · MRI routinely · frog-leg (too painful)

**Septic nonunion labs** — **CRP most sensitive for occult infection**, ESR, WBC

### Winquist classification

| Type | Comminution |
|---|---|
| **0** | None |
| **I** | Insignificant |
| **II** | **> 50% cortical contact** |
| **III** | **< 50% cortical contact** |
| **IV** | **Segmental** — no contact between main fragments |

![Winquist classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1040-winquist-classification.jpg)

### AO / OTA 32

| Type | Subtypes |
|---|---|
| **A — simple** | A1 spiral · A2 oblique > 30° · A3 transverse < 30° |
| **B — wedge** | B1 spiral · B2 bending · B3 fragmented |
| **C — complex** | C1 spiral · C2 segmental · C3 irregular |

## 5 Management

```
Femoral shaft fracture
├─ Resuscitated → reamed, statically locked IMN < 24 h (WBAT)
│      ├─ antegrade = gold standard
│      └─ retrograde: neck fracture, floating knee, acetabulum, bilateral, obese, pregnant, polytrauma
├─ Unstable / head injury with ICP problems / vascular / severe open → ex-fix → IMN within 2–3 wk
└─ Ipsilateral neck fracture → fix NECK first (open if displaced), then retrograde nail / plate
```

### Treatment options

| Option | Indication | Fact |
|---|---|---|
| Spica / long leg cast | Children (< 5 yr spica), unfit adults | — |
| **Antegrade reamed IMN** | **Gold standard** | **98–99% union**, infection \~2%; **< 24 h** → ↓ ARDS, VTE, LOS, cost |
| **Retrograde IMN** | **Ipsilateral neck fracture**, **floating knee**, **acetabular fracture**, **bilateral femurs**, **polytrauma**, **morbid obesity**, **pregnancy (1st trimester)**, infra-isthmal | Not with skeletal immaturity, knee sepsis, knee soft-tissue injury |
| **Ex-fix → IMN within 2–3 wk** | Unstable polytrauma, vascular injury, severe open | Union + infection = acute nailing; **infection ↑ if ex-fix > 28 days**; ↓ ARDS / fat embolism; knee stiffness |
| **Plate** | Neck fracture needing screws, distal meta-diaphyseal fracture, no canal access | **↑ infection, nonunion, hardware failure** |

**Immediate (retrograde / antegrade) nailing** — safe for **GSW** femur fractures

**WB after statically locked appropriately sized nail** — **immediately** (even comminuted)

### Antegrade vs retrograde nailing

| | **Antegrade** | **Retrograde** |
|---|---|---|
| Union | Equal | Equal |
| Pain | **Hip pain**, abductor weakness, **abductor HO** | **Knee pain**, **symptomatic distal locking screws** |
| Setup | Fracture table / lateral | **Supine, all limbs accessible, compare rotation + length** |
| Bilateral femurs | Reposition | **Shorter op — no re-prep** |

**Retrograde preferred because** — **less hip pain**\
✗ Wrong options: union · infection · time · less knee pain

**Long-term weakness after antegrade nail** — **hip abduction + knee extension** (quads + abductors weaker)

**Stable, floating knee + open ankle** — **retrograde femur nail + tibial nail + ankle I&D + ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: ex-fix

**Femur + ipsilateral open tibia, stable** — **reamed retrograde nail, static locking**\
✗ Wrong options: unlocked · unreamed · dynamic locking (length unstable)

**Bilateral femurs, resuscitated (lactate 1.2)** — **reamed retrograde nailing of both**\
✗ Wrong options: traction · ex-fix · plates · unreamed antegrade

### Antegrade technique & entry points

**Positioning** — fracture table (perineal post, well-leg lithotomy) or **sloppy lateral**

| Entry | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|
| **Piriformis fossa** | **Colinear with shaft** | Hard in obese; **most damage to piriformis tendon, abductors, MFCA** (→ AVN in children) |
| **Trochanteric** | Less abductor injury, easier | Not colinear → **trochanteric-design nail** required; **too lateral → varus** |

**Trochanteric start** — **depends on trochanter position relative to shaft axis** + nail geometry (tip or just medial)\
✗ Wrong options: "always at the tip"

**Varus causes** — **trochanteric start too lateral** · **straight (piriformis) nail through trochanteric portal** · wrong-side trochanteric nail · (trochanteric nail through piriformis → **valgus**)

**Too ANTERIOR start (≥ 6 mm)** — ↑ **hoop stress → iatrogenic proximal femur fracture ("bursting")** (esp. piriformis nail); **over-ream ≥ 0.5 mm** to reduce · 3 mm anterior improves **cephalic screw position** in CMN

**Too POSTERIOR start + straight nail (larger radius of curvature) + short / bowed femur** — **anterior cortical perforation** of distal femur / anterior thigh pain → prevent: **more anterior start**, **smaller radius of curvature (more curved) nail**, **anterior** blocking screws, **smaller nail**, notched tip\
✗ Wrong options: larger nail · posterior blocking screw · straighter nail

**Insertion** — nail inserted **rotated 90° internally** then rotated back (uses bow, avoids medial comminution)

**Piriformis nail** — single sagittal bow (either side) · **trochanteric nail** — double bow, side-specific (twist on insertion); 3-point fit

### Reamed vs unreamed nailing

**Reamed** — **↑ union**, ↓ time to union, **no ↑ pulmonary complications**, **no ↑ infection in open**; unreamed → **4.5× nonunion** (Canadian RCT)

**Why reaming helps** — **reaming debris ≈ iliac crest autograft** (osteogenic cells) · stimulates periosteal flow · **larger nail (stiffness ∝ r⁴)** · more isthmic contact · fluted nails ↓ marrow pressure (greatest at nail insertion)

**Unreamed** — consider only with **bilateral pulmonary injury**; **biggest nonunion risk factor** among options\
✗ Wrong options: open reduction · cerclage · retrograde · staged ex-fix

**Nail diameter** — **10 mm ≈ larger nails** for union\
✗ Wrong options: bigger canal needs bigger nail

### Retrograde technique

**Start** — **centre of notch (AP)** + **extension of Blumensaat line (lateral)** (posterior → cruciate injury); medial condylar start needs curved nail (else valgus) · knee flexed **30–50°**

**Steps** — reduce before reaming (F-tool, bumps, Schanz joysticks); ream 1–1.5 mm over nail; **nail \~1 cm deep to cartilage**; guidewire **proximal to lesser trochanter**; **long nail to LT**

**Proximal interlocks** — safe **above lesser trochanter**; **A→P screws below LT → femoral nerve branches + deep femoral artery** at risk

**Periprosthetic (CR TKA)** — start **more posterior → hyperextension (recurvatum)**; arthrotomy needed; PS box may need burr; backup = locking plates (multiple distal points)

### Ipsilateral femoral neck + shaft

**Priority** — **neck first** (AVN / nonunion) → **open reduction + screws** if displaced → **reamed retrograde nail** (or plate) for shaft

**Two implants** — **lower malreduction** than single cephalomedullary nail\
✗ Wrong options: single CMN (**highest malreduction**; no faster neck healing, less pain)

**Missed neck found after antegrade nail** — screws **anterior to nail** (difficult)

### Polytrauma & head injury

**Head injury** — early stabilisation **does not worsen neurological outcome** if **hypotension + hypoxia avoided**; **intra-op hypotension → lower GCS at discharge**

**Severe head injury with ↑ ICP (bifrontal contusion, ICP 30)** — **ex-fix femur**\
✗ Wrong options: due to L4 burst, open tibia, LC1, rib fractures

**SAH + femur — adverse outcome from?** — **intra-operative hypotension** (traction if ICP labile)

**GCS 13, normal CT head, stable** — **early fixation, no neuro risk**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ pulmonary risk · delay · DCO · chest injury always contraindicates

**Unilateral contusion + chest tube, now stable (BD −2)** — **reamed IMN**

**Shock, bilateral contusions, BD −10** — **ex-fix**\
✗ Wrong options: plating · reamed / unreamed nails

**Borderline after splenectomy, bilateral open femurs + pelvis** — **bilateral femoral ex-fix + anterior pelvic ex-fix**

**BD −6, lactate 3.5, contusions, hip reduced** — **bilateral temporising ex-fix**

### Distal interlocking

**Perfect circles** — hole widened **proximal–distal → adjust abduction / adduction** · widened **anterior–posterior → adjust internal / external rotation**

**Computer navigation** — **↓ fluoroscopy time**\
✗ Wrong options: accuracy, time, image quality, length

**Single distal screw failure** — **4-point bending → screw shaft fractures inside the nail**\
✗ Wrong options: pull-out · head shear

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **HO** | **25%**, rarely significant (abductors after antegrade) |
| **Pudendal nerve** | **10%** with traction on fracture table |
| Femoral artery / nerve | Retrograde proximal locking screws |
| **Malrotation** | **Proximal fractures 30%**, distal 10%; fracture table, **comminution**, night surgery |
| Delayed union | Dynamise ± graft |
| **Nonunion** | **< 10%**; NSAIDs, **smoking**, **indomethacin** |
| Infection | < 1% → remove nail, ream, ex-fix if unhealed |
| Weakness | Quads + abductors |
| Iatrogenic fracture | Anterior start ≥ 6 mm, under-reaming |
| MAD | Lengthening → **lateral** MAD · shortening → **medial** MAD |
| **Anterior cortical perforation** | Radius mismatch; posterior start + straight nail |
| **Compartment syndrome** | **Ballistic > blunt** |

### Rotational malalignment

**Most associated factor** — **fracture comminution** (TO, OB) · also night-time surgery\
✗ Wrong options: entry point · level · surgeon experience · closed technique

**Fracture table** — **↑ internal malrotation** (vs manual traction supine)

**Direction by level** — **proximal fracture → INTERNAL malrotation** (proximal fragment ER by iliopsoas, rotators, abductors) · **distal fracture → EXTERNAL malrotation** (gastrocnemius / plantaris ER distal fragment) · antegrade vs retrograde and entry type don't matter

**Lesser trochanter larger on injured side (ER proximal fragment)** — **internal rotation malalignment**

**Iatrogenic internal malrotation** — **distal segment internally rotated relative to proximal** (↑ anteversion)

**CT measurement (Jeanmart)** — angle between **posterior condylar tangent** and **femoral neck axis**; rotation = β − α; compare sides

**Thresholds** — **< 10° = normal variation** · **≤ 15° well tolerated** · **> 15° + symptomatic → derotation**\
✗ Wrong options: dynamisation

**Acceptable differences** — **9° IR + 14° ER**\
✗ Wrong options: 18°

**CT: ER malalignment on exam** — **more ER, less IR** of hip (tibia as pointer)

**Worked example** — right neck IR13 / condyles ER17, left neck ER13 / condyles ER3 → right distal ER30 vs left IR10 → **rotate right distal fragment INTERNALLY 40°**

**Correction** — intra-op: remove distal locks + derotate · after union: **osteotomy**

![CT measurement of femoral version (Jeanmart)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1040-axial-ct-scan-determining-the-femoral-neck-anteversion-and-p.jpg)

### Nonunion

**Definition** — **no healing by 9 months** or **no progress over 3 months**; broken distal locks = race lost

| Nonunion | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Aseptic, after reamed nail (smoker)** | **Reamed antegrade exchange nailing** (✗ Wrong options: graft in situ · stimulator · biopsy) |
| Hypertrophic, **undersized nail** | **Reamed exchange nailing** |
| **Hypertrophic, sub-isthmal** | **Nail retention + plate augmentation + graft** (highest union + immediate WB) |
| **Failed exchange nailing** (union only \~53%) | **Bone graft + compression plating** |

**Exchange nailing works by** — stiffer larger nail, better isthmic fit, reaming graft · **dynamic locking** + grafting ↑ union · **smoking** = biggest culprit when it fails

**Indomethacin HO prophylaxis (25 mg TDS × 6 wk)** — **↑ long-bone nonunion** (inhibits IGF-1); radiation preferred

### Skeletal traction (TO)

**Upper tibial pin (sliding) traction (TO)** — **counter-traction by raising foot end of bed** (\~1 inch per lb) with BB splint · **Thomas splint = fixed traction** (ring in groin)\
✗ Wrong options: couch · none · muscle spasm

## X vs Y

| | **Piriformis entry** | **Trochanteric entry** |
|---|---|---|
| Alignment with shaft | **Colinear** | Lateral → nail-specific design |
| Soft tissue | Piriformis, abductors, MFCA | Gluteus medius |
| Error | Too anterior → bursting | Too lateral → **varus** |

| | **Proximal shaft fracture** | **Distal shaft fracture** |
|---|---|---|
| Malrotation tendency | **Internal** | **External** |

| | **Exchange nailing** | **Plate augmentation** |
|---|---|---|
| Best for | Isthmal, undersized nail | **Sub-isthmal hypertrophic** |
| WB | Immediate | Immediate (nail kept) |

## How it's asked

**"Neck fractures diagnosed late without fine-cut CT?"** → **\~30%**

**"Associated with \~5% of shaft fractures?"** → **ipsilateral neck fracture**

**"Vertical basicervical neck line usually with?"** → **ipsilateral shaft fracture**

**"Highest malreduction for neck + shaft?"** → **antegrade cephalomedullary nail**

**"Best algorithm (displaced neck + shaft)?"** → **ORIF neck, then reamed retrograde nail**

**"Next step before antegrade nail?"** → **hip radiograph / CT**

**"2 mm CT negative — what else?"** → **intra-op fluoroscopy of neck**

**"Groin pain 2 wk after nailing?"** → **hip radiograph**

**"Best study for occult neck fracture?"** → **2 mm CT**

**"6% concomitant fracture — image?"** → **ipsilateral hip**

**"CT sign of occult neck fracture?"** → **> 1 mm capsular distension**

**"Most piriformis tendon injury?"** → **piriformis-entry antegrade nail**

**"Larger radius of curvature nail risks?"** → **anterior perforation of distal femur**

**"Varus after subtroch nailing related to?"** → **piriformis nail through trochanteric portal**

**"Start 3 mm anterior with CMN benefit?"** → **better head screw placement**

**"Start too anterior → ?"** → **iatrogenic proximal fracture**

**"Trochanteric start A (lateral) → ?"** → **varus**

**"Proper trochanteric start?"** → **depends on trochanter vs shaft axis**

**"Antegrade ↑ vs retrograde?"** → **hip pain**

**"Femur + ipsilateral open tibia?"** → **reamed retrograde, static locking**

**"8° ER on CT scanogram?"** → **observe**

**"Reamed vs non-reamed — higher?"** → **union**

**"Biggest nonunion risk among options?"** → **unreamed nail**

**"Persistent oligotrophic nonunion after exchange nail?"** → **smoking**

**"Failed exchange nail?"** → **bone graft + compression plating**

# Flail Chest

*Trauma · 23 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Flail chest** — **segmental fractures of ≥ 3 consecutive ribs** → hemothorax / pneumothorax

**Incidence** — \~300,000 / yr; **\~10% of blunt thoracic trauma** (OB); more ribs → ↑ morbidity + mortality

**Who** — bimodal: young trauma · elderly osteopenic

**Commonest ribs** — **7–10**

**Mortality** — **up to 33%** with flail chest · **2.5% with surgical stabilisation**

### Associated injuries

| Ribs | Association |
|---|---|
| **1–3** | **Mediastinal injury (aorta, brachial plexus)**, worse mortality |
| **1–4** (upper) | **Progressive displacement of ipsilateral clavicle fracture** |
| **9–12** | **Intra-abdominal injury — liver + spleen** |
| Any | Hemopneumothorax, pulmonary contusion, scapula + clavicle fractures |

### Anatomy

**Ribs** — 12 pairs; **11–12 = floating** (no costal cartilage to sternum)

**Intercostal neurovascular bundle** — **posteroinferior**, in **costal groove** → avoid when plating

![Intercostal neurovascular bundle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1061-neurovascular-bundle-anatomy.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — direct blunt / penetrating; fracture at impact or **rib angle (posterolateral bend) = weakest point**; pathological / metastatic

**Pathophysiology** — negative-pressure ventilation → **paradoxical motion** → pain → ↓ respiratory effort → **pneumonia, ARDS**, intubation

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain, chest-wall bruising, respiratory difficulty

**Paradoxical respiration** — segment **sinks in on inspiration**, **expands on expiration**

**Palpation** — bony / soft-tissue crepitus; visible deformity

![Flail chest and paradoxical respiration](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1061-flail-chest-and-paradoxical-respiration.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| Standard AP / lateral CXR | **Low sensitivity**; may show hemothorax |
| **Dedicated rib series** | **More sensitive** (oblique views, higher energy) |
| **CT** | **Best** for displacement, segmental injury, **need for surgery** + thoracic / abdominal injuries |

## 5 Management

```
Rib fractures
├─ No respiratory compromise, no flail → O2, incentive spirometry, multimodal analgesia
└─ Flail (≥ 3 consecutive segmental) / failure to wean / open / intractable pain → ORIF
       └─ haemodynamically unstable / spine or pelvis first → delay
```

### Operative criteria

| Indication (ORIF) | Contraindication |
|---|---|
| **Flail segment (≥ 3 consecutive segmental ribs)** | **Haemodynamic instability** |
| **Failure to wean from ventilator** | **Spinal / pelvic fractures needing fixation first** |
| **Open rib fractures** | — |
| Displaced fractures with **intractable pain** | — |
| **Symptomatic nonunion** | — |

**Guidelines** — Chest Wall Injury Society SSRF algorithm

![SSRF rib fracture algorithm](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1061-ssrf-rib-fractures-algorithm.jpg)

### Technique

| Approach | Fractures |
|---|---|
| **Lateral thoracotomy** (lateral decubitus) | Anterolateral + posterolateral |
| **Posterior paramedian** | Very posterior, near **costovertebral junction** |
| **Inframammary** (supine) | Anterior + **costochondral dislocations** |

**Implants** — plate + screw constructs · intramedullary splints

### Operative rib fixation — outcomes

**Surgery vs nonop** — **↓ ICU stay** · **↓ total hospital stay** · **↓ pneumonia** · ↓ ventilation days, ↓ tracheostomy, ↓ deformity, ↓ mortality, ↓ cost

**Long-term respiratory function** — **equivalent**\
✗ Wrong options: superior

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Nonunion** | Chest pain **> 3 months** → nonunion work-up before fixation |
| **Intercostal neuralgia** | Bundle **posteroinferior** to rib |
| Periscapular weakness | — |
| **Pneumonia** | — |
| **Restrictive** pulmonary function | — |

## X vs Y

| | **Ribs 1–3** | **Ribs 9–12** |
|---|---|---|
| Associated injury | **Aorta, brachial plexus** (mediastinal) | **Liver, spleen** |

## How it's asked

**"Flail ORIF vs nonop?"** → **shorter ICU stay**\
✗ Wrong options: longer / equal hospital stay · superior long-term lung function · equal pneumonia

**"Definition of flail chest?"** → **≥ 3 consecutive ribs fractured segmentally**

**"Lower rib fractures associated with?"** → **liver + spleen injury**

**"Upper rib fractures + clavicle?"** → **progressive clavicle displacement**

**"Best imaging for surgical decision?"** → **CT**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Foot Compartment Syndrome

*Trauma · 24 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & causes

**Pathophysiology** — trauma → bleeding + oedema → ↑ interstitial pressure → vascular occlusion → **myoneural ischaemia**

**Causes** — **fractures 69%** (**5–17% of calcaneus fractures**), **crush**, severe ankle sprain, GSW, tight casts / dressings, burns, post-ischaemic swelling, bleeding disorders, vascular injury

### Foot compartment anatomy

**Number** — **9** (Orthobullets, TO; controversial) · **8** (OB: lateral, medial, central, 4 interosseous)

| Compartment | Contents |
|---|---|
| **Medial** | **Abductor hallucis, flexor hallucis brevis** |
| **Lateral** | **Abductor + flexor digiti minimi brevis** |
| **Interosseous (× 4)** | **Interossei — each in its own compartment** |
| Central — **superficial** | **Flexor digitorum brevis** |
| Central — middle | **Quadratus plantae** |
| Central — **deep (calcaneal / adductor)** | **Adductor hallucis** + posterior tibial NV bundle |

**Trap (TO)** — "**adductor compartment = adductor hallucis + interossei**" = **wrong**; interossei form **4 separate** compartments

![The 9 compartments of the foot](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1065-illustration-of-the-9-compartments-of-the-foot.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptom** — **pain out of proportion**

**Exam** — pain on **passive toe dorsiflexion (MTPJ)** (stretches intrinsics; may be absent in foot) · tense swollen foot · ↓ 2-point discrimination

**Pulses** — **present pulses do not exclude**; absent DP may be expected with severe foot trauma

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — rule out fracture only; **diagnosis is clinical ± pressures**

### Compartment pressure measurement & thresholds

**Indications** — polytrauma, obtunded / unreliable, **children**, equivocal exam (not needed when clinically unequivocal → straight to theatre)

| Compartment | Needle entry |
|---|---|
| **Medial** | **Base of 1st MT**, 2 cm into abductor hallucis |
| **Central** | **Base of 1st MT**, directed **lateral + plantar through abductor hallucis** (deeper than medial) |
| Interosseous | **2nd, 3rd, 4th web spaces**, plantar 2 cm through extensor fascia |
| Lateral | **Midshaft 5th MT**, 1 cm medial + plantar |

### Diagnostic criteria

| Fasciotomy if | Observe if |
|---|---|
| **Δp (diastolic − compartment) < 30 mmHg** | **Δp > 30 mmHg** |
| **Absolute 30–45 mmHg** (historic, controversial) | Exam not consistent |
| Clinical diagnosis | — |

**Intra-op** — anaesthesia lowers diastolic → **compare to pre-op diastolic**

## 5 Management

```
Suspected foot compartment syndrome
├─ Clinically unequivocal → emergent fasciotomy (no pressures)
├─ Equivocal / obtunded / child → measure pressures
│      ├─ Δp < 30 or 30–45 mmHg → fasciotomy
│      └─ Δp > 30, exam normal → observe
└─ Fasciotomy → delayed closure ± skin graft
```

### Fasciotomy technique

| Approach | Releases | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Dual dorsal (gold standard)** — **medial to 2nd MT** | 1st + 2nd interosseous, **medial**, **deep central** | Access to **Chopart, Lisfranc, TMT**; no calcaneus fixation |
| Dual dorsal — **lateral to 4th MT** | 3rd + 4th interosseous, **lateral**, superficial + middle central | — |
| **+ Medial incision** | **Calcaneal compartment** | — |
| **Single medial** (along abductor hallucis in arch) | **All 9** | **Technically demanding** |

![Dual dorsal fasciotomy incisions](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1065-surgical-approach-to-foot-compartment-syndrome.jpg)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Claw toes (fixed flexion)** | Intrinsic contracture → **release FDB + FDL at digits** |
| **Chronic pain + hypersensitivity** | Difficult to manage |

## X vs Y

| | **Dorsal incisions** | **Medial incision** |
|---|---|---|
| Access | All compartments + **midfoot fractures** | **All 9** / calcaneal |
| Downside | No calcaneus access | Technically challenging |

## How it's asked

**"Central compartment needle path + abnormal values?"** → **lateral + plantar through abductor hallucis under 1st MT base; 30–45 mmHg or Δp < 30**\
✗ Wrong options: Δp > 30

**"Foot compartments — EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **adductor compartment = adductor hallucis + interossei** (interossei are separate)

**"Superficial central compartment (TO)?"** → **flexor digitorum brevis**

**"Late complication?"** → **claw toes**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Galeazzi Fractures

*Trauma · 25 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Galeazzi** — **distal ⅓ radial shaft fracture + DRUJ injury**

**DRUJ instability by fracture level** — radial fracture **< 7.5 cm from articular surface → 55% unstable** · **> 7.5 cm → 6%** · closer = more unstable

### Anatomy

**Sigmoid notch** — shallow concavity on ulnar border of distal radius

**Primary DRUJ stabilisers** — **volar + dorsal radioulnar ligaments**

**DRUJ most stable in** — **supination**

![Distal radioulnar articulation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1029-shows-the-distal-radioulnar-articulation.jpg)

### Associated DRUJ ligament injury

**Most commonly injured** — **dorsal radioulnar ligaments**\
✗ Wrong options: volar long radiolunate · RSC · ligament of Testut · SL

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **FOOSH with forearm in pronation** · direct blow to dorsolateral wrist

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — point tenderness; test **pronation / supination for instability**; DRUJ stress → wrist / midline forearm pain

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral **forearm, elbow and wrist**

| Sign of DRUJ injury | View |
|---|---|
| **Widened DRUJ** | **AP** |
| **Dorsal or volar ulnar displacement** | **Lateral** |
| **Radial shortening ≥ 5 mm** | AP |
| Ulnar styloid fracture | AP |

### OTA classification (radius / ulna 22)

| Code | Description |
|---|---|
| **22-A2.3** | Simple radius fracture + DRUJ dislocation |
| 22-A3.3 | Simple both-bone (distal radius zone) + DRUJ dislocation |
| 22-B2.3 | Wedge radius + DRUJ dislocation |
| 22-B3.3 | Wedge both-bone + DRUJ dislocation |

## 5 Management

```
Galeazzi (adult) — fracture of necessity
├─ ORIF radius (volar Henry, anatomic plate, restore bow)
└─ Then assess DRUJ in supination
       ├─ stable → long-arm immobilisation in supination 6 wk
       ├─ reducible but unstable → cross-pin ulna to radius (4–6 wk)
       ├─ irreducible → open reduction (ECU interposed)
       └─ large ulnar styloid fragment → ORIF styloid + supination
```

### Treatment — radius ORIF then DRUJ assessment

**Indication for surgery** — **all adults**; **acute fixation far superior to late reconstruction**

**Radius** — **volar (Henry) approach**, **anatomic plating**, **restore radial bow**

**DRUJ assessment** — only **after rigid radius fixation**; gross ulnar motion in supination = unstable; **radius fixation alone does not guarantee DRUJ stability**

**Pinning** — **1–2 × 1.2 / 1.6 mm K-wires transversely, proximal to sigmoid notch** → **above-elbow cast in supination × 6 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: pinning in **pronation**

**Approach for DRUJ** — **dorsal capsulotomy**

### Irreducible DRUJ — ECU interposition

**Block to reduction after anatomic radius** — **ECU (extensor carpi ulnaris) tendon**\
✗ Wrong options: median nerve · FCR · pronator quadratus · FCU

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Compartment syndrome** | High-energy crush, open, vascular injury, coagulopathy; **pain on passive stretch most sensitive** |
| NV injury | Uncommon except **type III open** |
| **Refracture after plate removal** | Early removal, **large 4.5 mm plates**, comminution, persistent lucency → **remove ≥ 18 months** |
| Nonunion / malunion | — |
| **DRUJ subluxation** | Gravity, **pronator quadratus**, **brachioradialis** |

## X vs Y

| | **Galeazzi** | **Monteggia** | **Nightstick** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fracture | **Distal ⅓ radius** | **Proximal ulna** | Isolated ulnar shaft |
| Joint | **DRUJ** | **Radial head / PRUJ** | None |
| Fix | Plate radius → assess DRUJ | Plate ulna → radial head reduces | — |

| | **Rolando** | **Smith** |
|---|---|---|
| Fracture | Comminuted intra-articular **1st MC base** | Distal radius, **volar angulation** |

## How it's asked

**"Plate radius + evaluate DRUJ?"** → **Galeazzi**\
✗ Wrong options: nightstick · Monteggia · Rolando · Smith

**"Definitive management?"** → **ORIF radius → assess DRUJ → percutaneous pin if unstable**\
✗ Wrong options: closed reduction + cast · PRUJ · palmaris graft (chronic only)

**"High-speed MVA, Galeazzi in adult?"** → **plate radius, closed reduction + evaluate DRUJ**\
✗ Wrong options: open TFCC repair · fix both bones · pin in pronation

**"Irreducible DRUJ after radius reduced?"** → **ECU**

**"Ligament most commonly injured?"** → **dorsal radioulnar ligaments**

**"DRUJ instability if fracture < 7.5 cm from joint?"** → **55%**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Geriatric Patient Trauma

*Trauma · 26 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Elderly** — commonly **age > 65**; **physiological age / frailty** more clinically relevant

**Growth** — **> 85-year group fastest growing** (30%, 2000–2010)

**Falls** — **30% of people > 65 fall ≥ 1×/yr** · **2.1 million fragility fractures / yr**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — mechanism: mechanical, **syncope**, **elder abuse** · **accurate medication list** (polypharmacy, **anticoagulants**) · baseline ambulation + mental status

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — **joint above + below** (other injuries, adjacent arthroplasty / implants)

### Pre-operative work-up

**ED** — **ECG, CXR, urinalysis, labs**

**Optimise** — coagulopathy, **heart failure**, sepsis · prevent: **avoid over-sedation + anticholinergics**, ulcer prophylaxis

**Cardiac risk** — **Revised Cardiac Risk Index (RCRI)** or **NSQIP calculator** → guides work-up + timing

| Situation | Next step |
|---|---|
| **Low risk, normal ECG, no CHF signs** | **Proceed with surgery** (general anaesthesia safe) |
| Signs / symptoms of **CHF** | **BNP, echocardiogram** |
| **Elevated MACE risk + very poor functional capacity** | **Pharmacological stress test** |

## 5 Management

### Treatment principles

**Nonop** — comorbidities, low function, family wishes → pain control, ease of transfer

**Operative goals** — **early mobilisation**, return to pre-injury function, pain control

**Acute arthroplasty** — **comminuted distal humerus (TEA)**, **proximal humerus (reverse TSA)**, **femoral neck**, acetabulum, intra-articular distal femur

**Adjuncts** — **fibular strut allograft (proximal humerus)**, multiple implants, **locking plates**

![Reverse TSA for proximal humerus fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob322053-proximal-humerus-fracture-treated-with-a-reverse-tsa.jpg)

### Perioperative optimisation — regional block, co-management, timing

**↓ Narcotic use while awaiting optimisation** — **pre-op fascia iliaca block**\
✗ Wrong options: PCA · skeletal traction · pain service consult alone · prophylactic haloperidol

**Hip fracture < 48 h** — ↓ complications, pain, LOS, **mortality**

**Ortho-geriatric co-management — proven** — **↓ mortality**, **↓ complications**, **↓ time to surgery**, **↑ mobility at 4 months**, ↓ cost; LOS conflicting

**Co-management — NOT proven** — **↓ need for post-discharge rehab facility**

## 6 Complications

### Mortality predictors

| Factor | 1-yr mortality |
|---|---|
| **Mechanical ventilation at admission** | **78.7% — highest** |
| ISS > 25 | 73.2% |
| Head injury | 51.1% |
| Hip fracture | \~30% (in-hospital **6%**) |
| Open fracture | ≈ hip fracture |

**Spinal cord injury** — **higher mortality** than in young

### Delirium

**Incidence** — **13–50%** post-op

**Risk factors** — dementia, polypharmacy, **narcotics**, dehydration, restraints, **Foley catheters**, frequent awakenings

**Treatment** — **co-management**, reorientation, minimise narcotics (**fascia iliaca block**), early mobilisation, early catheter removal

**Medical complications** — pneumonia, **PE / DVT**, **pressure ulcers**, MI, stroke

## How it's asked

**"Reduce narcotics pre-op in hip fracture?"** → **fascia iliaca block**

**"Ortho-geriatric co-management — EXCEPT?"** → **decreased need for rehab facility**

**"Low-risk patient, normal ECG — next?"** → **proceed with surgical fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: BNP · echo · stress test · neuraxial only

**"Highest 1-yr mortality predictor?"** → **need for mechanical ventilation**\
✗ Wrong options: head injury · ISS > 25 · hip fracture · open fracture

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Gun Shot Wounds

*Trauma · 27 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**GSW** — **2nd leading cause of death in US youth** (after MVA) · buttock GSW ≈ **8%** of extremity GSWs

### Ballistics — kinetic energy transfer

**KE = ½ m v²** — **velocity matters more than mass** (m at 2v > 2m at v); injury rises **exponentially** with velocity

**Damage** — missile passage + **secondary shock wave** + **cavitation**; fractures possible without direct impact

**Max energy transfer** — **penetrating (no exit)** > perforating (exits) · **yaw 90° (sideways)** > nose-on; yaw ↓ with longer travel

**Thresholds (TO)** — **50 m/s penetrates skin** · **65 m/s fractures bone** · larger calibre → slower (TO)

**Contamination (TO)** — **bullets are not sterile** (heat + barrel friction insufficient); skin flora + clothing; **civilian GSW infection risk is LOW**

### Associated conditions

**Lead intoxication (plumbism)** — **intra-articular** missile; **lead soluble in synovial fluid**; blood lead **peaks 4–6 months (TO)** → neurotoxicity, **anaemia**, emesis, **abdominal colic**; local **lead synovitis → chondrolysis, hypertrophic arthritis**

**GSW to hip / acetabulum** — **bowel perforation > vascular > urogenital**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Velocity classification

| Velocity | Muzzle speed | Weapon | Gustilo equivalent |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Low** | **< 350 m/s** (**< 1,200 ft/s**) | **Handguns (except magnums)** | **I or II** (by wound size) |
| **Intermediate** | **350–650 m/s** (Orthobullets) · 350–600 m/s (TO); 1,200–2,000 ft/s | **Shotguns**, magnum handguns | Variable — mostly III |
| **High** | **> 600 m/s** (**> 2,000 ft/s**) | **Military + hunting rifles** | **III regardless of wound size** |

**Low-velocity cut-off** — < 350 m/s or **< 1,200 ft/s** (Orthobullets, TO) · **< 2,000 ft/s** (OB) — read the options

**Shotgun wounding** — **shot pattern, load (pellet size), distance**; **< 21 ft (close range) ≈ high velocity**; **wadding** → contamination

**Low-velocity GSW + fracture (OB)** — **< 8 h = Gustilo I** · **> 8 h = Gustilo II**

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| Radiographs | Bone involvement, pattern |
| **CT** | **Intra-articular missile**; **hollow viscus injury** communicating with fracture (pelvis / spine) |
| **CT abdomen** | **Hip / pelvic GSW → exclude bowel perforation (commonest associated injury)** (✗ Wrong options: bladder US · MRI · CTA · CT acetabulum first) |
| **Sigmoidoscopy** | **Blood on DRE** or path **crossing midline at / below pelvic brim** |
| **CT with intra-articular air** | **\~100% sensitive + specific for traumatic arthrotomy** (more than saline load test; less cost-effective) |

### Vascular injury assessment & infection risk

| Finding | Action |
|---|---|
| **Hard signs** — pulsatile bleeding, **expanding haematoma**, thrill / bruit, pulse deficit, ischaemia | **Operative exploration** |
| Soft signs — haematoma, bleeding at scene, unexplained hypotension | Further assessment |
| **ABI < 0.9** (e.g. 0.85) | **Vascular surgery consult** ± CTA |
| Symmetric pulses | **Do not exclude** injury → **ABI first** (✗ Wrong options: CTA as screen) |

**ABI** — **highly sensitive + specific**, quick, non-invasive · CTA expensive, finds irrelevant occult findings · duplex user-dependent

**Wound location alone** — **not** an indication for exploration

**Vascular injury + fracture** — **↑ infection** → antibiotics · **retained fragments do not ↑ infection**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ malunion · hardware failure · hip disarticulation

## 5 Management

```
Gunshot wound
├─ Low velocity, no fracture / stable fracture → local wound care, tetanus ± oral antibiotic
├─ Low velocity, unstable fracture → treat as CLOSED fracture (nail / ORIF)
├─ High velocity / close-range shotgun → open-fracture protocol: debride + ex-fix
└─ Transabdominal → laparotomy first, then hip / spine decisions
```

### Low-velocity wounds without fracture — local wound care

**Treatment** — **irrigation + local wound care in ED**, tetanus, ± **short oral antibiotic** (cephalosporin) (TO, OB)\
✗ Wrong options: OR debridement · IV × 7 d · primary closure · remove all fragments

**True statement (TO)** — **low-velocity GSW → local debridement, oral antibiotics, tetanus**\
✗ Wrong options: infection risk high · all grade III · formal debridement always

**Low-velocity arm wound, entry + exit, no fracture (TO)** — **local wound care + oral antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: surgical debridement + IV

**Primary closure** — **contraindicated**

**Antibiotics** — controversial; give if contaminated · **single long-acting IM cephalosporin = 48 h IV (TO, Geissler)** · **oral = IV** for prophylaxis (TO)

**Subcutaneous bones (ulna, tibia) (TO)** — exposed fragments → **treat as open fracture**

**Retained fragments (TO)** — benign; remove if **palm, sole, periarticular, intra-articular**

### Fracture management after GSW (nail, external fixation, debridement)

**Low-velocity femoral shaft** — **reamed antegrade or retrograde IMN** (direction + timing don't matter); union + infection = closed fractures\
✗ Wrong options: ex-fix · formal I&D · IV antibiotics 6 wk

**IMN (TO)** — **statically locked** for low / mid-velocity femur + tibia (incl. subtroch, supracondylar)

**High-velocity / shotgun / vascular injury (TO)** — **ex-fix** → **IMN \~2 weeks** when wound healed

**Shotgun humerus with radial nerve weakness** — **I&D + ex-fix**\
✗ Wrong options: acute fibular osteoseptocutaneous flap · nerve repair + retrograde nail · CT + ORIF · coaptation splint

**Shotgun wadding (TO)** — **must be removed** (organic) · **lead shot — leave** (removal damages tissue)

**Humerus + dense radial nerve palsy** — **closed IMN relatively contraindicated** → plate + explore, or open the fracture to protect nerve\
✗ Wrong options: age < 25 · GSW · athlete · comminution

### GSW to hand / foot

**Antibiotics** — gross contamination, **joint penetration**, contamination unclear

**Surgery** — **articular involvement**, **unstable fracture**, **≥ 8 h presentation**, **tendon involvement**, **superficial fragments in palm / sole**

**Foot: unstable 1st MT base + cuneiform, plantar fragments** — **surgical debridement + ex-fix / ORIF + IV antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: irrigation + cast ± antibiotics

**Type I stable, < 8 h** — tetanus, surface irrigation, cast / hard-sole shoe · **type II unstable → ex-fix + repeat debridement**

### Transabdominal / bowel-contaminated GSW — antibiotics & debridement

**Peritonism + positive FAST** — **laparotomy first**\
✗ Wrong options: DPL · CT · saline load · arthroscopic bullet removal

**Laparotomy precedes arthrotomy** for transabdominal trajectories

**Spine bullet + bowel injury, neuro intact** — **broad-spectrum IV antibiotics × 7 days**\
✗ Wrong options: gram-negative only · oral · observation · decompression

**Spine bullet + bowel (cecum), weakness, missile not in canal** — **broad-spectrum IV antibiotics 7–14 days**\
✗ Wrong options: fragment removal · oral 3–5 d · **methylprednisolone — contraindicated in GSW**

**Bullets through alimentary canal → SCI** — **removal not required**

**Spine surgery indications** — **instability** · **deficit matching compression by missile** · **lead in contact with CSF** · retained canal fragment + **incomplete** deficit (relative)

**Transabdominal hip GSW (blood on DRE)** — **hip debridement + ≥ 24 h antibiotics (gram +, gram −, enteric)**\
✗ Wrong options: bullet removal unless intra-articular · single dose · omit gram +

**Extra-articular, stable, no rectal blood (low velocity, bullet in ilium)** — **empiric antibiotics + observation**\
✗ Wrong options: laparotomy · bullet removal · tract debridement · sigmoidoscopy

**Acetabular GSW — septic arthritis risk** — **associated Letournel pattern** + **bowel injury** = independent risks → **least risk: anterior wall, no bowel injury**

**Pelvic / acetabular GSW complications** — septic arthritis, **enterocutaneous / enteroacetabular / vesicoacetabular fistula**, infected nonunion, iliac vessel injury

![Bullet embedded in L3 vertebral body](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1059-shows-bullet-embedded-in-l3-vertebral-body.jpg)

### Intra-articular bullets & traumatic arthrotomy

**Retained intra-articular bullet** — **arthrotomy / removal** → lead synovitis, plumbism, **third-body wear**, sepsis

**Transabdominal intra-articular GSW** — contaminated → **arthrotomy** (after laparotomy)

**Transarticular, no retained bullet / IA pathology** — **IV antibiotics + local care, no arthrotomy** (OB)

**Low-velocity knee GSW, no fracture (TO)** — **arthroscopic debridement + IV antibiotics** (TO) · contrast OB: no IA pathology → antibiotics alone — **read the options**\
✗ Wrong options: open debridement · local care + oral

**Arthroscopy (TO)** — ↓ morbidity, faster recovery; locate bullet → mini-arthrotomy

**Antibiotics for IA fractures (TO)** — **1–2 days** if bullet through clean skin / clothing · **1–2 weeks broad-spectrum** if through **lung, bowel, contaminated skin**

**Small traumatic arthrotomy (< 5 cm), minimal contamination** — **no surgery**: bedside irrigation, tetanus, antibiotics\
✗ Wrong options: saline load always needed · CT air not sensitive · retained lead needs no surgery · cost equal (surgery \~$10,000 more)

![CT of intra-articular ankle (pilon) GSW](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1059-l-ankle-gsw-pilon-ct-sag.jpg)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Compartment syndrome** | Muscle oedema with higher-velocity wounds; ballistic femur > blunt |
| **Plumbism / lead arthropathy** | Intra-articular retained bullet |
| **Infection** | ↑ with **vascular injury**, **bowel transit**, high velocity, associated acetabular pattern |
| Septic arthritis | Transabdominal → hip / acetabulum |

## X vs Y

| | **Low velocity** | **High velocity / shotgun close range** |
|---|---|---|
| Treat as | **Closed fracture** | **Open fracture (Gustilo III)** |
| Debridement | Superficial / local | **Aggressive, repeated** |
| Antibiotics | Gram + / oral, short | **Broad spectrum** |
| Femur | **Immediate reamed IMN** | **Ex-fix → IMN \~2 wk** |

| | **Bullet passed through bowel → spine** | **Bullet passed through bowel → hip joint** |
|---|---|---|
| Treatment | **IV broad-spectrum 7–14 d; leave bullet** | **Debride hip** + ≥ 24 h antibiotics |

## How it's asked

**"Greatest kinetic energy transfer?"** → **penetrating, mass m, velocity 2v, yaw 90°**

**"True about firearm wounds (TO)?"** → **low velocity → local debridement, oral antibiotics, tetanus**

**"Handgun arm wound, no fracture (TO)?"** → **local wound care + oral antibiotics**

**"Forearm GSW 1,000 ft/s, no fracture?"** → **irrigation + local wound care in ED**

**"Spine bullet, cecum injury, weakness?"** → **IV broad-spectrum antibiotics 7–14 days**

**"L2 bullet, bowel laceration, intact?"** → **IV broad-spectrum 7 days**

**"Bullet in ilium, no rectal blood, stable?"** → **empiric antibiotics + observation**

**"Hip GSW — image for commonest associated injury?"** → **CT abdomen (bowel perforation)**

**"Buttock GSW, blood on DRE, hip involved?"** → **hip debridement + 24 h gram +/−/enteric cover**

**"Flank GSW, peritonism, FAST positive?"** → **laparotomy**

**"Acetabular GSW least likely septic?"** → **anterior wall, no bowel injury**

**"Low-velocity femoral shaft GSW?"** → **reamed antegrade or retrograde IMN**

**"Shotgun humerus?"** → **I&D + external fixation**

**"Foot GSW, unstable fracture, plantar fragments?"** → **debridement + fixation + IV antibiotics**

**"Relative contraindication to closed humeral nail?"** → **radial nerve palsy**

**"Low-velocity femur GSW + vascular injury ↑?"** → **infection**

**"Thigh GSW, ABI 0.85?"** → **vascular surgery consult**

**"Low-velocity knee GSW, no fracture (TO)?"** → **arthroscopic debridement + IV antibiotic**

**"Bullet passed through hip into iliac wing, no IA pathology?"** → **IV antibiotics without arthrotomy**

**"Traumatic arthrotomy — true?"** → **small minimally contaminated wounds need no surgery**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Gustilo Classification

*Trauma · 28 / 67*

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Gustilo-Anderson classification

| | **I** | **II** | **IIIA** | **IIIB** | **IIIC** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Energy | Low | Moderate | High | High | High |
| **Wound** | **≤ 1 cm** | **1–10 cm** | Usually > 10 cm | Usually > 10 cm | Usually > 10 cm |
| Soft tissue | Minimal | Moderate | Extensive | Extensive | Extensive |
| Contamination | Clean | Moderate | Extensive | Extensive | Extensive |
| Comminution | Minimal | Moderate | Severe | Severe | Severe |
| **Periosteal stripping** | No | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **Coverage** | Local | Local | **Local (incl. skin graft / primary closure)** | **Rotational or free flap** | Typically flap |
| **Vascular** | Normal | Normal | Normal | Normal | **Arterial injury requiring repair** |

![Gustilo IIIB — periosteal stripping, needs flap](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1003-grade3b.jpg)

### Grading rules

**Most accurate grading** — **intra-operatively** after debridement (definitive)

**Automatically ≥ IIIA** — **farm injuries** · **open femur** (energy needed) · **segmental fractures** · **high-velocity GSW**

**IIIA vs IIIB** — decided by **coverage**: **skin graft / primary closure = IIIA** · **flap (gastrocnemius, free) = IIIB** — **not wound size**

**IIIC** — **vascular repair needed**, regardless of soft tissue; ABI 0.6 without repair = **not IIIC**

**Not part of Gustilo** — **fasciotomy need**, closed fractures

## 5 Management

### Antibiotics by grade

| Grade | Antibiotic |
|---|---|
| **I + II** | **1st-generation cephalosporin (cefazolin)** |
| **III (A, B, C)** | **Cefazolin + aminoglycoside (gentamicin)** — gram-negative cover; **controversial** |
| **Farm injury / bowel contamination** | **+ Penicillin** (**Clostridium**, anaerobes) |
| **Fresh or salt water** | **Fluoroquinolone (ciprofloxacin)**; also for cephalosporin / clindamycin allergy |
| **Salt water** | **Doxycycline + 3rd / 4th-gen cephalosporin (ceftazidime)** |

### Timing

**Start** — **ASAP**; **infection ↑ if delayed > 3 h** from injury

**Duration** — **24–72 h after I&D**

**Tetanus booster** — if **none in last 5 years**

## X vs Y

| | **IIIA** | **IIIB** | **IIIC** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Key feature | Coverage possible locally | **Flap needed** | **Vascular repair needed** |
| Example | 8 cm avulsion **skin-grafted**; open femur closed primarily | **Gastrocnemius flap** for proximal tibia | **Pale foot after reduction** |

## How it's asked

**"Segmental tibia, 8 cm avulsion, skin graft?"** → **IIIA**\
✗ Wrong options: IIIB (no flap)

**"Which matches IIIB?"** → **proximal tibia with soft-tissue loss needing gastrocnemius flap**\
✗ Wrong options: pale foot = IIIC · 8 cm closed primarily = IIIA · fasciotomy not graded · closed pilon

**"Open femur, 3 cm wound, ABI 0.6, closed primarily?"** → **IIIA**

**"Most accurate grading?"** → **intra-operative**

**"Farm injury — add?"** → **penicillin**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hand & Forearm Compartment Syndrome

*Trauma · 29 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Causes

**Pathophysiology** — trauma → bleeding + oedema → ↑ interstitial pressure → vascular occlusion → **myoneural ischaemia**; muscle damage from **3–4 h warm ischaemia** ("time is tissue")

**Fractures (commonest)** — **distal radius (adults)** · **supracondylar humerus (children)**; **floating elbow = highest paediatric risk**

**Other** — **crush**, contusion, GSW, tight casts / dressings, **IV / intra-arterial extravasation**, **burns (circumferential eschar)**, post-ischaemic swelling, bleeding disorders, arterial injury, **drug use (found down)**

**Obtunded patients** — **79% obtunded**, **68% from IV / intra-arterial extravasation** (Ouellette)

### Anatomy

**Upper extremity commonest site** — **forearm, volar compartment**

| Region | Compartments |
|---|---|
| Arm | **2** — anterior, posterior |
| **Forearm** | **3** — **volar (most often)**, dorsal, **mobile wad (BR, ECRL, ECRB; rarely)** (Orthobullets, OB) · **4** — dorsal, **superficial volar, deep volar**, mobile wad (TO) |
| **Hand** | **10** — **thenar, hypothenar, adductor pollicis, 4 dorsal interossei, 3 volar interossei** |

**VIC — commonest muscle + nerve (TO)** — **FDP** + **median nerve**

![Compartments of the hand](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1064-compartments-of-the-hand.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis & fasciotomy

| Finding | Significance |
|---|---|
| **Pain out of proportion** | **First symptom**; absent with nerve damage; hard in polytrauma, sedated, children |
| **Pain on passive finger stretch (MCP / phalangeal extension)** | **Most sensitive** / early |
| **Tense hand in intrinsic MINUS position** | **Most consistent** clinical finding (✗ Wrong options: intrinsic plus) |
| Paraesthesia / hypoaesthesia | Nerve ischaemia |
| **Paralysis** | **Late** — full recovery rare |
| **Absent pulses** | **Late** — amputation usually inevitable |
| Cool, pale hand | Late; suggests **arterial injury** |
| Loss of vibration sense | Not specific |

**Earliest feature of VIC (TO)** — **pain on passive stretch of middle + ring fingers** (FDP of these fingers first)\
✗ Wrong options: skin changes · thumb stretch · intrinsic minus

**Forearm CS + numb thumb / index** — **acute carpal tunnel** → release CTR with fasciotomy

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — rule out fracture only · **no advanced imaging, CTA or Doppler** — delays surgery

### Diagnostic criteria

| Fasciotomy | Observe |
|---|---|
| **Clinical diagnosis** (no pressures needed) | Exam not consistent |
| **Δp < 30 mmHg** (diastolic − compartment) | **Δp > 30** |
| Absolute **> 30 mmHg** | — |

**Pressures indicated** — obtunded, intubated, **after a block**, polytrauma, equivocal

**Intra-op Δp < 30** — check **pre-op diastolic**; follow post-op (anaesthesia lowers diastolic)

### Normal hand compartment pressure

**Normal hand** — **0–15 mmHg** (e.g. **10 mmHg**) · **> 15 mmHg pathological** (some) · finger pressures **not measured** · volar–dorsal interosseous fascia incompetent

## 5 Management

```
Suspected hand / forearm compartment syndrome
├─ Clinical diagnosis → emergent fasciotomy of all involved compartments
│      └─ median nerve symptoms → add carpal tunnel release
├─ Obtunded / equivocal → pressures → Δp < 30 → fasciotomy
└─ Never: analgesia + wait, nerve block, overnight observation, CTA
```

**Crush hand, pain on passive MCP / wrist motion, normal NV** — **surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: imaging · Doppler · admit + observe · analgesia

**Peripheral nerve block** — **contraindicated** when CS suspected (masks symptoms)

### Forearm fasciotomy

| Incision | Releases | Interval |
|---|---|---|
| **Volar** — radial to FCU at wrist → **medial epicondyle**, extend distally | **Volar compartment + carpal tunnel** + lacertus fibrosus | **FCU ulnar, FDS radial** → open deep fascia |
| **Dorsal** — 2 cm distal to lateral epicondyle → mid-wrist | **Mobile wad + dorsal** | **EDC / ECRB** |

**Post-op** — **leave open** (VAC / wet-to-dry) → **re-debride 48–72 h** → delayed closure or **STSG**

![Volar forearm fasciotomy incision](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1064-volar-incision.jpg)

### Hand fasciotomy

| Incision | Releases |
|---|---|
| **2 dorsal — over 2nd + 4th MCs** | **Dorsal + volar interossei**, **adductor pollicis** (1st volar interosseous + adductor via ulnar side of 2nd MC) |
| **Radial side of 1st MC** | **Thenar** |
| **Ulnar side of 5th MC** | **Hypothenar** |

**All 10 compartments** — released; wounds open → primary closure or STSG

![Hand fasciotomy incisions](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1064-hand-compartment-incisions.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Volkmann ischaemic contracture

**Cause** — missed CS; **brachial artery obstruction (tight plaster, tourniquet)** → muscle necrosis → fibrosis

**Posture** — **elbow flexion, forearm pronation, wrist flexion, thumb adduction (+ flexion), MCP extension, IP flexion** = **intrinsic-minus (claw) hand**

**Progression (TO)** — FDP (middle + ring) → **FPL** → FDS, FCR, FCU → extensors (severe); median + ulnar neuropathy add to claw

| Tsuge stage | Muscles | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Mild** | **Finger flexors** | **Dynamic splinting, tendon lengthening** |
| **Moderate** | **Wrist + finger flexors** | Excise necrotic muscle, **median + ulnar neurolysis**, **BR → FPL, ECRL → FDP** transfers, **flexor slide** |
| **Severe** | **Flexors + extensors** | As moderate ± **free functioning muscle transfer** |

**Prognosis** — loss of function, VIC, neurological deficit, infection, amputation

### Extravasation injury mimic

**Purple glove syndrome** — **IV phenytoin extravasation**: oedema, **violaceous** discoloration, pain; resolves with **stopping IV + elevation + monitoring**

**Mechanism** — **vesicant leaking into extravascular space** (phenytoin pH ≈ 12, precipitates)\
✗ Wrong options: axillary DVT · necrotising fasciitis · dermatomyositis · flexor tenosynovitis

**Irritant vs vesicant** — irritant = inflammation only · **vesicant = direct toxicity** (slough, blisters, necrosis, ± CS)

**Agents** — phenytoin, **TPN**, vasopressors, arginine, chemotherapy, contrast

**Management** — stop infusion, mark border, elevate, immobilise, heat / ice, antidotes, irrigation, debridement ± fasciotomy

## X vs Y

| | **Early** | **Late** |
|---|---|---|
| Findings | **Pain out of proportion, passive stretch pain**, swelling | **Paralysis, absent pulses, pallor** |

| | **Compartment syndrome** | **Purple glove (phenytoin)** |
|---|---|---|
| Treatment | **Fasciotomy** | **Stop IV, elevate, observe** |

## How it's asked

**"Earliest feature of VIC (TO)?"** → **pain on passive stretch of middle + ring fingers**

**"Crush hand, passive pain, no fracture — next?"** → **surgical intervention**

**"Burn eschar — early CS finding?"** → **pain on phalangeal extension**\
✗ Wrong options: absent pulses · Δp 38 · intrinsic plus · paralysis

**"Most supports hand CS?"** → **swelling + pain out of proportion**\
✗ Wrong options: vibration loss · finger pressure 15 · intrinsic plus · cool pale hand

**"Forearm crush, thumb / index numbness, passive pain?"** → **emergent forearm fasciotomy + carpal tunnel release**

**"Normal hand compartment pressure?"** → **10 mmHg**

**"Swollen violaceous hand on IV phenytoin?"** → **extravasation of a vesicant**
