# Hip Dislocation

*Trauma · 30 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Who** — young, **high-energy** trauma (MVA **dashboard**); rare but **high rate of associated injuries** (**95%**)

**Direction** — **posterior 90%** · anterior **\~10%** (Orthobullets) · **10–15%** (TO)

**Stability** — bony geometry + **labrum, capsule, ligamentum teres**, muscles

### Associated injuries (knee, aorta)

| Dislocation | Associated injury |
|---|---|
| **Posterior** | **Posterior wall fracture (commonest; up to 70%, TO)**, femoral head fracture (**5–15%**, TO · 10–15%, OB) — anterior head fragment, **sciatic nerve (peroneal division)**, **ipsilateral knee (up to 25%)**, AVN |
| **Anterior** | **Femoral head impaction / chondral fracture — commonest (TO)** (superolateral, **Hill-Sachs-like**); acetabulum, neck, GT, shaft; **AIIS avulsion** (superior type); femoral vessels + **femoral nerve** (less common) |

**Ipsilateral knee on MRI** — **93% abnormal**: **effusion 37%**, **bone bruise 33%**, **meniscal tear 30%** → most likely concomitant = **ipsilateral meniscus tear**\
✗ Wrong options: contralateral ACL · SDH · ankle · lumbar burst

**Thoracic aortic rupture** — associated with **posterior hip dislocation** (deceleration); **bilateral pulse deficit → proximal large-vessel injury** → CXR mediastinum, thoracic surgery

## 2 Mechanism

| Hip position | Result |
|---|---|
| **Flexed + adducted (+ IR)**, axial load via knee (**dashboard**) | **Posterior — pure** (more flexion / adduction → simple) |
| Flexed + **abducted** | **Posterior fracture-dislocation** |
| **Abduction + ER + extension** | **Anterior superior (pubic)** |
| **Abduction + ER + flexion** | **Anterior inferior (obturator)** |

![Dashboard injury mechanism](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1035-dashboard-injury-mechanism.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

| Type | Limb attitude |
|---|---|
| **Posterior** | **Flexion, adduction, internal rotation**, shortened |
| **Anterior pubic (superior)** | **Extension + external rotation** |
| **Anterior obturator (inferior)** | **Flexion, abduction, external rotation** |
| **Irreducible variant** | **Neutral, mildly shortened** — not overtly flexed / IR, limb locked |

**Exam** — ATLS; **sciatic nerve (10–20%)**; knee stability; **CXR for aortic injury**

**FNB attitude question (TO)** — answer key not provided by the source; map: **FADIR → posterior**, **FABER → anterior obturator**, **extension + abduction (ER) → anterior pubic**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Classification

**Simple** — pure dislocation · **Complex** — + acetabulum or proximal femur fracture

| Thompson-Epstein (posterior) (TO) | Description |
|---|---|
| 1 | ± Minor fracture |
| 2 | **Large single posterior rim** fragment |
| 3 | **Comminuted rim** ± major fragment |
| 4 | **Acetabular floor** fracture |
| 5 | **Femoral head** fracture |

| Epstein (anterior) (TO) | Description |
|---|---|
| **Type 1 — superior** | **Pubic, subspinous** |
| **Type 2 — inferior** | **Obturator, thyroid, perineal** |
| Subtypes | **A** simple · **B** head fracture · **C** acetabular fracture |

### Key radiology findings

| Finding | Meaning |
|---|---|
| AP + **cross-table lateral** | **Anterior vs posterior**; **check femoral neck before reduction** |
| **Shenton's line broken** | **All dislocations** (inferior neck ↔ superior obturator foramen) |
| **Head smaller** than other side, over roof, **lesser trochanter hidden (IR)** | **Posterior** |
| **Head larger**, **medial / inferior** to acetabulum | **Anterior** |
| **Cranial head close to ilium + ER (prominent LT)** + IA fragments | **Irreducible variant** (✗ Wrong options: absent fragments · hidden LT · Shenton's (all dislocations)) |
| **Post-reduction CT — mandatory in ALL** | Head fractures, **loose bodies / incarcerated fragments** (even if concentric on X-ray), acetabular fractures |
| Post-reduction AP, inlet / outlet, **Judet** | — |
| MRI | Controversial, not routine (labrum, cartilage, vascularity) |

**Advanced imaging before reduction** — **no** → reduce first

## 5 Management

```
Hip dislocation
├─ Femoral neck fracture → NO closed reduction; fix neck first (ORIF)
├─ Otherwise → emergent closed reduction (GA / deep sedation) → post-reduction CT
│      ├─ concentric, stable, no fragments → protected WB 4–6 wk
│      └─ incarcerated fragment / non-concentric → open reduction ± removal
└─ Irreducible → urgent open reduction (posterior → KL · anterior → Smith-Petersen)
```

### Reduction — urgency & technique (including children)

**Timing** — **within 6 h** (OB, TO) · **within 12 h** (Orthobullets, OB) — read the options; **> 12 h → 5.6× AVN odds (TO)**; ↑ adverse events

**Hip before wrist** — reduce hip first; **CT after reduction**\
✗ Wrong options: CT / MRI first

**Technique** — supine, **traction in line with deformity**, adequate sedation + relaxation, assess stability

| Manoeuvre (TO) | Dislocation | Key steps |
|---|---|---|
| **Allis** | Posterior | Inline traction, **knee 90°**, assistant stabilises pelvis, extend + ER |
| **Bigelow** | Posterior | Traction, knee 90°, **extension, abduction, ER** |
| **East Baltimore lift** | Posterior | Hip + knee 90°, physician + assistant lift under popliteal fossa |
| **Allis leg extension** | Anterior | Inline traction; **pubic → hyperextension** |
| **Reverse Bigelow** | Anterior | Traction in line → **adduct, IR, extend** |

**Child (10-yr, posterior)** — **closed reduction under GA with fluoroscopy** → avoids **physeal separation (epiphyseal displacement)**, confirms concentricity\
✗ Wrong options: ED sedation · no fluoroscopy · open first

**After reduction (TO)** — **WBAT**, ROM / strengthening, **jog 6–8 wk**, **running / sport 3–4 months**; follow **18 months–2 yr** for AVN

**Simple dislocation (Orthobullets)** — **protected WB 4–6 wk**

### Post-reduction CT

**Next step after closed reduction (any)** — **CT hip + pelvis**\
✗ Wrong options: skeletal traction · EUA fluoroscopy · spica · TDWB · observation

**Skeletal traction** — if acetabular fracture / loose body / unstable

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Irreducible** dislocation | **Urgent open reduction** |
| **Incarcerated fragment** on CT, **non-concentric** reduction | Open reduction / fragment removal |
| **Delayed presentation** | Open reduction |
| **Associated femoral neck fracture** | **Stabilise neck before reduction** |
| Acetabular / femoral head fractures | ORIF |
| Arthroscopy | No established indication (loose bodies, labrum) |

### Anterior / obturator & irreducible dislocations — recognition & open approach

**Approach** — **posterior → Kocher-Langenbeck** · **anterior → Smith-Petersen** (or Watson-Jones)

**Irreducible femoral head fracture-dislocation** — head **buttonholes through labrum-bone interval**; **Smith-Petersen**: ↓ op time, ↓ blood loss, better view, **spares head supply** (anterior capsule contributes little)

**Smith-Petersen interval** — **sartorius (femoral) ↔ TFL (superior gluteal)**; deep: rectus femoris ↔ gluteus medius

**Other intervals** — Watson-Jones: TFL ↔ gluteus medius (both **superior gluteal**) · Hardinge: splits gluteus medius + vastus lateralis (no internervous plane) · **posterior (Moore / Southern)**: splits gluteus maximus

**Posterior approach risk** — **lateral epiphyseal artery within 15 mm of external rotator insertions**

**Irreducible without PW fracture (Mehta)** — **urgent ORIF without closed attempts** → avoid iatrogenic neck fracture

### Associated femoral head fracture — management

**Infrafoveal head fracture, stable hip, 8 mm displacement (TO)** — **ORIF via anterior approach**\
✗ Wrong options: traction · excision · posterior ORIF

**Approach rule (TO)** — **Pipkin I / II → anterior** · **Pipkin IV → posterior**

**Nonop Pipkin I / II (TO)** — **< 2 mm displacement**, stable hip, **no interposed fragment**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Rate / fact |
|---|---|
| **Post-traumatic arthritis** | **Up to 20% simple** (commonest long-term, TO); markedly ↑ complex; **anterior 17%** (TO) |
| **AVN** | **5–40%** (Orthobullets) · **2–10% if reduced < 6 h** (TO); ↑ with time to reduction; **anterior 4%** (TO) |
| **Sciatic nerve** (peroneal division) | **8–20%** (Orthobullets) · **10–15%** (TO); ↑ with delayed reduction |
| HO | **2.8–9%** (TO); repeated reduction attempts |
| **Recurrent dislocation** | **< 2%** |

## X vs Y

| | **Posterior** | **Anterior** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **90%** | \~10% |
| Attitude | **Flexion, adduction, IR** | **ER** + abduction (obturator: flexion · pubic: extension) |
| X-ray head size | **Smaller** | **Larger** |
| Associated | **PW fracture**, sciatic nerve, knee | **Head impaction**, femoral NV |
| AVN | Higher | **4%** |
| Open approach | **Kocher-Langenbeck** | **Smith-Petersen** |

| | **Anterior pubic (superior)** | **Anterior obturator (inferior)** |
|---|---|---|
| Hip at injury | **Extension** | **Flexion** |
| Attitude | **Extension + ER** | **Flexion, abduction, ER** |
| X-ray | Head superior | **Head inferior** |

## How it's asked

**"After closed reduction — next?"** → **CT hip + pelvis**

**"Normal exam after reduction — next?"** → **CT**

**"Commonest association with anterior dislocation (TO)?"** → **femoral head (impaction) fracture**

**"Most likely concomitant injury (dashboard)?"** → **ipsilateral knee meniscus tear**

**"Injury associated with thoracic aortic rupture?"** → **posterior hip dislocation**

**"Flexed, abducted, ER limb?"** → **anterior obturator dislocation**

**"Irreducible fracture-dislocation — Smith-Petersen interval?"** → **sartorius (femoral) + TFL (superior gluteal)**

**"X-ray warning of irreducible dislocation?"** → **cranial head + externally rotated femur**

**"Return to running after dislocation (TO)?"** → **WBAT, jog by 8 wk, run by 3 months**

**"10-yr-old posterior dislocation?"** → **closed reduction under GA with fluoroscopy**

**"Hip + wrist injury — next?"** → **closed reduction of hip**

**"Infrafoveal fracture, 8 mm, stable (TO)?"** → **ORIF via anterior approach**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Humeral Shaft Fractures

*Trauma · 31 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **3–5% of all fractures** · **20% of humeral fractures** · 7–11.3 / 100,000

**Age** — bimodal; **60% > 50 yr** · < 50: 70% men · > 50: 70% women

**Location** — proximal ⅓ 30% · **middle ⅓ 60% (commonest)** · distal ⅓ 10%

**Mechanism** — **ground-level fall 60%** · MVA \~30% · pathological 4.3% · open 3%

**Risk factors** — previous fracture, **smoking (men)**, age, osteoporosis

| Level | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| Proximal ⅓ | FOOSH, elderly (impaction at surgical neck) |
| Middle ⅓ **transverse** | **Direct blow** |
| Middle ⅓ **spiral** | FOOSH / **torsion (throwing, arm wrestling)** |
| Distal ⅓ | Fall on flexed elbow |

**Associated** — **floating elbow** (+ radius / ulna; commoner in children) · ipsilateral shoulder dislocation (**posterior** > anterior)

### Surgical anatomy & approaches

**Canal** — ends **2–3 cm proximal to olecranon fossa**

**Deforming forces** — fracture **proximal to pectoralis major** → **deltoid abducts proximal fragment**

**Radial nerve course** — exits axilla posterior to brachial artery → **triangular interval** → between **medial + long heads** of triceps → **spiral groove** (**medial to long + lateral heads, 2 cm proximal to deep head**) → pierces **lateral intermuscular septum \~10 cm proximal to radiocapitellar joint** (**20 cm** above medial, **14 cm** above lateral epicondyle) → anterior **\~7.5 cm** from articular surface (**safe zone — never posterior within 7.5 cm**)

**Radial nerve in contact with bone** — **\~6.5 cm** of diaphysis in spiral groove

**Ulnar nerve** — enters posterior compartment at **arcade of Struthers**, \~8 cm above medial epicondyle

**Axillary nerve** — **4–7 cm below acromion** (limits proximal posterior exposure)

**Posterior antebrachial / inferior lateral brachial cutaneous nerve** — branch of **radial**; trace proximally to find radial nerve (**Gerwin modification**, paratricipital)

**Compartments** — anterior: biceps, brachialis, coracobrachialis, brachial vessels, **musculocutaneous, median, ulnar** · posterior: triceps, **radial nerve**

![Radial nerve course in the arm](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1016-radial-nerve.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — shortening + **varus**; **document radial nerve pre- and post-reduction** (wrist + thumb IP extension, dorsal hand sensation)

**Radial nerve palsy** — "**A-OK**" + finger crossing intact, ↓ wrist / finger extension, dorsal hand numbness → **Holstein-Lewis**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **AP + lateral humerus**, joint above + below | Standard |
| **Transthoracic lateral** | **Sagittal (apex anterior) deformity**; rotate patient, not distal fragment |
| Traction views | Shortened / extension (not routine) |
| CT | Intra-articular extension |
| CTA | Vascular injury |
| **EMG / NCS** | Nerve palsy follow-up — **not acutely** |

### Classification

| System | Categories |
|---|---|
| Descriptive | Proximal / middle / distal ⅓ · spiral, transverse, comminuted |
| **OTA 12** | **A simple · B wedge · C complex** |
| Garnavos | Location **P / M / D** (+ j = joint) · morphology **S** simple (T transverse / oblique, S spiral) · **I** intermediate (1–2 butterflies) · **C** complex (≥ 3 butterflies / comminution) |
| **Holstein-Lewis** | **Spiral distal ⅓** → radial nerve palsy **7–22%** (Orthobullets) · **22%** (OB); entrapment risk; **7.5% of shaft fractures** |

## 5 Management

```
Humeral shaft fracture
├─ Isolated, closed, acceptable alignment → coaptation splint 7–10 d → functional brace
│      └─ radial nerve palsy alone → still brace (observe nerve)
├─ Absolute indication (open, vascular, plexus, floating elbow, compartment syndrome) → ORIF
└─ Polytrauma / lower-limb fractures → plate (WB through arm) · DCO → splint first
```

### Functional bracing — indications, acceptable alignment & contraindications

| Acceptable alignment (adult) | Limit |
|---|---|
| **Anterior (sagittal) angulation** | **< 20°** |
| **Varus / valgus** | **< 30°** |
| **Rotation** | **< 30°** (Orthobullets, TO) · **< 15°** (OB) |
| **Shortening** | **< 3 cm** |

**TO trap (TO)** — "**angulation < 30°**" is **NOT** acceptable (must be < 20°) · **shortening < 3 cm, rotation < 30°, angulation < 20°** (TO)

**Child (TO)** — **20–30° varus**, **20° apex-anterior**, **15° IR**, **≤ 2 cm shortening**

**Infant / toddler** — **coaptation splint + bandage arm to thorax**\
✗ Wrong options: hanging cast · flexible nails · ex-fix · plate

**Technique** — coaptation splint / hanging cast **7–10 days** → **Sarmiento brace** (2.5 cm below axilla → 2.5 cm above condyles); **no sling** (gravity reduction); **valgus mould** vs varus; weekly X-ray × 3 wk, then every 3–4 wk

**Hanging cast** — **avoid in transverse fractures** (distraction)

**At 9 days, acceptable** — **functional brace 6–8 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: continue splint (stiffness) · ORIF

**Contraindications (absolute)** — severe soft-tissue injury / bone loss · **vascular injury needing repair** · **brachial plexus injury** (no muscle tone → nonunion)

**NOT contraindications** — **radial nerve palsy (closed)** · comminution · 1 cm shortening · 20° varus

**Outcomes** — union **93.5%** (Sarmiento < 2% nonunion); time **10.7 wk**; malunion 12%; **proximal ⅓ oblique / spiral nonunion 29% (Rutgers-Ring)** · 54% proximal ⅓ (Orthobullets)

**Varus malunion** — **commonest deformity** (esp. distal / transverse) → **no effect on function**\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion · pain · satisfaction · nerve recovery

**Nonop nonunion** — higher than all surgical techniques (contemporary literature); 33% nonunion in one series

### Operative indications (floating elbow, polytrauma, plexus injury) & weight bearing

| Absolute | Relative |
|---|---|
| **Open fracture** | **Bilateral humerus** |
| **Vascular injury requiring repair** | **Polytrauma / lower-limb fracture** (crutch WB) |
| **Brachial plexus injury** | **Pathological** (life expectancy > 6 mo) |
| **Floating elbow (ipsilateral forearm)** | Burns / soft tissue precluding brace |
| **Compartment syndrome** | **Distraction**, **segmental**, **short oblique / transverse (OTA A)**, intra-articular, long oblique proximal |
| **Periprosthetic at stem tip** | **Obesity**, large breasts |
| **Inability to maintain reduction** | Radial nerve palsy (relative only) |
| **Progressive nerve deficit after manipulation** | **Young athlete wanting quick return** |

**NOT an operative indication (TO)** — **closed fracture with radial nerve palsy**\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion · floating elbow · pathological with secondaries

**Strongest indication** — **ipsilateral both-bone forearm fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: radial nerve palsy · long oblique · comminution · Holstein-Lewis

**Flaccid limb, C5–T1 avulsions** — **ORIF with plate**\
✗ Wrong options: splint · brace · ex-fix · sling

**Floating elbow (olecranon + humerus)** — **ORIF both + early ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: brace humerus · splint 4 wk

**Polytrauma (humerus + femur)** — **plate humerus + IMN femur** → crutch WB

**WB after plate / IMN** — **full WB**; **no effect on union**\
✗ Wrong options: partial (can't use crutches)

**Plateau + humerus ORIF + contralateral femur IMN** — **NWB plateau side leg**, **WBAT arm + nailed femur**

**DCO, multiple GSWs + abdominal injury** — **closed reduction + splint in ED**\
✗ Wrong options: I&D · ex-fix in ED · ORIF · IMN

**15-yr quarterback, torsional spiral, wants early return** — **surgical fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: splint / brace · EMG acutely

**Ex-fix indications** — high-energy comminution, open, defect, floating elbow, **unstable polytrauma**, **vascular injury**; 80% good, **pin-track infection 12%**

**TEA** — comminuted distal fractures, **low-demand > 65**, osteoporosis / **RA**

### Plate vs intramedullary nail

| | **Plate (ORIF)** | **IMN** |
|---|---|---|
| Union | **Equal** (90–92%) | **Equal** |
| Time to union | 11.9 wk | **10 wk** |
| Op time | 88 min | **61 min (faster)** |
| **Shoulder pain / impingement / stiffness** | **Lower** | **Higher (16–37%)** — rotator cuff (supraspinatus) |
| **Functional shoulder score (ASES) at 1 yr** | **Equal** | **Equal** |
| Radial nerve palsy | Equal | Equal |
| Infection | 5.4% | **1.2%** — **lower** (Orthobullets, OB) · no difference (OB) — read the options |
| Reoperation | 7.6% | 11.6% (NS) · **↑ revision** (McCormack, OB) |
| Total complications | Lower | **Higher** |
| **Hardware complications** | Lower | **Higher** |
| Malrotation | Lower | **Higher → ↓ ROM** |

**IMN — EXCEPT** — "**increased shoulder ROM**" is false

**Antegrade IMN — true** — **higher shoulder impingement**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ ROM · less radial nerve palsy · less nonunion

**IMN relative indications** — **pathological**, segmental, osteoporotic, poor skin, polytrauma

**MIPO** — anterolateral incisions + extraperiosteal tunnel; **lacks nerve visualisation → ↑ radial nerve risk** (Orthobullets) · **lower transient palsy than ORIF** (OB) — read the options

### Plate technique

**Implants** — **3.5 / 4.5 mm (narrow or broad) DCP / LC-DCP** (LC-DCP: easier contour, ↓ stress shielding, periosteum spared)

**Simple pattern** — **lag + compression** (absolute stability) · **comminuted** — **bridge** mode

**Bone defect** — **≤ 3 cm → shorten** · **> 3 cm → graft**

**Nerve transposition during ORIF** — **no benefit**

### Approaches

| Approach | Use | Key facts |
|---|---|---|
| **Anterior (brachialis split)** | Middle ⅓ | **Intramuscular internervous plane** — **lateral brachialis (radial) / medial brachialis (musculocutaneous)** in \~80% |
| **Anterolateral** (extends deltopectoral) | Proximal–middle ⅓ | Radial nerve **between brachialis + brachioradialis** distally; cephalic vein, anterior circumflex |
| **Posterior triceps-split** | Distal–middle ⅓ | **55%** exposed (no nerve mobilisation), **76%** with; no internervous plane; avoids ulnar nerve dissection |
| **Paratricipital (triceps-sparing)** / **medial reflection of lateral + medial heads** | Distal | **\~94% exposure (greatest)**; lateral window → radial nerve; medial window → mobilise ulnar nerve |
| **Lateral** | Distal ⅔ | **Highest iatrogenic radial palsy (20%)** |
| Medial | Brachial artery, median + ulnar nerves | Rarely for fractures |

**Iatrogenic radial palsy** — **lateral 20%** > **posterior 11%**; only **approach** predicts risk

**All posterior exposures** — limited proximally by **axillary nerve**

![Anterolateral plating of the humeral shaft](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1016-al-fixation.jpg)

### IMN technique & interlock nerve risk

**Antegrade** — supine; split deltoid (axillary nerve); entry **through supraspinatus as medial as possible** at apex, near **musculotendinous junction** (avascular insertion), **≤ 1 cm**

**Retrograde** — prone / lateral; posterior supracondylar cortex

**Don't ream across the fracture** — radial nerve

| Interlock | Nerve at risk |
|---|---|
| **Distal anterior → posterior** | **Musculocutaneous → lateral antebrachial cutaneous** (numb radial volar forearm) |
| **Distal lateral → medial** | **Radial** (over-penetration → **ulnar**) |
| Anteromedial → posterolateral oblique | Median |
| **Proximal (antegrade)** | **Axillary** + circumflex vessels |

### External fixation pin safe zones

**Distal pins** — **no safe zone** → **mini-open** to bone (radial nerve)\
✗ Wrong options: percutaneous arc · direct posterior (triceps, supine patient)

**Proximal pins** — anterolateral, mini-open (**axillary nerve**, biceps tendon); **most distal pin just above olecranon fossa**

## 6 Complications

### Radial nerve palsy — natural history, recovery order, EMG timing, exploration & tendon transfer

**Incidence** — **12.3% (8–15%)** (Orthobullets) · **11–11.8%** (OB); **distal ⅓ 22%**; **10–20% secondary** (develop during treatment, TO)

**Fracture type** — **transverse 21.2% > spiral 19.8%** > oblique 8.4% > comminuted 6.8% · level: distal > middle > proximal

**Injury type** — **closed → neurapraxia** · **low-velocity GSW → axonotmesis** · **open (high energy) → neurotmesis** (laceration) · complete palsy 50–68% (TO)

**Spontaneous recovery** — **\~70% by 7 weeks** (OB, TO > 70%) · **77.2%** (Orthobullets) · **85–90% within 3 months** · full at **\~6 months** · 100% of low-energy by 4 months (TO) · **88.6% overall** · iatrogenic 93.9%

**At exploration** — **in continuity 62.7%** · lacerated 26.8% · incarcerated 10.5% · transected only **12%** (Pollock, TO)

**Recovery order (proximal → distal)** — **brachioradialis (radial styloid) → ECRL (2nd MC base)** → supinator → ECRB (3rd MC) → EDC → ECU → EDQ → APL → **EPL** → EPB → **EIP (last)**

**First to recover crossing the wrist** — **ECRL** (inserts **base of 2nd MC**) → **wrist extension in radial deviation** first

**Last** — **EIP → index MCP hyperextension**

| Situation | Management |
|---|---|
| **Closed, primary palsy** | **Brace + observe** (✗ Wrong options: explore · IMN + explore) |
| **Low-velocity GSW + palsy** | **Splint + observe**, short antibiotics |
| **Open fracture + palsy** | **I&D, ORIF + explore nerve** |
| **High-energy open + brachial artery** | **Neurotmesis** likely |
| **Secondary palsy after closed reduction** | **Early exploration** (controversial) |
| Holstein-Lewis | **Controversial** ORIF + explore |

**Early exploration (TO)** — **open**, vascular injury, **secondary palsy after reduction**, **high-velocity GSW**, sharp / penetrating, severe soft tissue

**Complete RNP after shaft fracture (TO)** — **hanging cast + explore if no recovery at 3 months**\
✗ Wrong options: explore + plate · brace + explore at 3 wk · nail + observe

| EMG / NCS timing | Source |
|---|---|
| **\~2 months** (baseline) | Orthobullets |
| **1–2 months** → repeat \~4 months; **no exploration between 1–4 months** | OB |
| 3–4 / 3–5 months | OB |
| No recovery on EMG at **6 wk → observe**, repeat at 12 wk | OB |

**EMG interpretation** — **fasciculations / reinnervation = recovery → observe** · **fibrillations = denervation → explore**

**Exploration timing** — **4–6 months** (Orthobullets, OB) · **3–4 months** if fracture healed (TO) · **3–6 months**, before 6 (TO) · **6 months** (OB) · **no advantage waiting > 7 months** (TO) — read the options

**Healed at 4 months, no recovery** — **EMG / NCS**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · explore immediately · MRI · CT

**Pre-op palsy, nerve protected at ORIF, 2 months** — **NCS / EMG**\
✗ Wrong options: tendon transfer · re-explore

**Exploration recovery** — **early (< 3 wk) \~90%** · **late (≥ 8 wk) \~68%**

**Tendon transfers (≥ 1 year)** — **wrist: PT → ECRB** · **fingers: FCR (or FCU) → EDC** · **thumb: PL → EPL** (FCU loses ulnar deviation power)

**Abbreviations** — PT pronator teres · ECRL / ECRB extensor carpi radialis longus / brevis · EIP extensor indicis proprius · EMG electromyography · NCS nerve conduction studies

### Predicting nonunion during bracing

**Motion + no callus at 6 weeks** — **PPV 90–100%**, **specificity 99–100%**, sensitivity 82%, NPV 97%

**Too early** — **4 weeks without callus → continue brace, review in 4 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: LIPUS · ORIF · IMN

**6 wk persistent motion (proximal ⅓)** — **surgery now**

**Gap predicts mobility** — **each mm of gap ↑ mobility risk 40%**; **gap, smoking, female sex** → **8 mm gap at 8 wk → ORIF plate**\
✗ Wrong options: stimulator · pinning

**RUSHU** — score per cortex at 6 wk (1 absent · 2 non-bridging · 3 bridging); **≥ 8 → 86% NPV** · < 8 → 65% PPV

**Nonunion risk by pattern (nonop)** — **OTA A (15–29%) > B (4%) > C (0%)**

**Nonunion treatment** — **plate + autograft > exchange nailing** · atrophic: debride + compression plate + graft (ICBG, **RIA**) or exchange nail (**ream ≥ 1 mm**) · hypertrophic: exchange nail / locked plate

**Malunion** — varus common, rarely matters; **transverse** patterns

## X vs Y

| | **Anterior approach** | **Triceps-split** | **Paratricipital** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Plane | **Brachialis split: radial / musculocutaneous (internervous)** | **No internervous plane** (all radial) | Not intramuscular |
| Exposure | Middle ⅓ | **55%** | **94%** |

| | **Neurapraxia** | **Axonotmesis** | **Neurotmesis** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Typical cause | **Closed fracture** | **Low-velocity GSW** | **Open high-energy** |
| Recovery | Spontaneous | Regenerates (Wallerian) | **Needs repair** |

## How it's asked

**"Maximum acceptable deformity (TO)?"** → **shortening < 3 cm, rotation < 30°, angulation < 20°**

**"Acceptable — EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **angulation < 30°**

**"NOT a contraindication to bracing?"** → **closed mid-shaft fracture with radial nerve palsy**

**"9 days, normal exam, acceptable?"** → **functional brace 6–8 wk**

**"Contraindication to functional brace?"** → **brachial plexus injury**

**"Closed fracture, normal neuro?"** → **functional bracing**

**"18-month-old humeral shaft?"** → **coaptation splint + bandage to thorax**

**"Thin 23-yr-old, transverse?"** → **coaptation splint → fracture brace**\
✗ Wrong options: hanging cast

**"Arm wrestling + wrist drop?"** → **splint → functional brace**

**"4 weeks, no callus, in brace?"** → **continue brace, review 4 wk**

**"25° varus persists — effect?"** → **no effect on function**

**"15° coronal, 18° sagittal, isolated?"** → **functional brace**

**"Distal shaft in Sarmiento — likely deformity?"** → **varus**

**"NOT an operative indication (TO)?"** → **closed fracture with radial nerve palsy**

**"Strongest surgical indication?"** → **ipsilateral both-bone forearm fracture**

**"Weight bearing: plateau + humerus ORIF + femur IMN?"** → **NWB plateau leg, WBAT arm + other leg**

**"DCO with multiple GSWs?"** → **closed reduction + splint in ED**

**"Absolute indication?"** → **floating elbow**

**"Flaccid limb, C5–T1 avulsion?"** → **plate fixation**

**"Polytrauma humerus + femur?"** → **plate humerus, IMN femur**

**"Floating elbow?"** → **ORIF both + early motion**

**"6 weeks persistent motion?"** → **surgery now**

**"Humerus with tibia nail + ankle — plan?"** → **plate + full WB**

**"Teen quarterback wants early return?"** → **surgical fixation**

**"Plate vs nail — plate has?"** → **lower shoulder pain**

**"IMN vs plate — EXCEPT?"** → **increased shoulder ROM**

**"IMN vs ORIF — accurate?"** → **higher total complication rate**

**"Antegrade nail — true?"** → **higher shoulder impingement**

**"IMN vs ORIF at 1 year?"** → **equal functional shoulder outcomes**

**"Contemporary IMN vs ORIF?"** → **IMN lower infection**

**"Complete RNP — plan (TO)?"** → **hanging cast, explore if no recovery at 3 months**

**"4 months, healed, palsy unchanged?"** → **EMG + NCS**

**"Last to recover?"** → **index MCP hyperextension (EIP)**

**"Loss of ECRL→EPL, open fracture + brachial artery?"** → **radial nerve neurotmesis**

**"Low-velocity GSW + palsy — true?"** → **splint + observe**

**"6 weeks, EMG no recovery?"** → **observation**

**"Open comminuted fracture + palsy?"** → **ORIF + radial nerve exploration**

**"First muscle to recover — insertion?"** → **radial styloid (brachioradialis)**

**"Last to return (figure)?"** → **EIP**

**"Highest radial nerve palsy risk pattern?"** → **distal ⅓ spiral (Holstein-Lewis)**

**"Palsy persists 2 months after ORIF?"** → **NCS / EMG**

**"12 months, no wrist extension?"** → **PT → ECRB**

**"First to recover after post-op palsy?"** → **brachioradialis / ECRL**

**"A-OK intact, wrist drop, dorsal numbness?"** → **Holstein-Lewis fracture**

**"Recovery by 7 weeks?"** → **70%**

**"First muscle crossing the wrist to recover?"** → **inserts on base of 2nd MC (ECRL)**

**"Distal A-P interlock risks?"** → **musculocutaneous nerve**

**"Radial nerve enters anterior compartment?"** → **10 cm proximal to radiocapitellar joint**

**"Posterior antebrachial cutaneous traces to?"** → **radial nerve**

**"Greatest exposure?"** → **posterior with elevation / medial reflection of lateral + medial heads**

**"Posterior approach — nerve at risk?"** → **radial**

**"A-P distal interlock injury — symptom nerve?"** → **lateral antebrachial cutaneous**

**"Anterior approach deep interval?"** → **musculocutaneous + radial**

**"Intramuscular internervous approach?"** → **anterior (brachialis split)**

**"Gerwin modification — landmark?"** → **inferior lateral brachial cutaneous nerve**

**"Ex-fix distal pins?"** → **no safe zone — mini-open**

**"When does no callus + motion become concerning?"** → **6 weeks**

**"8 mm gap at 8 weeks?"** → **ORIF with plate**

**"Specificity of motion at 6 weeks?"** → **99%**

# Humeral Shaft Nonunion

*Trauma · 32 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — failure to heal by **6 months** (OB) · bone-stimulator criteria: **> 9 months**, or **no progress over 3 months**

**Primary nonunion** — **nonop 2–33%** (Orthobullets, OB) · 2–10% (OB) · **surgery 5–10%** · \~10% either (OB)

**Location** — **proximal ⅓** highest (pectoralis major + deltoid deforming forces; **biceps tendon interposition**)

| Risk factor | Type |
|---|---|
| **Vitamin D deficiency (commonest metabolic)**, osteoporosis | Biological |
| **Infection** (incl. **P. acnes**) | Biological |
| **Smoking**, obesity, malnutrition, **NSAIDs**, alcohol, noncompliance | Patient |
| **Open fracture**, segmental | Injury |
| **Unstable pattern**, inadequate fixation | Mechanical |
| **Shoulder / elbow stiffness** (motion → fracture site), GH arthritis | Mechanical |

**Not predictive** — radial nerve palsy, no callus at **3 weeks**, hand dominance

### Anatomy

**Nutrient artery** — **medial** aspect of mid–distal ⅓ diaphysis

![Nutrient artery of the humerus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12767-nutrient-artery-of-humerus.jpg)

### Genes & lab markers

**Metabolic work-up — vitamin D deficiency** — **most common** correctable abnormality → correct before surgery\
✗ Wrong options: hypogonadism · thyroid · PTH · calcium · iron · magnesium

| Abnormality (nonunions, OB) | Attributed share |
|---|---|
| Calcium deficiency | 35% |
| Thyroid disorder | 24% |
| Central hypogonadism | 15% |
| Parathyroid disorder | 11% |

**Labs** — **CRP, ESR, CBC** (infection) · **total protein, albumin** · **vitamin D, TSH, PTH**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain with use; **gross motion** at fracture

**Exam** — brace fit, skin, atrophy, angulation; **radial nerve function**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| AP + lateral humerus, shoulder, elbow | No consolidation, **hypertrophic callus**, **pseudarthrosis**, broken hardware |
| **CT** | **Extent of bridging**, planning; **not needed if X-ray already diagnostic** |

### Predictors of nonunion

**Most predictive** — **persistent fracture mobility at 6 weeks** (extremely sensitive + specific)

### Infection work-up

**Before any revision** — **ESR + CRP (labs)** · **3–5 cultures** for atrophic · septic → **two-stage**\
✗ Wrong options: CT · brace · revision without infection work-up

**Septic nonunion with bone loss** — resect + **antibiotic spacer**

**Types** — **hypertrophic, oligotrophic, atrophic** (atrophic ↑ with bone loss, open fracture, infection)

## 5 Management

```
Humeral shaft nonunion
├─ Rule out infection (ESR, CRP) + metabolic (vitamin D)
├─ Symptomatic → compression plate (4.5 mm) ± autograft (gold standard)
│      ├─ after nail → remove nail + compression plate + graft
│      └─ very proximal / distal, poor bone → dual plating
└─ Low demand, high risk, asymptomatic → brace ± stimulator
```

### Treatment options

| Option | Indication | Fact |
|---|---|---|
| Brace ± **bone stimulator** | **Low demand, high risk, asymptomatic** | Stimulator not if **pseudarthrosis, gap > 5 mm, poor blood supply** |
| **Compression plating + bone graft** | **Symptomatic nonunion — gold standard** | **Superior to IMN**; \~100% union; **ICBG donor morbidity 44%** |
| **Dual plating** (orthogonal) | **Very proximal / distal**, poor metaphyseal bone, micromotion after one plate | **92–100% union at 16 wk** |
| **Cortical strut allo- / autograft** (intramedullary or medial + lateral plate) | **Severe osteopenia**, bone loss, recalcitrant | **95–100%** union |
| **Free fibula** | **Recalcitrant atrophic** | — |
| Induced membrane / fibula / cancellous allograft | **Bone loss** | — |
| BMP | **Limited role** — no proven benefit | — |

**Prognosis** — **83–100%** union after surgery · plating ± graft or IMN + graft **> 85%**

### Compression plating with bone graft (after nail or brace)

**Technique** — anterior / anterolateral / **posterior (radial nerve neurolysis)**; debride fibrous tissue + bone ends; **maximal cortical contact**; **4.5 mm compression plate**

**Graft** — **ICBG autograft if atrophic** · DBM, **RIA**, local callus · **graft in hypertrophic — controversial**

**After failed IMN** — **nail removal + plate + graft**: **9/9 union vs exchange nailing 4/10 (McKee)**\
✗ Wrong options: exchange nailing · dynamisation · MUA · nail removal + brace

**Atrophic after nail, smoker** — **nail removal + compression plate + bone graft**\
✗ Wrong options: augmentative plate (**hypertrophic only**) · plate without graft · smoking cessation alone

**After failed ORIF (screw pull-out), normal ESR / CRP** — **compression plating + autograft**\
✗ Wrong options: spacer · ex-fix · brace · retain implants

**Delayed union in brace at 16 wk (atrophic)** — **ORIF + autograft**\
✗ Wrong options: stimulator + sling · IMN (nonunion + cuff damage) · locked plate + allograft

**Transverse, 6 months, gross motion** — **ORIF + autologous graft**\
✗ Wrong options: ultrasound · bisphosphonates · nails

## 6 Complications

### Postoperative nerve deficit with nonunion

**Commonest** — **radial nerve** palsy after nonunion surgery — **iatrogenic 7–19%**

| Procedure / pathology (OB) | Typical nerve palsy |
|---|---|
| **Humeral nonunion fixation** | **Radial** |
| Reverse TSA | Axillary |
| Hook of hamate fracture | Ulnar |
| Medial-entry supracondylar pins | Ulnar |
| Lunate dislocation | Median (acute CTS) |

## X vs Y

| | **Hypertrophic** | **Atrophic** |
|---|---|---|
| Problem | **Stability** | **Biology** |
| Treatment | Compression plate (± augment plate); graft controversial | **Compression plate + ICBG**; resect necrotic bone ± shorten |

| | **Plate + graft** | **Exchange nail** |
|---|---|---|
| Union after failed nail | **9/9** | 4/10 |
| Preferred | **Yes** | No (cuff damage) |

## How it's asked

**"Painful nonunion 13 months after IMN?"** → **nail removal + plate fixation**

**"Hypertrophic nonunion after nail, 9 months?"** → **nail removal, ORIF + bone graft**

**"Transverse, 6 months, gross motion?"** → **ORIF + autologous bone graft**

**"16 weeks in brace, gross motion?"** → **open plating + autograft**

**"Atrophic after nail, smoker?"** → **nail removal + compression plate + bone graft**

**"Nonunion after ORIF, normal markers?"** → **compression plating + autograft**

**"Metabolic cause of nonunion?"** → **vitamin D deficiency**

**"Broken screw 8 months after nonunion surgery — next?"** → **laboratory evaluation (ESR, CRP)**

**"Most predictive of nonunion?"** → **persistent mobility at 6 weeks**

**"Nerve palsy after humeral nonunion surgery?"** → **radial nerve**

# Intertrochanteric Fractures

*Trauma · 33 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Share** — **\~50% of hip fractures** · 150,000 / yr (US) · F 500 vs M 200 / 100,000 / yr

**Who** — average **\~80 yr** (older than neck fractures) · **F:M 2:1 to 8:1**

**Risk factors** — osteoporosis, age, comorbidities, ADL dependency, recurrent falls (dementia, Parkinson's, vision, drugs)

### Osteoporosis — sentinel fractures & bisphosphonates

**Sentinel fracture** — **proximal humerus fracture → ↑ hip fracture risk (for 1 yr)**\
✗ Wrong options: sacral · radius · fibula · distal humerus

**Early bisphosphonate after fragility hip fracture** — **greatest BMD gain in first year**; **no ↑ nonunion, delayed union or peri-implant fracture**; oral ≈ IV

### Anatomy

**Neck-shaft** — **130 ± 7°** · anteversion **10 ± 7°**

**Calcar femorale** — dense vertical wall from **posteromedial shaft → posterior neck**; its involvement decides stability

**Femoral radius of curvature** — **114–120 cm**; **smaller (more bowed)** in **elderly, Asian, short stature**

| Fragment | Deformity | Muscles |
|---|---|---|
| **Proximal** | **Flexion** | **Iliopsoas**, sartorius, rectus, pectineus |
| **Proximal** | **External rotation** | Short external rotators |
| **Proximal** | **Abduction** | **Gluteus medius + minimus**, maximus, TFL |
| **Distal** | **Adduction + shortening → varus** | Adductors, gracilis |

**Blood supply** — rich (**trochanteric anastomosis**: MFCA + LFCA ascending branches, superior + inferior gluteal) → **nonunion < 2%**

**Trabeculae** — **Ward's triangle** = lowest BMD, bounded by primary compressive, principal tensile, secondary compressive trabeculae

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — **shortened, externally rotated**, GT tenderness, pain on log-roll, **no straight-leg raise**

**History** — pre-injury function (predicts outcome), antecedent pain (OA / pathological), **anticoagulants**, ASA

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use / fact |
|---|---|
| AP pelvis, AP hip, **cross-table lateral**, **full-length femur** | Pattern; **posterior comminution** (lateral); subtroch extension, lesions, nail length, **bow**, canal |
| **Traction internal-rotation view** | **Better classification**, assess reduction |
| **MRI** | **Occult fracture (best; T1 100% sensitive, T2 84%)** · **isolated GT fracture → MRI for IT extension** (✗ Wrong options: CT · traction view · fix first) |
| CT (1–2 mm) | **Second line** if no MRI: sens 86%, spec 98% |
| Bone scan | Rare; **false negative up to 72 h**; sens up to 98% |

**MRI findings** — marrow oedema on **STIR / fat-suppressed T2**; low-signal **T1 line**

### Stability classification

| | **Stable** | **Unstable** |
|---|---|---|
| Definition | **Intact posteromedial cortex** → resists medial compression | Collapses into **varus** / shaft **medialises** |
| Examples | 2-part; small LT fragment without medial comminution | **Large / comminuted posteromedial (LT) fragment** · **reverse obliquity (transtrochanteric)** · **subtrochanteric extension** · **lateral wall < 20.5 mm** |

**Lateral wall thickness** — measured **3 cm below innominate tubercle at 135°** to fracture line; **< 20.5 mm → postop lateral wall fracture → CMN, not SHS**; 25 mm = **not** a risk

| AO / OTA 31A | Description |
|---|---|
| **A1** | Simple 2-part pertrochanteric, **intact lateral cortex** |
| **A2** | Separate **posteromedial fragment**, **intact lateral cortex** |
| **A3** | Through **lateral + medial cortex** (reverse oblique / transverse) |

![AO/OTA 31A classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1038-ao-ota-31a-classification.jpg)

| Evans | Description |
|---|---|
| Stable | Posteromedial cortex intact / minimal comminution |
| Unstable | Greater posteromedial comminution — **convertible to stable with medial cortical opposition** |
| **Reverse obliquity** | Unstable — **adductors medialise shaft** |

![Reverse obliquity intertrochanteric fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1038-ap-x-ray-demonstrating-a-reverse-oblique-fracture-pattern.jpg)

### Reduction quality — cortical support

| Medial cortical support (TO) | Proximal fragment |
|---|---|
| **Positive — most acceptable** | **Medial** to shaft cortex |
| Neutral | Anatomical |
| Negative | **Lateral** — collapses |

## 5 Management

```
Intertrochanteric fracture
├─ Stable (intact posteromedial + lateral wall ≥ 20.5 mm) → SHS or CMN (equal outcomes)
├─ Unstable / reverse obliquity / subtroch extension / thin lateral wall → CMN
│      └─ subtroch extension ≤ 3–4 cm → short CMN · more distal → long CMN
└─ Comminuted + severe OA / bone won't hold → calcar-replacing arthroplasty
```

### Treatment options

| Option | Indication | Fact |
|---|---|---|
| Nonop (NWB, bed → chair) | **Non-ambulatory**, too high risk, skin breakdown, incomplete fracture | **1-yr mortality 84.4%**; pneumonia, UTI, sores, DVT |
| **SHS (most common ORIF)** | **Stable** | **Cheapest**, **spares abductors**, dynamic compression; **2-hole side plate = 4-hole** |
| **CMN** | **Stable or unstable** — **AAOS strong for unstable, reverse oblique, subtroch extension** | Load-sharing, **shorter lever arm**, **intramedullary buttress limits medialisation**, ↓ / = blood loss; **violates abductors**; dearer; **↑ use over last decade** |
| Proximal femoral locking plate | Young + unstable (rare) | Surgeon-dependent; **no early WB** |
| 95° blade plate / DCS | Rare | Acceptable for reverse oblique |
| **Arthroplasty (long-stem, calcar-replacing)** | Salvage, severe comminution, **pre-existing OA**, very osteoporotic | Fix GT; more blood loss, time, cost |

### Stable vs unstable patterns — implant choice (SHS vs cephalomedullary nail, reverse obliquity)

**Stable** — **SHS = CMN outcomes**; **reduction quality > implant choice**

**Reverse obliquity** — **NOT SHS (56% failure)** → shear, **shaft medialisation + cut-out** → **CMN** (trochanteric or piriformis), **blade plate, DCS, locking plate, ex-fix** all acceptable

**SHS advantages over CMN** — **none** for DVT, blood loss, nonunion, biomechanics\
✗ Wrong options: all

**CMN preferred because** — **stability for the fracture pattern**\
✗ Wrong options: shorter LOS · less abductor damage · easier THA conversion · cheaper

**Most cost-effective (stable)** — **SHS**\
✗ Wrong options: cannulated screws · hemi

**Spare abductors** — **SHS**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral position · lag screw vs blade · omitting distal lock

**Short vs long CMN** — equal function, reoperation, peri-implant fracture, cut-out, mortality · **long: ↑ OR time, ↑ blood loss, ↑ postop anaemia + transfusion**, radiation, bow mismatch · **short tolerates 3–4 cm subtroch extension**

**Distal locking screw** — **protective against peri-implant fracture** (omitting ↑ refracture)

**SHS contraindicated** — reverse oblique, **lateral wall comminution / < 20.5 mm**, **subtrochanteric fractures**

### Tip-apex distance & cut-out

**TAD** — **sum of tip → apex distances on AP + lateral**, corrected for magnification

**Goal** — **TAD < 25 mm** · **> 45 mm → 60% failure** · 27 / 32 / 40 mm → ↑ **cut-out**

**Strongest predictor of cut-out** — **TAD**\
✗ Wrong options: age · stability · reduction · plate angle

**Lag screw position** — **centre–centre (TO)**; **low-central AP + central lateral** acceptable; **never peripheral / posterior** (head rotates → superior cut-out)\
✗ Wrong options: posterior on lateral

**Lag screw vs helical blade** — controversial · blade compacts bone, ↑ cut-out resistance (biomechanical), but **much higher "cut-through"**

![Tip-apex distance](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1038-tad-illustration.jpg)

| Failure | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Cut-out** | **Anterocranial** perforation **with varus collapse** |
| **Cut-through** | **Central** perforation **without collapse** — **helical blade** |

### Lateral wall integrity & sliding hip screw failure

**Intra-op lateral wall fracture** — **↑ collapse / sliding**\
✗ Wrong options: nail length · WB status

**Predictor of postop lateral wall fracture with SHS** — **lateral wall thickness**\
✗ Wrong options: reverse obliquity · T-score · calcar comminution

**Highest SHS failure** — **wall 18.5 mm + TAD 28.5 mm**

**DHS failure — EXCEPT** — **2-hole side plate**\
✗ Wrong options: reverse oblique · TAD 27 / 32 · lateral wall comminution

**Left-sided unstable fracture + DHS** — **anterior spike** (screw torque) → **derotation wire** first

**SHS problems in unstable fractures** — excessive collapse, **limb shortening**, shaft medialisation

### Anterior cortical perforation & nail technique

**Cause** — **radius-of-curvature mismatch (nail straighter / larger radius than bowed femur)** + **posterior start on GT**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior start · large implant · lateral position

**Prevent** — lateral view of distal femur while seating · avoid posterior start

**Too-lateral start (> 3 mm)** — **varus** → collapse + cut-out

**Guidewire stuck in nail (no fragment)** — **remove nail, check guidewire bend**\
✗ Wrong options: insert lag wire · re-ream · smaller nail · tighten jig

**PFN-A2 vs PFN-A (TO)** — **all**: **less lateral cortex pressure (flat lateral surface)**, **smaller proximal diameter (16.5 vs 17.0 mm)**, **smaller blade**; higher bend point, **5° ML bend** — for **Asian femora**

**PFN types (TO)** — conventional PFN (**2 screws**: lag + anti-rotation) → **Z / reverse Z** · **PFN-A** (single **helical blade**, unreamed, 2003)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Cut-out / implant failure** | **Commonest after CMN**; **4–20%**, within **4 months** (Orthobullets) · 3 months (OB) |
| Nonunion / malunion | **< 2%**; diagnosis: pain + lucency **4–7 months**; rule out infection |
| Peri-implant fracture | **1–3% at 1 yr**; short = long; short → **at nail tip**, long → around rod |
| Anterior perforation | Long CMN, bow mismatch, posterior start |
| **Anaemia** | **> 30% transfused**; threshold **≤ 8 g/dL** if asymptomatic; **TXA (strong)** |

### Failed fixation — conversion to THA

**Z effect (TO)** — **lag screw migrates laterally**, varus collapse, **derotation screw perforates head**

**Reverse Z effect (TO)** — **derotation screw migrates laterally + lag screw migrates medially** (TO case answer)

**Cause** — unstable calcar, **deficient lateral buttress**, no medial support; head vs neck compressive-strength mismatch

| Failure | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Elderly / joint damaged by cut-out** | **THA** (✗ Wrong options: NWB · valgus osteotomy · revision ORIF · resection) |
| **Malunion + hardware into acetabulum** | **THA with calcar-replacing stem bypassing hardware** (✗ Wrong options: bipolar (acetabular erosion)) |
| **Active 72-yr, cut-out + acetabular erosion** | **Conversion THA** (✗ Wrong options: exchange CMN · osteotomy · proximal femoral replacement · hemi) |
| **Young / intact head** | **Valgus osteotomy ± graft** / revision ORIF |

### Mortality, surgical timing & perioperative care

**1-yr mortality** — **15–30%** (Orthobullets) · 20–30% (OB) · \~10% at 1 mo, 20% at 4 mo, **30% at 1 yr**; **nonop 84.4%**

**↑ Mortality** — **male (25–30% vs 20%)**, **IT > neck fracture**, **age > 85**, **≥ 2 comorbidities**, **ASA III–IV**, delay **> 48 h** (> 2 days, Orthobullets · ≥ 3 days, OB)

**Recognised predictor** — **ASA class**\
✗ Wrong options: WB status · comminution · device · anaesthetic

**Not associated with ↑ mortality** — **surgery within 48 h**, **spinal vs general (REGAIN)**, nail length, interdisciplinary care (↓)

**Delay > 48 h** — **↑ 30-day mortality**, **↑ pneumonia**\
✗ Wrong options: OR time · transfusion · infection

**Timing** — **24–48 h** (AAOS moderate) · **co-management** (AAOS strong)

**Dementia patient** — **geriatric co-management, avoid centrally-acting drugs (meperidine)**, rapid optimisation, **surgery < 48 h**\
✗ Wrong options: neurology consult · routine echo · nephrology for normal CrCl (low muscle mass masks renal impairment)

**Routine echo** — **delays surgery** without changing outcome (unless valve disease, arrhythmia, worsening CHF, CAD)\
✗ Wrong options: hospitalist · fascia iliaca block · Foley · fluids cause delay

**DAPT (aspirin + clopidogrel)** — **continue, proceed when optimised**\
✗ Wrong options: vitamin K (warfarin) · idarucizumab (dabigatran) · FFP · stop + wait 48 h

**Function at 1 yr** — **41%** regain status · 40% more aids · 12% household · 8% non-functional · **rule of thirds**: ⅓ regain, ⅓ lose a level, ⅓ die

**Post-op** — **WBAT** ↓ complications

## X vs Y

| | **SHS** | **CMN** |
|---|---|---|
| Stable fractures | **Equal** | **Equal** |
| Unstable / reverse oblique / subtroch | No | **Yes** |
| Cost | **Lower** | Higher |
| Abductors | **Spared** | Violated |
| Lever arm | Longer (extramedullary) | **Shorter** |
| THA conversion | **Easier** | Harder |

| | **Short CMN** | **Long CMN** |
|---|---|---|
| Blood loss / transfusion | Lower | **Higher (anaemia)** |
| Peri-implant fracture | Equal (at tip) | Equal (around rod) |
| Anterior perforation | — | **Risk** |

## How it's asked

**"NOT appropriate for reverse obliquity?"** → **sliding hip screw**

**"55-yr MVA, reverse obliquity?"** → **cephalomedullary nail**

**"Adequate fixation EXCEPT (reverse oblique)?"** → **dynamic hip screw**

**"IMN vs SHS — true?"** → **IMN use has increased in last 10 years**

**"SHS advantage over CMN?"** → **none of the above**

**"Most cost-effective implant?"** → **sliding hip screw**

**"Stable fracture — true?"** → **no difference between SHS and IMN**

**"Why CMN over SHS?"** → **improved stability for fracture pattern**

**"SHS appropriate for?"** → **stable IT + femoral neck stress fracture**

**"2 cm subtroch extension, cardiac patient?"** → **short CMN**

**"Reduce abductor injury?"** → **sliding hip screw**

**"Long vs short nail?"** → **↑ postoperative anaemia**

**"Optimal lag screw position (TO)?"** → **central AP + central lateral**

**"Strongest predictor of cut-out?"** → **tip-apex distance**

**"TAD 40 mm, superior screw?"** → **cut-out**

**"How is TAD measured?"** → **sum of tip–apex on AP + lateral**

**"Commonest CMN complication?"** → **implant cut-out**

**"Failure commoner with helical blade?"** → **central perforation without collapse (cut-through)**

**"↑ collapse / sliding?"** → **intra-op lateral wall fracture**

**"Minimum side-plate holes (stable 2-part)?"** → **two**

**"Left unstable fracture + DHS deformity?"** → **anterior spike**

**"DHS failure — EXCEPT?"** → **2-hole side plate**

**"Predictor of postop lateral wall fracture?"** → **lateral wall thickness**

**"Lateral wall 25 mm risk?"** → **none of the above**

**"Highest SHS failure combination?"** → **wall 18.5 mm + TAD 28.5 mm**

**"SHS inadvisable if?"** → **lateral wall 20 mm**

**"PFN-A2 vs PFN-A (TO)?"** → **all of the above**

**"Anterior perforation from antegrade nailing?"** → **radius-of-curvature mismatch**

**"Anterior perforation risk factors?"** → **mismatch + posterior start**

**"Guidewire won't come out?"** → **remove nail, assess wire bend**

**"Nail radius > femur?"** → **anterior cortical perforation**

**"PFN failure — screws migrating opposite (TO)?"** → **reverse Z effect**

**"Failed SHS, 82-yr?"** → **THA**

**"Malunion + hardware into acetabulum?"** → **THA with calcar replacement bypassing hardware**

**"Cut-out + acetabular erosion, active 72-yr?"** → **conversion THA**

**"Predictor of mortality?"** → **ASA classification**

**"↑ 1-yr mortality EXCEPT?"** → **fixation within 48 hours**

**"Dementia + IT fracture — plan?"** → **geriatric co-management, avoid central drugs, surgery < 48 h**

**"Delay > 48 h causes?"** → **↑ 30-day mortality**

**"Aspirin + clopidogrel?"** → **no change, proceed when optimised**

**"What delays surgery?"** → **pre-op echocardiogram**

**"Negatively affects mortality?"** → **delay beyond 48 hours**

**"Isolated GT fracture — next?"** → **MRI**

**"Fracture most increasing later fracture risk?"** → **hip fracture**

**"Early bisphosphonate — true?"** → **greatest BMD gain within first year**

**"Most acceptable cortical support reduction (TO)?"** → **positive**

# Isolated Ulnar Shaft Fracture

*Trauma · 34 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rare** — **< 1% of upper-limb fractures** · men 10–20 yr, women > 60 yr

### Associated conditions

**Elbow / DRUJ** — **Monteggia**, **Essex-Lopresti** → image wrist + elbow

**Compartment syndrome** — up to **15%**

### Atypical (bisphosphonate-associated) fracture

**Pattern** — **isolated transverse proximal ⅓ ulna**, **thickened posterior cortex**, minimal comminution, prodromal ache → **alendronate**\
✗ Wrong options: teriparatide · semaglutide · levothyroxine · methotrexate

**Location** — **20–45% of ulnar length from olecranon tip** · ASBMR major / minor criteria (as for atypical subtrochanteric)

**Outcome** — **delayed / nonunion \~50%** → resect diseased bone + plate

### Anatomy

**Forearm axis** — **radial head (proximal) → ulnar fovea (distal)**; radius rotates around ulna; ulnar malangulation → ↓ rotation

**Interosseous membrane** — **5 ligaments**; **central band = key part to reconstruct** · accessory band, distal oblique bundle, proximal oblique cord, dorsal oblique accessory cord

![Interosseous membrane](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422969-interosseous-membrane.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Direct blow ("nightstick")** · indirect: MVA, fall from height (axial load)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — deformity, open wounds, **tense compartments**, **pain on passive finger stretch**; radial + ulnar pulses; median, radial, ulnar nerves

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP + lateral forearm** ± obliques + **ipsilateral wrist + elbow**

**Radial head aligned with capitellum on every view** — excludes Monteggia

| OTA (ulna shaft) | Subtypes |
|---|---|
| **A — simple** | A1 spiral · A2 oblique · A3 transverse |
| **B — wedge** | B2 intact · B3 fragmentary |
| **C — multifragmentary** | C2 intact segmental · C3 fragmentary |

![OTA type A ulnar shaft fractures](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422969-type-a.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Isolated ulnar shaft fracture
├─ Distal ⅔, < 50% displacement + < 10° angulation → brace / Muenster cast / soft dressing
├─ Displaced distal ⅔ or any proximal ⅓ → ORIF (3.5 mm DCP, 6 cortices each side)
├─ Gustilo I–IIIA open → primary ORIF · IIIB → ex-fix
└─ Bone loss / nonunion → autograft, vascularised fibula, Masquelet
```

### Operative criteria

| Nonoperative | Operative |
|---|---|
| **Distal ⅔** (nightstick) | **Displaced distal ⅔** |
| **< 50% displacement** | **Proximal ⅓** |
| **< 10° angulation** | **Open** (I–IIIA primary ORIF; IIIB ex-fix) |
| e.g. **20% translation, < 5° angulation** | **Any adult radial shaft or both-bone fracture** (even undisplaced) |

### Nonoperative management — outcomes & acceptable displacement

**Minimally displaced** — **equivalent clinical outcomes** to surgery; **slower union**; union **> 92%**\
✗ Wrong options: long-arm cast needed · 20° rotation loss · wrist / elbow stiffness

**> 10° angulation or > 50% displacement** — **loss of forearm rotation** if treated nonop

**Options** — **functional brace**, **Muenster cast** (interosseous mould), **soft compression + immediate motion** — all similar; **above- vs below-elbow = no difference**; review at \~1 wk; ≥ 6 wk

![Muenster cast](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422969-muenster-cast.jpg)

### Operative technique

**ORIF** — **subcutaneous approach**; **3.5 mm DCP**; compression mode; **6 cortices** above + below; locking in osteoporosis; bridge if comminuted; 2.0 / 2.7 lag screws\
✗ Wrong options: **4.5 mm — refracture after removal**

**Post-op** — **early ROM**; **NWB \~6 wk** (secondary displacement)

**Ex-fix** — **Gustilo IIIB**; distal pins in **2nd + 3rd MC**, **≤ 4 mm** pins

**IMN** — **poor soft tissue**; through **posterior olecranon**; union ≈ ORIF; **7% nonunion** forearm nailing (Street)

**Grafting** — open fracture with bone loss (delayed) / nonunion; **autograft is critical** (ICBG, proximal tibia, **RIA**); **vascularised fibula — large defects, ↓ infection**

**Masquelet (induced membrane)** — stage 1: I&D + **cement spacer** + temporary fixation → stage 2: graft into membrane at **4–6 wk**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| Infection | **3%** after ORIF; open fractures |
| **Compartment syndrome** | **Up to 15%**; crush, open, **low-velocity GSW**, vascular, **DIC** |
| **Nonunion** | **< 5%** compression plate · **up to 12%** bridge plating of comminution |
| Malunion | **Radial bow restoration ∝ function** |
| NV injury | **PIN with Monteggia**, type III open → **observe 3 months**, explore if no return |
| **Refracture** | **Up to 10%** with early removal; **remove ≥ 1 yr** (ulna, Orthobullets) · ≥ 18 months (Galeazzi note); 4.5 mm plates, comminution, lucency → **brace 6 wk + protect 3 months** after removal |

### Nonunion

| Type | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Atrophic** | **ORIF (3.5 mm plate) + autogenous cancellous graft** (✗ Wrong options: dynamic splint · graft alone · pinning · ultrasound) |
| Infected / atrophic with defect | **Masquelet** |
| Hypertrophic | **Debridement + compression plating** |

## X vs Y

| | **Isolated ulna (nightstick)** | **Radius shaft / both-bone (adult)** |
|---|---|---|
| Minimal displacement | **Nonop OK** | **ORIF always** |
| Risk if nonop | Little | Displacement, malunion, ↓ rotation |

| | **Nightstick** | **Atypical (bisphosphonate)** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | Distal ⅔ | **Proximal ⅓** |
| Pattern | Direct blow | **Transverse + thick posterior cortex**, prodrome |
| Union | > 92% | **\~50% delayed / nonunion** |

## How it's asked

**"Nonop vs operative — true?"** → **equivalent clinical outcomes**

**"Which forearm fracture can be nonop in adult?"** → **isolated mid-shaft ulna, 20% translation, < 5° angulation**

**"Atrophic nonunion after nonop at 1 year?"** → **ORIF + autogenous bone graft**

**"Proximal ⅓ transverse ulna + thick posterior cortex?"** → **alendronate**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Knee Dislocation

*Trauma · 35 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **0.02%** of orthopaedic injuries; under-reported — **\~50% self-reduce** before ED

**Who** — **M:F 4:1** · **morbid obesity → "ultra-low-energy" dislocation** with ADLs + ↑ neurovascular injury

**Mechanism** — high energy (MVC, **dashboard → axial load on flexed knee**, fall) · low energy (sport, walking) · **hyperextension → anterior** · **posterior force on proximal tibia → posterior**

**Associated** — **vascular**, **common peroneal nerve (\~25%)**, **fractures 60%**, patellar tendon rupture, avulsions, displaced menisci

### Anatomy

**Ligaments at risk** — ACL, PCL, MCL, LCL, PLC (bony stability minimal)

**Popliteal artery tethered** — **proximally at adductor hiatus** (fibrous tunnel) + **distally at soleus arch**

**Geniculate collaterals** — can give **palpable pulses despite complete popliteal occlusion**

### KD classification & cruciate injury pattern

| Kennedy (tibial direction) | Share | Key fact |
|---|---|---|
| **Anterior** | **30–50% — most common** | **Hyperextension**; vascular injury = **intimal tear (traction)** |
| **Posterior** | 30–40% — 2nd | **Highest vascular injury → complete popliteal artery tear** |
| Lateral | 13% | Varus / valgus |
| Medial | 3% | Varus / valgus |
| **Rotational (posterolateral)** | 4% | **Usually irreducible** (buttonholed MFC) |

| Schenck | Ligaments torn |
|---|---|
| **KD I** | Multiligament with **ACL or PCL** intact |
| **KD II** | **ACL + PCL** only |
| **KD III** | ACL + PCL + **PMC (IIIM)** or **PLC / LCL (IIIL)** — **IIIL → highest peroneal nerve risk** |
| **KD IV** | ACL + PCL + PMC + PLC — **highest vascular injury (5–15%)** |
| **KD V** | Multiligament + **periarticular fracture** |

**KD IV cruciate pattern (TO)** — **ACL midsubstance + PCL femoral avulsion** · ACL: midsubstance 45% > femoral 34% > tibial 21% · PCL: **femoral avulsion 76%** > midsubstance 17% > tibial 7%

![Buttonholing of the medial femoral condyle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1043-buttonholing-of-femoral-condyle.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Diagnosis** — **instability on exam** (X-ray may be normal); **recurvatum** in extension\
✗ Wrong options: procurvatum

**Dimple / pucker sign** — **MFC buttonholed through medial capsule** → **irreducible posterolateral** dislocation → **no repeated closed reduction (skin necrosis)**

**Exam before + after reduction** — DP + PT pulses vs other side; peroneal + tibial nerves; **serial exams mandatory**

### Clinical tests for combined injuries (dial, ERR, collaterals)

| Test (TO) | Positive means |
|---|---|
| **Dial test ↑ ER (> 10–15°) at 30° only** | **PLC** |
| **Dial test at 30° AND 90°** | **PLC + PCL** |
| **Anterior drawer with foot in EXTERNAL rotation** | **ACL + MCL** (anteromedial rotatory instability) |
| **External rotation recurvatum test** | **ACL + PLC** (recurvatum, varus, anterior tibial translation; sensitivity 7.5%) |
| **Varus / valgus stress at 30°** | **Isolated collateral (LCL / MCL)** — **best position**; > 10 mm + soft end-point = complete |
| Varus stress opens **in full extension** | **LCL + PLC + PCL** |
| Valgus stress opens **in full extension** | **MCL + POL / posteromedial capsule + PCL** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| Pre- + post-reduction AP / lateral | **Asymmetric joint space**, **Segond (lateral tibial avulsion)**, osteochondral defects |
| CT | Fractures on post-reduction films (eminence, tubercle, plateau) |
| **MRI** | **After reduction, before hardware** — ligaments, menisci, planning |

![Knee dislocation radiograph](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1043-knee-dislocation.jpg)

### Vascular injury — incidence, ABI, CT angiography & exploration

**Incidence** — **5–15% all** (Orthobullets) · 10–40% / 18–45% / \~16% / \~25% (OB) · **40–50% anterior or posterior** · **low-velocity anterior \~40%** (OB) — read the options

**ABI = ankle systolic ÷ brachial systolic** (normal 0.9–1.1) — **< 0.9 → concern** (e.g. **0.8**) · **< 0.85** (TO); ABI < 0.9: **sens 87%, spec 97%**; **> 0.9 → NPV 100%**

```
After reduction
├─ Pulses absent / cool limb → emergent vascular exploration (no imaging delay)
├─ Pulses present, ABI < 0.9 → CT angiography → vascular surgery if injury
├─ Pulses present, ABI > 0.9 → serial exams (2–4 hourly, 48 h)
└─ Pulses absent pre-reduction, return after (TO) → CT angiography (intimal tear)
```

**Present pulses** — **do not exclude** popliteal injury (collaterals) · **pulse oximetry** doesn't exclude either

**Ischaemia > 8 h** — **amputation up to 86%** (Orthobullets) · > 6–8 h **\~85%** (OB)

**Bilateral, ABI 0.78 left / 0.96 right** — **CTA left only, observe right**

**ABI 0.0 after posterior dislocation** — **complete popliteal artery tear**

**Ischaemic after reduction, BMI 52** — **emergent vascular exploration** · on-table angiogram\
✗ Wrong options: ex-fix first · CTA · IR stenting

**Surgery for** — flow-limiting intimal flap, absent pulses, ischaemia, haemorrhage, expanding haematoma · **non-flow-limiting flap → observe** (anticoagulation doesn't help)

## 5 Management

### Reduction & initial stabilisation (irreducible posterolateral dislocation, spanning ex-fix)

**Orthopaedic emergency** — **reduce first, then reassess pulses**

| Direction | Reduction |
|---|---|
| Anterior | Traction + anterior translation of **femur** |
| Posterior | Traction, extension, anterior translation of **tibia** |
| Medial / lateral | Traction + translation |
| Rotatory | Traction + rotate opposite to deformity |

**Splint** — **20–30° flexion**

**Absent pulses, peroneal palsy (wrestler)** — **closed reduction in ED + reassess**\
✗ Wrong options: acute reconstruction · MRI · arteriogram first

**Symmetric pulses, ACL + PCL + PLC on MRI** — **emergent reduction + EUA, repeat NV exam, admit overnight, delayed reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: discharge · angiogram routinely · immediate reconstruction

**Irreducible posterolateral + pucker sign** — **reduction in OR** → **open reduction via anteromedial approach** (medial parapatellar) + **spanning ex-fix**; pulses still absent → **popliteal exploration**\
✗ Wrong options: femoral distractor closed · traction pin · posterior approach

**After vascular repair + fasciotomy, unstable knee** — **spanning ex-fix**\
✗ Wrong options: cylinder cast · acute ligament surgery · arthroscopy

### Operative criteria

| Open reduction | Spanning ex-fix |
|---|---|
| **Irreducible / posterolateral** | **Vascular repair** (protect it) |
| Open fracture-dislocation | Open fracture-dislocation |
| **Obesity** (hard closed) | **Compartment syndrome** |
| Vascular injury | **Obesity (BMI > 50)**, polytrauma |

**Ex-fix position** — **20–30° flexion**, reduced in AP + sagittal planes

**Nonop definitive** — rare; **worse outcomes** (stiffness + instability)

### Timing of ligament reconstruction

**Acute (< 3 wk)** — **↑ residual instability** + **↑ stiffness recalcitrant to treatment**\
✗ Wrong options: less instability · best outcomes

**Staged** — more good / excellent outcomes (older data; now ≈ equal); **no algorithm proven superior**

**Early treatment improves outcomes** (Orthobullets treatment section) vs staged better (technique section) — **read the options**

**Arthroscopic** — risky if large capsular tear (**fluid extravasation → compartment syndrome**); **PLC + PMC need open** reconstruction

**Hinged ex-fix** — adjunct, ↓ re-rupture

### Combined ligament reconstruction — timing, sequence & graft fixation order

**ACL + PCL (± collaterals) (TO)** — **simultaneous reconstruction at 2–4 weeks**

**Fixation order (TO)** — **PCL femoral → ACL femoral → PCL tibial → ACL tibial**

**Tension angles (TO)** — **PCL at 70–90°** (anterolateral bundle 90°, restore step-off) **first** → **LCL at 20–30°** + slight valgus → rest of PLC at 60°, neutral rotation → **ACL at 30°** → posteromedial corner last

**PLC before ACL (TO)** — ACL tension in PLC-deficient knee **↑ external tibial rotation**

**ACL + grade 1 MCL (TO)** — **hinged brace (locked 30°) \~2 wk, then ACL reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: MCL repair · brace 1 month · conservative

| MCL grade with ACL (TO) | Plan |
|---|---|
| **1** | ACL surgery at **2 wk**, no motion restriction |
| **2** | ACL within 2 wk; **7 days** brace locked at 30° |
| **3** | ACL at **3 wk**; 10–14 days locked at 30° |

**Acute MCL repair (TO)** — intra-articular entrapment, large bony avulsion, plateau fracture, complete tibial-side avulsion (athlete), AMRI, valgus laxity at 0° in valgus knee · **MCL heals nonop > 90%**

**ACL + PLC (LCL, popliteus, biceps), PCL intact (TO)** — **ACL + PLC reconstruction** within 3 wk\
✗ Wrong options: PLC **repair** (higher failure) · delayed ACL at 6 wk

**PLC repair only for (TO)** — **avulsions off fibular head** (FCL, PFL, biceps) or popliteus, capsular / meniscocapsular

**PLC static stabilisers (TO)** — **FCL (LCL), popliteus tendon, popliteofibular ligament**; up to **16%** of knee ligament injuries

| Fanelli PLC (TO) | Findings |
|---|---|
| A | ↑ ER 10° — PFL, popliteus |
| B | + slight varus (5–10 mm) — + FCL |
| C | + severe varus (> 10 mm) — + capsule, cruciate |

**Dial + at 30° and 90° (TO)** — **arthroscopic PCL + LaPrade PLC reconstruction** · **LaPrade = anatomical (LCL, popliteus, PFL)** · Larson = LCL + PFL only

### O'Donoghue unhappy triad

**Triad (TO)** — **ACL + MCL + medial meniscus** (lateral force, fixed foot) · arthroscopically **lateral meniscus** tears commoner with acute ACL\
✗ Wrong options: PCL · LCL

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Stiffness (arthrofibrosis)** | **Most common (38%)**; delayed mobilisation → early motion, LOA, MUA |
| Laxity / instability | **37%**; redislocation rare |
| **Vascular** | Emergent repair + **prophylactic fasciotomy** |
| **Peroneal nerve** | **25%** (16–40%) |

### Peroneal nerve injury — examination & EMG

**Commonest nerve** — **common peroneal (25%)** > tibial (subclinical tibial in \~half of peroneal cases); **most associated structure** after posterior dislocation\
✗ Wrong options: popliteal artery 16%

**Pattern** — **KD IIIL**, anterior + posterolateral dislocations; ↑ with **vascular injury**, male, **↑ BMI**, **fibular head fracture**

**Exam** — **weak ankle dorsiflexion**, EHL (great toe extension), **eversion (peroneus brevis)**, numb **dorsum + 1st web**\
✗ Wrong options: plantar foot · plantarflexion · sural · saphenous

**Level** — **common peroneal at fibular neck**\
✗ Wrong options: deep / superficial branches alone

**Recovery** — **\~50% partial** (Orthobullets) · complete 30–50% · **partial palsy \~85% vs complete \~35%** · < ⅓ regain dorsiflexion (OB)

**EMG / NCS** — **unreliable before 4–6 wk**; at **3 months with weak TA firing → NCS / EMG**\
✗ Wrong options: PT alone · neurolysis · PTT transfer yet

**Partial palsy** — wait **6–12 months**; **AFO** vs equinus · **complete neurotmesis on early EMG → early repair / transfer**

### Foot drop — tendon transfer (tibialis posterior)

**Transfer** — **tibialis posterior: medial navicular → dorsal lateral cuneiform through interosseous membrane** (gold standard)\
✗ Wrong options: FDL · PL · PB (peroneal-innervated) · FHL→EHL

**Bridle procedure** — PTT + **tibialis anterior + peroneus longus** anastomosis · tenodesis to TA → weaker

**1 yr foot drop, nerve in continuity** — **PTT transfer**\
✗ Wrong options: re-neurolysis · cable graft · EHL transfer · ankle fusion

**Contraindication** — **rigid deformity / can't passively dorsiflex past neutral** · **weak PTT / S1 radiculopathy** also limits\
✗ Wrong options: TA 0/5 · prior nerve surgery · L4 radiculopathy · corrected gastrocnemius contracture

### Septic arthritis after reconstruction

**4 wk after ACL, fever, ESR 105, CRP 44 (TO)** — **arthroscopic debridement + graft retention + IV antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: graft removal first

| Gächter (TO) | Findings | Approach |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Turbid effusion, hyperaemic synovium | Arthroscopic |
| 2 | **Purulent**, fibrin, hypertrophic synovium | Arthroscopic |
| 3 | Adhesions, necrotic synovium / cartilage | Arthrotomy |
| 4 | Cartilage necrosis, **bone erosion** | Arthrotomy |

## X vs Y

| | **Anterior** | **Posterior** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **Most common** | 2nd |
| Mechanism | **Hyperextension** | Dashboard |
| Artery | **Intimal tear** | **Complete tear** |

| | **Dial + 30° only** | **Dial + 30° and 90°** |
|---|---|---|
| Lesion | **PLC** | **PLC + PCL** |

## How it's asked

**"Pulses return after reduction (TO)?"** → **CT angiography**

**"Vascular injury rate (low-velocity anterior)?"** → **40%**

**"Irreducible, pucker sign, pulses lost?"** → **open reduction anteromedial + spanning ex-fix → explore artery**

**"Bilateral, ABI 0.78 / 0.96?"** → **CTA left, monitor right**

**"Reduced, ABI 0.95?"** → **serial examination**

**"Ischaemic after reduction, BMI 52?"** → **emergent vascular exploration**

**"Absent pulses + peroneal palsy — initial?"** → **closed reduction in ED, reassess**

**"How is ABI calculated + threshold?"** → **ankle systolic / brachial systolic; 0.8 = concern**

**"ABI 0.8 after reduction?"** → **CT angiography**

**"ABI 0.0 posterior dislocation?"** → **complete popliteal artery tear**

**"Can't dorsiflex after multiligament injury?"** → **common peroneal nerve**

**"Nerve injury level (figure)?"** → **common peroneal nerve**

**"Additional exam finding with ACL + PCL + varus laxity?"** → **difficulty dorsiflexing ankle**

**"Highest rate of associated injury?"** → **peroneal nerve**

**"3 months, weak TA firing?"** → **NCS / EMG**

**"KDIIIL deficit?"** → **weak ankle dorsiflexion**

**"Muscle affected if nerve injured?"** → **EHL**

**"Structure affected after anterior dislocation?"** → **peroneus brevis (5th MT base)**

**"Most common nerve finding?"** → **weak ankle dorsiflexion**

**"Dynamic transfer for foot drop?"** → **medial navicular → dorsal lateral cuneiform (PTT)**

**"Foot drop 1 yr, nerve in continuity?"** → **tibialis posterior transfer through IOM**

**"Contraindication to tendon transfer?"** → **rigid deformity, can't dorsiflex past neutral**

**"Anterior dislocation, symmetric pulses?"** → **reduce + EUA, observe overnight, delayed reconstruction**

**"After vascular repair, unstable knee?"** → **spanning external fixation**

**"Irreducible, ABI 0.92?"** → **reduction in the operating room**

**"Failed ED reduction — clinical image?"** → **skin pucker (dimple)**

**"Acute (< 3 wk) reconstruction — true?"** → **more stiffness recalcitrant to treatment**

**"ACL + PCL graft fixation sequence (TO)?"** → **PCL femoral, ACL femoral, PCL tibial, ACL tibial**

**"ACL + grade 1 MCL (TO)?"** → **brace 2 wk then ACL reconstruction**

**"ACL + PCL timing (TO)?"** → **simultaneous at 2–4 weeks**

**"ACL + LCL / popliteus / biceps, PCL intact (TO)?"** → **ACL + PLC reconstruction**

**"Dial + at 30° and 90° — treatment (TO)?"** → **arthroscopic PCL + LaPrade reconstruction**

**"Confirms PCL + PLC (TO)?"** → **dial test + at 30° and 90°**

**"Test for ACL + MCL (TO)?"** → **anterior drawer with foot externally rotated**

**"Position to test collaterals (TO)?"** → **30° flexion**

**"ER 15° more at 30°, more at 90° (TO)?"** → **PCL + PLC**

**"Positive ER recurvatum test (TO)?"** → **ACL + PLC**

**"Infection 4 wk after ACL (TO)?"** → **arthroscopic debridement, graft retention, IV antibiotics**

**"Unhappy triad (TO)?"** → **MCL + medial meniscus + ACL**

**"Unhappy triad EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **PCL**

**"KD IV cruciate pattern (TO)?"** → **ACL midsubstance + PCL femoral avulsion**
