# Leg Compartment Syndrome

*Trauma · 36 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & causes

**Commonest site** — **leg** · **anterior compartment** most often (TO)

**Fractures** — **69%** (Orthobullets) · **75%** (TO) · **tibial shaft = commonest cause** (TO) · **proximal tibia** commonest overall (other TO) · **plateau 12% vs shaft 3%**, **Schatzker VI** high risk (TO)

**Other causes** — crush, contusion, GSW, **tight casts / dressings**, IV extravasation, burns, post-ischaemic swelling, bleeding disorders / anticoagulants, arterial injury, osteotomy (**Blount's**), **lithotomy position**, snake bite, exercise

| Risk factor | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Young age** | **Highest 12–19 yr**, then 20–29 (more muscle, less room) |
| **Diaphyseal fracture** | More muscle than tendon |
| Open, **segmental / comminuted** | Higher energy (Orthobullets, OB) · open **not** associated (one OB item) — read the options |
| **Higher BMI (children)** | **Most significant paediatric factor** in OB |
| Paediatric | **Older (> 14 yr)**, male, MVC / motorbike, **ipsilateral fibula fracture**, IMN · **< 12 yr protective** |
| Polytrauma (TO) | ↑ lactate / base deficit, transfusion, coagulopathy (warfarin) |

**Not associated (OB)** — male sex, BMI ≥ 30 (adult), open fracture, nicotine

**Anterior compartment vulnerability (TO)** — **type I (slow-twitch, oxidative) fibres** → most sensitive to hypoxia

### Pathophysiology — venous outflow obstruction

**Cascade** — trauma → bleeding + oedema → ↑ interstitial pressure → **↓ venous outflow relative to arterial inflow** → myoneural ischaemia

**Fasciotomy effect** — **↑ venous outflow**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ arterial inflow · ↑ interstitial pressure · ↓ perfusion · ↓ bleeding (may ↑ by losing tamponade)

**Irreversible damage** — within **6 h**

### Compartment anatomy & nerve deficits

| Compartment | Muscles | Nerve / vessel | Deficit |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Anterior** | TA, EHL, EDL, peroneus tertius | **Deep peroneal**, anterior tibial artery | **1st web space** numb, ↓ dorsiflexion |
| **Lateral** | Peroneus longus + brevis | **Superficial peroneal** (only nerve) | **Dorsum of foot — hallux, 3rd + 4th toes** |
| **Deep posterior** | **TP, FDL, FHL, popliteus** | **Tibial nerve**, posterior tibial artery | **Plantar foot numb + pain on passive toe EXTENSION** |
| Superficial posterior | Gastrocnemius, soleus, plantaris | Sural | Plantarflexion |

![Leg compartments](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1001-compartments.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

### Clinical signs & risk factors (analgesia requirement, children, young age)

**Earliest / most important (TO)** — **pain out of proportion + pain on passive stretch**\
✗ Wrong options: tight compartment · ↑ pressure · pulse loss + paraesthesia

**Most sensitive before ischaemia** — **pain on passive stretch**

**Late** — **paralysis** (rare full recovery), **absent pulses** (amputation usually inevitable), pallor · **pulses and capillary refill intact in ACS** (TO)

**Early sensory (TO)** — ↓ 2-point discrimination / vibration

**Children** — **"3 A's": anxiety, agitation, increasing analgesic requirement** · **increasing analgesia = most sensitive** · **duration before fasciotomy = most important outcome variable** · **mechanism is NOT the best predictor**\
✗ Wrong options: 5 P's

**Pain unreliable** — polytrauma, **sedated (impossible)**, children, nerve injury

**After 5-h tibial nailing: pain on passive toe flexion, pulse present (TO)** — **operative decompression**\
✗ Wrong options: measure pressure · Doppler · dextran

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — rule out fracture only · **CTA / Doppler / ABI / duplex not diagnostic** (duplex = DVT)

### Diagnosis — pressure measurement & delta-P thresholds

### Diagnostic criteria

| Fasciotomy | Observe |
|---|---|
| **Δp (diastolic − compartment) ≤ 30 mmHg** (TO: **< 30 for > 2 h** if minimal signs) | **Δp > 30** + exam not consistent |
| Clinical diagnosis (no pressures needed) | — |
| Absolute **> 30–45 mmHg** (historic; **43% unnecessary fasciotomies** — McQueen) | — |

**Normal compartment pressure** — **< 10 mmHg** (TO)

**Most accurate** — **Δp < 30**\
✗ Wrong options: palpation · paraesthesia · absolute 25

**Intra-op** — anaesthesia ↓ diastolic → **use PRE-op diastolic** · e.g. **intra-op 29 with pre-op DBP 58 → concerning**\
✗ Wrong options: MAP

**Intra-op 28 mmHg, BP 84/57 (pre-op 132/84)** — **re-evaluate in recovery**\
✗ Wrong options: fasciotomy · pressors

**31–35 mmHg, BP 115/78, pain unchanged with stretch** — **support BP + serial exams**\
✗ Wrong options: fasciotomy · MRA · EMG

**Hypotensive polytrauma (80/50), pressures 21–25** — **Δp < 30 → immediate fasciotomy**

**Alert, stable pain, DBP 98, pressure 35 (Δp 63)** — **reduce + splint, serial exams**\
✗ Wrong options: fasciotomy · analgesia alone

| Technique point | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Site** | **Within 5 cm of fracture** (highest pressure; also centre of muscle belly) |
| **Interobserver reliability** | **Poor** |
| Anterior | 1 cm lateral to tibial crest |
| Deep posterior | Just posterior to medial tibial border → toward fibula |
| Lateral | Just anterior to posterior fibular border |
| Superficial posterior | Mid-calf |

**Obtunded / intubated** — **measure pressures** · AAOS CPG: **repeated / continuous** monitoring\
✗ Wrong options: palpation · myoglobinuria (lactate / troponin better but inferior)

**Continuous monitoring (slit catheter, anterior)** — **most sensitive + specific in sedated**: sens 94%, spec 98%, PPV 93%, NPV 99%

**Child post-op: tense calf, cool foot, ↓ pulses, little pain** — **measure compartment pressures**\
✗ Wrong options: vascular consult · ABI · duplex

**Child after Blount osteotomies, sedated, passive toe pain** — **remove cast + measure pressures**\
✗ Wrong options: elevate · more analgesia · muscle relaxant

**Children** — measure **under sedation** if suspected · **haemophilia → Factor VIII before** measuring

## 5 Management

```
Suspected leg compartment syndrome
├─ Cast / dressing, no clear CS → bivalve + spread, split padding, ankle neutral–30° PF
├─ Clinically certain → emergent 4-compartment fasciotomy (+ stabilise fracture)
├─ Equivocal / obtunded → pressures → Δp ≤ 30 → fasciotomy
└─ Missed (late, established) → no fasciotomy
```

### Cast-related — ankle position & bivalving

**Safest ankle position in cast (TO)** — **neutral to 30° plantarflexion** · anterior lowest at neutral, deep posterior lowest in PF · resting PF \~37°\
✗ Wrong options: dorsiflexion

**Cast + increasing pain + toe numbness** — **bivalve + spread cast, remove padding**\
✗ Wrong options: elevate · morphine · ice · observe

**Already bivalved, ankle dorsiflexed, recurrent numbness** — **reposition ankle into slight plantarflexion**\
✗ Wrong options: fasciotomy · re-reduction

**Impending CS (TO)** — **limb at heart level** (elevation ↓ arterial inflow)

### Emergent fasciotomy with fracture stabilisation

**Tibial shaft fracture + CS at 6 h (TO)** — **fasciotomy then reamed IMN in same sitting** · reamed = unreamed ICP\
✗ Wrong options: unreamed · delay nail · ex-fix

**Sequence (TO)** — **fasciotomy first**, then stabilise; **cast contraindicated** with ACS; avoid excess traction, thigh-bar pressure, high elevation

**Severe pain despite narcotics, passive stretch pain** — **immediate 4-compartment fasciotomy + ex-fix**\
✗ Wrong options: 2-compartment · fix first

**Teen, knee injury, firm compartments, pressures 33–38 (BP 100/50)** — **emergent 4-compartment fasciotomy + ORIF**

### Fasciotomy technique (incisions, compartments, SPN risk)

| Technique | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Dual incision** — 2 × 15–18 cm, **8 cm skin bridge** | **Anterolateral**: protect **SPN**; anterior fasciotomy **1 cm in front of** septum, lateral **1 cm behind** · **Posteromedial**: protect **saphenous vein + nerve**; superficial posterior; **detach soleal bridge from tibia → deep posterior** |
| **Single lateral (perifibular)** | Fibular head → ankle; anterior + lateral, then superficial posterior; **deep posterior via interosseous membrane behind fibula** |

**Soleus elevated from tibia** — to reach **deep posterior** compartment

**Medial incision does NOT release** — **EHL (anterior)**\
✗ Wrong options: TP, FDL, popliteus, soleus

**Single lateral — nerve at risk proximally** — **superficial peroneal** (OB) · **common peroneal** (Orthobullets) — read the options

**Closure** — **delayed primary / STSG at 3–7 days** (Orthobullets) · 7–10 days (TO)

**Principles (TO)** — extensile incisions, release all involved compartments, protect vital structures, debride

**Contraindication** — **missed (established) compartment syndrome**

![Dual-incision fasciotomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1001-illustration-double-incision-technique.jpg)

### Hyperbaric oxygen

**Mechanism** — **↑ oxygen diffusion gradient** (100% O₂ at ↑ pressure)

**Indications** — CO poisoning, **gas gangrene**, crush, compartment syndrome, **necrotising fasciitis**, chronic osteomyelitis, grafts / flaps, burns

## 6 Complications

### Late sequelae — contracture & tendon transfers

| Missed compartment | Deformity | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Anterior + deep posterior** | Weak TA + TP → **PL overpowers → cavovarus** (flexible) | **Peroneus longus → dorsolateral midfoot** + Achilles lengthening |
| **Anterior + lateral** | **Foot drop**, flexible equinovarus (TP 5/5) | **PTT through IOM to TA + peroneus tertius (above ankle)** + anterior debridement + **TAL** (✗ Wrong options: PL transfer (weak) · fusion) |
| **Deep posterior** | **Clawing all toes**, equinocavovarus, no heel rise; equinus **not relieved by knee flexion** | **Posterior capsule release + TAL + excise scarred muscle / tendon** |

**Transfer rule** — transferred muscle **loses one grade** → needs **5/5**

**Equinus relieved by knee flexion** — gastrocnemius slide · not relieved → **Achilles lengthening**

## X vs Y

| | **Lateral compartment** | **Anterior compartment** | **Deep posterior** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Nerve | **SPN** | **DPN** | **Tibial** |
| Sensory loss | **Dorsum (hallux, 3rd, 4th)** | **1st web** | **Plantar foot** |
| Stretch pain | Inversion | Plantarflexion of toes / ankle | **Toe extension** |

## How it's asked

**"Decompression threshold (TO)?"** → **Δp < 30 mmHg for > 2 h**

**"Most accurate diagnosis?"** → **diastolic − compartment < 30**

**"Intra-op 28 mmHg, hypotensive?"** → **repeat evaluation in recovery**

**"Post-op 31–35 mmHg, BP 115/78?"** → **continued monitoring + serial exams**

**"Concerning measurement?"** → **intra-op 29 with pre-op diastolic 58**

**"Hypotensive polytrauma, pressures 21–25?"** → **immediate fasciotomy**

**"8-yr-old tense calf, cool foot post-op?"** → **measure compartment pressures**

**"Pressure measurement — true?"** → **poor interobserver reliability**

**"Sedated on pressors, swollen calf?"** → **measure pressures**

**"Most sensitive + specific in sedated?"** → **continuous pressure monitoring**

**"Alert, DBP 98, pressure 35?"** → **closed reduction + splint, serial exams**

**"Most important sign (TO)?"** → **pain out of proportion + pain on passive movement**

**"Pain after 5-h nailing, pulse present (TO)?"** → **operative decompression**

**"Most sensitive sign in child (supracondylar)?"** → **increasing analgesia requirement**

**"Earliest sign in child?"** → **pain out of proportion**

**"Paediatric CS — EXCEPT?"** → **mechanism is the best predictor**

**"Blount osteotomy, passive toe pain?"** → **remove cast + measure pressures**

**"Factor most associated (18-yr-old)?"** → **age < 20**

**"Most significant paediatric risk factor?"** → **higher BMI**

**"Fasciotomy effect?"** → **↑ venous outflow**

**"Lateral compartment pressure 47 — pathophysiology + deficit?"** → **↓ venous outflow; numb dorsum (hallux, 3rd, 4th)**

**"Isolated lateral compartment — deficit?"** → **numb dorsum of foot (hallux, 3rd, 4th)**

**"Deep posterior compartment signs?"** → **plantar paraesthesia + pain on passive toe extension**

**"Perifibular approach — proximal risk?"** → **superficial peroneal nerve**

**"Soleus elevated to reach?"** → **deep posterior compartment**

**"Not released by medial incision?"** → **EHL**

**"Safest ankle position in cast (TO)?"** → **neutral to 30° plantarflexion**

**"Already bivalved, recurrent numbness?"** → **reposition ankle into slight plantarflexion**

**"Long-leg cast, increasing pain + tingling?"** → **bivalve + spread cast**

**"Tibial fracture + CS at 6 h (TO)?"** → **fasciotomy + reamed IMN same sitting**

**"Teen, pressures 33–38?"** → **emergent 4-compartment fasciotomy + ORIF**

**"Uncontrolled pain, passive stretch pain?"** → **immediate 4-compartment fasciotomy + ex-fix**

**"Anterior + deep posterior CS → cavovarus?"** → **peroneus longus to dorsolateral midfoot**

**"Foot drop 2 yr, PTT 5/5?"** → **PTT through IOM to TA + peroneus tertius, TAL**

**"Toe clawing + equinus after segmental tibia?"** → **posterior capsule release, TAL, excise scar**

**"↑ oxygen diffusion gradient?"** → **hyperbaric oxygen**

# Monteggia Fractures

*Trauma · 37 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Monteggia** — **proximal ⅓ ulna fracture + radial head dislocation** (PRUJ disruption)

**Who** — **rare in adults** (high energy) · **children 4–10 yr** commoner, different protocol

**Associated** — olecranon fracture-dislocation, radial head (\~5%), coronoid, LCL injury, **terrible triad**

**Key ligament** — **annular ligament**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Bado classification — direction of radial head dislocation

**Rule** — **radial head dislocates toward the apex of the ulnar fracture** (apex anterior → anterior)

| Bado | Share | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **60%** | Proximal / middle ulna, **anterior** dislocation — **commonest in children + young adults** |
| **II** | 15% | **Posterior** dislocation — **70–80% of adult Monteggia**; **worst prognosis** |
| **III** | 20% | Ulnar **metaphysis** (distal to coronoid), **lateral** dislocation — **most often irreducible (annular ligament)** |
| **IV** | 5% | **Ulna + radius** fractures, dislocation any direction |

![Bado type I — anterior dislocation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1024-type-i-lateral.jpg)

| Jupiter (type II) | Ulnar fracture level |
|---|---|
| **IIA** | **Coronoid** level |
| **IIB** | Metaphyseal-diaphyseal junction |
| **IIC** | Distal to coronoid (diaphysis) |
| **IID** | Extends into distal half |

![Jupiter classification of Bado II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1024-jupiter-classification-of-type-ii.jpg)

**Monteggia "variant"** — + **radial head / neck fracture** → separate approach; ↑ OA, instability, nonunion, ↓ ROM

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral **elbow, wrist, forearm**; **radiocapitellar line** congruent on every view

**CT** — coronoid, olecranon, radial head involvement

## 3 Presentation

**PIN palsy** — **most likely nerve injury**; **wrist extension in radial deviation** (ECRL spared), weak thumb + **MCP extension**, sensation intact

## 5 Management

```
Monteggia fracture
├─ Child, stable ulna, reduced RC joint → closed reduction + cast (supination for Bado I / III)
├─ Adult (almost all) → ORIF ulna (plate) → radial head usually reduces
│      └─ radial head still out → check ulna (revise) → then open reduction (annular ligament)
└─ Transverse / short oblique, easily reduced → closed reduction + IM fixation of ulna
```

### Treatment options

| Option | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Closed reduction + cast** | **Children**, stable transverse ulna; **supination for Bado I + III**; oblique / unstable → pinning |
| **ORIF ulna + closed radial head reduction** | **Adults**, open, **unstable (long oblique)**, comminuted |
| **ORIF ulna + open radial head reduction** | **Radial head won't reduce after correct ulnar ORIF**, complex injury, **variants** |
| Closed reduction + **IM ulnar fixation** | Transverse / short oblique, easily reduced |

**Technique** — lateral decubitus; posterior incision lateral to olecranon tip; **FCU / anconeus** proximally, **FCU / ECU** distally; **dorsal plating**; **dorsal cortex** to judge length; **distractor** if comminuted; fluoroscopy through full motion

**Radial head open reduction** — **posterolateral (Kocher)**; separate lateral incision

### Persistent radial head dislocation — ulnar malreduction / length

**After ulnar fixation, radiocapitellar joint dislocated** — **revise ulnar ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: radial neck osteotomy first · radial head arthroplasty · splint in supination · shortening osteotomy

**Commonest cause of persistent / recurrent dislocation** — **failure to restore ulnar length / anatomic alignment**\
✗ Wrong options: annular ligament not repaired · missed radial head fracture · LCL · fixation rigidity

**Late (6 mo) subluxation + stiffness** — **ulnar corrective osteotomy + open radial head reduction**

### Irreducible radial head — annular ligament interposition

**Non-concentric after reduction (child, posterolateral)** — **annular ligament interposed** · **Bado III** most often\
✗ Wrong options: LUCL · MCL bands · anconeus

**How common** — "often found interposed" (Orthobullets) · "rare complication" in adults (OB) — read the options

### Radial head excision — MCL competence

**Chronic malunion** — ulnar osteotomy + radial head reduction · or **radial head excision** (restores rotation)

**Must be competent before excision** — **anterior bundle of MCL** (primary valgus stabiliser)\
✗ Wrong options: posterior bundle (secondary) · brachialis · RCL / LUCL (varus)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **PIN palsy** | **Up to 10%** acute; blunt trauma from radial head → **observe 2–3 months** (usually resolves) → NCS if not |
| **Radial head malunion / redislocation** | **Ulnar malalignment** |
| **Stiffness** | **Diagnosis delayed > 2–3 wk** → complications ↑↑ |
| Synostosis, nonunion, plate failure | Type II: **nonunion + plate failure** |

### Outcomes & prognostic factors

**Bado II** — **highest complication rate**, worst prognosis; nearly all lose some ROM\
✗ Wrong options: full recovery · casting = surgery · nerve injury common · ulnohumeral instability

**Worse DASH** — **radial head / neck fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: age · radius injury type · treatment method

## X vs Y

| | **Monteggia** | **Galeazzi** |
|---|---|---|
| Fracture | **Proximal ulna** | **Distal ⅓ radius** |
| Joint | **Radial head / PRUJ** | **DRUJ** |
| Key to reduction | **Ulnar length + alignment** | Radial ORIF then DRUJ |
| Nerve | **PIN** | — |

## How it's asked

**"Apex-anterior proximal ulna — radial head direction?"** → **anterior**

**"Radial head out after ulnar fixation?"** → **revision ORIF of ulna**

**"Commonest cause of persistent dislocation?"** → **failure to restore ulnar length**

**"Pain + ↓ motion 6 months after ORIF?"** → **inadequate ulnar reduction**

**"Child, non-concentric after closed reduction?"** → **annular ligament interposition**

**"Bado II in 54-yr-old — counsel?"** → **high complication rate**

**"Worse functional scores?"** → **radial head / neck fracture**

**"Must be competent before radial head excision?"** → **anterior bundle of MCL**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Morel-Lavallee Lesion

*Trauma · 38 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Morel-Lavallée lesion (MLL)** — **closed degloving**: shear separates **dermis + subcutaneous fat from deep fascia**

**Incidence** — **up to 8% of acetabular fractures** (Orthobullets) · up to 10% (TO)

**Site** — **proximal lateral thigh (peritrochanteric) — commonest**; gluteal, lumbosacral, abdominal (hypermobile skin)

**Risk** — **high-energy shear**; **obese**; with **pelvic, acetabular, proximal femur** fractures

### Anatomy — plane of fluid collection

**Layers** — epidermis → dermis → subcutaneous fat → **[MLL] → deep fascia** → muscle

**Plane** — **deep to subcutaneous fat, superficial to deep fascia**\
✗ Wrong options: dermis (seroma) · subcutaneous (oedema) · intramuscular (haematoma) · deep to muscle

![Morel-Lavallée anatomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422820-morel-lavallee-anatomy-illustration.jpg)

### Pathophysiology (4 stages)

**Stages** — shear separation → **subdermal plexus** lymphatics + vessels bleed → replaced by **serosanguinous fluid** → **pseudocapsule** (chronic, untreated)

**Bacterial colonisation** — **up to 46%**

## 3 Presentation

**Missed** — **up to 33%** at index evaluation (distracting injuries, habitus)

**Exam** — **ecchymosis, swelling, fluctuance, skin hypermobility** · discoloration may take **days**

**Earliest indicator (TO)** — **paraesthesia of overlying skin**

**Chronic** — dull pain, contour deformity, cosmesis

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use / finding |
|---|---|
| Radiographs | Underlying fractures; soft-tissue swelling |
| **CT** | **Size → management**; fluid in fascial plane (soft-tissue window) |
| **Ultrasound** | **Hypoechoic** space superficial to fascia; localise for drainage |
| **MRI** | **Best extent** (avg **30 × 12 cm**); **acute: T1 low, T2 high** · **subacute: T1 + T2 high** with **capsule low on both** |

![CT soft-tissue window — Morel-Lavallée lesion of the thigh](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422820-left-thigh-mll-soft-tissue-window.jpg)

**Not to do for MLL** — MRI + core biopsy (sarcoma work-up), CT chest / abdomen / pelvis (metastasis work-up), admit for antibiotics (abscess)

## 5 Management

```
Morel-Lavallée lesion
├─ Small (< 50 cm³) → compression ± percutaneous drainage + drain
├─ Large (> 50 cm³) / failed nonop → open I&D + drain
│      └─ overlies fracture incision → dual-incision I&D, same sitting as fixation
└─ Chronic pseudocyst → open debridement + capsule resection
```

### Operative criteria

| Treatment | Indication | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **Compression** (ace wraps, bike shorts) | **< 50 cm³** | Needs compliance |
| **Percutaneous drainage + drain** (US-guided, 1–2 cm incision, irrigate) | **< 50 cm³** | **Inferior** to open (can't debride necrotic tissue); **needle aspiration → high recurrence** |
| **Single-incision I&D** | **> 50 cm³** or failed nonop; **not in fracture approach** | **75% resolution** |
| **Dual-incision I&D** | **Lesion overlies fracture approach** / found intra-op | Higher infection; serial I&D |
| **Open debridement + capsule resection** | **Chronic pseudocyst** | Often multiple surgeries |

### Chronic lesion — open debridement & drain

**Large symptomatic lesion at 1 month** — **open I&D + drain** via **limited approach** (protect compromised subdermal plexus)\
✗ Wrong options: needle decompression · biopsy · antibiotics

**Trochanteric fracture + MLL (TO)** — **fix fracture + incision, open debridement at same time**\
✗ Wrong options: delay fixation · observe · fix + ambulate only

**Technique** — scrub-brush / Cobb debridement · **≥ 15 Fr drain** exiting away, remove at **< 20–30 mL/day** · primary vs delayed closure controversial (VAC + repeat I&D at **48–72 h**)

**Dual-incision** — proximal + distal ends of lesion; **close proximal primarily**, **distal: VAC → repeat I&D 48–72 h**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Recurrence** | **Most common** — **up to 56% nonop**, **15–20% after open** → repeat I&D + drain, VAC, **sclerotherapy (talc / doxycycline)** |
| **Pseudocyst** | Chronic untreated → **resect capsule** |
| Skin necrosis | Delay, repeated I&D → skin graft |
| **SSI after pelvic / acetabular surgery** | **MLL = independent risk factor** |

**Prognosis** — better if **small, acute, away from incisions**

### Differentiation from necrotising fasciitis (LRINEC)

**LRINEC (TO)** — **CRP, WBC, haemoglobin, sodium, creatinine, glucose**; **≥ 6 = high risk** · **not included: SGPT (ALT)**

## X vs Y

| | **Morel-Lavallée** | **Haematoma / seroma** |
|---|---|---|
| Plane | **Subcutaneous fat ↔ deep fascia** | Intramuscular / dermis |
| Skin | **Hypermobile**, fluctuant | Not hypermobile |

## How it's asked

**"Plane of fluid collection?"** → **deep to subcutaneous fat, superficial to deep fascia**

**"Trochanteric fracture + thigh fullness (TO)?"** → **fixation combined with incision + open debridement**

**"Large fluctuant lesion 1 month after crush?"** → **open I&D + drain**

**"NOT in LRINEC (TO)?"** → **SGPT**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Necrotizing Fasciitis

*Trauma · 39 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & risk factors

**Necrotising fasciitis** — life-threatening infection spreading rapidly **along fascial planes**; overlying cellulitis ± (early skin deceptively benign)

**Immune suppression** — **diabetes (commonest comorbidity, 71%)**, AIDS / HIV, cancer, **obesity**, alcohol

**Bacterial entry** — **IV drug use**, injections, insect bites, **skin abrasions (turf)**, abdominal / perineal surgery

### Microbiology & empiric antibiotics (polymicrobial vs group A strep)

| Type | Organisms | Setting |
|---|---|---|
| **1 — polymicrobial (80–90%)** | **4–5 species**: **non-group A strep**, **anaerobes (Clostridia)**, facultative anaerobes, **gram-negative enteric bacilli (Enterobacteriaceae)**; synergy | **Diabetics, cancer**, post-op abdominal / perineal |
| **2 — monomicrobial (5%)** | **Group A β-haemolytic Streptococcus** (commonest monomicrobial) | **Healthy**, extremities |
| **3** | **Vibrio vulnificus** (gram-negative rods) | **Marine** exposure |
| 4 | Fungal | — |

**Commonest overall** — **polymicrobial** · **commonest single isolate** — **group A strep** (Fontes, Wong)\
✗ Wrong options: GAS · MRSA · S. aureus

**Polymicrobial culture most likely in** — **diabetic with necrotising soft-tissue infection**\
✗ Wrong options: chronic PJI (CoNS) · nail through shoe (Pseudomonas) · neonate hip (GBS) · acute PJI (S. aureus)

**MRSA** — **not** the commonest monomicrobial cause

![Necrotising fasciitis — bullae and discoloration](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1007-screen-shot-2017-04-02-at-9-29-47-am.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Early** — rapidly progressing cellulitis / abscess, **minimal swelling**, no discoloration

**Late** — **severe pain (out of proportion)**, high fever, rigors, **tachycardia, hypotension**

**Skin** — **bullae (purple / haemorrhagic)**, ischaemic patches, cutaneous gangrene, induration, **subcutaneous emphysema (gas only 13%)**

**Red flag** — **worsening despite IV antibiotics** + systemic toxicity

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Imaging** — **not required**; **CT / MRI delay surgery** (only show oedema / thickening) · **gas → urgent debridement**

**Frozen section** — 1 × 1 × 1 cm, bedside or OR; confirms early cases; **never delay surgery** · histology: **fascial necrosis**, organisms in fascia, PMNs, **fibrinous thrombi + vessel wall necrosis**

### Diagnosis — LRINEC score & laboratory markers

| LRINEC component | Points |
|---|---|
| **CRP ≥ 150 mg/L** | **4 (largest)** |
| **WBC** 15–25 · **> 25 × 10⁹/L** | 1 · **2** |
| **Hb** 11–13.5 · **< 11 g/dL** | 1 · 2 |
| **Na < 135 mmol/L** | 2 |
| **Creatinine > 141 µmol/L** | 2 |
| **Glucose > 10 mmol/L** | 1 |

**Score** — max **13**; **≥ 6 → high risk**; original **PPV 92%** · later **sens 68%, spec 85%**

**NOT in LRINEC** — **ESR**, potassium, bicarbonate, calcium, **SGPT / ALT**

**Missing value given CRP, WBC, glucose, Cr, Na** — **haemoglobin**

**Most valuable lab (with Na 130, CRP > 200)** — **WBC > 25** → **emergent debridement ± amputation**\
✗ Wrong options: ESR · MRI

## 5 Management

```
Suspected necrotising fasciitis
├─ Resuscitate + broad-spectrum IV antibiotics immediately
├─ Emergent radical debridement (skin, fat, fascia, muscle) — don't wait for imaging / ICU / culture
│      └─ life-threatening → amputation
└─ Serial debridements; hyperbaric O₂ if anaerobes
```

### Emergent surgical debridement

**Worsening despite cefazolin, hypotension** — **urgent aggressive debridement of skin, fat + fascia**\
✗ Wrong options: triple antibiotics alone · ICU + ID consult · gentle closure · HBO / IVIG first

**Diabetic, bullae, CRP > 200, WBC 25k, Na 127** — **emergent debridement ± amputation**\
✗ Wrong options: vascular exploration · ICU + escalate antibiotics · MRI · CT

**IVDU / HIV hand, CRP 178, WBC 29k** — **emergent debridement**\
✗ Wrong options: mark margins + observe · vascular consult · oral antibiotics · next-day surgery

**Operative findings** — **liquefied fat**, **"dishwater" pus**, muscle necrosis, venous thrombosis

**Multiple debridements** — often required · **multidisciplinary team**

### Antibiotics

| Stage | Regimen |
|---|---|
| **Empiric** | **Penicillin + clindamycin + metronidazole + aminoglycoside** (clindamycin ↓ amputation odds) · or **vancomycin + clindamycin + cefepime** |
| **Empiric with CKD (avoid nephrotoxins)** | **Linezolid + meropenem** (MRSA, GAS, gram-neg, anaerobes) (✗ Wrong options: vanc + gent · vanc alone · gent alone · micafungin) |
| Strep / Clostridium | **Penicillin G** |
| Polymicrobial | **Imipenem / doripenem / meropenem** |
| MRSA | + **vancomycin or daptomycin** |

**Hyperbaric oxygen** — adjunct, esp. **anaerobes / Clostridia** (↑ leukocyte O₂ killing, perfusion, antibiotic uptake) · HBO + IVIG only after surgery\
✗ Wrong options: "contraindicated in Clostridia"

## 6 Complications

### Mortality predictors — time to surgery, vasopressors

**Mortality** — **\~30% (32%)**; **higher in older** patients; **amputation vs salvage — similar**\
✗ Wrong options: 15%

**Reduces mortality** — **shorter time from admission to surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI findings · pressors · organism ID · location

**Delay > 24 h** — **↑ mortality (≈ 4×)**\
✗ Wrong options: always trial antibiotics · single debridement always enough · HBO useless · single-discipline team

**Strongest predictor of death** — **vasopressor requirement outside anaesthesia**\
✗ Wrong options: IVDU · cellulitis · ICU admission · debridement size (predicts final defect)

### Gas gangrene — clostridial toxins

**C. perfringens myonecrosis toxin (TO)** — **alpha toxin** (haemolysis, myonecrosis, gas gangrene)\
✗ Wrong options: beta · epsilon · iota

## X vs Y

| | **Type 1** | **Type 2** |
|---|---|---|
| Organisms | **Polymicrobial** | **Group A strep** |
| Host | **Diabetic / immunosuppressed** | **Healthy** |
| Frequency | **80–90%** | 5% |

| | **Necrotising fasciitis** | **Cellulitis** |
|---|---|---|
| Progression | Rapid despite antibiotics | Responds |
| Signs | **Bullae, pain out of proportion, shock** | Erythema |
| LRINEC | **≥ 6** | Low |

## How it's asked

**"Most common single isolate?"** → **group A streptococcus**

**"Initial antibiotics, CKD + HIV + IVDU?"** → **linezolid + meropenem**

**"Polymicrobial culture most likely?"** → **diabetic necrotising soft-tissue infection**

**"Diabetic foot spreading despite IV antibiotics — organism?"** → **polymicrobial**

**"True statement?"** → **gram-negative enteric bacilli common in polymicrobial NF**

**"Teen with turf abrasion, bullae, shock — true?"** → **polymicrobial > monomicrobial**

**"Most valuable lab + next step?"** → **WBC > 25; emergent debridement ± amputation**

**"Missing value for LRINEC?"** → **haemoglobin**

**"Worsening on cefazolin, hypotensive?"** → **aggressive debridement of skin, fat + fascia**

**"Diabetic calcaneal ulcer, new bullae?"** → **emergent debridement ± amputation**

**"IVDU hand, CRP 178, WBC 29k?"** → **emergent surgical debridement**

**"Reduces mortality?"** → **shorter time from admission to surgery**

**"Strongest mortality predictor?"** → **vasopressor requirement**

**"True about treatment?"** → **delay > 24 h ↑ mortality**

**"Clostridial myonecrosis toxin (TO)?"** → **alpha**

# Neonatal Forearm Compartment Syndrome

*Trauma · 40 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rare** — case reports; largest series **24 cases / 20 yr**

**When** — **first 24 h of life**, present **at birth**

**Where** — forearm, wrist, hand; **unilateral**; R = L; **dorsum > volar**

| Risk factor | Type |
|---|---|
| **Hypercoagulable — polycythaemia** | Intrinsic |
| **Prematurity**, multiple gestation | — |
| **Oligohydramnios** | Extrinsic |
| Maternal diabetes, abnormal lie, neonatal respiratory distress | — |

### Pathophysiology

**Commonest** — **idiopathic**

**Extrinsic (compression)** — fetal posture, **oligohydramnios**, **umbilical cord loops**, **amniotic bands**, birth trauma

**Intrinsic (clotting)** — **hypercoagulable state** → arterial / venous thrombosis

## 3 Presentation

**Pathognomonic** — **skin lesion in ALL**: **bullae**, erythema, ulcers, **eschar**, distal hand / digit **oedema**, fingertip gangrene

**Nerves** — **radial + PIN > ulnar = median**; ↓ spontaneous movement

**Usually missed** → found only after complications

![Neonatal forearm compartment syndrome](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4003-neonate.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — skeletal changes **late**: **physeal distortion** (widening, flaring, premature closure, angulation), shortening, LLD

**MRI** — **late cases** with full-thickness necrosis + contracture (fasciotomy futile) → map necrosis, guide debridement

**Labs** — **CBC, ESR, CRP normal** (vs infection)

**Compartment pressure — NOT measured** — **no neonatal Δp standards**; **DBP at birth < 40 mmHg** → small rise impairs perfusion

### Differential

| Condition | Distinguishing feature |
|---|---|
| Cellulitis | Mother well, cultures negative |
| **Necrotising fasciitis** | Most easily confused; **skin + subcutaneous only** → **excision**, not fasciotomy; confirmed at surgery |
| Vascular injury | **Absent pulses / Doppler**; with brachial plexus lesions |

## 5 Management

```
Neonatal forearm compartment syndrome
├─ Diagnosed < 24 h → emergency fasciotomy (volar, dorsal, mobile wad + carpal tunnel) ± STSG
├─ Hypercoagulable → anticoagulants / thrombolytics
└─ Late → salvage (debride, neurolysis, release, lengthening, angular correction, tendon reconstruction)
```

**Best outcome** — **fasciotomy within first 24 h** · **salvage inferior**

**6-h-old, 34 wk, oligohydramnios, polycythaemia, bullae** — **neonatal compartment syndrome → fasciotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: cellulitis + antibiotics · NF debridement / HBO · needle manometry

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Ischaemic muscle contracture** | Debridement + release |
| **Fingertip gangrene** | — |
| **Physeal distortion** | Lengthening, angular correction |
| **Nerve dysfunction** | Neurolysis |

**Prognosis** — **missed diagnosis = worst**; **early fasciotomy = best**

## How it's asked

**"Neonate with bullous forearm, oligohydramnios, polycythaemia?"** → **neonatal compartment syndrome; fasciotomy**

**"Why no needle manometry in neonates?"** → **no Δp standards; DBP < 40 mmHg**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Olecranon Fractures

*Trauma · 41 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — 12 / 100,000 / yr · **\~10% of upper-limb fractures** · mean age **\~57**; **bimodal**

**Mechanism** — **direct blow → comminuted** · **indirect (FOOSH) → transverse / oblique**

**Transolecranon fracture-dislocation** — severe axial load; **distal humerus driven through olecranon** = **anterior** dislocation; **PRUJ intact** (vs Monteggia)

### Anatomy

**Greater sigmoid (semilunar) notch** — olecranon + coronoid; articulates with trochlea (flexion-extension + stability)

**Triceps** — inserts posterior proximal ulna, blends with periosteum (**radial nerve, C7**) · **anconeus** — lateral olecranon (radial nerve)

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — posterior elbow · **palpable gap** → displaced / comminuted

**Can't extend elbow** — **extensor (triceps) mechanism discontinuous**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**True lateral** — **essential** for fracture pattern · radiocapitellar view → radial head / capitellum · CT → comminuted planning

### Classifications

| Colton | Description |
|---|---|
| **Nondisplaced** | Displacement **doesn't increase with flexion** |
| Avulsion | Displaced |
| Oblique / transverse | Displaced |
| Comminuted | Displaced |
| **Fracture-dislocation** | **Transolecranon** |

| Schatzker | Pattern |
|---|---|
| **A** | Simple transverse |
| **B** | Transverse **impacted** |
| **C** | **Oblique** |
| **D** | **Comminuted** |
| **E** | More distal, extra-articular |
| **F** | Fracture-dislocation |

![Schatzker classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1022-schatzker-classification.jpg)

| AO | Pattern |
|---|---|
| A | Extra-articular |
| B | Intra-articular |
| C | Intra-articular **radial head + olecranon** |

**Mayo** — comminution, displacement, fracture-dislocation

## 5 Management

```
Olecranon fracture
├─ Nondisplaced, extensor intact → cast 45–90°, motion at 1 wk
├─ Displaced, transverse, no comminution → tension band (or IM screw)
├─ Comminuted / oblique past coronoid / Monteggia / fracture-dislocation → plate
└─ Elderly low-demand → nonop or excision + triceps advancement (< 50% joint)
```

### Operative criteria

| Implant | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Tension band / IM screw** | **Transverse, no comminution**, through **midpoint of trochlear notch**, **proximal to coronoid** |
| **Plate** | **Comminution**, **oblique extending distal to coronoid**, **coronoid involvement**, **Monteggia**, **fracture-dislocation**, articular impaction, **open** |
| **Excision + triceps advancement** | **Elderly osteoporotic**, **< 50% of joint**, too comminuted / small, nonunion; **no instability** |
| **Nonop cast** | Nondisplaced + intact extensor; **displaced in low-demand elderly** |

### Tension band wiring — indications & biomechanics

**Mechanism** — **converts triceps distraction (tension) into articular compression**\
✗ Wrong options: neutralisation · shear · bending

**Best for** — **transverse fracture through midpoint of trochlear notch**\
✗ Wrong options: comminuted · coronoid · Monteggia · oblique distal

**Technique** — K-wires **engaging anterior cortex** (↓ migration) but **no over-penetration**; **18-gauge wire / thick suture** figure-of-8 through drill hole

**Cons** — **hardware removal 40–80%**; **no axial stability in comminution**; can **narrow sigmoid notch** if comminuted

**Relative TBW indications** — tip fractures, transverse, osteoporotic, displaced

### Plate fixation — comminution, fracture-dislocation & open fractures

**Comminution** — **the** indication for plate over TBW\
✗ Wrong options: displacement · intra-articular · obliquity alone

**Comminuted, past coronoid (24-yr)** — **plate + screws**\
✗ Wrong options: IMN · TBW + IM screw · triceps advancement · TEA

**Articular comminution + depressed fragment (62-yr)** — **plate**

**Bridge plating** — **comminuted, non-reconstructable, young / active (45-yr)**\
✗ Wrong options: osteotomy fixation · simple transverse · 91-yr osteoporotic (excise) · child apophysis

**Transolecranon fracture-dislocation (open or closed)** — **plate** → best function\
✗ Wrong options: TBW / IM screw (need cortical contact) · excision (anterior instability) · cast

**Technique** — **dorsal (tension) side**; **lag screws for oblique**; ⅓ tubular too weak for comminution; triceps advanced over plate; **removal \~20%**

![Plate and screw fixation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1022-plate-and-screws.jpg)

### Elderly low-demand — nonoperative or excision with triceps advancement

**82-yr nursing home, comminuted 25% joint, osteopenic** — **cast in 90° flexion, short immobilisation then ROM** · Duckworth: good long-term results\
✗ Wrong options: 45° × 8 wk (stiffness) · pinning · TBW · locking plate

**79-yr RA + osteoporosis, comminuted proximal 30%** — **fragment excision + triceps advancement**\
✗ Wrong options: IM screw · TBW · TEA · lag + plate

**Excision ideal** — **85-yr, comminuted, < 50% of proximal joint**\
✗ Wrong options: young · > 50% · coronoid-level oblique

**Technique** — triceps reattached **adjacent to articular surface** with nonabsorbable sutures through drill holes

**Downside** — **↓ extension strength**; **instability** if ligament injury missed

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Symptomatic hardware** | **Most common** (subcutaneous; poor seating) |
| **Stiffness** | **\~50%**; rarely functional |
| **HO** | ↑ with **head injury** |
| **AIN injury** | **K-wire over-penetration of anterior (volar) cortex** — can't flex thumb IP / index DIP → observe |
| **↓ Forearm rotation** | **K-wire through volar cortex** or too radial |
| PTOA, ulnar nerve symptoms, ↓ extension strength | — |
| Nonunion | **Rare** |

### Postoperative stiffness — therapy & splinting

**Arc 40–90° at 2 months, rotation preserved** — **formal therapy + static progressive splinting** first\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic / open release · hardware removal · steroid + aggressive ROM

**Failed nonop** — limited open release, interposition, arthroscopy, **TEA if > 65**

## X vs Y

| | **Tension band** | **Plate** |
|---|---|---|
| Fracture | **Transverse, simple** | **Comminuted, oblique past coronoid, dislocation** |
| Principle | **Tension → compression** | Neutralisation / bridging |
| Removal | **40–80%** | \~20% |

| | **Transolecranon dislocation** | **Monteggia** |
|---|---|---|
| PRUJ | **Intact** | **Disrupted** |
| Radial head | Moves with ulna | **Dislocated** |

## How it's asked

**"Force at articular surface with tension band?"** → **compression**

**"Best indication for tension band?"** → **transverse through midpoint of trochlear notch**

**"Comminuted, distal past coronoid?"** → **plate + screws**

**"Articular comminution + depressed fragment?"** → **ORIF with plate**

**"Bridge plating most appropriate?"** → **comminuted fracture in 45-yr-old**

**"Transolecranon fracture-dislocation — stability?"** → **plate fixation**

**"Justifies plate choice?"** → **comminution**

**"Plate over tension band when?"** → **comminuted**

**"Open comminuted transolecranon — best function?"** → **ORIF + plate**

**"58-yr osteopenic, comminuted, marginal impaction?"** → **ORIF + plate**

**"Most common TBW complication?"** → **symptomatic implants**

**"Nerve at risk with anterior K-wire over-penetration?"** → **AIN**

**"Impaired forearm rotation after TBW?"** → **K-wire through volar cortex**

**"79-yr RA, comminuted proximal 30%?"** → **fragment excision + triceps advancement**

**"82-yr low-demand, comminuted 25%?"** → **cast in 90° flexion**

**"Excision + triceps reattachment ideal for?"** → **85-yr, comminuted, < 50% of joint**

**"Stiff 40–90° at 2 months?"** → **therapy + static progressive splinting**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Open Fractures Management

*Trauma · 42 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **30.7 / 100,000 / yr** · average age **45**

**Commonest sites** — **tibia + finger phalanx** · tibia = commonest long-bone fracture, **up to \~24% open**

**Associated injuries** — **30%** · **compartment syndrome still possible** with an open wound

**Wound near a fracture** — treat as **open until proven otherwise**

**Psychiatric history** — **pre-existing depression = independent predictor of complications**; depression + substance abuse commonest in polytrauma

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — wound size **may not reflect deep damage** · **ABI if vascular concern** (> 0.9 normal; **< 0.9 → vascular consult + angiogram**)

**Traumatic arthrotomy** — **saline load test** or **CT (more sensitive for knee)**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — joint above + below · **CT** — periarticular injury, **knee arthrotomy (intra-articular air)**

### Classification & scoring (Gustilo, Ganga, AO soft tissue)

| Gustilo (TO) | Wound | Soft tissue |
|---|---|---|
| I | **< 1 cm**, clean | Minimal |
| II | **1–10 cm**, moderate | **No periosteal stripping** |
| **IIIA** | Any size (**high energy**) | **Local coverage possible** |
| **IIIB** | — | **Periosteal stripping + exposed bone → flap needed** |
| **IIIC** | — | **Arterial injury needing repair** |

**6 × 4 cm dirty crush wound, distal tibia, exposed bone + periosteal stripping (TO)** — **IIIB**

**Automatically type III (TO)** — **gunshot**, **farm injury**

**Gustilo drawbacks (TO)** — many modifications, wide IIIB spectrum, wound-size based, **doesn't address salvage**, **poor interobserver reliability**

| Ganga Hospital Open Injury Score (TO) | Points |
|---|---|
| **Covering (skin + fascia)** | 1–5 (circumferential skin loss = 5) |
| **Skeletal (bone + joint)** | 1–5 (**bone loss ≥ 4 cm = 5**) |
| **Functional (musculotendinous + nerve)** | 1–5 (loss of ≥ 2 compartments / subtotal amputation = 5) |
| **Comorbidities — 2 each** | **Debridement > 12 h**, **age > 65**, sewage / farm contamination, **SBP < 90**, compartment syndrome / other major injury same limb, DM / cardiorespiratory, **polytrauma ISS > 25** / fat embolism |

**Ganga facts (TO)** — designed for **IIIB**; **scored AFTER debridement** (not in ED before); **max 29**; **better reliability than Gustilo**; **doesn't predict survival** of polytrauma

**Ganga thresholds (TO)** — **≤ 14 → salvage** · **15–16 = grey zone** · **≥ 17 → amputation** · ("≥ 14 amputation threshold" in another TO item — read the options)

**AO soft-tissue (TO)** — **extensive closed degloving = IC4** (IC closed vs IO open)

**Tscherne** — closed / open soft-tissue grading

## 5 Management

```
Open fracture
├─ ED: IV antibiotics (ASAP, < 1–3 h), tetanus, splint, saline dressing
├─ OR: debridement (< 24 h; IIIB tibia < 12 h; gross contamination 6–12 h) + low-pressure saline / soap
│      └─ temporary ex-fix (high-energy LL) ± antibiotic beads / NPWT
└─ Definitive fixation + coverage < 7 days → bone defect: Masquelet / fibula / transport
```

### Antibiotic prophylaxis — choice, timing & infection risk

**First thing to do (grade III tibia)** — **IV antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: bedside I&D · splint · CTA · OR first

**Timing** — **as soon as possible**; **> 3 h → ↑ infection (4.7% → 7.4%)**; **within 1 h of arrival**; **> 66 min independent risk in type III tibia** (OB)

**Most modifiable infection risk** — **delay in antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: free vs rotational flap · flap at 4 days · nail vs plate · debridement at 15 h · personal transport · reamed nailing

**Septic nonunion after 15-h transport delay** — **prolonged time to antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: coverage < 7 days · unreamed nail · vitamin D · low-pressure irrigation

**Multidisciplinary training** — **↓ time to antibiotics**

| Wound | Regimen |
|---|---|
| **Gustilo I / II** (e.g. 3 cm wound) | **Cefazolin** (clindamycin / vancomycin if allergic; fluoroquinolone 2nd line) |
| **Gustilo III** | **Cefazolin + aminoglycoside** · or 3rd-gen cephalosporin alone · or vancomycin + cefepime |
| **Farm / soil / bowel** (e.g. hay loft, lawnmower) | **+ high-dose penicillin → Clostridium** |
| Fresh water | Fluoroquinolone or 3rd / 4th-gen cephalosporin |
| Salt water | Doxycycline + ceftazidime or fluoroquinolone |

**Clostridium** — **gram-positive, obligate anaerobic, spore-forming rod**, **catalase negative**; C. perfringens (gas gangrene), C. tetani, C. difficile, **C. botulinum (botulism)**

**Duration** — **24 h after injury if closed primarily** · **24 h after final closure** (**48 h type III**) (Orthobullets) · **72 h or until closure** for grade III tibia (TO) · 24–72 h after I&D (Gustilo note) — read the options

### Tetanus prophylaxis

**Booster within 5 yr** — **no prophylaxis**\
✗ Wrong options: vaccine · TIG

**Grade II, no booster since high school** — **Td** + cefazolin within 3 h + I&D

**Toxoid** — **0.5 mL** any age · **TIG** — < 5 yr 75 U · 5–10 yr 125 U · > 10 yr 250 U · **separate syringes + sites, IM**

**Depends on** — vaccination history (3 doses), last dose, wound severity

### Debridement as the key infection deterrent

**Most important infection deterrent** — **surgical debridement of devitalised tissue**\
✗ Wrong options: bacitracin · NPWT · 6-h window · pulse lavage

**6-hour rule** — **not supported** · **delayed debridement doesn't ↑ infection if antibiotics given** (TO) · **< 24 h** (GOLIATH: type III); **IIIB tibia < 12 h**; gross contamination **6–12 h**

**Technique** — extend wound longitudinally; **remove bone fragments without soft-tissue attachment**; debride **beyond** zone of injury; repeat every **24–48 h**

**Exception to I&D** — **low-energy puncture open fracture in children**

**ED** — remove gross debris, **don't remove bone**, saline-soaked dressing; **no aggressive / antiseptic irrigation in ED**

**Most common source of infection (TO)** — **hospital-acquired**\
✗ Wrong options: site of injury · soil · haematogenous

**Infection risk factors (TO)** — **inadequate skeletal stabilisation**, organic contamination, poor debridement, dead space, debridement > 24 h, shock, ARDS, age > 65, DM, smoking, compartment syndrome, malnutrition

### Irrigation — pressure & solution (FLOW)

**Pressure** — **low-pressure (bulb / gravity) = high-pressure** for **infection + union**; **cheaper**; pulse lavage damages bone, pushes bacteria deep, ↑ rebound contamination at 48 h\
✗ Wrong options: nerve injury · wound problems

**Solution** — **saline (± castile soap)**; **soap < antibiotic solution for wound-healing problems** · **saline = betadine** · peroxide / iodine / alcohol harm osteoblasts + cartilage

**Volume** — **3 L per Gustilo grade** (**9 L for type III**)

**After debridement of IIIB** — **saline, low-pressure gravity flow**

**Low-pressure parameters (TO)** — 14 psi / 550 pulses·min⁻¹ vs high 70 psi / 1,050

### Negative pressure wound therapy

**Benefits** — **↑ granulation tissue**, **↑ angiogenesis**, ↓ oedema, ↑ wound-bed flow, ↓ complex reconstruction, **outpatient** care\
✗ Wrong options: **↓ angiogenesis** · ↓ bacterial count · ↓ hospital stay · fewer debridements

**NPWT vs conventional (WOLLF)** — **higher cost**; no difference in deep infection, amputation, LOS, disability at 1 yr

**Not an indication (TO)** — **ulcer with necrotic wound bed**\
✗ Wrong options: necrotising fasciitis post-debridement · large diabetic ulcer · dehiscence

**Open > 7 days** — ↑ infection

### Local antibiotics

**Beads / spacer** — PMMA + **heat-stable vancomycin / tobramycin**; heavy contamination, large soft-tissue or bony defect

### Temporising spanning external fixation

**Polytrauma, open distal femur + proximal tibia** — **I&D + knee-spanning ex-fix**\
✗ Wrong options: traction · I&D alone · ORIF · nails

**Child (10 yr), open tibia, gravel** — **I&D + ex-fix** · flexible nails also an option\
✗ Wrong options: reamed nail (physes) · K-wires · cast · submuscular plate

**Definitive internal fixation vs ex-fix** — **IF: faster union, better function, shorter stay**; **definitive ex-fix → poor function (LEAP)**

### Soft-tissue coverage timing — flap within 7 days

**Target** — **< 7 days**; **infection odds +16% per day beyond day 7** · flap **> 7 days → ↑ amputation, osteomyelitis, pneumonia** (DVT same)

**Older thresholds** — ≤ 72 h (early studies, OB218814) · **LEAP: no difference at 3 days** · closure > 5 days risk (one OB item)

**Flap by tibial level** — **proximal ⅓ → gastrocnemius** · **middle ⅓ → soleus** · **distal ⅓ → free flap (within 7 days)**

**ORIF before / at / after fasciotomy closure** — no difference

### Bone defects — induced membrane (Masquelet) & vascularised fibula

**Masquelet stage 1** — **debridement + cement spacer + temporary fixation**

**Stage 2 at 4–6 wk** — **remove spacer, PRESERVE membrane, graft into cavity (around retained nail)**\
✗ Wrong options: debride membrane · exchange nail · remove nail · spacer only

**Membrane biology** — BMP-2, **VEGF, IL-6, type I collagen** — **peak at 1 month (\~4 wk)**, baseline \~6 months · defects **up to 25 cm**

**7 cm tibial defect, refuses transport** — **ORIF + vascularised fibular autograft** (osteogenic + inductive + conductive)\
✗ Wrong options: DBM · calcium phosphate · cortical allograft (not osteogenic) · amputation

**Other** — distraction osteogenesis (transport)

### Aseptic nonunion after nailing — exchange nailing

**"Elephant foot", gaps on 4 cortices after unreamed nail** — **hypertrophic nonunion = lack of stability**\
✗ Wrong options: infection · blood supply (atrophic) · vitamin D · OI

**Treatment** — **reamed exchange nailing** (stiffness ∝ diameter⁴, isthmic fit, reaming graft)\
✗ Wrong options: observe · antibiotic nail · new locking screws · unreamed exchange

**Nonunion rates** — **up to 17% closed, 40% open** tibia

**SPRINT** — reamed benefit **only in closed** tibia; open: reamed = unreamed

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Fracture-related infection** | **< 1% type I → 30% type III** |
| **Osteomyelitis** | **1.8–27%**; **tibia commonest** |
| Risk factors | **Blast mechanism**, acute amputation, **coverage > 7 days**, higher Gustilo, contamination, **tibial location** |
| **Depression** | **Commonest post-op issue** after open tibia (✗ Wrong options: arthritis · malunion) |

### Outcomes & infection risk factors (blast, definitive ex-fix, location)

**↑ Infection** — **blast mechanism**\
✗ Wrong options: coverage 3–7 days · NPWT · debridement 6–24 h · grafting > 3 months

**Poor function in type III tibia** — **definitive external fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: coverage < 3 days · grafting at 3 months · debridement 6–24 h · free flap

**Ballistic fracture — highest infection** — **tibial shaft** (subcutaneous) · **blunt > penetrating** infection\
✗ Wrong options: radius, femur, metacarpal, humerus

### Psychological sequelae — depression

**Pre-existing depression** — **independent predictor of complications**, ↑ readmission; psychiatric comorbidity **up to 40%**; ortho patients **less** likely to get psych follow-up

**Predictor of depressive symptoms** — **previous psychiatric diagnosis** (also unemployment)\
✗ Wrong options: employment · implant · male · private insurance

### Jehovah's Witness — bloodless resuscitation

**Unstable intra-op, GCS 8, open pelvis** — **plasma expanders**\
✗ Wrong options: blood · cell saver · ask family · ethics delay

**Prohibited** — allogeneic blood, pre-donated autologous · **personal choice** — cell salvage, haemodilution, EPO, dialysis

## X vs Y

| | **Low-pressure** | **High-pressure lavage** |
|---|---|---|
| Infection / union | Equal | Equal |
| Cost | **Lower** | Higher |
| Tissue damage | Less | **Bone + soft tissue damage** |

| | **Gustilo** | **Ganga** |
|---|---|---|
| Timing | Intra-op | **After debridement** |
| Purpose | Grade + antibiotics | **Salvage vs amputation (IIIB)** |
| Reliability | Poor | **Better** |

![Type IIIA open tibia fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1004-clinical-picture-of-type-iiia-open-tibia.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Grade 3 tibia antibiotic duration (TO)?"** → **72 hours**

**"Lawnmower soil wound — penicillin organism true?"** → **may cause botulism (Clostridium)**

**"3 cm wound, exposed bone — antibiotic?"** → **cefazolin**

**"Factor increasing infection (10 h to hospital)?"** → **delay in IV antibiotics**

**"Farm injury, 0.5 cm wound?"** → **cephalosporin + aminoglycoside + penicillin**

**"Penicillin covers?"** → **Clostridium**

**"Septic nonunion — associated factor?"** → **prolonged time to antibiotics**

**"Independent infection risk (zip line)?"** → **timing of antibiotics**

**"Multidisciplinary training effect?"** → **↓ time to antibiotics**

**"IIIB — factor most ↑ infection?"** → **no prompt antibiotics**

**"Grade II, no recent booster?"** → **I&D, cefazolin within 3 h, Td**

**"First thing for grade III tibia?"** → **IV antibiotics**

**"Booster 12 months ago?"** → **no tetanus prophylaxis**

**"Irrigation after debridement?"** → **saline, low-pressure gravity flow**

**"High vs low pressure — true?"** → **no difference in union**

**"Shotgun hand — accurate?"** → **saline = betadine**

**"True about debridement (TO)?"** → **delay doesn't ↑ infection with antibiotics**

**"Most important infection deterrent?"** → **surgical debridement of devitalised tissue**

**"Not an NPWT indication (TO)?"** → **ulcer with necrotic bed**

**"NPWT benefits EXCEPT?"** → **decreases angiogenesis**

**"NPWT vs damp-to-dry advantage?"** → **accelerated granulation tissue**

**"NPWT vs conventional — true?"** → **higher cost**

**"Flap > 7 days?"** → **↑ amputation**

**"Distal ⅓ tibia coverage?"** → **free flap within 7 days**

**"Masquelet stage 2?"** → **remove spacer, preserve membrane, graft cavity**

**"Masquelet membrane — true?"** → **1-month membrane has highest IL-6**

**"7 cm defect, no transport?"** → **ORIF + vascularised fibula**

**"Masquelet stage 1?"** → **debridement, cement spacer, temporary fixation**

**"True about compound fractures (TO)?"** → **inadequate stabilisation ↑ infection**

**"Commonest source of infection (TO)?"** → **hospital-acquired**

**"Poor function in type III tibia?"** → **definitive ex-fix**

**"↑ Infection risk (blast soldier)?"** → **blast mechanism**

**"Ballistic fracture with highest infection?"** → **tibial shaft**

**"10-yr-old open tibia with gravel?"** → **I&D + ex-fix**

**"Polytrauma, open distal femur + proximal tibia?"** → **I&D + spanning ex-fix**

**"Elephant-foot nonunion — cause?"** → **lack of stability**

**"Hypertrophic nonunion, broken locks?"** → **reamed exchange nailing**

**"Open tibia — highest post-op risk?"** → **depression**

**"Psychiatric comorbidity — true?"** → **pre-existing depression predicts complications**

**"Depression risk factor?"** → **previous psychiatric diagnosis**

**"Jehovah's Witness unstable?"** → **plasma expanders**

**"Ganga — false (TO)?"** → **predicts polytrauma survival**

**"6 × 4 cm crush, exposed bone (TO)?"** → **IIIB**

**"Closed extensive degloving AO (TO)?"** → **IC4**

**"Ganga — EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **scored in ED before debridement**

**"Ganga grey zone (TO)?"** → **15–16**

# Osteomyelitis - Adult

*Trauma · 43 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & sites

| Host | Typical site |
|---|---|
| **Dialysis** | **Spine + ribs**; **sternoclavicular joint** |
| **IV drug use** | **Medial / lateral clavicle**; SC / AC joint (**Pseudomonas**) |
| Diabetes | Foot, decubitus ulcers |
| Adult haematogenous | **Vertebrae (commonest)** |

**Risk factors** — trauma / surgery, immunocompromise, IVDU, poor vascularity, DM, sickle cell, neuropathy, malnutrition

### Mechanism

| Route | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Haematogenous** | **Commonest in children**; **vertebrae in adults**; **S. aureus** |
| **Contiguous** | Surgery, trauma, wounds, **poor vascularity**; bacterial > mycobacterial / fungal |
| **Direct inoculation** | Penetrating, **open fracture**, surgical contamination |

### Organisms

| Age / setting | Organism |
|---|---|
| Neonate (< 4 mo) | S. aureus, Enterobacter, **GAS + GBS** |
| 4 mo–4 yr | S. aureus, GAS, **Kingella kingae**, Enterobacter |
| 4 yr–adult | **S. aureus (80%)**, GAS, H. influenzae |
| **Adult** | **S. aureus** ± Enterobacter / Strep |
| **Sickle cell** | **Salmonella** (S. aureus still commonest) |
| **IVDU (SC / AC joint)**, nail through rubber sole | **Pseudomonas** |
| HIV + cat scratch | **Bartonella** |
| Immunosuppressed, long IV lines, TPN | Fungal |
| Pott's disease | TB |

**Mycotic (infected) aneurysm (TO)** — **S. aureus** (+ Salmonella); "mycotic" ≠ fungal\
✗ Wrong options: H. influenzae · Strep viridans · Pseudomonas

### Biofilm & bacterial growth stages

| Stage | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Planktonic** | **Free-floating** bacteria **attach to inert substrate** + apoptosis → biofilm matrix |
| **Sessile** | **Quorum sensing** → matures; **no-growth** state; virulence genes; **antibiotic resistant** |
| Dispersion | Releases planktonic bacteria |

**Biofilm** — **extracellular polymeric substance / exopolysaccharide (glycocalyx)**; poor antibiotic penetration + ↓ metabolism · **PJI > 4 wk → explant**

### Metaphyseal osteomyelitis & joint spread

**Intracapsular metaphyses (TO)** — **proximal radius**, **proximal humerus**, **proximal femur**, **distal tibia / fibula** → septic arthritis · **NOT head of ulna**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain; **fever (acute)**; sepsis signs

**Draining sinus** — **chronic**; **probe to bone → chronic osteomyelitis** (diabetic ulcer probing bone = osteomyelitis, not Charcot — improves with elevation)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Radiographs** | Lag **\~2 wk**; need **50% bone loss**; chronic: **sequestrum** (dead bone, nidus), **involucrum** (new bone around), **Brodie abscess**, sclerosis, periosteal reaction |
| CT | Necrotic bone, planning (hardware artifact) |
| **MRI** | **Best for early diagnosis + localisation**; **negative rules out**; **may overestimate extent**; T2 oedema |
| **Penumbra sign (T1)** | **Bright rim** (vascularised membrane) around dark abscess, dark sclerotic outer rim → **intraosseous abscess → surgery** |
| **Tc bone scan** | Normal X-ray + no MRI; **sensitive, not specific**; negative rules out |
| **Gallium** | Diabetic foot / no MRI; **cellulitis false positive** |

![Penumbra sign on T1 MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1057-t1-sequence-showing-a-penumbra-sign-in-the-distal-femur.jpg)

### Genes & lab markers

| Test | Fact |
|---|---|
| WBC | **Raised in only ⅓** of acute |
| **ESR** | Raised **\~90%**; **fall = favourable** |
| **CRP** | **Most sensitive (97%)**; falls faster than ESR |
| Blood cultures | Often negative; guide haematogenous cases |
| **Sinus tract culture** | **Unreliable** (contaminants) |
| **Bone culture** | **Gold standard** for antibiotics (off antibiotics) |

**Histology** — **acute: live osteocytes + neutrophils** · **chronic: empty lacunae, marrow fibrosis, lymphocytes + plasma cells** (plasma cell = chronic)

**Most accurate diagnosis vs Ewing (TO)** — **bone biopsy** · **"biopsy all infections, culture all tumours"**\
✗ Wrong options: indium scan · MRI (next step) · LDH

### Classification

**Timing** — acute **< 2 wk** · subacute 1–several months · chronic > several months

| Cierny-Mader stage (anatomic) | Extent |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Medullary** |
| **2** | **Superficial (cortex only)** |
| **3** | **Localised** — cortex + medulla, **stable** |
| **4** | **Diffuse** — full thickness, **unstable** |

| Host | Status |
|---|---|
| **A** | Normal |
| **BL / BS** | **Locally / systemically compromised** (smoking, venous stasis) |
| **C** | **Treatment worse than infection** |

**Cierny-Mader uses** — **host status + extent of infected bone** · e.g. medullary, smoker + venous stasis → **1B**

## 5 Management

```
Adult osteomyelitis
├─ Acute → culture-directed IV antibiotics
│      └─ fails / abscess / subacute → I&D + antibiotics
├─ Chronic → debride to bleeding bone (paprika), remove sequestrum + non-essential hardware, manage dead space, stabilise
└─ Surgery not feasible → suppressive antibiotics · unsalvageable → amputation
```

### Chronic post-traumatic osteomyelitis — debridement, hardware removal & local antibiotics

**Principles** — remove **all necrotic tissue + sequestrum**; debride to **punctate bleeding ("paprika sign")**; **remove non-essential hardware**; **stability required** (**ex-fix preferred**); dead-space management; culture-directed antibiotics; often **staged**

**Dead space** — vascularised bone graft, local / free flaps, **PMMA antibiotic beads**, VAC, Masquelet, Ilizarov

**Draining sinus 18 months after GSW** — **surgical debridement + culture + IV antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: oral · needle biopsy + IV · tumour work-up

**Infected nonunion with hardware** — **I&D, remove hardware, over-ream canal, ex-fix + culture-directed antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: suppression · AKA · NPWT without stability · retain hardware + acute graft

**MRSA infected nonunion with nail + sinus** — **debride, excise sinus (rule out Marjolin), exchange to antibiotic-cement nail + 6 wk IV**\
✗ Wrong options: retain nail · larger nail · remove without stabilising · add ring fixator

**Localised, healed tibia, low-virulence S. aureus** — **curettage + absorbable antibiotic beads + IV 1–4 wk + oral 6 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: segmental resection · amputation · free flap

**Stable patient, multiple sinuses after forearm ORIF** — **scheduled (non-urgent) I&D + hardware removal**\
✗ Wrong options: suppression · sinus culture · MRI · urgent admission

**Antibiotic nail** — PMMA + **vancomycin + tobramycin**; **peak elution 24 h**; elutes **up to 4 months** · tobramycin concentration-dependent, vancomycin time-dependent

**Prognosis better** — **metaphyseal > diaphyseal** · worse: polymicrobial, **MRSA**, contralateral amputation, poor host

**Medical failure** — infection **> 3 months**, multidrug-resistant, polymicrobial\
✗ Wrong options: CRP > 20 · gram-positive · long bone

**Adjuncts** — **suppressive antibiotics** (high relapse when stopped) · **hyperbaric O₂** (refractory)

**Recurrence** — **\~30%** despite treatment

![Paprika sign — debridement to bleeding bone](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1057-paprika-sign-as-evidence-of-sufficient-debridemnt-of-bone.jpg)

### Negative pressure dressing mechanism

**Benefits** — removes inhibitory interstitial fluid, protein + electrolytes; ↓ slime / anaerobic colonisation; arteriolar dilation → granulation; **↓ capillary afterload → ↑ inflow**; draws edges in; **VEGF release** · **EXCEPT "↑ capillary afterload"**

### Vertebral osteomyelitis & discitis

**Dialysis, back pain, ↑ ESR, MRI: disc T2 bright + T1 dark, endplate enhancement** — **osteomyelitis + discitis**\
✗ Wrong options: lymphoma · renal osteodystrophy · metastasis (disc spared) · osteoporotic fracture

**MSSA bacteraemia, L3–4, neuro intact** — **IV antibiotics + lumbar orthosis**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy (organism known) · repeat MRI · surgery

### CRMO

**CRMO / SAPHO (TO)** — **associated with skin lesions** (palmoplantar pustulosis, pustular psoriasis); **no organism**; autoimmune; **antibiotics no role**

| Variant (TO) | Who | Site |
|---|---|---|
| **Sternocostoclavicular hyperostosis** | **Men 40–50** | Sternum, medial clavicles, spine |
| **Subacute recurrent multifocal** | **Children + adolescents** | Metaphyses (distal femur, tibia), medial clavicle |

## 6 Complications

### Marjolin ulcer (squamous cell carcinoma)

**Marjolin ulcer** — **SCC (\~90%)** in a **chronic draining sinus**; **\~1%** of chronic osteomyelitis; tibia > femur; growing tissue + **foul smell**

**Precursor** — **chronic osteomyelitis**\
✗ Wrong options: adamantinoma · OFD · EG · cortical desmoid

**Treatment** — **wide resection** (Orthobullets) · **amputation** usually (OB) — read the options

**Other** — persistence, sepsis, amputation

## X vs Y

| | **Sequestrum** | **Involucrum** |
|---|---|---|
| What | **Dead bone — nidus** | **New bone around it** |
| Treatment | **Remove** | — |

| | **Acute** | **Chronic** |
|---|---|---|
| Histology | **Neutrophils**, live osteocytes | **Plasma cells**, empty lacunae, fibrosis |
| Treatment | Antibiotics ± drainage | **Debridement** essential |

## How it's asked

**"Better prognosis in chronic tibial osteomyelitis?"** → **metaphyseal infection**

**"Medullary, smoker + venous stasis?"** → **stage 1B**

**"Cierny-Mader considers?"** → **host status + extent of bone**

**"Draining sinus after GSW?"** → **debridement, culture, IV antibiotics**

**"Infected nonunion with hardware, 2 yr?"** → **I&D, remove hardware, over-ream, ex-fix, culture-directed antibiotics**

**"MRSA sinus after nail, 4 yr?"** → **debride, excise sinus, antibiotic cement nail, 6 wk IV**

**"Localised chronic tibial OM 42 yr later?"** → **curettage + absorbable beads + short IV + oral**

**"Arrow on CT (dead bone)?"** → **sequestrum**

**"Forearm sinuses after ORIF, stable?"** → **scheduled I&D + hardware removal**

**"Tissue growing from 35-yr sinus?"** → **squamous cell carcinoma**

**"Precursor of SCC over tibia?"** → **chronic osteomyelitis**

**"Peak elution of antibiotic nail?"** → **24 hours**

**"Free bacteria attaching to substrate?"** → **planktonic**

**"Dialysis patient with disc destruction?"** → **osteomyelitis + discitis**

**"MSSA discitis, neuro intact?"** → **lumbar orthosis + IV antibiotics**

**"Most accurate test (TO)?"** → **bone biopsy**

**"Dialysis + SC joint swelling?"** → **chronic osteomyelitis**

**"Diabetic ulcer probing to bone?"** → **osteomyelitis**

**"Needs surgery?"** → **penumbra sign on MRI**

**"Post ex-fix wrist infection image?"** → **Brodie's abscess**

**"Cell suggesting chronic OM?"** → **plasma cell**

**"NPWT benefits EXCEPT?"** → **↑ capillary afterload**

**"OM → septic arthritis EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **head of ulna**

**"Mycotic aneurysm organism (TO)?"** → **Staphylococcus aureus**

**"CRMO — true (TO)?"** → **associated with skin lesions**
