# Other Trauma Topics

*Trauma · 44 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Transfer to Level 1 trauma centre

**Associated with transfer** — **multiple medical comorbidities**\
✗ Wrong options: female · private insurance · Caucasian · ISS < 10

**Unnecessary transfers associated with** — **African-American race, comorbidities, Medicaid, male sex** · severe polytrauma → better outcomes at Level 1

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Associated Lisfranc injury

**Cuboid compression ("nutcracker") fracture** — twisting, midfoot swelling + ecchymosis → **look for subtle Lisfranc injury**\
✗ Wrong options: peroneal tear · lateral process talus · talar neck · deltoid

## 5 Management

### Common peroneal nerve palsy — tendon transfer

**Lacerated CPN, 1 yr, no anterior / lateral function, posterior compartments 5/5** — **split posterior tibial tendon transfer**\
✗ Wrong options: split anterior tibial (denervated) · gastrocsoleus recession (no contracture) · subtalar fusion (doesn't fix drop) · FHL transfer

### Nonunion — compression plating

**Hypertrophic forearm nonunion (1 yr)** — **compression plating alone** (stability problem, biology intact)\
✗ Wrong options: bracing · ICBG (atrophic) · reamed IMN (not used in forearm) · stimulator (atrophic adjunct)

### Masquelet induced-membrane technique

**Stage 1** — debride, **antibiotic cement spacer**, provisional fixation (ex-fix or internal) → 6 wk culture-specific antibiotics if infected, repeat stage 1 as needed

**Stage 2 at 4–6 wk** (peak **BMP, VEGF, IL-6**) — incise membrane, remove spacer, **autograft (RIA or ICBG — equivalent)**, close membrane, definitive fixation

**True** — **induced membrane prevents graft resorption**\
✗ Wrong options: large articular defects (use allograft / prosthesis) · ICBG > RIA · stage 2 at 8 wk · antibiotics disrupt membrane

**Uses** — tibia (original, 2000), femur, humerus, **forearm** (e.g. GSW defects)

## X vs Y

| | **Hypertrophic nonunion** | **Atrophic nonunion** |
|---|---|---|
| Problem | **Instability** | **Biology** |
| Treatment | **Compression plate** | Plate + **graft** ± stimulator |

## How it's asked

**"Cuboid nutcracker — problematic associated injury?"** → **Lisfranc injury**

**"CPN laceration, PTT 5/5?"** → **split posterior tibial tendon transfer**

**"Hypertrophic forearm nonunion?"** → **compression plating**

**"Masquelet — true?"** → **membrane prevents graft resorption**

**"Factor associated with Level 1 transfer?"** → **multiple medical comorbidities**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Patella Fracture

*Trauma · 45 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1% of skeletal injuries** · **6–9% open** · M:F 2:1 · age 20–50

**Associated** — femoral neck fracture, **posterior wall acetabulum**, knee dislocation (dashboard)

### Anatomy

**Patella** — **largest sesamoid**; cartilage on **superior ¾** (up to 1 cm), **inferior ¼ non-articular**; **lateral facet larger**

**Function** — ↑ extensor power **30–50%** (moves line of pull anterior)

**MPFL** — medial epicondyle ↔ adductor tubercle → upper ⅔ medial patella; restrains lateral translation **0–30°**

**Quadriceps tendon layers** — superficial **rectus femoris** · middle **VM + VL** · deep **vastus intermedius**

**Retinaculum** — fascia lata, VM, VL → **repair with fixation**

### Patellar blood supply

**Ring** — genicular arteries (descending / supreme, superior + inferior medial + lateral, anterior tibial recurrent) → **mid-patellar + polar systems**

**Dominant supply** — **distal (inferior) pole**, **inferomedial** quadrant → its excision **most ↓ perfusion**\
✗ Wrong options: superior pole · superolateral (least)

![Blood supply of the patella](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1042-blood-supply-to-patella.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

| Mechanism | Pattern | Retinaculum |
|---|---|---|
| **Direct blow** (dashboard, fall) | **Comminuted**, chondral damage | **May be intact** |
| **Indirect eccentric quad contraction** (flexing against contracted quads) | **Transverse / inferior pole avulsion** | **Torn (typical)** |
| Child **8–10 yr** | **Sleeve fracture** | High suspicion |

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — palpable defect, haemarthrosis; **can't straight-leg raise → extensor mechanism + retinaculum disrupted** (aspirate + lidocaine if pain limits)

**Saline load** — rule out joint communication (open)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View / study | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Lateral (30° flexion)** | **Best for transverse**; step-off |
| **Axial (sunrise / Merchant)** | **Best for vertical** |
| **Patella alta (Insall-Salvati > 1.0)** | **Patellar tendon** disruption |
| **Patella baja (< 1.0)** | **Quadriceps tendon** disruption |
| **CT** | **Distal pole comminution (sagittal)**, stress fracture, nonunion; **changes plan in 50%** |
| MRI | Not typically needed |

### Classification

| Pattern | Note |
|---|---|
| Nondisplaced | — |
| **Displaced** | **Step-off > 2–3 mm** or **gap > 1–4 mm** |
| Transverse · pole / sleeve · **vertical** · marginal · osteochondral · comminuted (stellate) | — |

**AO / OTA 34** — **A extra-articular · B partial articular · C complete articular**

![Patella fracture patterns](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1042-shows-fracture-types.jpg)

### Bipartite patella

**Bipartite** — **failure of fusion of ossification centres**; **2–3%**; **superolateral**; **bilateral 50%**; **smooth corticated borders**; SLR intact\
✗ Wrong options: failed apoptosis (discoid meniscus) · eccentric contraction · direct blow

## 5 Management

```
Patella fracture
├─ SLR intact + step-off < 2–3 mm + gap < 3 mm (or vertical) → brace / cast in extension, WBAT
├─ SLR lost / step-off > 2–3 mm / gap > 3 mm / open / osteochondral → ORIF (keep patella)
│      └─ comminuted extra-articular inferior pole < 40–50% → partial patellectomy + tendon advancement
└─ Unsalvageable comminution / infection / tumour → total patellectomy (+ VMO advancement)
```

### Operative criteria

| Nonop | ORIF |
|---|---|
| **Intact extensor mechanism (SLR)** | **Extensor failure (no SLR)** |
| Step-off < 2–3 mm, gap < 3 mm | **Step-off > 2–3 mm**, **gap > 3 mm** (Orthobullets) · gap > 4 mm, step > 2 mm (TO) |
| **Vertical** patterns | **Open**, loose bodies, **osteochondral**, **sleeve (child)** |
| Major comorbidity | — |

**Most important factor for surgery in minimally displaced (TO)** — **ability to extend the knee**\
✗ Wrong options: > 2 mm · age · WB

**Nonop** — immobiliser / brace in extension, **WBAT**; ROM 1–2 wk; resistance at 6 wk; **> 95% good** · brace \~10° flexion avoids baja (TO)

### Fixation choice — tension band, screws & partial patellectomy

**Tension band** — **converts anterior tensile force → articular compression**; needs compressible fracture + intact opposite cortex

| Construct | Fact |
|---|---|
| **K-wires (0.062) + 18-gauge wire** | **Highest migration (K-wires bent only proximally)**, re-operation |
| K-wires + **suture** | **75% tensile strength** of wire, similar clinically, **less removal** |
| **4.0 mm cannulated screws + wire** | **Stronger than K-wires**; **less symptomatic hardware** |
| **Plate (mini-fragment / mesh)** | **Biomechanically superior**; comminution, distal pole, osteoporosis; less gapping |
| Cerclage | Alone or augment |

**Plate construct associated with** — **improved biomechanical stability**\
✗ Wrong options: less knee pain · less infection

**Technique** — midline incision; minimal stripping; retinacular repair; tension wire **superiorly** (more cover); tension in **2 places**; avoid over-tension + prominent screw tips

**Post-op** — WBAT in hinged brace, **flexion limited to 30° × 4 wk**

**Union** — **> 95%** any technique (nonunion higher if open)

**Partial patellectomy** — **comminuted extra-articular inferior pole < 40%** of height (Orthobullets) · **< 50%** (OB) when ORIF impossible · **advance tendon close to articular surface** (prevents tilt) · hyperextension, transosseous tunnels / anchors ± cerclage to tubercle\
✗ Wrong options: vertical · bipartite · stellate · chronic quad rupture

**Inferior pole, no SLR** — **ORIF or excision + patellar tendon advancement** (ORIF preferred; ✗ Wrong options: cast · brace · allograft · cerclage alone)

**Total patellectomy** — rare (unsalvageable, infection, tumour); **> 50% loss of extensor strength**; **VMO advancement** improves

**Osteochondral fracture after patellar dislocation (vertical, TO)** — **internal fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopy · cast · patellectomy

## 6 Complications

### Symptomatic hardware & anterior knee pain

**Most common** — **symptomatic hardware (up to 50%)**; thin, open fracture, **K-wire TBW** → **remove after union**

**Removal rate** — **\~18%** elect removal (OB) · **≥ 50% after TBW** (OB) — read the options

**Painful kneeling at 1 yr, no OA** — **hardware removal**\
✗ Wrong options: PF arthritis treatment · MUA · nonunion surgery · steroid

**First 6 months** — **anterior knee pain** + quadriceps deficit = main impairment\
✗ Wrong options: osteonecrosis · refracture

**After cannulated screw TBW** — **implant removal ≥ 50%**; flexion + quad strength reduced, extensor lag may persist, pain worse than norms

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **PF osteoarthritis** | **Up to 50%** → TKA |
| **Osteonecrosis** | **Up to 25%**, **proximal pole**, asymptomatic, **revascularises by 2 yr** (Orthobullets) · < 5%, inferior pole (OB) |
| Loss of reduction | 0–22%; age, osteoporosis |
| Nonunion | **< 1–5%**; open |
| Infection | 0–5% (open **10–11%**) |
| Weakness / extensor lag | Patellectomy, poor retinacular repair |
| Stiffness | Long immobilisation |

**Poor prognosis** — comminution, open, **smoking**

## X vs Y

| | **Patella alta** | **Patella baja** |
|---|---|---|
| Insall-Salvati | **> 1.0** | **< 1.0** |
| Tendon torn | **Patellar** | **Quadriceps** |

| | **Bipartite** | **Fracture** |
|---|---|---|
| Edges | **Smooth, corticated** | Sharp |
| Site | **Superolateral** | Any |
| SLR | Intact | Often lost |

## How it's asked

**"Deciding factor for surgery (TO)?"** → **ability to extend the knee**

**"Partial patellectomy indication?"** → **severely comminuted inferior pole**

**"Inferior pole, no SLR — best?"** → **ORIF or excision + tendon advancement**

**"Highest hardware migration?"** → **K-wire TBW bent proximally only**

**"Plate construct associated with?"** → **improved biomechanical stability**

**"Painful kneeling 1 yr after TBW?"** → **remove hardware**

**"Most common complication after fixation?"** → **symptomatic implant**

**"Main impairment at 6 months?"** → **anterior knee pain**

**"Cannulated screw TBW — true?"** → **implant removal ≥ 50%**

**"Dominant arterial entry?"** → **distal pole**

**"Excision causing most ischaemia?"** → **inferomedial quadrant**

**"Superolateral smooth fragment, SLR intact?"** → **failed fusion of ossification centres**

**"Vertical osteochondral fracture after dislocation (TO)?"** → **internal fixation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pelvic Ring Fractures

*Trauma · 46 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Associated injuries

**Orthopaedic** — chest up to **63%** · long bones **50%** · spine **25%**

**Non-orthopaedic** — head + abdomen **40%** · urogenital (**sexual dysfunction up to 50%**) · **> 50%** have other significant injuries

**Children (open triradiate)** — iliac wing weaker than ligaments → **rami + iliac wing** fractures, rarely surgical

### Anatomy

**Ring** — sacrum + 2 innominates; **displacement needs disruption in 2 places**

| Ligament | Resists |
|---|---|
| Symphyseal | External rotation |
| **Sacrospinous** | **External rotation** |
| **Sacrotuberous** | **Shear + flexion** (and ER) |
| **Posterior SI complex** | **Strongest in body**; **more important than anterior** |
| Anterior SI | ER after floor + anterior fail |
| Interosseous SI | AP translation |
| **Posterior SI** | **Cephalad-caudad displacement** |
| Iliolumbar | Rotation; augments posterior SI |

**Anterior structures** — \~**40%** of pelvic stability (OB)

**Vessels** — internal iliac dives posteriorly at SI joint → **posterior division → superior gluteal** · anterior → obturator · **posterior venous plexus = 80–90% of bleeding**

**Nerves** — **lumbosacral trunk crosses ala + SI joint**; **L5 root over sacral ala \~2 cm medial to SI joint**

### Corona mortis

**Anastomosis (TO)** — **external iliac (inferior epigastric / accessory obturator) ↔ internal iliac (obturator)** · **NOT internal pudendal**

**Location** — behind superior ramus, **3–7 cm** (TO) · mean **6.2 cm** (Orthobullets) from symphysis → risk in **ilioinguinal / modified Stoppa**

![Posterior sacroiliac ligaments](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1030-shows-the-posterior-si-ligaments.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — gentle iliac crest rotation **once** (low sensitivity) · ER limb, LLD · **perineal / scrotal / labial / flank haematoma**, **Morel-Lavallée**

**Neuro** — **L5 + S1 commonest**; rectal tone; **10–15%** neuro injury

**Mandatory** — **vaginal + rectal exam** (occult open fracture) · **gross haematuria** commonest GU sign (M 21% vs F 8%)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Beam | Shows |
|---|---|---|
| **AP** | — | ATLS; asymmetry, rotation |
| **Inlet** | **\~40° caudad** (25–45°); **S1 overlaps S2** | **AP translation**, **internal / external rotation**, **SI widening**, **ala impaction** |
| **Outlet** | **\~40° cephalad** (up to 60°); **symphysis over S2** | **Vertical translation**, flexion / extension, **sacral foramina + fracture location** |
| **Lateral sacrum** | — | **Transverse sacral / U-type → spinopelvic dissociation** (kyphosis) |
| **Single-leg stance (flamingo)** | Alternating | **Chronic instability** — **vertical translation of pubis** |
| **CT** | Routine | Posterior ring, comminution, foramina, **sacral dysmorphism** |

**Instability signs** — **> 5 mm posterior SI displacement**, **posterior sacral gap**, **avulsions (ischial spine / tuberosity, sacrum, L5 TP)** · **lateral sacral avulsion = instability (APC)**

**Isolated rami fractures — next view (TO)** — answer key not provided by the source; inlet / outlet views complete the pelvic series

**Labs** — **Hb, lactate, base excess**

![Inlet view technique](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1030-shows-how-to-perform-inlet-xray.jpg)

### Occult instability — stress exam, single-leg stance & lateral views

**Binder + early CT** — **underestimate severity** → **fluoroscopic stress exam under anaesthesia (EUA)**\
✗ Wrong options: remove binder in unstable patient · thinner CT cuts · Judet

**Binder on, CT "normal" but retroperitoneal haematoma** — **keep binder; EUA in OR**

**APC-I (< 2.5 cm), EUA + CT normal** — **protected WB + pain control**

### Classifications

| Tile | Description |
|---|---|
| **A — stable** | A1 avulsion / iliac wing · A2 stable ring · **A3 transverse sacral (Denis III)** |
| **B — rotationally unstable, vertically stable** | **B1 open book (ER)** · **B2 LC (IR)** (B2-1 ipsilateral rami, **B2-2 contralateral = bucket handle**) · B3 bilateral |
| **C — rotationally + vertically unstable** | C1 unilateral (iliac / SI / sacral) · C2 one B + one C · C3 bilateral C |

| Young-Burgess | Injury |
|---|---|
| **APC I** | Symphysis **< 2.5 cm** |
| **APC II** | Symphysis **> 2.5 cm**, **anterior SI open**, **sacrospinous + sacrotuberous torn**, **posterior SI INTACT** |
| **APC III** | **Anterior + posterior SI torn (SI dislocation)** |
| **LC I** | **Oblique / transverse ramus + ipsilateral anterior sacral ala compression** |
| **LC II** | Rami + **ipsilateral posterior ilium crescent fracture-dislocation** |
| **LC III** | Ipsilateral LC + **contralateral APC (windswept)** — rollover, pedestrian |
| **Vertical shear** | Posterior + superior force; **highest hypovolaemic shock (63%)**, mortality up to 25% |

**Tilt fracture** (displaced superior ramus, women) — **lateral compression**; vaginal exam for open fracture

**Sacrospinous + sacrotuberous torn** — **APC II**\
✗ Wrong options: APC I · LC II (protected by IR)

![Young-Burgess classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1030-shows-the-young-burgess-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Unstable pelvis + haemodynamic instability
├─ Binder / sheet over greater trochanters + 1:1:1 transfusion
├─ Positive FAST / laparotomy → anterior ex-fix (± pelvic packing)
├─ Still unstable → angiography + embolisation (or packing — institutional)
│      └─ still bleeding → repeat angiogram (new source 68%)
└─ Resuscitated (lactate ≤ 4, pH ≥ 7.25, BE ±5.5) → definitive fixation
```

### Haemorrhage control — pelvic binder / sheet & placement

**First step in unstable patient** — **pelvic binder / sheet**\
✗ Wrong options: C-spine film · ex-fix in OR · angiography · packing · IS screws · inlet / outlet first · CT first

**Centre over** — **greater trochanters** · + IR of legs, tape ankles\
✗ Wrong options: iliac crest / ASIS / umbilicus (ineffective, blocks abdomen)

**Force** — **180 ± 50 N** to stabilise symphysis diastasis (Bottlang)

**Controls** — **retroperitoneal venous bleeding** (holds **up to 4 L**)

**Cautions** — skin necrosis (brief "binder holiday" 1–2 h after 24 h) · hypothetical LC over-rotation / bladder injury (no clinical evidence) · **bladder injury is NOT a contraindication**

**Iliac wing fracture + superior migration** — **skeletal traction**\
✗ Wrong options: binder (worsens) · ex-fix

**Vertical shear** — **skeletal traction + binder**

![Pelvic sheet application over the greater trochanters](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1030-shows-technique-for-applying-pelvic-antishock-sheet.jpg)

### Haemorrhage — angiography, embolisation, packing & arterial sources

**Sources** — **venous 80% (Orthobullets) · 90% (OB)**, cancellous bone · **arterial 10–20%** · intra-abdominal up to 40%

| Pattern | Artery |
|---|---|
| **APC (posterior ring)** | **Superior gluteal** |
| **LC (anterior ring)** | **Internal pudendal**, **obturator** |

**Commonest embolised** — **internal iliac** + branches > superior gluteal > obturator > internal pudendal

**Laparotomy needed** — **ex-fix** (stabilises clot, aids laparotomy)\
✗ Wrong options: internal fixation (contamination)

**Ex-fix benefit** — **maintains stable clot**\
✗ Wrong options: rigid fixation (long lever arm)

**Packing** — **ex-fix first**, then **6–8 cm subumbilical midline**, 3 lap pads each side **below brim** (SI joint → anterior → retropubic)

**Class III shock despite binder + 1:1:1, FAST negative** — **emergent angiography**

**Persistent bleeding after SGA embolisation** — repeat angiogram → **new source (68%)** > re-bleed (18%) > both (14%)

**Pudendal bleed after splenectomy, lactate > 5** — **IR embolisation** before definitive fixation

**CTA** — **NPV 98–100%** for arterial bleeding

**Embolisation complications** — **gluteal necrosis, impotence**, ↑ infection (**bilateral nonselective internal iliac**)

### Massive transfusion ratio

**PRBC : FFP : platelets = 1 : 1 : 1** (↓ mortality); coagulopathy hardest to correct

### External fixator pins — iliac crest vs supra-acetabular

| | **Supra-acetabular (AIIS)** | **Iliac crest** |
|---|---|---|
| Purchase | **Superior bone (biomechanically better)** | Thin pillar (gluteal pillar) |
| Fluoroscopy | **Needed**: **obturator outlet** (entry, teardrop), **iliac oblique** (above sciatic notch), **obturator inlet** (between tables) | **Less reliance** — fast |
| Future incisions | **Doesn't interfere** with ilioinguinal / lateral window | Interferes |
| Risk | **LFCN**, hip capsule (**start ≥ 2 cm above joint**) | — |
| Pin infection | Equal | Equal |

**Neither** gives definitive posterior stability

**Ex-fix indications** — ER patterns (**APC, VS**), ongoing bleeding, **before laparotomy** · not for ilium / acetabular fracture

### Anterior fixation nerve complications (LFCN, femoral nerve)

**Supra-acetabular pin / INFIX** — **LFCN (lateral thigh numbness) — commonest**

**INFIX too deep** — **femoral nerve palsy** (weak knee extension, **medial thigh + leg numbness**) · also HO, infection

### Iliosacral & transsacral screws — fluoroscopy, L5 root injury & sacral dysmorphism

| View | Guides |
|---|---|
| **Outlet** | **Superior-inferior** |
| **Inlet** | **Anterior-posterior** |
| **Lateral sacral** | **Iliac cortical density** (anterior safe limit) → avoids **L5** |

**Errant (anterior) screw** — **L5 root → weak great toe extension**\
✗ Wrong options: knee extension L4 · plantarflexion S1

**AP breach (TO)** — answer key not provided by the source; **inlet** shows anterior-posterior position (Orthobullets)

**Transiliac screw start** — posterior iliac crest at S1 level, behind sacrum (lateral view)

**Sacral dysmorphism (20–40%)** — **up-sloping ala**, **irregular / elliptical foramina**, **residual S1 disc**, **mamillary processes**, **tongue-and-groove SI** · **S1 corridor smaller + oblique**, **S2 corridor LARGER** → transiliac-transsacral at S2 · NOT unique: "S1 narrower than S2" · not a contraindication

**Anterior sacral retractors (Stoppa / lateral window)** — **L4 + L5** roots

### Fixation strategy & failure (vertical sacral fracture, symphyseal plates, sequence)

| Injury | Definitive treatment |
|---|---|
| **APC I** | Nonop, protected WB |
| **APC II** | Symphyseal plate / ex-fix **± posterior** |
| **APC III** | **Multi-hole symphyseal plate + SI screws** / posterior plate |
| **LC I** | Mostly nonop (**WBAT incomplete**; protected if complete / comminuted) |
| **LC II** | **ORIF ilium (crescent)** |
| **LC III / VS** | Posterior plate / SI screws |

**APC III stable after embolisation** — **multi-hole non-locking symphyseal plate + IS screw**\
✗ Wrong options: 2-hole · ex-fix definitive · locked plate + sacral plate

**Multi-hole vs 2-hole** — **lower failure + malunion**; reoperation equal · **locked = non-locked** stability

**APC II anterior + posterior** — **↓ anterior plate failure + malunion** (no better outcome)

**Most stable for VS** — **IS screw + anterior internal fixation**

**Highest loss of reduction** — **vertical sacral fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior fixation type · screw length · number · age

**Anterior approach to SI — relative contraindications** — **comminuted sacral fracture** (L5 at risk), morbid obesity, iliac crest ex-fix, colostomy

**Ipsilateral pelvic ring + acetabulum** — **fix ring (SI joint) first**

**Subacute malunion (6 wk), flexed / shortened / ER** — **anterior ORIF + posterior fixation**

**S2 alar-iliac vs iliac screws (TO)** — alar: deeper start (15 mm, less prominence), no connector, **MORE SI joint violation** (incorrect: "less violation")

### Operative criteria

| Operative | Nonoperative |
|---|---|
| **Symphysis > 2.5 cm** | **APC I < 2.5 cm**, intact posterior ring |
| **SI displacement > 1 cm** | **LC I** (< 1 cm posterior displacement) |
| **Sacral displacement > 1 cm** | Isolated rami |
| Rotated / displaced hemipelvis | **Peripartum diastasis < 4 cm** (bed rest + binder) |
| Open fracture | Children with open triradiate (most) |
| Peripartum **> 4–6 cm** / chronic pain | — |

**Open pelvic fracture** — aggressive debridement; **diverting colostomy** (perineal / rectal)

### Stable injuries — nonoperative management (LC1, children, peripartum, elderly)

**Peripartum diastasis (2 wk post-delivery)** — **protected WB + binder + observation**\
✗ Wrong options: ex-fix · plate · symphysiotomy

**LC I + contralateral pilon** — **WBAT pelvis side, NWB pilon side**

**Child 7 yr, parasymphyseal, posterior stable** — **WBAT** · surgery if acetabular / triradiate displacement **> 2 mm**; ex-fix if ring **> 2 cm**

**Elderly LC I (incomplete sacral buckle)** — **bilateral WBAT**\
✗ Wrong options: TDWB · screws

**AP I in cowgirl** — **nonop + gradual WBAT**\
✗ Wrong options: binder long-term · ORIF

**LC incomplete sacral, 56-yr** — **immediate WBAT** (screws if pain prevents mobilising or complete / comminuted)

**LC1 surgery** — **↓ short-term pain** (3–12 months) but **↑ narcotics post-op**, not complication-free

**Rehab after fixation** — weight-of-limb on posterior injury side, full WB other side; bilateral posterior → bed-to-chair

## 6 Complications

### Urological injury, sexual dysfunction & childbirth

**GU injury** — **12–20%** (males 21%) · \~15% (OB)

**Commonest urological** — **posterior urethral tear** > bladder rupture (mortality 22–34%)

**Urethral injury signs** — **blood at meatus**, **high-riding mobile prostate**, haematuria, can't void → **retrograde urethrogram (RUG)**\
✗ Wrong options: cystogram · urinalysis · CT · DPL

**Site (TO)** — **membranous urethra** (classic); commonest adult site distal to membranous; children more proximal

**Management** — **suprapubic catheter** (relative contraindication to symphyseal plating) · repair rupture before / with fixation

**Long-term GU** — **urethral stricture (commonest)**, impotence, incontinence, infection

**APC / saddle-horn (horse) injury** — **sexual dysfunction = commonest long-term sequela** (18 / 20); **males up to 90%**, **dyspareunia 60%** females · **35–56%** overall

**Women** — **dyspareunia + caesarean section** ↑ · dyspareunia risk: diastasis, bladder disruption, symphyseal plating, residual > 5 mm

### Mortality predictors & causes of death

**Mortality** — **1–15% closed** · **up to 50% open** · isolated pelvic ring **< 15%** · shock: **25–52%**

| Pattern | Mortality / cause |
|---|---|
| **APC III** | **Highest mortality (\~37%)**, blood loss, **transfusion** · APC overall 26% |
| **LC** | \~13%; **closed head injury = commonest cause of death** |
| VS | 25% |
| CMI | 17.1% |
| Acetabulum | \~1.5% |

**Best predictor** — **shock on presentation** · **transfusion in first 24 h** · also **age > 60**, SBP < 90, ISS / RTS, **> 4 units**, APC III

**APC deaths** — **pelvic + visceral injury / haemorrhage**

### Postoperative infection risk (obesity, embolisation)

**Strongest predictor** — **obesity (↑ BMI)** · also DM, long op, long ICU, **transfusion volume**, GU / abdominal injury, **Morel-Lavallée**, open fracture, **bilateral internal iliac embolisation**\
✗ Wrong options: angiography alone · venous injury · ex-fix · early fixation · head injury (HO)

| Other | Fact |
|---|---|
| **DVT / PE** | DVT **\~60%**, PE \~27%, fatal 2% → LMWH; **IVC filter if head injury** |
| Chronic instability | Rare → flamingo views |
| L5 injury | Anterior SI screw |
| **Poor outcome** | **SI incongruity > 1 cm**, **LLD > 2 cm**, malunion, nonunion, neuro / urethral injury |

## X vs Y

| | **APC II** | **APC III** |
|---|---|---|
| Posterior SI ligaments | **Intact** | **Torn** |
| Stability | Rotationally unstable | **Rotationally + vertically unstable** |

| | **Inlet** | **Outlet** |
|---|---|---|
| Beam | **Caudad** | **Cephalad** |
| Shows | **AP translation, rotation, SI widening** | **Vertical translation**, foramina |
| IS screw guide | **Anterior-posterior** | **Superior-inferior** |

## How it's asked

**"Tile B1 (TO)?"** → **open book**

**"APC II — not disrupted?"** → **posterior SI ligaments**

**"APC II — true description?"** → **symphysis diastasis, anterior SI + sacrotuberous torn, posterior SI intact**

**"LC II radiograph?"** → **crescent fracture on impact side**

**"Sacrospinous + sacrotuberous torn?"** → **APC II**

**"LC III image?"** → **windswept pelvis**

**"Lateral sacral avulsion indicates?"** → **pelvic instability**

**"Tilt fracture mechanism?"** → **lateral compression**

**"Hypotensive rollover, widened symphysis?"** → **pelvic binder**

**"Binder centred over?"** → **greater trochanters**

**"Binder controls?"** → **retroperitoneal venous bleeding**

**"Ex-fix greatest benefit?"** → **maintains stable clot**

**"Iliac wing fracture + superior migration after splenectomy?"** → **skeletal traction**

**"Pelvic packing technique?"** → **ex-fix then subumbilical lap pads**

**"Repeat angiogram after SGA embolisation?"** → **new arterial source**

**"Persistent bleed, pudendal haematoma?"** → **IR embolisation**

**"Commonest embolised vessel?"** → **internal iliac artery**

**"Pins in position B advantage?"** → **superior bone purchase**

**"Iliac crest pin advantage?"** → **less fluoroscopy**

**"Nerve at risk with supra-acetabular pins?"** → **LFCN**

**"INFIX nerve injury symptom?"** → **lateral thigh numbness**

**"INFIX, weak knee extension at 1 month?"** → **medial thigh + leg numbness (femoral nerve)**

**"Lateral sacral view protects?"** → **L5 nerve root**

**"Stoppa sacral retractor risks?"** → **L5 nerve root**

**"Lateral sacral view used for?"** → **percutaneous IS screws**

**"IS screw complication?"** → **weak great toe extension**

**"Outlet view guides?"** → **superior-inferior orientation**

**"Transiliac screw start?"** → **posterior iliac crest at S1 behind sacrum**

**"Dysmorphic sacrum — true?"** → **S2 safe zone larger**

**"NOT unique to dysmorphism?"** → **S1 corridor narrower than S2**

**"S2AI vs iliac screws — incorrect (TO)?"** → **less SI joint violation**

**"Fixation failure predictor?"** → **vertical sacral fracture**

**"Most stable construct?"** → **IS screw + anterior internal fixation**

**"Contraindication to anterior SI plating?"** → **sacral fracture**

**"Ring + transverse acetabulum — fix first?"** → **SI joint**

**"Highest loss of reduction?"** → **vertical sacral fracture**

**"APC III definitive?"** → **multi-hole non-locking plate + IS screw**

**"Subacute malunion 6 wk?"** → **anterior ORIF + posterior fixation**

**"APC II anterior + posterior vs anterior only?"** → **less anterior plate failure**

**"Anterior fixation — true?"** → **multi-hole lower failure than 2-hole**

**"Transfusions predict?"** → **mortality**

**"APC III highest rate of?"** → **transfusion**

**"LC death cause?"** → **closed head injury**

**"Most predictive of mortality?"** → **transfusions in first 24 h**

**"Best predictor of mortality?"** → **shock on presentation**

**"Isolated pelvic ring mortality?"** → **< 15%**

**"Highest mortality pattern?"** → **APC III**

**"Closed head injury death — pattern?"** → **LC II**

**"Urethral injury site (TO)?"** → **membranous urethra**

**"Commonest urological injury?"** → **posterior urethral tear**

**"Saddle horn → erectile dysfunction?"** → **APC injury**

**"Rule out urethral injury?"** → **retrograde urethrogram**

**"Female after displaced pelvic fracture?"** → **dyspareunia + caesarean section**

**"Horse rider groin pain — long-term?"** → **sexual dysfunction**

**"APC II plated female — long-term risk?"** → **dyspareunia**

**"Most frequent APC II complication?"** → **sexual dysfunction**

**"APC I after EUA?"** → **protected WB + pain control**

**"Alternating single-leg stance views?"** → **pelvic ring instability**

**"Lateral pelvic view identifies?"** → **spinopelvic dissociation**

**"Binder, CT negative, retroperitoneal haematoma?"** → **keep binder, EUA in OR**

**"Binder + early CT effect?"** → **underestimates; EUA**

**"Flamingo views assess?"** → **vertical translation of pubis (chronic)**

**"Outlet view shows?"** → **vertical translation**

**"Postpartum symphysis pain?"** → **protected WB, binder, observation**

**"LC I + right pilon WB?"** → **WBAT left, NWB right**

**"7-yr parasymphyseal, stable posterior?"** → **nonop WBAT**

**"72-yr LC I?"** → **bilateral WBAT**

**"Cowgirl AP I?"** → **nonop + gradual WBAT**

**"Strongest infection predictor?"** → **obesity**

**"BMI 37 — true?"** → **higher infection risk**

**"↑ infection after angiography?"** → **bilateral internal iliac embolisation**

**"Massive transfusion?"** → **PRBC, FFP, platelets 1:1:1**

**"Corona mortis EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **internal pudendal artery**

# Proximal Humerus Fracture Nonunion and Malunion

*Trauma · 47 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & risk factors

**Proximal humerus** — **4–5% of all fractures**

| Nonunion risk | Factor |
|---|---|
| **Fracture** | **2-part surgical neck**, **head split**, **displaced tuberosity** |
| **Patient** | **Osteoporosis**, CKD, alcohol / steroids, smoking |

**Malunion origin** — initial displacement, **secondary displacement**, fixation failure

| Malunion | Typical position |
|---|---|
| **Head** | Varus / valgus, **impacted > 1 cm**, articular incongruity |
| **Greater tuberosity** | **Posterior, superior, externally rotated** |
| **Lesser tuberosity** | **Medial** |

**Associated** — cuff tear, **osteonecrosis**, stiffness, PTOA, instability, **subacromial impingement**

### Anatomy

**Head** — spheroidal 90%, \~**43 mm**; **30° retroversion** (to transepicondylar axis); **neck-shaft 130–140°**

**Greater tuberosity** — medial edge **\~10 mm lateral** to canal axis · superior edge **\~6 mm below** head top

![Normal proximal humeral angles](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12283-uglovi.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain, weakness, atrophy

**Cuff** — **GT malunion → weak abduction + ER** · **LT malunion → weak IR** · head malunion → apprehension

**Check** — **axillary nerve**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — true AP, scapular Y, **axillary** ± apical oblique, Velpeau, West Point

### Diagnostic criteria

| Malunion | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **Head** | **> 45° in any plane**, symptomatic incongruity, **neck-shaft < 120° or > 150°** |
| **Tuberosity** | **> 1 cm** from native position |

**CT** — bone stock, **glenoid version + stock**, articular injury · **MRI** — cuff / labrum, **deltoid atrophy (axillary nerve)**, LHB, **osteonecrosis**

**Labs** — CBC, ESR, CRP, cultures (infection) · EMG if nerve concern

### Classifications

| Beredjiklian | Description |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Tuberosity malposition (> 1 cm)** |
| **II** | **Articular incongruity** (extension, OA) |
| **III** | **Articular surface malalignment (> 45°)** |

| Boileau (fracture sequelae) | Description | Arthroplasty |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Head necrosis / impaction** | Anatomic possible (tuberosities preserved) |
| **II** | **Chronic dislocation / fracture-dislocation** | Anatomic possible |
| **III** | **Surgical neck nonunion** | **Reverse** |
| **IV** | **Severe tuberosity malunion** | **Reverse** |

## 5 Management

```
Proximal humerus nonunion / malunion
├─ Low demand / painless / can't rehab → NSAIDs, physio, injection
├─ Good bone stock, articular surface + head blood supply intact → ORIF ± osteotomy ± graft
└─ Poor bone stock, incongruity, AVN, chronic dislocation, resorbed tuberosities, cuff tear → arthroplasty (reverse preferred)
```

### Malunion — osteotomy vs reverse shoulder arthroplasty

**Head deformity** — minor: **tuberoplasty ± acromioplasty, capsular release, bursectomy** · severe: **varus / valgus ± rotational osteotomy + lateral plate** (young / active; ± strut allograft)

**Greater tuberosity** — **< 1.5 cm → arthroscopic subacromial decompression ± cuff repair** · **> 1.5 cm → tuberosity osteotomy**

**24-yr, 45° varus after ORIF, stiff at 9 months** — **revision ORIF + osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: MUA · resurfacing · hemi · RSA

**Reverse vs hemiarthroplasty for sequelae** — **greater functional improvement**, **lower complication + reoperation** (less cuff dependence)\
✗ Wrong options: more complications · weaker

**68-yr active, surgical neck nonunion, osteoporotic** — **reverse TSA** (Boileau III)\
✗ Wrong options: anatomic TSA · hemi (inferior) · locking plate

**Nonop outcome** — impacted varus / valgus → good–excellent, **\~90%** of normal function

**Surgery for malunion** — **higher complications** than acute fracture surgery

### Nonunion — ORIF with bone graft vs arthroplasty

**69-yr, painful nonunion after ORIF** — **shoulder arthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: revision ORIF · valgus osteotomy · fusion · head resection

**72-yr, 2-part nonunion, GOOD bone stock, no OA / AVN, tuberosities fine** — **ORIF (locked plate) ± bone graft**\
✗ Wrong options: CRPP · hemi · graft alone · RSA + latissimus (latissimus gives **ER**, not IR)

**Arthroplasty instead when** — **marked osteopenia, AVN, resorbed tuberosities, pre-existing cuff tear**

**45-yr nonunion after IM nail** — **compression plating + bone graft** · union **> 90%**\
✗ Wrong options: exchange nail · hemi · RSA (too young) · add screws

## 6 Complications

**After surgery** — persistent pain + weakness, stiffness, loss of fixation, infection, bleeding

## X vs Y

| | **ORIF ± osteotomy** | **Reverse TSA** |
|---|---|---|
| Bone stock | **Adequate** | Poor |
| Head | Intact blood supply, congruent | AVN, incongruity, chronic dislocation |
| Patient | Young / active | Older; Boileau III–IV |

## How it's asked

**"24-yr, 45° varus malunion?"** → **revision ORIF + osteotomy**

**"Reverse vs hemi for malunion?"** → **greater functional improvement**

**"68-yr osteoporotic surgical neck nonunion?"** → **reverse TSA**

**"69-yr painful nonunion after ORIF?"** → **shoulder arthroplasty**

**"72-yr nonunion, good bone stock?"** → **ORIF ± bone graft**

**"45-yr nonunion after IM nail?"** → **compression plating + bone graft**

# Proximal Humerus Fractures

*Trauma · 48 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **4–6% of all fractures** · **3rd commonest non-vertebral fracture > 65 yr** · **F:M 2:1**

**Commonest pattern** — **2-part surgical neck** · \~80–85% minimally displaced

**Risk factors** — osteoporosis, diabetes, **epilepsy**, female · complexity ↑ with age

**Mechanism** — elderly low-energy fall (90% in > 60) · young high-energy

**Associated** — **axillary nerve (commonest)**; arterial injury **5–6%** (older, surgical neck, subcoracoid dislocation; collaterals mask pulses)

### Anatomy

**Anatomic neck** = old physis · **surgical neck** fractures more often · neck-shaft **135°**

| Deforming force | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Pectoralis major** | Shaft **anterior + medial** |
| Deltoid | Proximal fragment lateral (abducts — axillary nerve, **posterior cord**) |
| **SS, IS, TM** | **GT superior + posterior (ER)** |
| **Subscapularis** | **IR articular segment / LT** |

**Ligaments** — SGHL: inferior translation at 0° abduction · **MGHL: AP at \~45°** · **IGHL: AP at 90°**

### Humeral head vascularity & ischaemia predictors (Hertel criteria)

**Main blood supply (TO)** — **posterior humeral circumflex artery (PCHA) — 64%** (Hettrich)\
✗ Wrong options: ACHA (old view) · axillary · profunda

**ACHA** — **anterolateral branch → arcuate artery** (main GT supply); 1990 study: supplies whole head except posteroinferior GT + head · **disrupted in \~80%** of fractures yet AVN infrequent → PCHA dominant

**PCHA** — through **quadrilateral space** with axillary nerve

**First site of osteonecrosis (TO)** — **superomedial** head (poorly vascularised)

| Hertel predictor | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Calcar (posteromedial metaphyseal extension) < 8 mm** | **Most predictive** |
| **Disrupted medial hinge (> 2 mm)** | 2nd |
| Anatomic neck fracture | Independent predictor |
| Angulation > 45° (> 30° in one OB item) | 3rd |
| Head split | Least of these |
| Displacement > 10 mm, complexity, medial metaphyseal comminution | — |

**Order of prediction** — **calcar < 8 mm > medial hinge > angulation > 45° > head split**

**NOT predictors (TO)** — **valgus displacement**, **surgical neck fracture**

**Long calcar (> 8 mm)** — **protective** · Hertel PPV **97%** (TO)

**Ischaemia ≠ AVN** — acute ischaemia **doesn't predict** later necrosis (Bastian & Hertel)

**Highest AVN risk** — **4-part fracture-dislocation** · 4-part head split **21–75%** · valgus-impacted 4-part low (posteromedial periosteum intact) · 3-part valgus impacted 5–10%

![Long vs short calcar](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1015-shows-long-vs-short-calcar.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — ecchymosis chest / arm / forearm · **deltoid + lateral shoulder sensation (axillary)** · chest wall

**Lesser tuberosity fracture** — **posterior dislocation until proven otherwise** (seizure, **electrocution** — subscapularis avulsion)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Trauma series: true AP (Grashey), scapular Y, axillary** | **Axillary view mandatory** (→ Velpeau if painful → CT) |
| Apical oblique, Velpeau, West Point | Additional |
| **Combined cortical thickness** (medial + lateral) | **> 4 mm → good screw purchase**; **< 4 mm precludes fixation (TO)** → sling / transosseous suture / hemi |
| **Pseudosubluxation** | Inferior head (haemarthrosis + atony) |
| AP in **IR** | LT in profile; **lightbulb sign** (posterior dislocation) |
| CT | Head / GT position, comminution, **head split** |
| MRI | Rarely; cuff tear |

### Classification

**Neer** — 4 segments: **GT, LT, articular surface, shaft**; part = **> 1 cm displacement or > 45° angulation**

**AO / OTA** — location, surgical neck status, dislocation

![Neer four-part classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1015-neer-four-part-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Proximal humerus fracture
├─ Minimally displaced (most) → sling 2–3 wk + early ROM
├─ GT > 5 mm (> 3 mm overhead) → ORIF (suture / screw)
├─ Young, displaced 2–4 part, good bone (> 4 mm cortex) → ORIF (calcar screw, medial support)
├─ Head split, 40–65 yr → hemiarthroplasty (or ORIF if young)
└─ Elderly, comminuted / dislocated / poor tuberosities → reverse TSA (cemented, tuberosity repair)
```

**Abbreviations** — ORIF open reduction and internal fixation · CRPP closed reduction percutaneous pinning · RSA reverse shoulder arthroplasty · GT / LT greater / lesser tuberosity · MRI magnetic resonance imaging

### Nonoperative management & natural history

**Indications** — minimally displaced, **GT < 5 mm**, poor surgical candidates; consider age, bone, dominance, health

**Technique** — **sling 2–3 wk + immediate PT / pendulum + elbow ROM within 1–2 wk** · early ROM **doesn't displace**\
✗ Wrong options: immobiliser 6 wk · hanging cast 6–8 wk

**Minimally displaced surgical neck** — **sling + recheck X-ray at 1 wk**

**One-part fracture** — **early structured PT**; union **> 90%**; at 1 yr **forward flexion restored, IR + ER slightly ↓, no functional deficit**; most return to work

**Impacted varus fractures (Court-Brown)** — **age = main outcome factor** · union 93% at 1 yr, **smoking ↑ nonunion 5.5×**\
✗ Wrong options: angulation · PT · dominance · bone

**Varus posteromedial vs valgus impacted nonop (TO)** — **varus posteromedial better** (TO)

**Surgical indications** — **head split**, irreducible dislocation, GT > 5 mm, young, **+ humeral shaft fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: age > 70 · comorbidity

**10 months, can't elevate, motion at fracture** — **nonunion** (surgical neck commonest)

### Greater tuberosity fractures — displacement thresholds & fixation

**Threshold** — **> 5 mm (general)**, **> 3 mm overhead / active** (OB, Orthobullets) · **> 1 cm** or > 20% head overlap; elderly tolerate **2 cm / 40%** (TO) — read the options

**Incorrect statement (TO)** — "**GT fixation required for ≥ 5 mm in active young**" (TO uses > 1 cm)

**> 5 mm residual** — **impingement → loss of abduction + ER**, **altered cuff mechanics** (✗ Wrong options: nonunion · AVN (rich supply)) (✗ Wrong options: IR loss · instability)

**Normal GT** — **5–8 mm below** top of head (≈ 6 mm → nonop sling)

**Fixation** — **heavy non-absorbable suture figure-of-8 / tension band (osteoporotic)** · **isolated screws young + good bone** · avoid prominent hardware

**Associations** — **anterior dislocation**, **longitudinal cuff tear**; **subscapularis NOT** a GT deforming force

**Healed GT, pain + weak overhead, full passive ROM at 5 months** — **MRI (cuff tear)**\
✗ Wrong options: more PT · MUA (passive full) · arthroscopy first · TSA

**Valgus-impacted 4-part, young laborer** — **ORIF**

**3-part, cortex 4.2 mm, active 54-yr** — **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: sling · hemi (< 4 mm) · RSA

**Seizure fracture-dislocation (young)** — **ORIF** after reduction (watch for occult neck extension before closed reduction)

**25-yr 4-part** — **ORIF** (retain native head; arthroplasty for elderly / failed fixation)

**Spot AP after trauma** — **axillary view** next

### CRPP

**Indication** — **2-part surgical neck**; 3-part / valgus-impacted 4-part with good bone, intact calcar · **highest complication rate**

**Technique** — threaded pins, don't cross cartilage, **ER the arm**, enter 2 cm below head

| Pin | At risk |
|---|---|
| **Lateral** | **Anterior branch of axillary nerve** |
| **Anterior** | **LHB tendon**, **musculocutaneous nerve** (\~17 mm), **cephalic vein** (\~11 mm) |
| GT pin through medial cortex | **PCHA** |

### Locked plating — screw penetration, varus collapse & calcar support

**Commonest complication** — **screw penetration / cut-out**: **14%** (Orthobullets, Südkamp) · **16–30%**, 23% (OB)\
✗ Wrong options: infection · AVN · axillary nerve · HO

**Mechanism** — inadequate head fixation → **varus collapse → penetration** · 40% of complications technical

**Prevent varus collapse** — **inferomedial calcar screw(s)** · **anatomic medial cortical apposition** · **fibular strut** (↓ head height loss + varus)\
✗ Wrong options: cortical + locking mix · tension band · CRPP · IMN

**Loss-of-reduction risks (TO)** — age, osteoporosis, smoking, **varus**, **medial comminution**, no cuff sutures

**Plate placement** — **lateral to bicipital groove + pectoralis tendon** (spare **ascending branch of ACHA**); locking plate more elastic than blade plate

**Rehab** — guided 3-phase program; **stiffness at 4 months with healed fracture → scar tissue**

**ORIF in > 60 yr** — **failure > 30%**, complications > 40%; **fracture type predicts failure**; fibular strut doesn't beat RSA

### Surgical approaches — axillary nerve at risk

**Axillary nerve** — **\~5–7 cm below acromion** (7 cm); **deltoid-split / extended anterolateral acromial approach → risk** (vs deltopectoral)

### Intramedullary nailing

**Indications** — **2-part surgical neck** (± shaft extension), **pathological**, 3-part GT (young), proximal humerus + shaft · not varus posteromedial with both tuberosities, head split, 4-part

**Facts** — superior deltoid split; straight nail through articular cartilage; **cuff pain**, migration · **distal lateral → medial screw: radial nerve**; **A→P: musculocutaneous**

### Hemiarthroplasty — height, tuberosity position & indications

**Indication** — **40–65 yr** with **head split** / complex fracture-dislocation likely to fail ORIF (**convertible stem**) · head depression > 40% (TO)

**Best predictor of success** — **anatomic tuberosity healing**\
✗ Wrong options: 25° retroversion · < 24 h surgery · labral repair; age > 75 worse

**Poor outcome (TO)** — **GT migration**\
✗ Wrong options: anteversion (**retroversion > 40°** worse) · no glenoid resurfacing

**Height** — **top of head 5.6 cm above superior pectoralis major tendon** (height + **retroversion** reference)

**GT position** — **\~8 mm below top of prosthetic head** (head-to-tuberosity distance 7–8 mm); malposition → **8× ER torque**

**Head too low / below GT** — **impingement, ↓ elevation + abduction**

**Can't elevate 6 months after hemi** — **GT malunion** · LT nonunion → weak lift-off

**Post-op caution** — **passive ER** (+ active IR) stresses **LT / subscapularis**

**Best hemi indication** — **46-yr 4-part with comminuted head split**\
✗ Wrong options: GT dislocation (ORIF) · 3-part intact calcar (ORIF) · OA (TSA) · cuff arthropathy (RSA)

**59-yr carpenter — hemi over ORIF because** — **head-split pattern**

### Reverse shoulder arthroplasty vs hemiarthroplasty / ORIF in the elderly

**Indications** — **low-demand elderly**, **non-reconstructible tuberosities**, poor bone, **fracture-dislocation**, > 70 yr comminuted · **60° initial varus** (> 20°) → arthroplasty

**70-yr comminuted metaphysis** — **cemented RSA + tuberosity repair**\
✗ Wrong options: uncemented (metaphyseal loss) · no tuberosity repair

**Biomechanics** — **COR medial (+ inferior) to native** (lateralised designs still medial) → **deltoid longer lever**; less tuberosity-dependent

**↑ ER after RSA** — **GT healing** (teres minor) · larger glenosphere, lateralised, **135° NSA**, more retroversion help\
✗ Wrong options: inferior tilt · small glenosphere · medialisation · 155° NSA

**GT not repaired / resorbed** — **clinically significant loss of ER**

**Contraindication** — **non-functioning deltoid (upper trunk / C5 / axillary palsy)**\
✗ Wrong options: irreparable cuff (that contraindicates hemi / TSA)

**RSA vs HA** — RSA: **higher complication rate** (OB) but **lower revision**; HA: **more IR**, more resorption issues, **greater motion loss**, unpredictable pain · equal complications (OB) — read the options

**Octogenarian fracture-dislocation** — **RSA > plate + hemi** outcomes

**Primary vs salvage RSA** — primary: **more ER + elevation**, better PROs · salvage after failed ORIF: worse function, ↑ instability, infection, complications · **RSA after failed nonop > after failed ORIF**

**Chronic fracture-dislocation + shaft (81-yr)** — **CT → long-stem RSA + cable / plate**

**Elderly 4-part, independent** — **arthroplasty (hemi or RSA)** · 73-yr 4-part → **RSA** · anatomic TSA not used for fractures

**RSA complications** — scapular notching (↓ by 135° NSA, inferior tilt, inferior offset), dislocation, **glenoid loosening (commonest revision cause)**, **acromial stress fracture**, infection

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Screw cut-out / penetration** | **Commonest after locking plate** |
| **AVN** | Better tolerated than LL; not related to fixation type |
| **Axillary nerve** | **Up to 58% (EMG)**; suprascapular 48% |
| **Malunion** | **Varus apex-anterior** or GT → **RSA** (not TSA) |
| **Nonunion** | **2-part surgical neck**; **age + smoking** |
| Missed posterior dislocation | **LT fracture** |
| LHB injury | Anterior CRPP pin |
| Adhesive capsulitis, PTOA, infection | — |

### Related anatomy (long head of biceps, deltoid innervation)

**LHB** — **supraglenoid tubercle**; **musculocutaneous**; **supination**; landmark in comminuted fractures

**Deltoid** — **axillary nerve (C5–6) ← posterior cord**

## X vs Y

| | **Hemiarthroplasty** | **Reverse TSA** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | **40–65**, head split | **Elderly**, poor tuberosities |
| Depends on | **Tuberosity healing** | **Deltoid** |
| Revision | Higher | Lower |

| | **ACHA** | **PCHA** |
|---|---|---|
| Role | Old "main"; arcuate → GT | **Main head supply (64%)** |

## How it's asked

**"Best predictor of ischaemia (TO)?"** → **calcar length**

**"NOT a predictor (TO)?"** → **valgus displacement**

**"Hertel EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **surgical neck fracture**

**"Initial AVN site (TO)?"** → **superomedial**

**"1990 study — ACHA supplies?"** → **whole head except posteroinferior GT + head**

**"Order of predictors?"** → **calcar > medial hinge > angulation > head split**

**"Most predictive (choose)?"** → **calcar 4–6 mm** / **disrupted medial hinge**

# Proximal Third Tibia Fracture

*Trauma · 49 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **5–11% of tibial shaft fractures** (\~10%)

**Mechanism** — low energy: **torsion → spiral oblique** (indirect) · high energy: direct

**Associated** — **compartment syndrome**, soft-tissue injury (**muscle injury severity → amputation need**)

### Characteristic deformity — valgus & procurvatum and its causes

| Force | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Patellar tendon** | **Proximal fragment extends → apex anterior (procurvatum)** |
| **Gastrocnemius** | **Distal fragment flexes** |
| **Pes anserinus** | Proximal fragment **varus** (per note) |
| **Anterior compartment** | **Valgus** |

**Classic malunion after IMN** — **valgus + procurvatum (apex anterior)** + anterior translation of proximal fragment\
✗ Wrong options: varus · recurvatum · rotation

**Causes** — wide metaphysis (no canal fit), **eccentric / medial start**, deforming muscles, **knee hyperflexion**, nail **Herzog curve** ("wedge effect")

**Malalignment rate** — **20–60%** (Orthobullets) · **58%** proximal ⅓ (Freedman; vs 7% middle, 8% distal) · **up to 84%** (OB)

**Other fractures' typical malunions** — humeral shaft: varus, shortening, IR · distal femur: varus, shortening, recurvatum · distal ⅓ tibia + intact fibula: varus + rotation · subtroch: **flexion, abduction, ER**

![Deforming forces on proximal tibia fractures](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1062-tibia.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — contusions, blisters, open wounds, **compartments** (passive toe stretch ± pressures) · DPN, SPN, sural, tibial, saphenous; DP + PT pulses

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral tibia + **knee + ankle** · findings: **proximal fragment extended, apex anterior, varus**; **distal fragment flexed**

**CT** — **intra-articular extension** (differential: plateau fracture, knee dislocation)

| AO 42 | Pattern |
|---|---|
| A | Simple |
| B | Wedge |
| C | Comminuted |

## 5 Management

```
Proximal ⅓ tibia fracture
├─ Closed low energy, acceptable alignment → long leg cast (10–20° flexion) → brace at 4 wk
├─ Soft-tissue compromise / polytrauma → ex-fix (convert to IMN 7–21 d)
├─ ≥ 5–6 cm proximal bone for 2 locking screws → IMN + anti-deformity tricks
└─ Very proximal / inadequate nail fixation → percutaneous locking plate
```

### Operative criteria

| Acceptable for cast | Limit |
|---|---|
| Varus / valgus | **< 5°** |
| AP angulation | **< 10°** |
| Cortical apposition | **> 50%** |
| Shortening | **< 1 cm** (hardest to control) |
| Rotation | **< 10°** |

| Option | Indication / fact |
|---|---|
| **Ex-fix** | Soft-tissue compromise, polytrauma; circular frame for very proximal; **more malalignment than IMN** |
| **IMN** | **≥ 5–6 cm proximal bone (2 locking screws)**; static locking proximal + distal; ≥ 2 proximal screws, **oblique** screws help |
| **Percutaneous locking plate** | Extreme proximal, transverse / oblique, good soft tissue; **lateral safer**; **lateral plate + medial comminution → varus collapse**; **↑ infection vs IMN in open** |

### Nailing technique — lateral start point, semi-extended / suprapatellar nailing, unicortical plate

**Start point** — **just medial to lateral tibial spine (AP)** + **proximal, anterior to articular margin (lateral)** → **lateral start prevents valgus**\
✗ Wrong options: medial start → valgus · distal + medial → anterior cortex cut-out

**Approach** — **lateral parapatellar** (needs mobile patella) helps lateral start · medial parapatellar → valgus

**Semi-extended / suprapatellar nailing** — neutralises extensor pull → **prevents procurvatum** · **suprapatellar does NOT affect valgus (coronal)**\
✗ Wrong options: hyperflexion (worsens) · radiolucent triangle

**Suprapatellar vs infrapatellar** — infrapatellar → **more anterior knee pain**; ROM, union, PF joint same

**Unicortical plate** — short ⅓-tubular plate anterior / anteromedial / posteromedial, 2 unicortical screws each side → holds reduction (prevents procurvatum + anterior translation)

**Universal / femoral distractor** — Schanz pins medial, parallel to joint (can double as blocking pins)

**Prevent apex anterior — all EXCEPT** — **anterior blocking screw** (posterior blocking screw, posterior starting hole, semi-extended interlocking, anteriorly directed nail all help)

**Preserve reduction — EXCEPT** — **countersinking the nail** (loses aperture fixation)\
✗ Wrong options: oblique proximal screws · blocking screws · lateral eminence start · unicortical plate

**Valgus prevention — NOT shown for** — **suprapatellar portal**

**Nail choice** — **Herzog curve proximal to fracture** (distal curve → anterior translation)

**Locking** — **static**; no acute dynamisation

### Blocking (Poller) screws — position & function

**Rule** — place in the **concavity** of the deformity, in the **proximal (metaphyseal) fragment** → narrows canal, redirects nail

| Screw | Prevents |
|---|---|
| **Posterior to nail, proximal fragment** (coronal screw) | **Procurvatum** (counters patellar tendon + gastrocnemius) |
| **Lateral to nail, proximal fragment** (sagittal screw) | **Valgus** |
| Anterior | **Worsens** procurvatum |
| Medial | **Worsens** valgus |

**Benefits** — obtain + maintain reduction, **↑ construct stiffness** (if left) · Ricci: < 5° valgus with screws vs > 6° without · union \~5.4 months (Krettek)\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ nail breakage · replace interlocks · larger nail

![Coronal blocking screw posterior in the proximal fragment](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1062-proximal-third-tibia-fx-surgical-technique-im-nail-coronal-b.jpg)

### Minimally invasive plating — superficial peroneal nerve risk

**Long lateral plate, percutaneous distal screws** — **superficial peroneal nerve** (± deep peroneal) → **small open incision for distal holes**\
✗ Wrong options: common peroneal · saphenous (medial) · peroneal artery · tendons

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Anterior knee pain** | **> 30% after IMN**; **nail removal helps \~50%** |
| **Malunion** | **Valgus + procurvatum (20–60%)** → ↑ arthrosis; revision IMN / osteotomy |
| Nonunion | **Rule out infection**; dynamise if axially stable |

## X vs Y

| | **Posterior blocking screw** | **Lateral blocking screw** |
|---|---|---|
| Prevents | **Procurvatum** | **Valgus** |
| Plane | Coronal screw (sagittal deformity) | Sagittal screw (coronal deformity) |

| | **Lateral start** | **Medial start** |
|---|---|---|
| Effect | **Prevents valgus** | **Causes valgus** |

## How it's asked

**"IMN deformities?"** → **apex anterior + valgus**

**"Ideal entry point?"** → **medial to lateral spine, anterior to articular margin**

**"Fracture most associated with procurvatum + valgus?"** → **proximal tibia**

**"Most common malalignment?"** → **valgus + procurvatum**

**"Counter patellar tendon pull?"** → **posterior blocking screw in proximal fragment**

**"Blocking screw advantage?"** → **↑ construct stiffness**

**"Blocking screw positions?"** → **posterior + lateral in proximal segment**

**"Avoid procurvatum?"** → **posterior blocking screw** / **semi-extended nailing**

**"Start point + blocking screw combo?"** → **lateral start + posterior screw**

**"Screws at marked sites prevent?"** → **valgus + procurvatum**

**"Herzog curve + screws?"** → **lateral + posterior screws; Herzog proximal to fracture**

**"Avoid apex-anterior complication?"** → **blocking screws**

**"Prevent apex anterior EXCEPT?"** → **anterior blocking screw**

**"Avoid characteristic deformity?"** → **lateral starting point**

**"NOT shown to prevent valgus?"** → **suprapatellar portal**

**"Prevent procurvatum + anterior translation?"** → **anterior unicortical plate**

**"Facilitate reduction (choose)?"** → **suprapatellar nailing**

**"Preserve reduction EXCEPT?"** → **countersinking the nail**

**"Infrapatellar vs suprapatellar?"** → **more anterior knee pain**

**"MIPO lateral plate nerve risk?"** → **superficial peroneal nerve**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Radial Head Fractures

*Trauma · 50 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — 1–4% of adult fractures · **20–30% of elbow fractures (commonest)** · mean age \~45 (85% 30–60) · **F:M 3:2**

**Mechanism** — **FOOSH with elbow extended + forearm pronated**

**Associated (30%)** — **LCL injury (commonest, up to 80% on MRI)**, MCL, **Essex-Lopresti**, coronoid, olecranon, **Monteggia**, **terrible triad**, **scaphoid**

### Anatomy

**Head** — **15° offset** from neck · **anterolateral third lacks subchondral bone** (fractures here) · 40° concave dish

**Safe zone (non-articular)** — **90–110° arc between radial styloid + Lister's tubercle** (straight lateral in neutral) · \~25% of head doesn't articulate with ulna

**Radiocapitellar joint** — **60% of elbow load**

| Ligament | Role |
|---|---|
| **LUCL** (supinator crest) | **Varus + ER (hypersupination)**; deficiency → **PLRI** |
| RCL | Into annular ligament |
| **Annular** | Holds head to lesser sigmoid notch; interposition → irreducible |
| **MCL anterior bundle** | **Primary valgus stabiliser** (radial head 2nd) |

**Radial head roles** — **secondary valgus restraint** (key if MCL deficient) · **longitudinal stability** (with IOM + DRUJ)

### Longitudinal instability (Essex-Lopresti) — radial head as buttress, excision contraindicated

**Essex-Lopresti** — **radial head fracture + IOM disruption + DRUJ injury** → proximal radial migration, ulnocarpal impaction\
✗ Wrong options: distal radius fracture

**Single best answer (TO)** — **interosseous membrane injury**; IOM gives **> 70% axial stability** (TO)

**Primary buttress against proximal migration** — **radial head** (then IOM) (OB) — read the options · TFCC (triangular fibrocartilage complex) also maintains length

**Excision in Essex-Lopresti (TO)** — if ever, delay **3 weeks** (IOM heals)

**Clues** — **wrist / DRUJ tenderness**, ballottement +, ulnar styloid base fracture, **radius pull test > 3 mm** (intra-op), DRUJ translation > 50% vs other side

**Late presentation** — only **20% satisfactory** vs 80% if acute

![Essex-Lopresti lesion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1019-essex-lopresti-lesion.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — lateral tenderness; **mechanical block to motion** (normal flexion 0–150°, pronation 75°, supination 85°) → **aspirate haematoma + intra-articular lidocaine** · PIN + ulnar nerve · pivot shift / posterolateral drawer (LUCL), valgus stress (MCL) · **palpate wrist + IOM**

**Pain limits exam at 1 wk** — **intra-articular lidocaine + re-examine**\
✗ Wrong options: CT · MRI · splint 2–3 wk · EUA

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| AP + lateral elbow, **forearm + wrist** | — |
| **Radiocapitellar (Greenspan)** | Oblique lateral, **45° cephalad** → head without coronoid overlap |
| **Fat pads** | Haemarthrosis; **posterior fat pad more sensitive** |
| CT | Comminution, fracture-dislocations (3D planning) |
| MRI | Ligaments; rarely changes management |
| Dynamic CT | Subtle DRUJ instability |

### Mason classification (Hotchkiss / Broberg-Morrey)

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| **I** | **< 2 mm**, **no block** |
| **II** | **> 2 mm** / angulated, **possible block** |
| **III** | **Comminuted**, displaced, **block** |
| **IV** | **+ elbow dislocation** |

![Mason classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1019-mason-classification.jpg)

**OTA 2R1** — A extra-articular · B partial articular · C complete articular · high interobserver variability

## 5 Management

```
Radial head fracture
├─ No block (Mason I / II) → sling 2–7 d + early ROM
├─ Block / displaced, ≤ 3 fragments, young → ORIF (headless screws ± plate in safe zone)
├─ > 3 fragments / dislocation / Essex-Lopresti / unstable → radial head arthroplasty (+ LCL repair)
└─ Low-demand, no instability → fragment excision (< 25–30%) or resection (never if unstable)
```

### Nonoperative vs ORIF — mechanical block & lidocaine test

**Mason I (violinist, no block)** — **sling \~2 days → active motion** · good **85–95%** · Mason II without block: early failure up to **12%**\
✗ Wrong options: cast ≥ 1 wk (worse function)

**Displaced partial articular + block (20–30° / 45–65° arcs)** — **ORIF** via **ECU / anconeus (Kocher)** with plate or screws\
✗ Wrong options: excision > 30% · medial approach · arthroplasty (older / ≥ 3 fragments)

**Mason II on CT, young** — **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · excision (< 25%) · CRPP (none in adults) · replacement

**Correct pairing** — **38-yr Mason II, 40° arc → ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: 4-part young → arthroplasty · resection in young Mason III · arthroplasty for minimally comminuted 3-part · excision with ulnar styloid / DRUJ injury

### Operative criteria

| Treatment | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Nonop** | Mason I; Mason II **without block** |
| **ORIF** | **Mason II with block**; Mason III / IV if feasible (**≤ 3 fragments**); open; complex elbow injury |
| **Arthroplasty** | **> 3 fragments** (≥ 3, OB), fracture-dislocation (> 30% articular), **Essex-Lopresti**, terrible triad, plastic deformity, malunion / nonunion |
| **Fragment excision** | Low demand, no instability, fragment **< 25%** (Orthobullets) · **< 30%** (OB) |
| **Resection** | Sedentary, **delayed isolated** fracture, salvage · not with **IOM injury (pull > 3 mm)**, coronoid fracture, MCL deficiency |

**ORIF outcomes** — **> 3 fragments → > 50% unsatisfactory** · isolated vs complex similar at 4 yr · fixation fails more than arthroplasty if elbow unstable

### Surgical approach & safe zone (PIN, forearm rotation, LUCL)

| Approach | Interval | Pros / cons |
|---|---|---|
| **Kocher** | **ECU (PIN) / anconeus (radial)** | **Less PIN risk**, extensile · **LUCL risk** if capsulotomy too posterior (stay anterior to crista supinatoris) |
| **Kaplan** | **EDC (PIN) / ECRB (radial)** | **Protects LUCL**, anterior fragment access · **more PIN risk** |
| EDC split | Through EDC | Anterior half, LCL spared |
| Posterior | Lateral flap | With olecranon / Monteggia |

**Approach** — **forearm pronated** (moves **PIN** anterior) · PIN crosses radius in supinator **\~4 cm distal** to head

**Plate application** — **forearm NEUTRAL → plate straight lateral** in safe zone · TO answer: "**just posterior to mid-axial line with forearm in pronation**" — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: pronation / supination

**Limits** — **within 90° arc (styloid–Lister's)** · **not distal to bicipital tuberosity (PIN)** · countersink articular implants

**Fixation** — headless / 2.0–2.4 mm screws (simple partial articular; **better ROM than plates**) · 1.5–2.0 mm plates for head + neck · plate removal common

**Least LUCL risk for arthroplasty** — **Kaplan (EDC / ECRB)**\
✗ Wrong options: Kocher (anconeus / ECU)

**Head-neck fracture past bicipital tuberosity + PIN palsy (GSW)** — **Kaplan + ORIF in safe zone** (neurolysis possible)

### Radial head arthroplasty — indications & sizing (overstuffing)

**4-fragment + dislocation** — **arthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: excision · ORIF · splint · hinged ex-fix

**Comminuted + dislocation** — **arthroplasty + LCL repair**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF + LCL · excision

**Osteoporotic 65-yr comminuted** — **metallic anatomic arthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: excision (wrist pain, instability, migration)

**Best indication** — **Mason III + IOM injury**

**Implants** — **loose-stem (spacer)**, press-fit, **bipolar** (may destabilise), **monoblock vs modular**, pyrocarbon (investigational) · **silicone abandoned** (fracture, synovitis)

**Overstuffing** — **> 2.5 mm** alters kinematics → pain, stiffness, **capitellar erosion** · **detect: visual widening of LATERAL ulnohumeral joint** → **remove + downsize**\
✗ Wrong options: medial on AP (only after 6 mm)

**Height** — proximal implant at **proximal lesser sigmoid notch**; **dish at level of lateral coronoid facet** · TO coronoid question: answer key not provided by the source

### Terrible triad & complex instability

**Terrible triad** — **elbow dislocation + radial head + coronoid fractures**

**Treatment** — **coronoid ORIF + radial head ORIF / arthroplasty + LUCL repair** → MCL repair ± hinged ex-fix if still unstable\
✗ Wrong options: **radial head excision — never** · transarticular screw · coronoid excision

**Most common complication** — **restricted motion (stiffness)**\
✗ Wrong options: redislocation · PIN · infection

**Distal humerus partial articular + radial head (23-yr)** — **ORIF both** (headless screws)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Stiffness** | 3–20%; **supination loss commonest**; functional arc **30–130° + 50 / 50 rotation** |
| PIN injury | Distal to bicipital tuberosity, retraction → splint, EMG later, transfers |
| Secondary displacement | **< 5%** of nonop |
| Nonunion / malunion | Common, often asymptomatic |
| Radiocapitellar OA | Common radiographically; overstuffing |
| **HO** | CNS injury, burns → indomethacin 6 wk; excise when mature (\~10% recur) |
| Resection sequelae | **Proximal migration, cubitus valgus, wrist pain, OA 73%** |

### Late sequelae — malunion & post-traumatic stiffness

**Malunited comminuted head 4 yr, stable DRUJ + elbow** — **radial head resection**\
✗ Wrong options: ligament reconstruction · ORIF · debridement · TEA

**Stiff 4 months after minimally displaced fracture** — **static / dynamic progressive splinting**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid · MUA (worsens) · home program · CPM

**8 months, arc 50–85°, ulnar paraesthesia, healed** — **anterior + posterior capsular release + ulnar nerve transposition**\
✗ Wrong options: head resection · partial release · injection

**60-yr, 1 yr after fracture, near-normal motion, intermittent pain (TO)** — answer key not provided by the source

### Paediatric radial neck — acceptable angulation

**10-yr-old (TO)** — **< 30° angulation + < 2 mm translation** (2–3 mm) → cast only; more → closed reduction (Israeli / Kaufman, Patterson, Neher-Torch), Metaizeau, joystick

## X vs Y

| | **Kocher** | **Kaplan** |
|---|---|---|
| Interval | **ECU / anconeus** | **EDC / ECRB** |
| Risk | **LUCL** | **PIN** |

| | **ORIF** | **Arthroplasty** |
|---|---|---|
| Fragments | **≤ 3** | **> 3** |
| Setting | Young, isolated | **Dislocation, Essex-Lopresti** |

## How it's asked

**"2 fragments + block?"** → **plate via ECU / anconeus**

**"Violinist Mason I?"** → **sling 2 days + active motion**

**"Mason II, young?"** → **ORIF**

**"Block 70–100°, rotation 10 / 10?"** → **ORIF**

**"Pain limits exam?"** → **intra-articular lidocaine + re-examine**

**"Correct pairing?"** → **ORIF for Mason II with 40° arc**

**"4 fragments + dislocation?"** → **radial head arthroplasty**

**"Intra-op overstuffing?"** → **remove + downsize implant**

**"Comminuted + dislocation in 32-yr?"** → **arthroplasty + LCL repair**

**"65-yr osteoporotic comminuted?"** → **metallic radial head arthroplasty**

**"40% fragment in 4 pieces after dislocation?"** → **radial head replacement**

**"Detect thick implant?"** → **visual widening of lateral ulnohumeral joint**

**"Safe plate position (TO)?"** → **just posterior to mid-axial line, forearm pronated**

**"Pronation during Kocher protects?"** → **PIN**

**"Forearm position for plating?"** → **neutral**

**"Plate limited to?"** → **90° arc safe zone**

**"Approach + fixation position?"** → **pronated for approach, neutral for plate**

**"Least LUCL injury?"** → **EDC / ECRB (Kaplan)**

**"GSW head-neck fracture + PIN palsy?"** → **Kaplan + ORIF in safe zone**

**"Terrible triad EXCEPT?"** → **radial head excision**

**"When is excision indicated (triad)?"** → **generally not indicated**

**"Distal humerus + radial head, 23-yr?"** → **ORIF both**

**"Terrible triad management?"** → **coronoid ORIF + radial head ORIF / arthroplasty + LUCL repair**

**"Commonest triad complication?"** → **restricted motion**

**"Essex-Lopresti involves (TO)?"** → **interosseous membrane injury**

**"Maintains radial length?"** → **TFCC**

**"Best arthroplasty indication?"** → **Mason III + IOM injury**

**"Radial head + positive ballottement?"** → **Essex-Lopresti**

**"Contraindicated with wrist tenderness?"** → **radial head excision**

**"Contraindication to excision?"** → **radius pull > 3 mm**

**"Ulnocarpal impingement after excision — missed?"** → **DRUJ + IOM injury**

**"Primary buttress against proximal migration?"** → **radial head**

**"Pull test 5 mm — contraindicated?"** → **radial head resection**

**"Malunited head, stable DRUJ, 4 yr?"** → **radial head resection**

**"Stiff after 10 wk therapy?"** → **progressive splinting**

**"Stiff 50–85° + ulnar symptoms?"** → **anterior + posterior release + ulnar transposition**

**"Child radial neck acceptable (TO)?"** → **< 30° angulation, < 2 mm translation**
