# Radius and Ulnar Shaft Fractures

*Trauma · 51 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Who** — men **10–20 yr**, women **> 60 yr**

**Mechanism** — **high energy** (direct blow, MVA, fall from height, sport) · low-energy both-bone → suspect wrist / elbow injury

**Associated** — **Galeazzi, Monteggia, Essex-Lopresti**, compartment syndrome

### Anatomy

**Rotation axis** — **radial head → ulnar fovea**; radius rotates around ulna

**Radial bow** — complex; **max \~15 mm at 60%** of radial length · ulna slight bow in distal 75%

**Nerves** — median between PT heads then FDS / FDP · ulnar between FCU heads → FDP ring + small · **PIN around radial neck through supinator** → posterior IOM → 4th compartment

**Vessels** — brachial bifurcates **1 cm below elbow** · radial artery adherent to FCR

### Interosseous membrane anatomy

**IOM** — oblique radius → ulna; load passes **distal radius → proximal ulna** · distal fibres tight in supination, central in neutral

| IOM ligaments (5) | Note |
|---|---|
| **Central band** | **Key load-bearer; reconstruct this** |
| Accessory band, distal oblique bundle, proximal oblique cord, dorsal oblique accessory cord | — |
| **NOT part of IOM** | **Annular ligament** |

**Essex-Lopresti (TO)** — **injury to interosseous membrane** (+ radial head fracture + DRUJ)

![Interosseous membrane](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1025-interosseus-membrae.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — deformity, open wounds, **tense compartments**, **passive finger stretch pain**; radial + ulnar pulses; median, radial, ulnar nerves; hand dominance + occupation

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral forearm **+ wrist + elbow**; **radial head aligned with capitellum on all views**; contralateral for planning · CT rarely

| OTA (radius / ulna shaft) | Subtypes |
|---|---|
| **A simple** | A1 spiral · A2 oblique · A3 transverse |
| **B wedge** | B2 intact · B3 fragmentary |
| **C multifragmentary** | C2 intact segmental · C3 fragmentary |

### Monteggia & Galeazzi variants (Bado, transolecranon, chronic)

**Bado IV (TO)** — radius + ulna fractures with radial head dislocation (TO image: anterior angulation + anterior head)

**Transolecranon fracture-dislocation — EXCEPT (TO)** — "**a Monteggia variant**" — it is an **anterior elbow dislocation with intact PRUJ**, mostly **bony**, often **coronoid** fracture

**Monteggia ORIF sequence (TO)** — ulna → coronoid → radial head (fix / replace) → LCL repair

**Old malunited ulna + posterior radial head dislocation, 60-yr, painful (TO)** — answer key not provided by the source

## 5 Management

```
Adult both-bone forearm fracture (> 10 yr)
├─ Closed / Gustilo I–IIIA → ORIF, two incisions, 3.5 mm plates (radius first if simpler)
│      ├─ simple → compression (absolute stability)
│      └─ comminuted → bridge plate (relative stability)
├─ Gustilo IIIB / severe soft tissue → ex-fix
└─ Bone loss → graft (< 5 cm) · vascularised fibula / Masquelet (large)
```

### Treatment options

| Option | Indication | Fact |
|---|---|---|
| Cast / brace (Muenster, above elbow) | **Rare** — non-displaced, non-surgical candidates | **> 50° rotation loss in 30%**, high nonunion |
| **ORIF** | **Nearly all ≥ 10 yr**; **Gustilo I–IIIA primary ORIF** | **> 95% union** compression · **88%** bridge plating |
| Ex-fix | **Gustilo IIIB** | Distal pins **2nd + 3rd MC**, **≤ 4 mm** |
| IMN | **Very poor soft tissue** only | No rotational / axial control, can't keep bow, **high nonunion** · **flexible nails contraindicated in length-unstable adult fractures** |

### Fixation principles & constructs

**Comminuted closed both-bone (22-yr)** — **ORIF without acute graft**\
✗ Wrong options: ex-fix · flexible nails · graft · brace

**Transverse + compression plates** — **absolute stability → direct (primary) healing by internal remodelling** (intramembranous)

**Comminuted (GSW)** — **ORIF with relative stability (bridge plate)** → endochondral (secondary) healing

**Greatest bending rigidity** — **3.5 mm stainless steel DCP on both bones**\
✗ Wrong options: titanium · ⅓ tubular

**Technique** — **3.5 mm DCP / LC-DCP** · **≥ 6 cortices** each side · **long plates** (torsion) · **contoured radius plate**, straight ulnar plate · locking in osteoporosis · 2.0 / 2.7 lag screws · dorsal = tension side (volar seats better)\
✗ Wrong options: 4.5 mm (refracture)

**Post-op** — early ROM; **NWB \~6 wk** (Orthobullets) · **WBAT if ≥ 8 cortices each side** (OB) — read the options · clinical healing \~3 months

**Polytrauma (femur IMN, simple fibula ORIF, forearm ORIF, humerus ORIF)** — **WBAT all extremities** (OB)

### Restoration of radial bow & forearm rotation

**Radial bow restoration** — **most important for function → forearm rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: wrist strength · elbow ROM · synostosis

**Limited pronosupination after long straight radial plate** — **loss of radial bow**\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion · PIN · ECU at DRUJ · HO

**Cause of poor pronation** — **straight plate on radius**\
✗ Wrong options: bridge plating · straight ulnar plate · dual incision

**Malunion > 20°** — limits rotation

![Radial bow measurement](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1025-radial-bow.jpg)

### Surgical approaches (Henry, PIN, two-incision synostosis risk)

**Two incisions** — **↓ synostosis** (1 / 65 vs 5 / 12 single) · no wound, neuritis, malunion benefit

| Approach | Use |
|---|---|
| **Volar Henry** | Distal + middle ⅓ radius |
| **Dorsal Thompson** | Proximal ⅓ radius |
| **Subcutaneous ulnar** | Ulna (dorsal ulnar cutaneous nerve at risk) |

**Proximal Henry** — **radial artery medially**, **supinator laterally**, forearm **SUPINATED** (moves PIN dorsally)

**Pronated during proximal Henry** — **PIN injury → weak thumb (+ finger) extension**\
✗ Wrong options: 1st web numbness (superficial radial) · opposition (median) · abduction (ulnar)

### Open fractures & bone loss (bone graft, vascularised fibula, GSW)

**Clean 2 cm open wound** — **I&D + ORIF + immediate closure**

**1 cm segmental gap after debridement** — **autograft**\
✗ Wrong options: TCP, CaPO₄, CaSO₄, **BMP-3 — not osteoinductive**

**Graft by defect size** — **< 5 cm → cancellous autograft** · larger → **vascularised fibula** (↓ infection), allograft, transport, Masquelet

**10 cm defect, strut allograft vs fibula** — **allograft → more infection** (+ nonunion, fracture, delayed union)

**GSW, large soft-tissue + bone loss** — **ORIF + free fibular osteoseptocutaneous flap**\
✗ Wrong options: strut / cancellous allograft (infection) · ex-fix definitive

**GSW comminuted, 2 cm wound** — **I&D + 3.5 mm LC-DCP bridge plate**\
✗ Wrong options: 4.5 mm · compression · delay · ex-fix

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Synostosis** | **3–9%**; **single incision**, proximal ⅓, head injury, polytrauma, fragments / hardware in interosseous space |
| Infection | 3% |
| **Compartment syndrome** | \~1% (up to 15%); **superficial volar release may suffice** (connected) |
| **Nonunion** | **< 5%** compression · 12% bridge; IMN |
| Malunion | Radial bow |
| Nerve | **PIN (Henry proximal, Monteggia)**; median (modified Henry); dorsal ulnar cutaneous → observe 3 months |
| **Refracture** | **Up to 10%** |

**Expected losses** — grip **25%**, pronosupination strength **30%**, wrist flexion 16%, extension 37% strength; motion loss < 10°

### Nonunion

**Atrophic** — **plate exchange + autogenous cancellous graft (ICBG) + compression plate** · smokers high risk\
✗ Wrong options: Ilizarov · stimulators · ulnar shortening · IMN · bone scan if labs normal

**Hypertrophic** — ↑ fixation (compression plate) · infected / atrophic → **Masquelet**

### Refracture after plate removal

**Greatest risk** — **removal < 1 yr** · **diaphyseal** location (correlates most) · **4.5 mm plates** · comminution, displacement, lucency, immediate activity

**NOT a risk** — **3.5 mm DCP**\
✗ Wrong options: locking screw removal · metaphyseal implants (lower)

**Prevention** — **functional brace 6 wk + protect 3 months**

### Heterotopic ossification — timing of excision

**Excision** — **as early as 6 months** (4–6 months) with **radiation ± indomethacin**\
✗ Wrong options: wait for ankylosis / maturity

### Paediatric forearm — deformity limiting rotation

**Most limiting (TO)** — **angulation** (10° → 20° rotation loss) > malrotation (10° → 10°) · **bayonet apposition doesn't limit**

| Age (TO) | Sagittal | Coronal |
|---|---|---|
| < 9 yr | 20° | 15° |
| 9–11 yr | 15° | 5° |
| 11–13 yr | 10° | 0° |

## X vs Y

| | **Absolute stability** | **Relative stability** |
|---|---|---|
| Construct | **Compression plate, lag screws** | **Bridge plate**, IMN, cast, ex-fix |
| Healing | **Primary (internal remodelling)** | **Secondary (callus, endochondral)** |
| Fracture | Simple transverse | Comminuted |

## How it's asked

**"Restoring radial bow improves?"** → **forearm rotation**

**"Limited pronosupination after straight plate?"** → **loss of radial bow**

**"Pronation 15° one year later — cause?"** → **straight plate on radius**

**"Comminuted closed both-bone?"** → **ORIF**

**"Compression plating healing?"** → **absolute stability, direct healing by remodelling**

**"Comminuted GSW radius?"** → **ORIF with relative stability**

**"Greatest bending rigidity?"** → **3.5 mm stainless steel DCP on both**

**"Two incisions advantage?"** → **lower synostosis**

**"Proximal Henry retraction + position?"** → **artery medial, supinator lateral, supinated**

**"Pronated during Henry → deficit?"** → **weak thumb extension**

**"Clean 2 cm open wound?"** → **ORIF + immediate closure**

**"1 cm segmental gap?"** → **bone grafting**

**"Strut allograft vs fibula?"** → **higher infection**

**"GSW with large bone + soft-tissue loss?"** → **ORIF + free fibular flap**

**"GSW comminuted both-bone?"** → **I&D + 3.5 mm bridge plate**

**"Atrophic radial nonunion?"** → **plate exchange + autogenous graft**

**"Atrophic ulnar nonunion, smoker?"** → **ICBG + compression plating**

**"Refracture risk EXCEPT?"** → **3.5 mm DCP**

**"Greatest refracture risk?"** → **removal < 1 yr**

**"Pianist refracture — accurate?"** → **diaphyseal removals higher risk**

**"Essex-Lopresti (TO)?"** → **interosseous membrane injury**

**"IOM ligaments EXCEPT?"** → **annular ligament**

**"Earliest HO excision?"** → **6 months**

**"Polytrauma WB status?"** → **WBAT all extremities**

**"Bado type (TO image)?"** → **IV**

**"Transolecranon EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **Monteggia variant**

**"Paediatric deformity most limiting rotation (TO)?"** → **angulation**

# Sacral Fractures

*Trauma · 52 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **30–45% of pelvic ring injuries**; bimodal (young high energy · elderly low-energy / insufficiency)

**Neurological injury** — **25%**; **missed in 75% of intact** and 50% with deficits

### Anatomy

**Sacrum** — 5 fused vertebrae; **4 foramina**; load from S1 → iliac wings → acetabulum

**Nerves** — **L5 root on top of sacral ala** · S1–S4 through foramina (**S1 + S2 most injured**) · **S2–S5: sphincter tone, bulbocavernosus reflex, perianal sensation**; **unilateral preservation enough for bowel + bladder**

**Perianal numbness** — **S2 root**\
✗ Wrong options: L3–S1

## 2 Mechanism

**MVA / fall from height** (landing on feet → U-type) · repetitive stress / osteoporosis → insufficiency

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — peripelvic pain; rotate iliac wings; **Morel-Lavallée** (lumbosacral degloving); **vaginal exam** (open); **rectal exam**, S2–S5 sensation, **anal wink, bulbocavernosus, cremasteric**; pulses ± ABI

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Radiographs** | **Show only 30%** |
| Lateral | Screening; poor quality |
| **Inlet** | Sacral canal, superior S1 |
| **Outlet** | **True AP of sacrum** |
| Signs | **L4 / L5 TP fractures**, asymmetric foramina, anterior ring injury, **stepladder sign** (AP; overriding transverse fragments) |
| **CT** | **Study of choice** (coronal + sagittal) · **sagittal → lumbopelvic instability** |
| MRI | **Neural compromise** suspected |

### Denis zones & neurological injury

| Denis | Location | Neuro deficit |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Lateral to foramina (ala)** | **5.9%** — lowest |
| **2** | **Through foramina** | **28.4%** |
| **3** | **Medial → spinal canal** | **\~60% — highest** (bowel / bladder) |

**Classified by most medial extent**

![Denis zone 2 sacral fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1032-zone-2.jpg)

### Transverse, U-type & Roy-Camille

**Transverse sacral fracture** — ↑ nerve dysfunction (Tile A3 / Denis zone III)

**U-type (H / λ)** — **bilateral vertical (zone II) + transverse (S2)** → **spinopelvic dissociation**; axial load (fall onto feet); high neuro deficit, **cauda equina**

![U-type sacral fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1032-u-type-illustration.jpg)

| Roy-Camille (zone 3 transverse, sagittal) (TO) | Description |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Angulated, no translation** |
| **2** | **Angulated + incomplete translation** |
| **3** | **Complete translation + displacement** |
| 4 (Strange-Vognsen) | **Comminuted (axial load)** |

**Isler (TO)** — L5–S1 facet: 1 lateral (stable) · 2 through facet · 3 medial (2 + 3 unstable)

**AO (TO)** — A sacrococcygeal (stable) · B unilateral longitudinal · C spinopelvic instability

## 5 Management

```
Sacral fracture
├─ < 1 cm displacement, no neuro deficit / insufficiency → progressive WB ± orthosis
├─ > 1 cm / soft-tissue compromise / failed nonop / displacement → fixation
│      ├─ sagittal plane (zone I–II) → IS / transsacral screws (S2 if dysmorphic)
│      └─ U-type / vertical zone II / spinopelvic → triangular osteosynthesis
└─ Neurological deficit → fixation + decompression
```

### Operative criteria

| Nonoperative | Operative |
|---|---|
| **< 1 cm displacement**, no deficit | **Displacement > 1 cm** |
| Insufficiency fractures | Soft-tissue compromise, open |
| — | **Persistent pain / displacement after nonop** |
| — | **Neurological deficit → + decompression** (indirect traction or laminectomy / foraminotomy) |
| — | Posterior cortical displacement **> 4 mm**, + EUA, lumbopelvic dissociation (TO) |

### Spinopelvic dissociation (U-type) & triangular osteosynthesis

**Most stable for unstable transforaminal / U-type** — **triangular osteosynthesis = lumbopelvic (L4 / L5 pedicle → iliac screws) + iliosacral screw**\
✗ Wrong options: ex-fix · IS alone · anterior plate + IS · transiliac bars · tension band

**U-type + bladder loss (TO)** — **triangular osteosynthesis** (allows laminectomy + early WB)\
✗ Wrong options: bilateral S1 screws · laminectomy + plating · transsacral screws alone

**Vertical zone II + dysmorphic sacrum (TO)** — **triangular osteosynthesis with S2 transsacral screw**\
✗ Wrong options: horizontal S1 screw (L5 injury) · posterior tension band (wounds, weak vs shear) · S1 TITS

**Neuro prognosis predictor** — **initial fracture displacement**\
✗ Wrong options: time to surgery · smoking · BMI · steroids

**Elderly bilateral ala fractures** — **U-type / lumbopelvic instability → fixation** · frail: 6 wk NWB then progressive (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: rami fractures alone · unilateral ala

### Iliosacral / transsacral screws — fluoroscopy & dysmorphism

| View | Shows |
|---|---|
| **Lateral sacral** | **Iliac cortical density (ICD)** — start **posterior to ICD** (normal sacrum) → protects **L5** |
| **Inlet** | Anterior limit; **anterior alar indentation** in dysmorphism (in-out-in → L5) (OB) |
| **Outlet** | Trajectory vs **S1 foramen**; **cephalad alar border** (TO: critical to avoid superior breach → L5) |

**L5 breach view** — **outlet (TO)** · **inlet (OB)** — read the options

**Start point (S1 transsacral)** — **posterior to ICD, anterior to neural tunnels** (narrow safe zone); SI screws slightly more posterior

**Trajectory** — **sacral fracture → horizontal transverse screw** · **SI joint disruption → caudal-to-cranial, posterior-to-anterior** · avoid **over-compression** (nerve injury) → positional screw

**Vessel at risk** — **deep superior branch of superior gluteal artery**\
✗ Wrong options: inferior gluteal

**Dysmorphism** — **ICD not colinear with alar slope** → misleading · **S1 corridor narrow + oblique**, **S2 corridor larger → S2 transsacral screw ("safe harbour")** (TO)

**Dysmorphic signs (TO)** — non-colinear SI joints (sacrum high), **acute alar slope**, **mamillary processes**, **tongue-in-groove SI**, residual S1–S2 disc / accessory foramina, crescent void

**Screws** — not in osteoporotic bone; can't remove fragments; malreduction possible

**Posterior tension band plating** — two posterior incisions; ± IS screws; **wound complications**

### Sacral fragility fractures

**Nondisplaced zone 1 insufficiency (79-yr)** — **WBAT + gait PT** · fixation if > 1 cm, neuro deficit, or failed nonop\
✗ Wrong options: EUA · NWB · lumbar MRI · fixation

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| VTE | Immobility |
| **Iatrogenic nerve injury** | Over-compression, malpositioned screws |
| Malreduction | **Vertical displacement** |
| Late | LE deficits, **urinary, rectal, sexual dysfunction** |

**Prognosis** — **neurological deficit = most important outcome factor**; **displacement ↑ neuro risk**

## X vs Y

| | **Zone 1** | **Zone 2** | **Zone 3** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Site | Ala | Foramina | Canal |
| Deficit | 5.9% | 28.4% | **\~60%** |

| | **SI joint screw** | **Sacral fracture screw** |
|---|---|---|
| Trajectory | **Caudal→cranial, posterior→anterior** | **Horizontal, transverse** |

## How it's asked

**"Perianal numbness root?"** → **S2**

**"Highest nerve injury pattern?"** → **Denis zone 3**

**"Most neuro deficit?"** → **zone 3 (central canal)**

**"Predicts neuro dysfunction (U-type)?"** → **fracture displacement**

**"Bowel / bladder dysfunction — pattern?"** → **zone III (medial to foramen)**

**"Vertical zone II + dysmorphism (TO)?"** → **triangular osteosynthesis with S2 transsacral screw**

**"U-type + bladder loss (TO)?"** → **triangular osteosynthesis**

**"Most stable for transforaminal fracture?"** → **triangular osteosynthesis**

**"Fall onto feet, perineal numbness?"** → **U-type spinopelvic dissociation**

**"Most stable for U-type?"** → **triangular osteosynthesis**

**"View preventing cephalad breach (TO)?"** → **outlet view**

**"Constricted S1 corridor — alternative (TO)?"** → **S2 transsacral screw**

**"View showing anterior indentation (dysmorphic)?"** → **inlet view**

**"SI screw — true?"** → **guide wire posterior to ICD is safe in normal sacra**

**"S1 transsacral start point?"** → **posterior to ICD, anterior to neural tunnels**

**"79-yr nondisplaced zone 1 insufficiency?"** → **WBAT + PT**

**"Lumbopelvic instability in elderly?"** → **bilateral sacral ala fractures**

**"Roy-Camille type (TO)?"** → **type 2 (angulated + incomplete translation)**

# Scapula Fractures

*Trauma · 53 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **< 1% of fractures**, 3–5% of shoulder girdle · age 25–50, M > F · mortality 2–5%

**Location** — **body / spine 45–50%** · glenoid 35% (neck 25%, fossa / rim 10%) · acromion 8% · coracoid 7%

**Mechanism** — **high energy 80–90%** (**MVC > 70%**) · FOOSH · dislocation: **anterior → anterior rim**, **posterior (seizure, shock) → posterior rim**

### Associated injuries (rib / chest injury, ISS)

**Associated injuries** — **80–95%** · **↑ Injury Severity Score**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ mortality · LOS · ICU

| Injury | Rate |
|---|---|
| **Chest injury** (commonest) | **80%** — pneumo / haemothorax > 30%, **contusion > 40%** |
| **Rib fractures (commonest fracture)** | **52–53%** |
| Head injury | 35–50% |
| Ipsilateral limb | 50% (**clavicle 25%**) |
| Spine | 26–30% |
| Pelvis / acetabulum | 15% |
| Vascular (subclavian / axillary) | 11% |
| Brachial plexus | 5–13% (**75% resolve**) |

**CT chest** — consider for ribs + lung injury

### Anatomy

**Body** — **18 muscles**; spine separates supra- / infraspinatus; **spinoglenoid notch** → infraspinatus only · scapular notch → supra + infraspinatus

**Glenoid** — pear-shaped, **1–5° retroversion**, **15° superior tilt**; labrum deepens **50%**

**Acromion** — pre- (tip), meso- (mid), meta- (base) acromion · **os acromiale** (unfused meso / meta) ≠ fracture

**Coracoid** — ossification centres open **to \~25 yr** (angle, tip); conjoint tendon, pectoralis minor; CC ligaments **\~25 mm from tip**

**SSSC (superior shoulder suspensory complex)** — **distal clavicle, acromion, coracoid, glenoid + AC + CC ligaments** · **double disruption = unstable ("floating shoulder")**

**Axillary nerve** — inferior to glenoid · motion ⅓ scapulothoracic, ⅔ glenohumeral

![Superior shoulder suspensory complex](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1013-superior-shoulder-suspensory-complex-sssc.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Often missed** with distracting injuries · diffuse pain, dyspnoea, chest pain · passive ROM only · check **axillary, radial, median, ulnar**, pulses

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Measurement |
|---|---|
| True AP, **Grashey**, **scapular Y**, axillary; **CXR** | Pneumothorax; **medial border–spine > 1 cm → scapulothoracic dissociation** |
| **Glenopolar angle (Grashey)** | Normal **30–45°**; **< 20–22° → poor / operative** |
| Lateral border offset | Medialisation |
| Angulation | **Scapular Y** |
| **CT (3D)** | Intra-articular, displacement > 1 cm, thoracic / spine injuries |
| MRI | SSSC ligament injury |

### Classifications

| Ideberg (glenoid, Goss) | Fracture |
|---|---|
| **Ia** | **Anterior rim** |
| **Ib** | **Posterior rim** |
| **II** | Fossa → exits **inferiorly** |
| **III** | Fossa → exits **superiorly** |
| **IV** | Fossa → exits **medially** through body |
| Va / Vb / Vc | II + IV · III + IV · II + III + IV |
| VI | **Severe comminution** |

**AO-OTA glenoid (more reliable)** — 14F0 extra-articular (neck) · 14F1 simple intra-articular (rim, transverse) · 14F2 multifragmentary · 14B body extension

| Kuhn (acromion) | Fracture |
|---|---|
| I | Non / minimally displaced |
| II | Displaced, subacromial space OK |
| **III** | **Displaced → narrows subacromial space** |

| Ogawa (coracoid) | Fracture |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Proximal to CC ligaments** |
| **II** | **Tip** (distal) |

![Ideberg classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1013-ideberg-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Scapula fracture
├─ Most (90% minimally displaced) → sling 2–3 wk + early motion (weekly X-ray × 3 wk)
├─ Glenoid: step-off > 4 mm or > 20–25% rim with subluxation → ORIF
├─ Body / neck: medialisation > 20 mm, angulation > 40°, GPA < 20–22°, translation > 1 cm → ORIF (Judet)
└─ Floating shoulder (SSSC double disruption) → fix (clavicle ± scapula)
```

### Operative indications — glenoid neck displacement, SSSC double disruption, scapular spine

| Fracture | ORIF if |
|---|---|
| **Body** | **Medialisation > 20 mm**, **GPA < 20–22°**, **angulation > 40°** (> 45°, OB), medialisation > 15 mm + angulation > 35° |
| **Neck** | **Angulation > 40°**, **translation > 1 cm**, GPA < 20–22°, **SSSC double disruption** |
| **Glenoid** | **> 20–25% anterior / posterior rim + subluxation**; **step-off > 4 mm** (> 5 mm OB · > 3–4 mm OB) |
| Acromion | **Kuhn III**, painful nonunion, impingement, double disruption |
| Coracoid | > 1 cm, nonunion, Ogawa I into body, double disruption (✗ Wrong options: Ogawa II tip (nonop even > 1 cm)) |
| Any | Open fracture |

**Neck: medial 3 mm, angulated 20°** — **sling 2 wk + PT**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF

**Displaced lateral scapular spine, weak abduction** — **ORIF** (poor deltoid lever, impingement)\
✗ Wrong options: stimulator · brace · acromioplasty · excision

**Displaced extra-articular scapula + comminuted clavicle** — **ORIF scapula + clavicle**\
✗ Wrong options: sling · fix one only

**Glenoid neck + strongest operative indication** — **ipsilateral distal clavicle fracture (floating shoulder)**\
✗ Wrong options: plexus injury (75% resolve) · LC1 pelvis · pneumothorax · 2 rib fractures

**Double disruption with neck fracture** — **AC ligament injury**\
✗ Wrong options: labrum, supraspinatus, transverse scapular ligament, coracohumeral ligament

**Nonop outcomes** — union 6–8 wk; near-normal function; **may displace in first 3 wk** (rib fractures, SSSC injury) · poor if GPA < 20°

### Floating shoulder

**Definition (TO)** — **ipsilateral clavicle + scapular neck fracture LATERAL to coracoid (anatomical neck)** · surgical neck (medial to coracoid) + clavicle + **CA + CC ligament disruption** also counts — TO explanation calls both correct

**Surgical neck** — medial to coracoid base (common) · **anatomical neck** — lateral (rare, unstable)

### Posterior (Judet) approach — internervous interval

**Modified Judet** — **infraspinatus (suprascapular n.) ↔ teres minor (axillary n.)**\
✗ Wrong options: supra / infraspinatus · teres minor / major (axillary / lower subscapular) · deltopectoral

**Incision** — along spine + down vertebral border ("L" / boomerang); detach posterior deltoid

**At risk** — **posterior branch axillary nerve** (teres minor retracted inferiorly), **suprascapular nerve + artery** (infraspinatus superiorly), circumflex scapular + posterior humeral circumflex vessels

**Other approaches** — straight posterior (isolated neck / lateral border) · **deltopectoral** (anterior glenoid, Ideberg II; axillary + musculocutaneous risk; subscapularis failure) · lateral mid-axial (inferior glenoid) · posterior over spine (acromion)

**Fixation** — 2.7 / 3.5 mm plates (locking on thin border) · rim: screws / arthroscopic anchors · acromion: lag + recon plate / tension band · coracoid: 2.7 / 3.5 mm screws

![Judet approach](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1013-judet-approach.jpg)

### Glenoid rim fracture — instability

**Large anterior-inferior rim fracture after dislocation** — untreated → **recurrent instability** (loss of concavity compression) → ORIF

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| PTOA | Residual step-off → arthroplasty |
| Malunion | Nonop, angulation / medialisation; glenoid → RSA |
| Recurrent instability | Young, large bone loss |
| Neurovascular | Scapulothoracic dissociation → **EMG at 3–6 wk** |

### Carpal fracture epidemiology

**Commonest carpal fracture (TO)** — **scaphoid** > triquetrum > trapezium

## X vs Y

| | **Surgical neck of scapula** | **Anatomical neck** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | **Medial** to coracoid | **Lateral** to coracoid |
| Frequency | Common | Rare |
| + clavicle | Floating shoulder if CA + CC torn | **Floating shoulder** |

## How it's asked

**"Scapula fracture association in polytrauma?"** → **↑ Injury Severity Score**

**"Most associated orthopaedic injury?"** → **rib fracture**

**"Most common associated fracture?"** → **rib fracture**

**"Most commonly associated injury?"** → **chest injury**

**"Neck 3 mm medial, 20° angulated?"** → **sling 2 wk + PT**

**"Displaced scapular spine, weak abduction?"** → **ORIF**

**"Scapula + comminuted clavicle?"** → **ORIF both**

**"Strongest operative indication with glenoid neck fracture?"** → **ipsilateral distal clavicle fracture**

**"Double disruption with neck fracture?"** → **AC ligament injury**

**"Judet interval?"** → **infraspinatus / teres minor (suprascapular / axillary)**

**"Large anterior rim fracture untreated?"** → **recurrent instability**

**"Floating shoulder definition (TO)?"** → **clavicle + scapular neck lateral to coracoid**

**"Commonest carpal fracture (TO)?"** → **scaphoid**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Scapulothoracic Dissociation

*Trauma · 54 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Mechanism & associations

**Mechanism** — **lateral traction** on the shoulder girdle (high energy, motorcycle) · heart, chest wall, lung trauma

**Orthopaedic** — scapula, **clavicle** (widely displaced), **AC separation**, SC dislocation · **flail extremity 52%**

**Vascular** — **subclavian artery (commonest)** > axillary

**Neurological** — **up to 90% (> 90%)**; ipsilateral **brachial plexus**, often **complete** · **neuro > vascular** injuries

### Anatomy

**Scapulothoracic joint** — sliding articulation over **ribs 2–7** · abduction **2:1 (GH 120° : ST 60°)**

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — massive shoulder swelling + bruising · **↓ / absent pulses** (hallmark) · neurological deficits (**critical**)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **AP chest** | **Medial scapular border > 1 cm further from spinous process** than other side |
| Shoulder views | Displaced clavicle fracture, **AC widening**, SC dislocation |
| **Chest CT (axial)** | **Asymmetric spinous process–scapula distance** |
| **Angiogram** | Subclavian / axillary injury |

![Scapulothoracic dissociation — laterally displaced scapula](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1014-xr-std.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Scapulothoracic dissociation
├─ No significant vascular injury, stable → supportive care (collaterals)
├─ Unstable + axillary injury → high lateral thoracotomy + repair
├─ Unstable + subclavian injury → median sternotomy + repair
├─ Clavicle / AC injury → ORIF (avoid neurogenic nonunion)
└─ Complete brachial plexus injury → early forequarter amputation
```

**Flail extremity treatment** — sling, tendon transfers, **forequarter amputation** · **\~21% early above-elbow amputation**

## 6 Complications

### Prognosis — neurovascular injury and flail extremity

**Mortality** — **10%**\
✗ Wrong options: 30%

**Best predictor of function** — **neurological status / deficit**\
✗ Wrong options: open wound size, contamination, debridement timing · pulses · age

**Worst outcome** — **complete brachial plexus injury** · vascular injuries usually repairable → don't worsen outcome

**Most likely outcome with neuro injury** — **flail extremity** (death also common)

**Uniquely associated** — **flail upper extremity**\
✗ Wrong options: malunion · intubation · abdominal / C-spine injury

**EMG** — **3–6 wk** after injury

## How it's asked

**"Best predictor of functional outcome?"** → **neurological deficit**

**"Likely outcome with neuro injury?"** → **flail extremity**

**"Exam finding predicting outcome?"** → **neurological compromise**

**"Most important outcome predictor (open humerus too)?"** → **neurovascular status**

**"Uniquely associated?"** → **flail upper extremity**

**"Prognosis — true?"** → **complete brachial plexus injury has the worst outcome**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Septic Arthritis - Adult

*Trauma · 55 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Joints (descending)** — **knee (\~50%)** > hip > shoulder > elbow > ankle > **sternoclavicular** (IV drug users)

**Risk factors** — **age > 80**, diabetes, RA, cirrhosis, HIV, crystal arthropathy, endocarditis / recent bacteraemia, **IV drug use**, recent joint surgery, prosthetic implant

### Pathophysiology

**Seeding** — **bacteraemia** · **direct inoculation** (trauma, surgery) · **contiguous spread** (adjacent osteomyelitis)

**Cartilage destruction** — irreversible; **begins by 8 hours** · proteolytic enzymes from **PMNs**

**S. aureus vs gonococcus — joint destruction** — **S. aureus → greater WBC influx (> 50,000)**\
✗ Wrong options: filamentous appendages (that is gonococcal adhesion; S. aureus uses MSCRAMMs) · IL-1 inhibitors / endotoxin (both organisms induce IL-1 + have endotoxin activity) · penicillin sensitivity

### Organisms

| Organism | Setting |
|---|---|
| **Staphylococcus aureus** | **Commonest (> 50%)** in all patients, **including IV drug users** · gram +ve cocci in **grape-like clusters** |
| **Neisseria gonorrhoeae** | **\~20%** · commonest in **healthy sexually active adolescents / young adults** · **intracellular + extracellular gram −ve diplococci** |
| Gram −ve bacilli (E. coli, Proteus, Klebsiella, Enterobacter) | **10–20%** · neonates, IV drug users, elderly, immunocompromised diabetics |
| Streptococcus (Group A pyogenes commonest; Group B agalactiae) | Gram +ve cocci in **chains** · Group B → infants, elderly, diabetics |
| **Pseudomonas aeruginosa** | **IV drug abuse** (commonest SC organism in the **1980s**) |
| Propionibacterium acnes | **Shoulder surgery** |
| Salmonella / Strep. pneumoniae | **Sickle cell disease** |
| Bartonella henselae | **HIV** |
| Pasteurella multocida | **Dog / cat bite** |
| Eikenella corrodens | **Human bite** |
| Fungal / Candida | Immunocompromised |

![Staphylococcus aureus — gram-positive cocci in clusters](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1058-staph-aerues-reference-medscape.jpg)

### Sternoclavicular septic arthritis

**Frequency** — **\~1% of septic joints** · IV drug users, **diabetes**, renal insufficiency / **haemodialysis**, immunocompromise, alcoholism, HIV

**Organism** — **S. aureus** — even in IV drug users · gonococcal, fungal, candida in HIV\
✗ Wrong options: Pseudomonas (↑ incidence but not commonest) · Strep. pneumoniae · S. epidermidis · P. acnes · gonococcus

**Presenting symptom** — **chest wall tenderness**\
✗ Wrong options: shoulder pain (much less often)

**Imaging** — **CT / MRI routinely before surgery** → **retrosternal abscess, chest wall phlegmon**, extent of osteomyelitis (may need general / cardiothoracic surgeon) · MRI better; CT if MRI unavailable

**Treatment** — **I&D + antibiotics**; **aspiration alone ineffective** · with **medial clavicular osteolysis (osteomyelitis) → resection of SC joint** (best outcome, lowest recurrence)\
✗ Wrong options: antibiotics alone · repeat aspiration · hyperbaric O₂ · simple I&D (high recurrence)

![Sternoclavicular septic arthritis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1058-sc-joint-septic-arthritis-moved.jpg)

### Septic olecranon / prepatellar bursitis

**Organism** — **S. aureus \~80%** → **gram +ve cocci in pairs and clusters** · mixed flora common\
✗ Wrong options: chains (Strep) · branching bacilli (Nocardia / Actinomyces) · gram −ve diplococci (Neisseria) · thin rods (E. coli)

**Route** — **direct inoculation** (poor bursal blood supply) · middle-aged men

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — joint pain · **fever in only 60%** · may look toxic

**Exam** — erythema, effusion, warmth, tenderness · **can't bear weight**, **can't tolerate PROM** · limb held in position of **maximum joint volume** → **hip in FABER**

![Hip held in FABER](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1058-faber-aafp-org.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Radiographs** (AP + lateral) | Joint space widening / effusion, periarticular osteopenia |
| **Ultrasound** | Confirms effusion in **large joints (hip)**; guides aspiration |
| **MRI** | Effusion + adjacent **osteomyelitis** |
| CT | Intra-articular air (traumatic arthrotomy adjunct) · SC joint abscess |

![Lateral knee — septic effusion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1058-lateral-knee-septic-effusion.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria

| Test | Septic value |
|---|---|
| Serum WBC | **> 10,000** with left shift |
| **ESR** | **> 30 mm/h** · may be normal early; rises in 2 days, may rise 3–5 days after antibiotics, normal by 3–4 wk |
| **CRP** | **> 1 mg/dL** · **most helpful** · rises within hours, **normalises within 1 wk** → **best to monitor treatment** |
| **Synovial WBC** | **> 50,000** (native joint) = diagnostic · lower counts still infected if gram stain / culture +ve |
| Synovial PMN | High (e.g. 90%) |
| Gram stain | Identifies organism only **⅓ of the time** |
| Glucose | **< 60% of serum** |
| Viscosity | **Negative "string" sign** (low viscosity) |

**Aspiration** — **gold standard**; send **cell count + differential, gram stain, culture, glucose, crystals**

**Antibiotics within 24 h before arthrocentesis** — **↓ synovial WBC → false negative**

**Crystals + septic** — coexist in **< 5%** · **crystals do NOT exclude infection**

**Septic shock** — hypotension needing **vasopressors to keep MAP ≥ 65 mmHg** + **lactate > 2 mmol/L** despite fluids → mortality **> 40%** · aspirate, then **immediate broad-spectrum IV antibiotics**

![Negative string sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1058-string-sign.jpg)

### Diagnosis — aspiration & coexisting crystals

**Red, hot 1st MTP, ↑ ESR / CRP** — **joint aspiration first** (septic vs gout)\
✗ Wrong options: I&D before diagnosis · oral antibiotics · indomethacin before aspiration · MRI

**IV drug user, ankle WBC 95,000 + negatively birefringent crystals** — **urgent I&D** · gout / pseudogout does not remove need for surgery\
✗ Wrong options: await culture · 24 h IV antibiotics then reassess · analgesia · ESR / CRP / urate

**Diabetic bacteraemia → septic knee, septic shock** — **S. aureus**\
✗ Wrong options: gonococcus · gram −ve bacilli · Strep. pyogenes · Pseudomonas

### Traumatic arthrotomy — saline load test

| Saline into knee | Sensitivity (1 cm inferolateral wound) |
|---|---|
| 100 mL | **< 75%** — insufficient |
| **110 mL** | **75%** |
| 145 mL | 90% |
| **155 mL** | **95%** |
| **175 mL** | **99%** |

**Technique** — **18 G** needle, saline with **passive ROM** · **egress through wound = positive** (arthrotomy)

**Traps** — **methylene blue does NOT ↑ sensitivity** · **exam alone performs worse** than SLT · **CT → intra-articular air** (adjunct; ✗ Wrong options: MRI routinely)

**100 mL, no egress, wound near patella** — **inject at least another 55 mL**\
✗ Wrong options: close + discharge · arthroscopy yet · MRI · secondary intention

**Positive** — surgical I&D (open / arthroscopic) to prevent septic arthritis

### Differential

**Crystal arthropathy** (gout, pseudogout) · **cellulitis** · **bursitis** (prepatellar)

## 5 Management

```
Suspected native-joint septic arthritis
├─ Aspirate first (cell count, gram, culture, glucose, crystals)
├─ WBC > 50,000 / organisms → urgent open or arthroscopic I&D + IV antibiotics
│      (crystals present → still I&D)
├─ Gonococcal → aspiration / arthroscopic drainage + ceftriaxone
│      └─ not responding → open I&D
└─ SC joint with osteolysis → CT / MRI, then joint resection
```

**Emergency** — orthopaedic surgical emergency; damage within **8 hours**

**Empirical antibiotics** — **young healthy: S. aureus + N. gonorrhoeae** · **immunocompromised: S. aureus + Pseudomonas** → switch to culture-directed

**Monitoring** — serum WBC, ESR, **CRP**

**Technique** — open or arthroscopic · remove all pus, irrigate · **synovectomy** as needed · fluid + tissue for culture

### Gonococcal arthritis

**Antibiotic** — **ceftriaxone** (3rd-gen cephalosporin; or cefotaxime / ceftizoxime) · **fluoroquinolones** if sensitive · **+ doxycycline** where chlamydia common\
✗ Wrong options: penicillin · tetracycline (high resistance) · erythromycin · vancomycin

**Forms** — **arthritis-dermatitis syndrome (\~60%)** · localised purulent arthritis (\~40%) · bacteraemic infection; newborns + adolescents

**Disseminated (polyarthralgia + trunk rash, gram −ve diplococci)** — **serial aspiration + antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: open I&D first (reserved for non-responders / severe) · I&D without antibiotics · colchicine · observation

**Workup** — screen for **HIV, syphilis, chlamydia** · response → fever, pain, leucocytosis resolve

**Clue** — young, sexually active, new vaginal discharge → take sexual history

### Paediatric septic hip — Kocher criteria & urgent washout (TO)

| Criterion (1 point each) (TO) | In original Kocher? |
|---|---|
| **Fever > 38.5 °C** | **Yes** |
| **ESR > 40** | **Yes** |
| **WBC > 12,000/mm³** | **Yes** |
| **Refusal to bear weight** | **Yes** |
| **CRP** | **No — added later by Caird** |

**Sensitivity order (TO)** — **fever > CRP > ESR > refusal to bear weight > WBC** · **CRP most sensitive lab**

**5-yr, fever 38.5 °C, ↑ CRP / WBC, effusion (TO)** — **emergency aspiration + washout, then IV antibiotics** · **US = investigation of choice** for the painful child's hip · arthrotomy gives tissue for culture\
✗ Wrong options: blood cultures + antibiotics · US-guided aspiration + antibiotics · antibiotics alone

**4-yr, fever, ↑ ESR / CRP, hip collection on US (TO)** — answer key not provided by the source

### Metaphyseal osteomyelitis & joint spread (TO)

**Intracapsular metaphyses (TO)** — **proximal radius, proximal humerus, proximal femur, distal tibia + fibula** → osteomyelitis decompresses into joint → septic arthritis

**EXCEPT (TO)** — **head (distal end) of ulna**

### Tuberculosis of joints (TO)

**Rice / millet / melon seed bodies (TO)** — **tuberculous synovitis / tenosynovitis**\
✗ Wrong options: PVNS · RA · Madura foot

**First radiographic sign of healing (TO)** — **remineralisation of bone** (trabecular pattern, ↓ osteopenia) · spine: + sharpened disc margins, ↓ paravertebral shadow, fusion\
✗ Wrong options: joint space reappears · sequestrum removal · bony ankylosis

**TB hip (TO)** — **second commonest osteoarticular TB** · usually ends in **fibrous ankylosis** · advanced → **FADIR** usually; complete iliofemoral ligament resorption → **FABER**

**FALSE statement (TO)** — "**Shanmugasundaram classification applies only to paediatric hip TB**" — it applies to **children and adults**

| Shanmugasundaram (1983, radiological) (TO) | Feature |
|---|---|
| Normal | Synovial disease |
| **Travelling / wandering acetabulum** | Roof lesion → head migrates upward |
| Dislocating | Posterior dislocation / subluxation (lax capsule) |
| Perthes type | Fragmented, dense, flat epiphysis |
| **Protrusio acetabuli** | Floor lesion → medial migration |
| Atrophic | Marked joint narrowing |
| **Mortar & pestle** | Head / acetabulum destroyed → mismatch |
| Unclassified (Agarwal, 8th) | Triradiate, pseudarthrosis coxae, ankylosed |

**Other TB hip classifications (TO)** — **Babhulkar + Pande 2002** (clinico-radiological) · Tuli modification · **Moon 2012**: **stable** — Perthes, atrophic, protrusio · **unstable** — travelling acetabulum, mortar + pestle, dislocating

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Arthritis** | Cartilage loss from **8 h** |
| **Fibrous ankylosis** | — |
| **Osteomyelitis** | Contiguous spread |

## X vs Y

| | **S. aureus** | **N. gonorrhoeae** |
|---|---|---|
| Share | **> 50%** | **\~20%** |
| Gram | +ve cocci, clusters | −ve diplococci, intra + extracellular |
| Host | All, incl. IV drug users | Healthy sexually active young |
| Synovial WBC | **Higher → more destruction** | Lower |
| Treatment | **Urgent I&D** + IV antibiotics | **Aspiration / drainage + ceftriaxone** |

| | **Septic arthritis** | **Gout** |
|---|---|---|
| Synovial WBC | **> 50,000** | Usually lower |
| Crystals | Coexist < 5% | **Negatively birefringent** urate |
| Treatment | **I&D** | **Indomethacin** |

## How it's asked

**"SC septic arthritis organism, IV drug user?"** → **S. aureus**\
✗ Wrong options: Pseudomonas

**"SC septic arthritis, true?"** → **CT / MRI routinely before surgery**

**"SC infection with medial clavicle osteolysis?"** → **resection of SC joint**

**"Saline volume for 95%?"** → **155 mL**

**"175 mL saline detects?"** → **99%**

**"100 mL, no egress — next?"** → **inject another 55 mL**

**"Saline load test — true?"** → **175 mL diagnoses 99%**\
✗ Wrong options: methylene blue ↑ sensitivity

**"Gonococcal antibiotic?"** → **ceftriaxone**

**"Young woman, vaginal discharge, septic knee — gram stain?"** → **intra + extracellular gram −ve diplococci**

**"Why S. aureus destroys more than gonococcus?"** → **greater WBC influx**

**"Disseminated gonococcal arthritis, first treatment?"** → **serial aspiration + antibiotics**

**"Red 1st MTP, ↑ CRP — next?"** → **joint aspiration**

**"WBC 95,000 + urate crystals?"** → **urgent I&D**

**"Septic knee in septic shock — organism?"** → **S. aureus**

**"Septic olecranon bursitis organism?"** → **gram +ve cocci in pairs and clusters**

**"Not in Kocher criteria (TO)?"** → **CRP**

**"Child, fever, ↑ CRP, hip effusion (TO)?"** → **emergency aspiration + washout + IV antibiotics**

**"Osteomyelitis NOT spreading to joint (TO)?"** → **head of ulna**

**"Melon seed bodies (TO)?"** → **tubercular synovitis**

**"First healing sign, TB foot (TO)?"** → **remineralisation**

**"False about TB hip (TO)?"** → **Shanmugasundaram only paediatric**
