# SI Dislocation & Crescent Fractures

*Trauma · 56 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Spectrum

| Injury | Posterior SI ligaments | Stability |
|---|---|---|
| **Incomplete SI dislocation** | **Intact** | **Rotationally unstable** |
| **Complete SI dislocation** | **Ruptured** | **Rotationally + vertically unstable** |
| **Crescent fracture (SI fracture-dislocation)** | Posterior ilium stays attached to sacrum | Anterior ilium dislocates with **internal rotation** |

**Crescent fracture** — **iliac wing fracture entering the SI joint**; posterior iliac "crescent" stays with sacrum = **Young-Burgess LC-II**

### Anatomy

**SI stabilisers** — sacrospinous, sacrotuberous, anterior + **posterior SI ligaments**

**L5 root** — crosses sacral ala **\~2 cm medial to SI joint**

**Superior gluteal artery** — crosses SI joint, exits via **greater sciatic notch**

## 2 Mechanism

**Lateral compression**, high energy · iliac wing fractures may be **open** or trap **bowel**

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — haemodynamics, detailed neuro, abdominal distension, **rectal exam**, **blood at meatus**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP pelvis + **inlet / outlet** · **CT** — posterior ring + sacrum

**Classification** — within **Young-Burgess** (crescent = **LC-II**) and **Tile**

![Crescent fracture on AP pelvis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1031-xray-ap-crescent-fx-wheeless-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

| Injury | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Vertical translation | **Immediate skeletal traction** |
| **Incomplete SI + symphysis diastasis** | **Anterior ring ORIF** |
| **Complete SI dislocation** | **Anterior + posterior fixation** |
| **Crescent fracture** | **ORIF of ilium** (restores posterior ligament stability) |

**Approaches** — anterior (lateral window, elevate iliacus to SI) · **posterior** (crescent fragment to intact ilium) · plates + **iliosacral lag screws**

### Iliosacral screws — fluoroscopic views & L5 root injury

| View | Shows |
|---|---|
| **Inlet** | **Anterior-posterior** position |
| **Outlet** | **Cephalad-caudad** position + **sacral neural foramina (best)** |
| **Lateral sacral** | Anterior alar cortex + nerve tunnel → **L5 safety** |

**In-out-in (too anterior) screw** — **L5 root → weak great toe extension / dorsiflexion (EHL)**\
✗ Wrong options: ankle DF (L4 TA) · plantarflexion (S1) · knee extension · bowel / bladder

**Positioning** — traction aids reduction; midline sacral bump

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **DVT** | **35–50%** |
| Neurological injury | L5 |
| Loss of reduction / fixation failure | — |

**Prognosis** — **accurate, stable SI joint reduction**

## How it's asked

**"Outlet view purpose during IS screws?"** → **sacral neural foramina**

**"In-out-in screw finding?"** → **no great toe extension**

**"Anterior alar penetration → weakness?"** → **great toe dorsiflexion**

**"Crescent fracture pattern?"** → **LC-II**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Sternoclavicular Dislocation

*Trauma · 57 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Types

**Traumatic** — **anterior (commoner)** · **posterior → mediastinal structures at risk** · high energy (MVA, contact sport)

**Atraumatic subluxation** — younger, **generalised ligamentous laxity**; with **overhead elevation**, reduces on lowering arm

**Medial clavicle physis** — **first bone to ossify, last physis to close (20–25 yr)** · under 20–25 yr "dislocation" is usually a **physeal fracture** · closure \~25 yr (OB)

### Stabilisers — posterior capsular ligament

**SC joint** — **diarthrodial saddle joint**, **incongruous (\~50% contact)**, **fibrocartilage**, intra-articular disc · stability is **ligamentous**

| Structure | Function |
|---|---|
| **Posterior capsular (SC) ligament** | **Most important — primary restraint to anterior-posterior stability** |
| Anterior SC ligament | **Primary restraint to superior displacement** of medial clavicle |
| Costoclavicular (rhomboid) — anterior fasciculus | Resists **superior rotation + lateral displacement** |
| Costoclavicular — posterior fasciculus | Resists **inferior rotation + medial displacement** |
| Intra-articular disc ligament | Prevents **medial displacement**; secondary restraint to superior displacement |

**Prevents posterior dislocation** — **posterior SC joint capsule**\
✗ Wrong options: first rib · disc ligament · costoclavicular · interclavicular

![Sternoclavicular joint ligaments](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1009-sternoclavicular-anatomy-illustration-moved.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Anterior** — palpable bump; prominence ↑ with abduction + elevation

**Posterior** — **dyspnoea, dysphagia**, tachypnoea, **stridor worse supine** · **paraesthesia** in arm · **venous congestion (unilateral jugular engorgement)** / ↓ pulse · ↓ arm ROM · turning head to affected side relieves pain

**Posterior complications** — **early**: pneumothorax, great vessel laceration / erosion / occlusion, **oesophageal rupture**, **brachial plexus compression**, respiratory distress, myocardial conduction abnormality · **late**: **tracheo-oesophageal fistula**, stridor, dysphagia

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| AP chest | Hard to see; often read as normal |
| **Serendipity view (40° cephalic tilt)** | **Anterior → clavicle above** contralateral · **posterior → clavicle below** |
| **CT (axial)** | **Study of choice** — direction, **mediastinal injury**, **distinguishes physeal fracture** |
| MRI | Adds nothing; delays treatment (avoid) |

![Serendipity view](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1009-seren.jpg)

![Axial CT — posterior SC dislocation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1009-ct-axial-posterior-dislocation-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Sternoclavicular dislocation
├─ Atraumatic subluxation → observation / reassurance
├─ Chronic anterior (> 3 wk), mildly symptomatic → sling, symptomatic
├─ Acute (< 3 wk) anterior or posterior → closed reduction under GA in OR ± thoracic back-up
│      ├─ stable → immobilise
│      └─ unstable → accept deformity (anterior) / open reduction + reconstruction
└─ Posterior, failed closed reduction or chronic → open reduction + soft-tissue reconstruction
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Atraumatic subluxation** | **Reassurance**, symptomatic |
| **Chronic anterior, minimally symptomatic (> 3 wk)** | **Sling**, unrestricted activity by **3 months** |
| **Acute anterior / posterior (< 3 wk)** | **Closed reduction under GA ± thoracic back-up** |
| **Acute posterior, failed closed reduction** (dysphagia, dyspnoea, ↓ pulses) | **Open reduction + soft-tissue reconstruction** |
| Chronic anterior / posterior, **persistently symptomatic** | **Open reduction + reconstruction** |
| **Chronic posterior (> 3 wk) with dysphagia** | **Open reduction in OR + thoracic back-up** (✗ Wrong options: sling (avoid late mediastinal problems)) |

### Posterior dislocation — reduction in OR with thoracic backup

**Posterior + ↓ pulses (16-yr)** — **reduction in OR with thoracic surgery back-up**\
✗ Wrong options: sling · ED reduction · ORIF + pinning of physis

**Closed reduction fails** — in **up to 75%** → open reduction

**Initial treatment** — **closed reduction in OR with towel clip** (anterior translation) · usually stable\
✗ Wrong options: observation · open first · ORIF · allograft reconstruction first

**After closed reduction** — **immobilisation 4 wk** (OB) · **Velpeau bandage 6 wk** (Orthobullets) — read the options · elbow exercises at 3 wk · sport at 3 months

**Cardiothoracic stand-by** — **reduction of posterior SC dislocation** · routine thoracic surgeon now **controversial** but still recommended\
✗ Wrong options: midshaft clavicle ORIF · distal clavicle excision · SC joint I&D · Judet scapula

**Never** — **ED reduction** (mediastinal injury), **compression plating across SC joint**, **K-wires** (migration)

**Closed reduction technique** — supine, arm at table edge, **abduct + extend** with **axial traction**, direct posterior pressure over medial clavicle · **towel clip / fingers** pull medial clavicle

### Open reduction & reconstruction

**Figure-of-8 tendon graft** — **2 drill holes in manubrium + 2 in medial clavicle**, **1 cm apart** · **gracilis or semitendinosus** · don't disrupt SCM sheath; clean capsulotomy for repair

**Medial clavicle excision** — outdated · resect **< 15 mm** to **preserve costoclavicular ligaments** (repair if injured) · Wirth + Rockwood: costoclavicular reconstruction ± medial head resection for unstable injuries

![SC joint reconstruction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1009-sc-joint-dislocation.jpg)

### Fixation hazards — pin migration

**Highest life-threatening complications** — **percutaneous pins (K-wires / Steinmann)** → migrate to **heart, pulmonary artery, aorta**\
✗ Wrong options: cannulated screws · cerclage · Balser plate · locking plate

### Anterior dislocation — natural history

**Anterior dislocation — true** — **rarely symptomatic when left unreduced** · surgery mainly **cosmetic**\
✗ Wrong options: stable after reduction (persistent instability common) · tracheal injury on reduction · fusion · acute medial clavicle excision

### Atraumatic spontaneous subluxation

**Diagnosis** — no trauma, **lump at base of neck**, worse **overhead** · settles in **4–6 wk** nonoperatively\
✗ Wrong options: unreduced posterior · hypoplasia · arthritis · hyperostosis

**Beighton-Horan ≥ 5 / 9 = hypermobility** · **treatment → observation**\
✗ Wrong options: figure-of-8 brace · resection · reconstruction · arthrodesis

**Type III SC instability (TO)** — **non-structural, abnormal muscle patterning** (inappropriate **pectoralis major**, EMG) → **biofeedback physiotherapy** · surgery only if structural abnormality\
✗ Wrong options: closed reduction + sling · open reduction + reconstruction · open reduction + sling

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Cosmetic deformity** | Anterior — usually well tolerated |
| Mediastinal injury | Posterior — great vessels, trachea, oesophagus |
| **Pin migration** | Heart, pulmonary artery, aorta → **avoid K-wires** |

## X vs Y

| | **Anterior SC dislocation** | **Posterior SC dislocation** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **Commoner** | Rarer |
| Risk | Cosmetic | **Mediastinal** |
| Serendipity | Clavicle **above** | Clavicle **below** |
| Acute | Closed reduction; accept if unstable | **Closed reduction in OR + thoracic back-up** |
| Chronic | **Observe** (if mild) | **Reduce** (open) |

## How it's asked

**"Posterior SC + ↓ pulses?"** → **reduction in OR with thoracic back-up**

**"3-month-old posterior SC with dysphagia?"** → **open reduction in OR with thoracic back-up**

**"Unilateral JVD, posterior SC — next?"** → **closed reduction in OR under GA, immobilise 4 wk**

**"Footballer posterior SC on CT — next?"** → **closed reduction in OR**

**"Best initial treatment, posterior SC?"** → **closed reduction with towel clip**

**"Which surgery needs cardiothoracic stand-by?"** → **posterior SC dislocation reduction**

**"Structure preventing posterior dislocation?"** → **posterior SC joint capsule**

**"Primary A-P restraint?"** → **posterior SC capsular ligament**

**"Anterior SC dislocation — true?"** → **rarely symptomatic when unreduced**

**"Atraumatic subluxation, Beighton 6?"** → **observation**

**"No trauma, neck lump, overhead?"** → **spontaneous SC subluxation**

**"Type III SC instability (TO)?"** → **biofeedback physiotherapy**

**"Device with most life-threatening complications?"** → **percutaneous pins**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Subtalar Dislocations

*Trauma · 58 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1% of all dislocations** · < 1 / 100,000 / yr · young / middle-aged men

**Definition** — dislocation of **both subtalar (talocalcaneal) + talonavicular** joints · + tibiotalar = **total talar dislocation (extrusion)**

**High energy** — **25% open** (**lateral more often open**) · irreducible **\~33%** (OB)

### Anatomy

**Talus** — **no muscle or tendon attachments** · inferior surface → posterior facet of calcaneus · head → navicular + sustentaculum tali · lateral process → posterior facet + fibula · posterior process: medial + lateral tubercles with **FHL groove**

| Talar blood supply | Supplies |
|---|---|
| **Artery of tarsal canal** (posterior tibial) | **Most of body — main supply** |
| Calcaneal branches (posterior tibial) | Posterior talus |
| Anterior tibial artery | Head + neck |
| Artery of tarsal sinus (perforating peroneal) | Head + neck |
| **Deltoid artery** (deep deltoid) | Body · may be the only supply left after talar neck fracture |

![Blood supply of the talus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1050-blood-supply-to-the-talus.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Medial** — **inversion of plantarflexed foot**; talar neck pivots on **sustentaculum tali**

**Lateral** — **eversion of plantarflexed foot**; **anterior process of calcaneus = fulcrum** for anterolateral talus

## 3 Presentation

**Medial** — foot **locked in supination** = **"acquired clubfoot"**

**Lateral** — foot **locked in pronation** = **"acquired flatfoot"**

![Medial subtalar dislocation — foot locked in supination](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1050-clinical-photo-subtalar-dislocation.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| AP + lateral foot | **Medial → talar head superior to navicular** (lateral view) · **lateral → talar head collinear / inferior to navicular** |
| **CT after reduction** | Associated fractures, **subtalar osteochondral debris** (residual subluxation) |

**Radiograph** — talonavicular dislocation is easily missed; it is part of every subtalar dislocation

### Anatomic classification (direction of midfoot / forefoot)

| Type | Features | Image |
|---|---|---|
| **Medial** | **Commonest** — **65–80%** (Orthobullets) · 65% / \~75% (OB) · **lateral malleolus buttress** · fractures: **dorsomedial talar head, posterior process of talus, navicular** | ![Medial](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1050-medial-dislocation.jpg) |
| **Lateral** | **More often open**, more associated fractures, **higher energy** · locked in pronation · fractures: **cuboid, anterior calcaneus, lateral process of talus, fibula** | ![Lateral](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1050-lateral-dislocation.jpg) |
| Anterior | Rare | — |
| Posterior | Rare | — |
| **Total (talar extrusion)** | Out of ankle + subtalar + talonavicular · **usually open** | — |

### Patterns & associated fractures (lateral more often open)

**Associated fractures** — **up to 44%** · **commonest: talus** · occult **lateral process of talus**\
✗ Wrong options: fibula · cuboid · calcaneus · navicular

**Medial vs lateral — true** — **lateral more likely open**\
✗ Wrong options: medial more associated fractures · lateral blocked by EDB · medial blocked by tibialis posterior · lateral more stable (medial more stable)

**Isolated injury, no fractures — other joint dislocated** — **talonavicular**\
✗ Wrong options: tibiotalar, calcaneocuboid, MTP, TMT

## 5 Management

```
Subtalar dislocation
├─ Closed → closed reduction under sedation (60–70% succeed)
│      ├─ stable → short leg NWB cast 4–6 wk
│      └─ irreducible → open reduction (up to 32%)
├─ Open → I&D + open reduction
└─ Unstable after reduction → transarticular pins / spanning ex-fix
```

**Closed reduction** — knee flexion + ankle plantarflexion → **distraction** → hindfoot inversion / eversion (opposite to dislocation) · lateral: traction + hyperpronation, then supination (OB) · **post-reduction CT**

### Irreducible dislocation — blocks (EDB medially, tibialis posterior laterally)

| Dislocation | Blocks (lateral / medial structures) | Approach |
|---|---|---|
| **Medial** | **EDB** (talar head **buttonholes** through EDB), **peroneal tendons**, **talonavicular capsule**, deep peroneal NV bundle | **Sinus tarsi** |
| **Lateral** | **Tibialis posterior (commonest)**, **FHL**, **FDL**, posterior tibial NV bundle | **Medial** (between tibialis anterior + posterior) ± sinus tarsi for debris |

**Not blocks** — tibialis anterior, EHL (too dorsal), plantar fascia, extensor retinaculum, CFL

**Irreducible medial** — **\~10%** even under anaesthesia (OB)

![Tibialis posterior interposed — lateral dislocation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1050-shows-posterior-tibial-tendon-interposed.jpg)

### Total talar dislocation

**Failed closed reduction (closed pantalar, no fracture)** — **percutaneous / open reduction + soft-tissue repair** + temporary pins / spanning ex-fix · open → urgent I&D + reduction (reimplant)\
✗ Wrong options: primary talectomy + tibiocalcaneal fusion · talectomy + total talus replacement · primary tibiotalar / subtalar arthrodesis

**Salvage later** — talectomy, fusion, talus replacement for **osteonecrosis / arthritis**

## 6 Complications

### Outcomes — arthritis & osteonecrosis

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Post-traumatic arthritis** | **Subtalar** commonest — radiographic **up to 89%**, **symptomatic 63%** |
| **Stiffness** | **Most common complication** (Orthobullets) |
| Talar osteonecrosis | **Total talar dislocation** — common; **without collapse at 12 months** |
| Infection | Open — poor muscle cover, poor vascularity |

**Most common complication** — **stiffness** (Orthobullets) · **post-traumatic arthritis** (OB) — read the options; ✗ Wrong options: osteonecrosis of talus

**Most symptomatic degenerated joint** — **subtalar**\
✗ Wrong options: tibiotalar · talonavicular · calcaneocuboid · Lisfranc

**12 months after total talar extrusion** — **talar osteonecrosis without collapse**\
✗ Wrong options: pantalar arthritis · collapse yet · navicular / calcaneus ON

**Poor prognosis** — **high energy**, **lateral**, **open**, **concomitant subtalar fractures**

## X vs Y

| | **Medial** | **Lateral** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **Commoner (65–80%)** | Less common |
| Mechanism | Inversion | Eversion |
| Foot | Supinated (acquired clubfoot) | Pronated (acquired flatfoot) |
| Talar head (lateral X-ray) | **Superior** to navicular | Collinear / inferior |
| Block | **EDB**, peroneals, TN capsule | **Tibialis posterior**, FHL, FDL |
| Open / fractures | Less | **More** |
| Outcome | Better, more stable | **Worse** |

## How it's asked

**"Irreducible lateral subtalar — block?"** → **tibialis posterior tendon**

**"Irreducible medial subtalar — block?"** → **extensor digitorum brevis**

**"Most common associated fracture?"** → **talus**

**"Joint dislocated with subtalar?"** → **talonavicular**

**"Lateral vs medial — true?"** → **lateral more likely open**

**"Most symptomatic arthritis?"** → **subtalar joint**

**"Most common complication (OB)?"** → **post-traumatic arthritis**

**"12 months after extruded talus?"** → **osteonecrosis without collapse**

**"Closed pantalar dislocation, failed closed reduction?"** → **open reduction + soft-tissue repair**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Subtrochanteric Fractures

*Trauma · 59 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — proximal femur fracture **from the lesser trochanter to 5 cm distal** to it

**Incidence** — **7–34% of femur fractures** · bimodal: **young high energy (MVC)** · **elderly low energy (fall)**

**Rule out** — pathological / **atypical femur fracture** (bisphosphonate — esp. **alendronate**, **denosumab**)

### Deforming forces & reduction manoeuvres

| Fragment | Deformity | Muscle |
|---|---|---|
| **Proximal** | **Abduction** | **Gluteus medius + minimus** (greater trochanter) |
| **Proximal** | **Flexion** | **Iliopsoas** (lesser trochanter) |
| **Proximal** | **External rotation** | **Short external rotators** (piriformis, gemelli, obturator internus) ± iliopsoas, gluteus maximus |
| **Distal** | **Adduction + shortening** | **Adductors** |

**Typical proximal deformity** — **flexion, abduction, external rotation** · lesser trochanter intact → **abduction + flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: adduction · extension · internal rotation

**Responsible muscles (proximal displacement)** — **gluteus medius + iliopsoas**\
✗ Wrong options: gluteus maximus + adductors · rectus · hamstrings

**Reduction of proximal fragment** — **adduction + extension + internal rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: abduction · flexion

**Bring distal to proximal** — after traction, **flex + externally rotate the distal fragment**\
✗ Wrong options: traction alone

**Biomechanics** — weight bearing → **compression medial cortex, tension lateral cortex**

![Deforming forces in a subtrochanteric fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1039-shows-deforming-forces-for-subtrochanteric-fracture.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — long-term **bisphosphonate / denosumab** · **prodromal thigh / groin pain** before a low-energy fall

**Exam** — can't bear weight · **shortening + varus** deformity · flexed proximal fragment may threaten skin

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral hip, AP pelvis, **full-length femur incl. knee** · traction views optional (comminution)

**Findings** — proximal fragment **flexed + abducted** · distal **adducted + ER**

**Bisphosphonate fracture** — **lateral cortical thickening ("beaking", "dreaded black line")** · ↑ diaphyseal cortical thickness · **transverse / short oblique** · **medial spike** · **no comminution** · varus remodelling

![Atypical femur fracture — lateral cortical thickening](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1039-ap-hip.jpg)

### Russell-Taylor classification

| Type | Description | Image |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **No extension into piriformis fossa** (historically → IM nail) | ![Russell-Taylor](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1039-shows-the-russel-taylor-classification.jpg) |
| **II** | **Extends into greater trochanter + piriformis fossa** (see on **lateral X-ray**) — historically fixed-angle plate; now nailable | ![Type II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1039-russel-taylor-type-ii.jpg) |

### AO/OTA (32, subtrochanteric = .1)

| Code | Pattern |
|---|---|
| 32-A3.1 | Simple, **transverse** |
| 32-B3.1 | Wedge, fragmented |
| 32-C1.1 | Complex, spiral |

### Diagnostic criteria — atypical femur fracture (ASBMR revised)

| Major (**4 of 5 needed**) | Minor (optional) |
|---|---|
| **No / minimal trauma** (standing-height fall or less) | Generalised ↑ diaphyseal cortical thickness |
| Starts at **lateral cortex**, **substantially transverse** (may go oblique medially) | **Prodromal groin / thigh pain** |
| **Noncomminuted** | **Bilateral** fractures |
| Complete: both cortices ± **medial spike** · incomplete: lateral cortex only | **Delayed healing** |
| **Localised periosteal / endosteal thickening of lateral cortex** | — |

**Excluded** — femoral neck, intertrochanteric with spiral subtrochanteric extension, pathological (tumour), periprosthetic

## 5 Management

```
Subtrochanteric fracture
├─ Non-ambulatory, unfit for surgery → observation + analgesia (limited role)
├─ Most → cephalomedullary IM nail (reamed, statically locked)
├─ Fixed-angle plate → femoral neck fracture, narrow canal, shaft deformity, preference
│      └─ 95° blade plate / condylar screw (not sliding hip screw)
└─ Symptomatic impending AFF (lateral cortical stress line) → prophylactic IM nail
```

### Implant choice — cephalomedullary nail vs plate

| Implant | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Cephalomedullary nail** | **Load-sharing**, preserves vascularity, **stronger in unstable patterns** · **reamed + statically locked → highest union** (✗ Wrong options: unreamed (reaming ↑ vascularity + union without ↑ pulmonary / transfusion risk)) |
| **95° blade plate / condylar screw** | **Load-bearing**; lateral approach (split / elevate vastus lateralis, **profunda perforators**) · **tension band** · devitalises fragments, weaker in comminution |
| **135° sliding hip screw** | **Contraindicated** → loss of fixation, **malunion**, distal fragment displaces **medially + proximally**, proximal rotates on single screw |

**CMN vs SHS** — CMN: **lower revision, less pain, better mobility, higher QoL + satisfaction** (EXCEPT; ✗ Wrong options: "higher pain")

**Failed 135° plate (reverse obliquity nonunion) — would have prevented** — **cephalomedullary nail**\
✗ Wrong options: longer plate · autograft · strut allograft · derotation screw

**Tension band principles** — **eccentric loading + implant on tensile side + opposite cortex resists compression** → **subtrochanteric femur**\
✗ Wrong options: comminuted tibia / humerus / olecranon distal to coronoid · valgus impacted proximal humerus

**Anterior distal femoral cortex perforation** — nail with **greater radius of curvature (straighter)** than femoral bow

### Nailing technique

| Position | Advantages |
|---|---|
| **Lateral** | **Easier reduction of distal to flexed proximal fragment**, easier entry (piriformis), vastus retraction |
| **Supine** | **Protects injured spine**, **access in polytrauma**, **easier fluoroscopy**, **easier to judge rotation**, shorter set-up |

**Lateral disadvantages** — harder imaging, rotation hard to judge, impractical in polytrauma · supine: harder start point

**Reduction** — **reduce before + during nail passage** (nail can't reduce) · percutaneous aids / **open clamp** · evaluate proximal fragment **before guidewire**

**Entry** — piriformis nail may avoid malreduction from the **valgus proximal bend** of trochanteric nails

![Clamp-assisted reduction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1039-shows-clamp-assisted-reduction-of-subtrochanteric-femur-frac.jpg)

### Atypical femoral fractures (bisphosphonate-associated)

**Cause** — **prolonged bisphosphonate (> 5 yr)** · low-energy fall · prodromal pain\
✗ Wrong options: acute trauma · metastasis · fibrous cortical defect · osteomyelitis

**Symptomatic lateral cortical thickening / "dreaded black line"** — **IM nail**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · physio · MRI · THA

**Bilateral changes, one side painful** — **nail the symptomatic side only** · **image the contralateral femur** in all\
✗ Wrong options: both · bone scan · oncology

**Progression of contralateral AFF** — **continued post-op bisphosphonate** · longer use (> 5 yr), **Asian race**, **varus proximal femur** → **stop bisphosphonates**\
✗ Wrong options: 2 yr · Caucasian · valgus · female sex

| Fixation of AFF | Risk |
|---|---|
| **IM nail** | **↑ iatrogenic fracture** (brittle, thick cortices; canal–nail mismatch) · **↑ nonunion → revision** |
| Plate | **↑ hardware failure** (varus, intramembranous healing inhibited) · no ↑ iatrogenic fracture |

**Main complication** — **delayed healing + nonunion** · **anatomic reduction = best predictor of union**\
✗ Wrong options: LLD · metastasis · femoral head AVN · occult neck fracture

**Teriparatide** — anabolic; **↑ serum calcium via ↑ bone resorption / turnover** (intermittent) · for non-union despite vit D, calcium, stopping bisphosphonate\
✗ Wrong options: vitamin D (gut uptake) · ATP analogues (non-nitrogen bisphosphonates) · mevalonate pathway (nitrogen bisphosphonates) · RANKL antibody (denosumab)

## 6 Complications

### Nonunion, malunion & infection

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Varus / procurvatum (flexion) malreduction** | **Most frequent intra-op complication** of antegrade nailing — abductors + iliopsoas vs adductors; portal made with distal femur adducted |
| **Nonunion** | Lowest with **reamed statically locked IMN** · treat with plating (corrects varus) |
| AFF | Iatrogenic fracture, nonunion, hardware failure |

**Most common deformity after nailing — muscles** — **hip abductors + iliopsoas**\
✗ Wrong options: internal rotators · quadriceps · hamstrings

**Atrophic nonunion + varus collapse after CMN** — **remove hardware, correct alignment, plate (or nail) + autograft**\
✗ Wrong options: dynamisation (fails with deformity) · Taylor frame (poorly tolerated) · unreamed exchange nail (same path) · plate around nail

**Reamed exchange nailing** — choice for femoral nonunion **without deformity**

**Draining sinus, healed fracture, heterotopic bone (3 yr)** — **I&D + hardware removal + HO excision + culture-directed antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: disarticulation · suppression alone · radiation / NSAIDs

![Varus malreduction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1039-shows-varus-malreduction-of-subtrochanteric-femur-fracture.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Typical subtrochanteric** | **Atypical (bisphosphonate)** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | Young, high energy | Elderly, low energy, prodromal pain |
| Pattern | Comminuted, spiral | **Transverse, medial spike, no comminution** |
| Cortex | Normal | **Lateral thickening / beaking** |
| Nail risk | Varus malreduction | **Iatrogenic fracture, nonunion** |

## How it's asked

**"Proximal fragment deformity?"** → **flexion, abduction, external rotation**

**"Muscles displacing proximal fragment?"** → **gluteus medius + iliopsoas**

**"Muscles causing common post-nail deformity?"** → **hip abductors + iliopsoas**

**"Reduction force on proximal fragment?"** → **adduction + extension**

**"Intra-op manoeuvre (proximal, metastatic hx)?"** → **extension + internal rotation**

**"Distal fragment manipulation after traction?"** → **flexion + external rotation**

**"Supine advantages EXCEPT?"** → **easy reduction of distal to flexed proximal fragment**

**"Best treatment?"** → **cephalomedullary nail**

**"Young, comminuted — maximise union?"** → **reamed statically locked IMN**

**"CMN vs SHS EXCEPT?"** → **higher pain**

**"Failed 135° plate — prevention?"** → **cephalomedullary nail**

**"Tension band construct suitable?"** → **subtrochanteric femur**

**"Anterior cortex perforation cause?"** → **nail with greater radius of curvature**

**"Painful lateral cortical thickening?"** → **IM nail**

**"Prodromal pain then curb-step fracture?"** → **bisphosphonate**

**"AFF nailing vs conventional — true?"** → **↑ iatrogenic fracture with IMN**

**"One painful side, bilateral changes?"** → **IM nail symptomatic side**

**"Contralateral progression?"** → **post-op bisphosphonate use**

**"Primary AFF complication?"** → **delayed healing + nonunion**

**"Teriparatide mechanism?"** → **↑ serum calcium through ↑ bone resorption**

**"Atrophic nonunion with varus collapse?"** → **remove nail, correct, plate + autograft**

**"Draining sinus 3 yr after fixation?"** → **I&D, remove hardware, excise HO, culture antibiotics**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Talar Neck Fractures

*Trauma · 60 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **commonest talus fracture (\~50%)** · talus fractures 0.1–2.5% of all fractures (TO) · **ipsilateral foot injuries up to 89%** (CT)

**Mechanism** — high energy · **forced dorsiflexion + axial load**

### Anatomy

**Talus** — **60–70% cartilage**, **no muscle / tendon attachments** · inferior → posterior facet of calcaneus · head → navicular + sustentaculum tali · posterior process: medial + lateral tubercles, **FHL groove**

**Neck vs body** — defined by the **lateral process** (articulates with **posterior facet of calcaneus**) · fracture **anterior → neck**, **posterior → body**

### Blood supply — artery of the tarsal canal & deltoid branch

| Source | Branch | Supplies |
|---|---|---|
| **Posterior tibial** | **Artery of tarsal canal** — **dominant** | **Most of body** (lateral ⅔ of body, OB) · forms anastomotic sling under neck with tarsal sinus artery |
| **Posterior tibial** | **Deltoid branch** (deep deltoid) | **Medial body** · **may be the only supply left in displaced fractures** |
| Anterior tibial (dorsalis pedis) | — | **Head + neck** |
| **Perforating peroneal** | **Artery of tarsal sinus** | Head + neck, lateral body |

**Extraosseous sources (TO)** — **posterior tibial, anterior tibial, peroneal** · **artery of tarsal canal = mainly intraosseous** → "NOT extraosseous" (TO)

**Retrograde supply** — so displaced neck fractures → high AVN

**Hawkins III — preserve** — **deltoid ligament / deltoid branch of artery of tarsal canal**\
✗ Wrong options: medial malleolus (**osteotomise** it) · dorsalis pedis · tarsal sinus · perforating peroneal · ATFL

**Vessel whose injury ↑ osteonecrosis** — **artery of the tarsal canal**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsalis pedis · perforating peroneal · lateral tarsal · tarsal sinus

![Blood supply of the talus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1048-blood-supply-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| AP, lateral foot | Radiograph sensitivity for talar injury only **74%** (TO) |
| **Canale view** | **Best view for talar neck** — **maximum equinus, 15° pronated, beam 75° cephalad** |
| **CT** | **Displacement, comminution, congruity**; **gold standard (TO)**; confirms Hawkins I has no step-off |
| **Mortise X-ray 6–8 wk** | **Hawkins sign** |

![Canale view](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1048-canale-view-illustration-moved.jpg)

### Hawkins classification & neck vs body distinction

| Hawkins | Dislocation | AVN — Orthobullets | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Nondisplaced** | **0–13%** | ![Hawkins I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1048-xray-lateral-hawkins-i-emedicine-moved.jpg) |
| **II** | **Subtalar** | **20–50%** | ![Hawkins II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1048-r-talar-neck.jpg) |
| **III** | **Subtalar + tibiotalar** | **20–100%** | ![Hawkins III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1048-talar-neck-fx-dislocation-lateral.jpg) |
| **IV (Canale)** | **Subtalar + tibiotalar + talonavicular** | **70–100%** | ![Hawkins IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1048-hawkins-4.jpg) |

**AVN rates — other sources** — **TO: I 0–50%, II 20–50%, III 50–80%, IV 90–100%** · TO question: **Hawkins III → 70%** · OB (Hawkins): I 10%, II > 40%, III > 90%, IV 100% · II 15–40% · III 69–100% — read the options

**Hawkins II with 20–50% risk** — pick the image with **subtalar dislocation only** · posterior process fractures → little AVN

## 5 Management

```
Talar neck fracture
├─ All → emergent closed reduction in ER
├─ Hawkins I (CT: no step-off) → SLC 8–12 wk, NWB first 6 wk
├─ Displaced (II–IV), extruded → ORIF (may delay for soft tissues)
│      ├─ soft tissue / patient unfit → ex-fix until safe
│      └─ medial comminution → medial bridge plate + positional screws
└─ Open / extruded talus → I&D + reimplant + ORIF ± ex-fix
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Hawkins I** | Cast **8–12 wk** (NWB 6 wk) · TO: type I nonoperative · nondisplaced neck **still usually fixed** (TO) — cast for non-ambulatory / unfit |
| **Hawkins II–IV** | **ORIF** — even if closed reduction looks good (✗ Wrong options: definitive closed · pins · primary subtalar / TTC fusion) |
| **Interposed fragment** | **Open reduction before screws** (✗ Wrong options: percutaneous screws) |
| **Talar extrusion** | **Reimplant + ORIF** |

### Treatment — ORIF, fixation construct & timing

**Approaches** — **two incisions** to see medial + lateral neck (comminution **dorsal + medial**)

| Approach | Interval / key point |
|---|---|
| **Anteromedial** | **Tibialis anterior ↔ tibialis posterior** · preserve **deep deltoid** · **medial malleolar osteotomy** if needed |
| **Anterolateral** | Tibia–fibula proximally, **line of 4th ray** · elevate **EDB**, clear subtalar debris |

**Avoid** — dissection superior + inferior to the neck (remaining blood supply)

**Strongest construct (TO)** — **posterior-to-anterior parallel dual screws**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior-to-posterior · crossed

**Medial comminution (Hawkins II)** — **ORIF with medial bridge plate + lateral screw in non-lagging mode** · mini-fragment plates buttress against varus\
✗ Wrong options: lag screws (shorten medial column → **varus**) · TTC fusion · closed + pins

**Timing** — **delayed fixation does NOT ↑ AVN** · **acute fixation → ↑ infection, skin necrosis, wound dehiscence** (77% vs 2–10%, TO) · open fractures → emergent debridement\
✗ Wrong options: acute → ↓ AVN · ↓ subtalar OA · ↓ varus · ↓ dehiscence

**Post-op** — **NWB 10–12 wk**

![Talar neck ORIF](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1048-shows-postoperative-talar-neck-fracture.jpg)

### Extruded talus — reimplantation

**Extruded body + neck fracture** — **I&D + reduce native talar body + ORIF neck**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · Steinmann pins + ex-fix definitive · allograft · remove + spacer

**Extruded talus with debris** — **bacitracin solution, irrigate, debride, reimplant**, ex-fix and / or ORIF\
✗ Wrong options: talectomy + ex-fix · tibiocalcaneal fusion · tibiotalar fusion · Syme

**Reimplantation — true** — **safe and can succeed** · **low infection** with I&D + antibiotics · **AVN \~90%, most without collapse** · \~63% need no secondary procedure\
✗ Wrong options: acute triple arthrodesis · BKA

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Rate / fact |
|---|---|
| **Subtalar arthritis** | **Most common complication — \~50%** (50–100%, OB; 54% PTA) |
| Tibiotalar arthritis | **33%** |
| **Osteonecrosis** | **31% overall** · **2nd commonest** |
| **Varus malunion** | **25–30%** (Orthobullets) · **one-third** (TO) |
| Nonunion | 10–12% |
| Symptomatic implants | \~18% |
| Wound dehiscence | \~3% |

**Order (OB)** — **subtalar arthritis > tibiotalar arthritis > osteonecrosis > varus malunion**

### Hawkins sign & osteonecrosis prognosis

**Hawkins sign** — **subchondral lucency of talar dome**, **mortise / AP at 6–8 wk** → **resorption = intact vascularity** → **AVN unlikely** → observe, progress weight bearing\
✗ Wrong options: cement · fusion · arthroscopy

**Hawkins sign positive** — **low risk of osteonecrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: sclerosis (bad) · diffuse osteopenia · medial malleolus fracture

**Absent sign** — doesn't prove AVN · up to **44% revascularise without collapse**

**Not related to Hawkins sign** — fixation construct; **reduction quality** affects AVN

### Osteonecrosis risk factors (displacement, open fracture; not surgical timing)

**Greatest impact** — **extent of initial fracture displacement**\
✗ Wrong options: fixation within 6–8 h · nicotine · PA screws · reduction quality

**↑ AVN** — displacement, **comminution**, **subtalar dislocation**, **extension into body**

**Not risk factors** — **delay > 24 h**, time to reduction > 12 h, surgery duration, polytrauma, staged ex-fix then ORIF

**Open fracture** — **independent AVN risk factor** (Orthobullets; OB — extruded talus) · **no significant difference** open vs closed (OB, Hawkins II) — read the options

**Dual-incision approach** — **associated with AVN** (Orthobullets; OB — vs lateral only) · **not linked** (OB, Hawkins II) — read the options

### Post-traumatic arthritis — most common complication

**Most likely long-term complication after anatomic ORIF** — **tibiotalar and / or subtalar arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: osteonecrosis · nonunion · infection · forefoot supination

**Hawkins II** — **subtalar arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: AVN · tibiotalar · talonavicular (not involved)

**Hawkins III — second commonest** — **osteonecrosis** (subtalar arthritis first)

### Varus malunion — effects & corrective osteotomy

**Cause** — **medial comminution** / non-anatomic reduction · Hawkins II treated closed

**Effects** — **↓ subtalar eversion** (TO + OB) · locked midfoot + hindfoot · **supination deformity** · **walks on lateral border** · later subtalar arthrosis\
✗ Wrong options: planovalgus (pronation)

**Treatment** — **talar neck medial opening wedge osteotomy** (tricortical ICBG)\
✗ Wrong options: TAA · lateral calcaneal closing wedge · calcaneal neck opening wedge · triple

**Dorsal impingement + ↓ ankle dorsiflexion** — **isolated dorsal malunion**\
✗ Wrong options: varus · valgus · plantar

**Hawkins sign 6 wk = poor prognosis (TO)** — false; **varus malunion ↓ eversion** = true

### Nonunion & salvage (revision ORIF, TTC fusion)

**Nonunion, viable body, no arthritis** — **revision ORIF + bone graft** (preserves motion) · vascularised graft if AVN\
✗ Wrong options: talectomy · ankle / TTC / triple fusion

**AVN + tibiotalar + subtalar arthritis** — **tibiotalocalcaneal fusion** (hindfoot nail)\
✗ Wrong options: TAA (poor talar stock) · isolated tibiotalar fusion · supramalleolar osteotomy · arthroscopic debridement

**Isolated tibiotalar arthritis** — tibiotalar fusion or TAA (needs talar bone stock)

![Hawkins sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1048-xray-ap-hawkins-positive-and-negative-moved.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Hawkins sign present** | **Absent / sclerosis** |
|---|---|---|
| Meaning | **Vascularity intact** (resorption) | Possible AVN |
| Prognosis | **Low AVN risk** | Risk — but 44% revascularise |
| Action | **Observe**, progress WB | Monitor |

| | **Varus malunion** | **Dorsal malunion** |
|---|---|---|
| Effect | **↓ subtalar eversion**, lateral border WB | **Dorsal impingement**, ↓ dorsiflexion |
| Fix | **Medial opening wedge osteotomy** | — |

## How it's asked

**"NOT extraosseous supply (TO)?"** → **artery of tarsal canal**

**"Structure to keep intact in ORIF?"** → **deltoid ligament**

**"Two-incision Hawkins III — preserve?"** → **deltoid branch of artery of tarsal canal**

**"Vessel injury ↑ osteonecrosis?"** → **artery of tarsal canal**

**"Subchondral lucency at 8 wk — next?"** → **observe; vascularity intact**

**"Good prognostic radiographic sign?"** → **talar dome subchondral lucency**

**"Hawkins sign positive?"** → **low osteonecrosis risk**

**"Bony resorption at 8 wk means?"** → **talar perfusion**

**"Hawkins III AVN rate (TO)?"** → **70%**

**"Greatest impact on AVN?"** → **initial displacement**

**"Delayed fixation — true?"** → **doesn't increase AVN**

**"AVN after open extruded neck fracture — risk factor?"** → **open fracture**

**"Hawkins II AVN — correlated?"** → **degree of initial displacement**

**"Second commonest complication, Hawkins III?"** → **osteonecrosis**

**"Most common complication?"** → **post-traumatic (subtalar) arthritis**

**"Varus malunion causes (TO)?"** → **↓ eversion**

**"Varus malalignment ↓ which motion?"** → **subtalar eversion**

**"Supination deformity after percutaneous screws?"** → **varus malunion**

**"Reconstruct varus malunion?"** → **talar neck medial opening wedge osteotomy**

**"Dorsal impingement, ↓ dorsiflexion?"** → **isolated dorsal malunion**

**"Strongest construct (TO)?"** → **posterior-to-anterior parallel screws**

**"Medial comminution — fixation?"** → **medial bridge plate + lateral non-lag screw**

**"Hawkins II reduced closed — next?"** → **ORIF**

**"Acute definitive fixation — true?"** → **higher infection**

**"Extruded talar body?"** → **reduce native body + ORIF**

**"Reimplantation — true?"** → **safe and can succeed**

**"Talar neck nonunion, viable body?"** → **revision ORIF + bone graft**

**"AVN + ankle + subtalar arthritis?"** → **TTC fusion**

**"Neck vs body landmark?"** → **lateral process (articulates with posterior facet)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Talus Fracture (other than neck)

*Trauma · 61 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **< 1% of all fractures** · talus = **2nd commonest tarsal fracture** (after calcaneus)

| Site | Share of talus fractures |
|---|---|
| Neck | Commonest (\~50%) |
| **Body** | **13–23%** |
| **Lateral process** | **10.4%** |
| **Head** | **Least common** |

**Mechanism** — body: high energy, hindfoot supinated or pronated · lateral process: see below

### Anatomy

**Talus** — **5 articular surfaces**, **\~70% cartilage**, **no muscle / tendon attachments** → direct extraosseous blood supply (artery of tarsal canal main; deltoid artery to body)

**Lateral process** — articulates with **posterior facet of calcaneus** + **lateral malleolus** (lateral margin of talofibular joint) · "lateral facet of calcaneus" (OB) — not medial facet

**Posterior process** — **medial + lateral tubercles** separated by **FHL groove** · **os trigonum** in up to **50%** — don't call it a fracture

## 3 Presentation

**Lateral process** — anterolateral ankle pain after snowboarding, **misdiagnosed as ankle sprain**

**Posterolateral tubercle** — pain with **FHL flexion / extension**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| AP + lateral | **Lateral process seen on AP**; X-rays may be **falsely negative** |
| Canale view | Talar neck (max equinus, 15° pronation, 75° cephalad) |
| **CT** | **Best for posterior, lateral, posteromedial process**; displacement, comminution, congruity; when suspicion high + X-rays negative |
| **MRI** | Confirms diagnosis when X-rays negative |

**Snowboarder, negative X-rays, can't bear weight — next** — **MRI** (OB) · **CT** when suspicion high (Orthobullets) — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: cast · bone scan · injection · repeat X-rays

![Lateral process fracture on AP](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1049-xray-ap-non-displaced-lateral-process-f-moved.jpg)

![Os trigonum — not a fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1049-xray-lat-os-trigonum-emedicine-moved.jpg)

### Anatomic classification

| Fracture | Type / description |
|---|---|
| **Lateral process 1** | **Extra-articular** |
| **Lateral process 2** | **Into subtalar + talofibular joint** |
| **Lateral process 3** | **Comminuted** |
| **Posteromedial tubercle** | Avulsion of **posterior talotibial / posterior deltoid** ligament · **CeDell fracture** (TO) |
| **Posterolateral tubercle** | Avulsion of **posterior talofibular ligament** · **Shepherd fracture** (TO) |
| Head | Least common |
| Body | Posterior to lateral process |

**Aviator's astragalus (TO)** — **talar neck** fracture (RAF pilots)

![Lateral process classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1049-lateral-process-classification.jpg)

### Lateral process fracture (snowboarder's fracture)

**Snowboarder's ankle (TO)** — **lateral process of talus fracture** · **15× likelier** in snowboarders; **⅓ of their ankle fractures**\
✗ Wrong options: ATFL avulsion · PTFL avulsion · medial process + deltoid

**Mechanism** — **dorsiflexion + axial load + eversion + external rotation** (Orthobullets, TO) · **inversion + ER** (OB) — read the options

**Subtalar arthritis** — **45%** regardless of treatment = **most common complication** · prognosis good with prompt treatment

**Excising a 1 cm fragment** — **lateral talocalcaneal ligament incompetent** (✗ Wrong options: bifurcate · peroneal retinaculum · arcuate · PTFL) · **tolerated — no ankle / subtalar instability** (✗ Wrong options: 5 mm)

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria

| Fracture | Nonop (**< 2 mm**) | Operative (**> 2 mm**) | Excise |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Lateral process** | **SLC 6 wk**, moulded to **support longitudinal arch** | **ORIF / K-wires** (lateral approach) | **Comminuted** |
| Posterior process | SLC 6 wk | ORIF posteromedial (± posterior / medial malleolar osteotomy) | **Comminuted, nonunion** |
| Head | SLC 6 wk | ORIF | — |
| **Body** | SLC 6 wk | **ORIF — combined medial + lateral** ± malleolar osteotomy | — |

**Displaced, non-comminuted, intra-articular lateral process** — **ORIF** · **4 mm displaced → ORIF, not cast**\
✗ Wrong options: excision · brace + PT · hard-soled shoe · cast (that is < 2 mm)

**Comminuted intra-articular, failed nonop** — **fragment excision**

| Approach | Use | Interval / risk |
|---|---|---|
| **Lateral** | Lateral process | Over **tarsal sinus**, reflect EDB distally |
| **Posteromedial** | Medial tubercle / whole posterior process displaced medially | **FDL ↔ NV bundle** |
| **Posterolateral** | Lateral tubercle | **Peroneals ↔ Achilles** · **sural nerve** · NV bundle **medial to FHL** |

### Talar body fracture — articular reduction

**Improves long-term outcome** — **primary bone healing at the articular surface** (anatomic reduction, **absolute stability**)\
✗ Wrong options: surgery < 48 h · minimally invasive · early WB · relative stability

### Open talus fracture — staged management

**Diabetic (HbA1c 10.1), contaminated open neck fracture + body extrusion** — **urgent (< 24 h) I&D + ankle-spanning ex-fix, delayed definitive fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: discharge + delayed single stage · Steinmann pins definitive · acute medial plating · acute TTC nail

**Timing** — acute definitive fixation of open fractures → **up to 75% early complications** (dehiscence, skin necrosis, infection) vs **2–10% delayed** · definitive at **1–3 wk** when swelling settles

**Talus cartilage** — **> 50% articular** → limited vascular inflow · **quality of reduction = most important outcome predictor**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **AVN** | **Hawkins sign (lucency) = revascularisation** · **sclerosis without Hawkins sign = AVN** |
| **Subtalar arthritis** | **45%** after lateral process fracture · anatomic reduction ↓ |
| **Talonavicular arthritis** | → **TN arthrodesis** |
| Malunion | — |
| Symptomatic nonunion | Pain **up to 2 yr** · posterior process → excise |

## X vs Y

| | **Shepherd fracture** | **CeDell fracture** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | **Lateral tubercle**, posterior process | **Medial tubercle** |
| Ligament | Posterior talofibular | Posterior talotibial / deltoid |
| Frequency | Commoner | Less frequent |
| Approach | Posterolateral | Posteromedial |

| | **Lateral process fracture** | **Ankle sprain** |
|---|---|---|
| Clue | Snowboarder, dorsiflexion + ER | Inversion |
| X-ray | **AP view**; may be negative | Normal |
| Next if negative | **MRI / CT** | — |

## How it's asked

**"Snowboarder's ankle (TO)?"** → **lateral process of talus fracture**

**"Snowboarder, negative X-rays — next?"** → **MRI ankle**

**"Excising 1 cm fragment → incompetent?"** → **lateral talocalcaneal ligament**

**"Displaced non-comminuted lateral process — diagnosis + treatment?"** → **lateral process fracture → ORIF**

**"Displaced lateral process fracture — treatment?"** → **operative fixation**

**"Lateral process fracture — true?"** → **subtalar arthritis commonest regardless of treatment**

**"Talar body — improves outcome?"** → **primary bone healing at articular surface**

**"Open contaminated talus, diabetic?"** → **I&D + spanning ex-fix, delayed fixation**

**"Shepherd fracture (TO)?"** → **lateral part of posterior process**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Terrible Triad Injury of Elbow

*Trauma · 62 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Components of the triad

**Triad (Hotchkiss 1996)** — **elbow dislocation (posterolateral)** + **radial head / neck fracture** + **coronoid fracture** · LCL injury almost always

**NOT part of it (TO)** — **olecranon fracture** · **biceps tendon tear**\
✗ Wrong options: LCL injury · coronoid · radial head (all part)

**Mason-Johnston type IV** (radial head fracture + dislocation) = terrible triad pattern (TO)

**Coronoid fracture types in the triad (TO)** — mostly **tip**, less often **anteromedial facet** (O'Driscoll)

![Terrible triad — lateral radiograph](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1021-xray-elbow-lat-terrible-triad.jpg)

### Anatomy — elbow stabilisers

| Primary (TO) | Secondary (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Ulnohumeral joint (coronoid)** — buttress vs varus + posterior dislocation | **Radial head** — valgus + PLRI |
| **Anterior bundle of MCL** | Flexor-pronator + extensor origins |
| **LCL complex** | Joint capsule |

**Radial head** — **secondary restraint to PLRI + valgus** (Orthobullets) · "**primary restraint to PLRI**" (OB) — read the options · neutral rotation: lateral non-articular margin between **radial styloid + Lister's tubercle**

**Coronoid** — **anterior + varus buttress**; \~60% of anterior articulation (TO); **resists posterior subluxation beyond 30° flexion** · fragment often has **anterior capsule** attached

**Anterior capsule insertion** — **5.9 mm from tip** (TO) · inserts more distally — small tip fractures often lack capsule (OB) · coronoid tip fracture is a **shear**, not an avulsion (OB)

| MCL bundle | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Anterior** | **Most important — valgus + PMRI restraint** · inserts **sublime tubercle (anteromedial facet of coronoid)**, **18.4 mm dorsal to tip** (✗ Wrong options: 3 mm distal to tip · radial tuberosity) |
| Posterior | Valgus instability in flexion |
| Transverse | — |

| LCL component (insert: **supinator crest**) | Fact |
|---|---|
| **LUCL** | **Most important; primary restraint to PLRI** · pushing up from chair |
| Radial collateral | Varus instability |
| Annular | Little stability |
| Accessory collateral | — |

**LCL injury** — usually **avulsed from the lateral epicondyle (humeral origin)** ± extensor origin rupture

![LCL complex](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1021-lcl.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanism & sequence of soft-tissue failure

**Mechanism** — fall on **extended arm, forearm supinated** → **valgus + axial + posterolateral rotatory** forces → **posterolateral dislocation** · \~60% of complex dislocations from **standing-height fall** (TO)

**Sequence (Horii circle)** — **LCL → anterior capsule (coronoid) → MCL** · **MCL torn last** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: MCL first

**EXCEPT (TO)** — "**LCL tear is uncommon due to valgus force**" — false; LCL fails first

**Supination + axial + posterolateral + valgus** — **coronoid + radial head fracture + dislocation**\
✗ Wrong options: supracondylar · anterior olecranon fracture-dislocation (direct blow at 90°) · coronoid + olecranon + LCL (**varus**)

| Pattern | Force | Fracture |
|---|---|---|
| **PLRI (terrible triad)** | **Valgus**, supination, axial | Radial head + coronoid **tip** |
| **PMRI** | **Varus** | **Anteromedial coronoid facet**, LCL; often no radial head fracture |

**PLRI far commoner** than PMRI

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain, **clicking + locking in extension** · varus / valgus instability · **check DRUJ (Essex-Lopresti)**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| Pre- + post-reduction X-rays | **Concentric ulnohumeral + radiocapitellar joints** · **radial neck line through capitellum centre** on every view |
| Lateral | **Coronoid fracture** |
| Wrist / forearm | When indicated (Essex-Lopresti) |
| **CT ± 3D** | Coronoid detail, fracture lines |

### Coronoid anatomy & biomechanics (MCL insertion, Regan-Morrey, approach)

| Regan-Morrey | Coronoid height | Fixation |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Tip** (< 10%) | **Not needed** — no stability conferred (cadaver) |
| **II** | **≤ 50%** | Usually not — fix MCL first if unstable |
| **III** | **> 50%** | **Fix** |

**Biomechanics** — **> 50% fractured → ↑ posterior axial displacement at all flexion angles incl. 30°** · **< 50% (I + II) → no significant change** · **anteromedial facet → PMRI**\
✗ Wrong options: 25% → displacement

**Fix the coronoid** — when > 10% (OB) · > 30–50% base (OB) · **type III** (OB) · decided by **fragment size + stability after radial head** — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: displacement > 5 mm · comminution · age

**Repair even type I (TO)** — **restores anterior bony buttress + anterior capsule insertion → prevents posterior subluxation** · OB: type I / II repair adds little\
✗ Wrong options: brachialis attachment · HO prevention · LUCL isometric point

**Isolated large coronoid fracture** — **medial FCU-splitting approach + buttress plate** → FCU = **ulnar nerve** · triad → through lateral radial head defect

![Anterior bundle of MCL](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1021-amcl.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Terrible triad (after reduction)
├─ Rare: concentric, small coronoid, radial head non-surgical, stable → splint 90° 7–10 d, early ROM
└─ Most → sequential repair, deep to superficial, then retest
       ├─ 1 coronoid (fix / capsular suture) → 2 radial head ORIF or replace
       ├─ 3 LCL repair to humerus → 4 MCL repair if still unstable
       └─ 5 hinged ex-fix if still unstable
```

### Operative criteria

| Structure | Fix / replace when |
|---|---|
| **Radial head ORIF** | **Non-comminuted, < 40% articular** · 2-part > 30% → ORIF |
| **Radial head arthroplasty** | **Comminuted > 3 fragments** (≥ 3 involving > 25%, OB) · > 40% articular · not age > 40, sagittal fracture, PIN palsy |
| **Radial head excision** | **Never** with instability / Essex-Lopresti · fragment excision only if < 25%, non-articulating, stable |
| **Coronoid** | Type III / large base (see above) |
| **MCL** | **Instability after LCL + fractures**, esp. **extension beyond 30°** · PMRI with type I / II coronoid |

### Standard fixation — radial head fixation / replacement, coronoid ORIF & LCL repair

**Standard (comminuted head + coronoid)** — **radial head arthroplasty + coronoid ORIF + LCL / LUCL repair** (McKee: 34 / 36 stable)\
✗ Wrong options: closed + early ROM · radial head resection · hinged brace · static ex-fix 6 wk · MCL routinely · ulnar nerve transposition

**LUCL** — **repair to the humerus** (avulsed from origin)\
✗ Wrong options: reconstruction acutely · repair to ulna · TEA

**Radial head ORIF** — 1.5 / 2.0 / 2.4 mm countersunk screws · plate **posterolateral, safe zone 90–110°** (styloid → Lister's) · 2.0 plate on neck → little motion loss

**Radial head implant** — articulate **2 mm distal to coronoid tip** · **avoid overstuffing** · undersizing → valgus instability

**Coronoid fixation** — through radial head defect · sutures via 2 drill holes ("lasso"), anchors · **posterior-to-anterior lag screws** if large (anterior-to-posterior, TO) · basal → medial plate

**LCL repair** — anchors / transosseous at **centre of capitellar curvature**, elbow 90° · **MCL intact → forearm pronated** · **MCL injured → supinated** (avoid medial gapping)

**Posterior skin incision** — reaches medial + lateral, fewer cutaneous nerve injuries, cosmetic

**Elbow stable after triad — single most important for rotatory stability** — **LCL complex repair / reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: radial head replacement / ORIF · capsular plication · MCL

### Persistent instability after lateral repair — MCL repair

**Still unstable after coronoid + radial head + LCL** — **repair MCL**\
✗ Wrong options: larger radial head (overstuffing) · resect head · cast 6–8 wk · reinforce LCL · Steinmann pin · ex-fix before MCL

**Residual posterolateral instability after fracture fixation alone** — **repair LCL complex**\
✗ Wrong options: MCL first · cast / brace · ex-fix

**Type I coronoid, unstable with valgus after head ORIF + LCL** — **MCL repair**\
✗ Wrong options: coronoid ORIF · convert to arthroplasty · splint

**Posteromedial subluxation after head + LCL, tip fracture** — **MCL via medial approach, protect MABCN (medial antebrachial cutaneous nerve)**\
✗ Wrong options: coronoid through lateral · medial brachial cutaneous · musculocutaneous

**Hinged ex-fix** — **last step**, after MCL

### Postoperative splinting position

| Repaired | Splint / passive motion |
|---|---|
| **LCL only (MCL intact)** | **Flexion + pronation** — pronation tightens MCL sling |
| MCL | Supination |
| **Both** | **Flexion + neutral** |

**Rehab** — **active ROM at 48 h** (dynamic stabilisers) · may block last 30° of extension early · prolonged immobilisation → poor results

## 6 Complications

### Complications — stiffness

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Post-traumatic stiffness** | **Most common** complication · **commonest needing reoperation** · prevent with early ROM |
| **Instability** | Commoner after **type I / II coronoid** not fixed · "most commonly persistent instability" (OB) — read the options |
| Fixation failure | **Radial neck** ORIF (poor vascularity → AVN, nonunion) |
| **HO** | Common; doesn't always need surgery · prophylaxis with **head injury / revision** |
| PTOA | Chondral damage, residual instability |
| PIN injury | Lateral approach — stay proximal to annular ligament, pronate, release supinator posteriorly |

**Prognosis** — historically poor: **persistent instability, stiffness, arthrosis**; good with stable fixation + early motion

## X vs Y

| | **PLRI (terrible triad)** | **PMRI** |
|---|---|---|
| Force | **Valgus**, supination | **Varus** |
| Coronoid | **Tip** | **Anteromedial facet** |
| Radial head | **Fractured** | Usually intact |
| Key repair | **LCL** | Coronoid buttress (medial) + LCL |

| | **LCL repair, MCL intact** | **MCL also injured** |
|---|---|---|
| Forearm during LCL repair | **Pronation** | **Supination** |
| Splint | **Flexion + pronation** | Neutral (both repaired) |

## How it's asked

**"Not part of terrible triad (TO)?"** → **olecranon fracture**

**"Not part of terrible triad, biceps option (TO)?"** → **biceps tendon tear**

**"Valgus + axial + posterolateral force (supinated)?"** → **coronoid + radial head fracture + dislocation**

**"Sequence of injury?"** → **LCL → anterior capsule → MCL**

**"Terrible triad EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **LCL tear uncommon due to valgus**

**"PLRI radiograph?"** → **terrible triad (radial head + coronoid tip)**

**"Coronoid > 50%, comminuted head?"** → **arthroplasty + coronoid ORIF + LCL repair**

**"Comminuted head + basal coronoid, 62-yr?"** → **head replacement + coronoid ORIF + LUCL ± MCL**

**"What decides ulna (coronoid) fixation?"** → **fragment size + stability after head replacement**

**"Restore stability in most cases?"** → **head fixation / replacement + coronoid fixation + LUCL repair**

**"Type II coronoid, nonreconstructable head?"** → **arthroplasty + coronoid ORIF + LCL repair**

**"Arthroplasty indication?"** → **> 3 fragments**

**"Unstable in valgus at 40° (comminuted head)?"** → **replacement + coronoid ORIF + LUCL repair**

**"Comminuted head in triad?"** → **radial head arthroplasty**

**"Where to repair LUCL?"** → **to the humerus**

**"Still unstable after full lateral repair?"** → **repair MCL**

**"Residual posterolateral instability < 35°?"** → **repair LCL complex**

**"Type I coronoid, valgus instability after head + LCL?"** → **MCL repair**

**"Unstable 0–20° after full repair?"** → **repair medial UCL**

**"Posteromedial subluxation — next step + nerve?"** → **MCL via medial approach, protect MABCN**

**"Most rotatory stability?"** → **LCL complex repair**

**"Late instability pushing from chair?"** → **LUCL**

**"Repair type I coronoid — rationale (TO)?"** → **anterior buttress + capsule insertion vs posterior subluxation**

**"Anterior MCL bundle inserts?"** → **anteromedial (sublime tubercle) coronoid**

**"Isolated coronoid — approach muscle nerve?"** → **ulnar (FCU split)**

**"Cadaver — > 50% coronoid?"** → **↑ posterior axial displacement at 30°**

**"Coronoid needing fixation?"** → **Regan-Morrey type III**

**"Splint if MCL intact?"** → **flexion + pronation**

**"Commonest complication requiring reoperation?"** → **loss of motion**

**"Most common complication?"** → **post-traumatic stiffness**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tibial Plafond Fractures

*Trauma · 63 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **5–10% of tibia fractures** (Orthobullets) · 5–7% (TO) · < 10% of lower-limb injuries · age 35–45, M > F

**Associated** — **fibula fracture 75% (commonest association, TO)** · ipsilateral limb injury 30% · **open 20%** · bilateral 5–10% · **syndesmotic injury \~3.4%**

### Fracture anatomy & patterns (Chaput fragment)

**Mechanism** — **high-energy axial load** (talus driven into plafond → **articular impaction + metaphyseal comminution**) · fall from height, MVC · low-energy rotation (skiing) less common

**Posterior pilon** — **rotation + axial impaction** (plantarflexed foot) → large posterior malleolus fragment, posteromedial comminution, **double-density sign** → ORIF

| Fragment (ligament attached) | Position |
|---|---|
| **Medial malleolar** | **Deltoid** |
| **Chaput** | **Anterolateral** — **AITFL** |
| **Volkmann** | **Posterolateral** — **PITFL** |
| Wagstaffe | **Fibular** (anterior tubercle) — other end of AITFL |

**3 fragments typical** (intact ligaments) · **4 classic** on X-ray: medial, anterior (Chaput), lateral (Wagstaffe), posterior (Volkmann)

**Syndesmosis** — AITFL (Chaput → Wagstaffe), **PITFL — strongest** (Volkmann → lateral malleolus), interosseous membrane + ligament, inferior transverse ligament

![Pilon fracture fragments](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1046-tibial-plafond-4-fracture-fragments.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — swelling, **fracture blisters**, open wounds, chronic skin / vascular change · **DP + PT pulses** (ABI / CTA if needed) · nerves · **compartment syndrome**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| X-ray | AP, lateral, mortise + **full-length tibia / fibula + foot** · lumbar films if indicated |
| **CT** | **After spanning ex-fix** (ligamentotaxis → better planning) (✗ Wrong options: before frame) · fine cuts ± 3D · **"Mercedes-Benz" sign** on axials |
| **CT soft-tissue windows** | **Tibialis posterior tendon entrapment** (posteromedial fragment) |
| MRI | Not used — poor bony detail |

![Mercedes-Benz sign on axial CT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1046-screen-shot-2021-04-28-at-10-00-25-pm.jpg)

### Classifications

| AO/OTA 43 | Pattern | Subtypes (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **Extra-articular** | 1 simple · 2 wedge · 3 multifragmentary |
| **B** | **Partial articular** | 1 split · 2 split-depression · 3 depression |
| **C** | **Complete articular** (articular dissociation from shaft) | 1 simple · 2 simple articular + multifragmentary metaphysis · 3 multifragmentary |

| Rüedi-Allgöwer | Pattern | Image |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Nondisplaced** intra-articular | ![Rüedi-Allgöwer](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1046-shows-the-ruedi-allgower-classification.jpg) |
| **II** | **Displaced, no comminution** (incongruous joint) | — |
| **III** | **Displaced + comminuted / impacted** | ![Type III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1046-type-iii-ruedi-allgower-ap.jpg) |

**Leonetti + Tigani (TO)** — **only CT-based classification** · I non-displaced (Ia) / extra-articular (Ib) · **II** 2-part (S sagittal, F frontal) · **III** 3-part (S / F) · **IV** 4-part / comminuted ("die-punch") · AO + Rüedi-Allgöwer are radiograph-based, poor for planning / prognosis

## 5 Management

```
Pilon fracture
├─ Non-displaced / unfit / high skin risk → long leg cast 6 wk
├─ Length-unstable, high energy → splint → spanning ex-fix (± fibula) → CT → delayed ORIF (10–14 d)
│      └─ open → I&D + ex-fix
├─ Simple articular extension → percutaneous screws + IM nail
└─ Non-reconstructable / elderly / labourer → primary arthrodesis (rare)
```

### Staged management — spanning ex-fix then delayed ORIF

**Sequence** — **splint → spanning ex-fix → CT → delayed ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: CT before ex-fix · acute ORIF · cast

**Comminuted closed pilon — next** — **spanning external fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF · percutaneous · cast · arthrodesis · CT / MRI first

**Open pilon (medial wound)** — **I&D + external fixation** · open + tibial defect → **ex-fix of tibia** only; graft at staged ORIF\
✗ Wrong options: immediate ORIF · I&D + splint · amputation

**Decreases wound complications** — **immediate spanning ex-fix ± immediate fibula fixation, delayed tibial reconstruction** (Sirkin)\
✗ Wrong options: immediate tibia · splint + ORIF at 1 wk · Ilizarov + limited fixation

**Obese smoker, swollen** — **closed reduction + spanning ex-fix**\
✗ Wrong options: splint → cast · ORIF · Blair fusion

**3 wk, swelling settled** — **ORIF tibia + fibula**\
✗ Wrong options: fibula only · walking cast · dynamise · fusion in young

**Two-stage ORIF vs LIFEF** — **two-stage ORIF preferred** (LIFEF: ↑ superficial infection, healing problems) · acute ORIF infection up to **55%** · staged \~**3%** · definitive ex-fix: pin infection, malalignment, nonunion

**Timing** — **skin wrinkles, blisters epithelialised, ecchymosis resolving (10–14 d)** · ex-fix until then

**Ex-fix** — **2 tibial half-pins + 1 transcalcaneal pin** ('delta' / 'A' frame) · **1st MT pin prevents equinus** · trans-navicular if calcaneal fracture · pins out of zone of injury / incisions

### Operative criteria

| Option | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Cast** | Stable, no articular displacement · critically ill / non-ambulatory · diabetes, PVD, neuropathy |
| **Spanning ex-fix** | **Most length-unstable** fractures (temporary) |
| **ORIF** | **Definitive for most**; outcome = **articular reduction** |
| Ex-fix / circular frame alone | Bone / soft tissue precludes ORIF · high union, pin-tract infection |
| **IM nail + percutaneous screws** | **Simple intra-articular extension** · ↑ **valgus + recurvatum** vs plate |
| **Primary arthrodesis** | Non-reconstructable, select elderly, labourers |

**Simple articular extension, swollen** — **percutaneous articular fixation + IM nail**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · immediate ORIF · uniplanar ex-fix

### Surgical technique & approaches

**Rüedi-Allgöwer principles (TO)** — **1 restore fibular length · 2 anatomic articular reduction · 3 cancellous autograft for defect · 4 medial column buttress** (now often anterolateral locking plate)

**Order after fibula plated** — **anatomic reduction + stabilisation of articular surface first** → then metaphysis (length, rotation, angulation — not anatomic) → plate to shaft · simple fibula: plate first · **comminuted fibula: after tibia**\
✗ Wrong options: plate first

**Alignment goals** — **< 5–10° varus / valgus**, **< 5–10° pro- / recurvatum** · skin bridge **> 7 cm**

| Approach | Plane / fact |
|---|---|
| **Anterolateral** | **Deep ↔ superficial peroneal nerves** · in line with **4th ray** · valgus-impacted / intact fibula · **whole articular surface visible** · extensors retracted **medially** · **SPN → dorsal foot numbness (commonest nerve problem)** · DPN at risk deep |
| **Anteromedial** | **Deep peroneal ↔ tibial nerve** · **extensile access to central impaction** |
| Direct anterior | **No true internervous plane**; not extensile |
| **Posterolateral** | Fibula (separate incision for skin bridge) · **bone graft for distal tibial nonunion** |

**Fibular fixation** — not essential · **↑ overall complications + hardware removal**; **no difference in reduction, alignment, satisfaction, arthrosis** (OB) · **TO: alignment DOES differ** (EXCEPT: "no difference in alignment" is false) — read the options

**Post-op** — ankle ROM at **2 wk** · NWB **6–12 wk**

### Fine-wire fixation vs ORIF

**43C open, FWF vs ORIF** — **FWF → higher complication rate** · same function, malunion, nonunion, PTOA · meta-analysis: frame vs ORIF — **no difference** in infection / complications (Orthobullets) — read the options

**Circular frame** — **distraction = reduction** · 2 AP half-pins proximally · **wires ≥ 1–2 cm from joint** (septic arthritis) · **60° safe arc** · HA-coated pins ↓ loosening

![Circular frame wire safe zones](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1046-screen-shot-2021-05-20-at-8-19-48-pm.jpg)

### Primary arthrodesis

**Position** — **neutral DF, 5–10° ER, 5° hindfoot valgus, 5 mm posterior talar translation** · anterior plate · cast 8 wk, FWB 12 wk, CT at 3 months

**TTC nail** — sacrifices subtalar motion, ↑ transverse tarsal arthritis, immediate WB

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Rate / fact |
|---|---|
| **Wound slough / dehiscence** | **9–30%** · wait 1–2 wk · free flap |
| **Infection** | **5–15%** · swelling at surgery, severity · **intra-wound vancomycin powder ↓ gram +ve deep SSI** |
| Malunion | 6–14% |
| **Nonunion** | **5%**, **metaphyseal junction** · comminution, open, bone loss, **smoking, NSAIDs** → rule out infection, graft + rigid fixation |
| **PTOA** | **1–2 yr** · non-anatomic reduction · TAA / fusion |
| **Stiffness** | **Up to 33% at 3 yr** · severity, obesity, ASA ≥ 3 |
| **Tibialis posterior entrapment** | Posteromedial fragment, **↓ inversion**, gap · **CT soft-tissue windows** |

### Complications — nonunion grafting, tendon entrapment, equinus

**Distal tibial nonunion graft — approach** — **posterolateral** (best soft-tissue cover)\
✗ Wrong options: anterior · anterolateral · anteromedial · lateral

**Inversion weakness + posteromedial gap** — **tibialis posterior entrapment**\
✗ Wrong options: deltoid · FHL (more posterior) · peroneus brevis · tibial nerve (plantar numbness)

**Tendoachilles lengthening indicated** — **DF −10° with knee flexed AND extended** (Silfverskiöld)\
✗ Wrong options: only with knee extended (gastrocnemius) · subtalar 5° (arthritis)

### Post-traumatic arthritis — chondrocyte death & IL-6

**Initial chondrocyte death** — **necrosis**, **superficial zone**, **at fracture margins** · **apoptosis = delayed**, also at margins\
✗ Wrong options: deep zone · remote

**Synovial fluid** — **↑ IL-6** (+ IL-8, MMP-1, -2, -3, -9, -10) · not linked to NWB duration or compliance\
✗ Wrong options: TGF-β · RANKL · IL-2 · cAMP

### Outcomes & predictors (education level, prolonged recovery, syndesmotic injury)

**Poor outcome / no return to work** — **lower education**, comorbidities (≥ 2), **male**, **work-related**, low income, married, definitive ex-fix\
✗ Wrong options: joint line · displacement · time to ORIF · open · varus collapse · marijuana · two plates · temporary ex-fix

**Recovery** — **improves for > 2 yr** · type C → SF-36 lower than pelvic fracture, AIDS, CAD

**Syndesmotic injury** — **\~3.4%** · **↑ amputation**, deep infection, reoperation · more segmental fibula fractures · no ↑ nonunion

### Return to driving — braking time

**Brake travel time** — **↑ until 6 wk after weight bearing begins** (long-bone + articular, right leg)\
✗ Wrong options: 8 wk · normal at WB

## X vs Y

| | **Anterolateral approach** | **Anteromedial approach** |
|---|---|---|
| Plane | **Deep ↔ superficial peroneal** | **Deep peroneal ↔ tibial** |
| Best for | Valgus impaction, intact fibula | **Central impaction** (extensile) |
| Nerve risk | **SPN — dorsal foot numbness** | Saphenous |

| | **Gastrocnemius contracture** | **Achilles contracture** |
|---|---|---|
| DF knee flexed | Normal | **Limited** |
| DF knee extended | **Limited** | **Limited** |
| Surgery | Gastrocnemius recession | **TA lengthening** |

## How it's asked

**"Comminuted closed pilon — next?"** → **spanning ex-fix**

**"Ex-fix for 3 wk, swelling settled?"** → **ORIF tibia + fibula**

**"Open pilon, medial wound?"** → **I&D + ex-fix**

**"Open pilon with defect — with I&D?"** → **ex-fix of tibia**

**"Correct sequence?"** → **splint → ex-fix → CT → delayed ORIF**

**"Decreases wound complications?"** → **spanning ex-fix ± fibula fixation, delayed tibia**

**"Obese smoker, swollen?"** → **closed reduction + spanning ex-fix**

**"CT timing?"** → **after ex-fix**

**"Optimise outcome, 43C, no wrinkles?"** → **ex-fix then delayed ORIF**

**"Chaput fragment ligament?"** → **AITFL**

**"Rotation + axial, plantarflexed?"** → **posterior pilon**

**"Pilon EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **no alignment difference with fibular fixation**

**"Next after plating fibula?"** → **anatomic articular reduction**

**"Simple articular extension, swollen?"** → **percutaneous screws + IM nail**

**"Fibular fixation — true?"** → **higher complication rate**

**"Anterolateral approach — true?"** → **dorsal foot numbness commonest nerve complication**

**"Central impaction — plane?"** → **tibial ↔ deep peroneal**

**"Poor outcome predictor?"** → **lower education**

**"Perceived outcome at 1 yr?"** → **improves over > 2 yr**

**"Syndesmotic injury — true?"** → **higher amputation rate**

**"Initial chondrocyte death?"** → **necrosis, superficial zone, fracture margins**

**"Synovial factor in PTOA?"** → **IL-6**

**"FWF vs ORIF in 43C?"** → **FWF higher complications**

**"Nonunion graft approach?"** → **posterolateral**

**"Inversion weakness, posteromedial gap?"** → **tibialis posterior entrapment**

**"TA lengthening indication?"** → **DF −10° knee flexed + extended**

**"Brake travel time?"** → **↑ until 6 wk after WB**
