# Tibial Plateau Fractures

*Trauma · 64 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1–2% of all fractures** · 10.3 / 100,000 / yr · mean age 52 · **bimodal**: men 40s (high energy), women 70s (low energy)

**Location** — **lateral 70–80%** · bicondylar 10–30% · medial 10–20%

**Mechanism** — **valgus → lateral** · **varus → medial** · **axial → bicondylar** · high energy → medial, fracture-dislocation, soft-tissue injury · low energy → lateral

### Plateau anatomy (medial vs lateral)

| | **Medial plateau** | **Lateral plateau** |
|---|---|---|
| Shape | **Concave, larger** (TO) | **Convex**, smaller |
| Height | **More distal** | **More proximal** |
| Load | **60% — primary weight-bearing** | 40% |
| Bone | Stronger | Less dense |
| Meniscus | Less mobile (coronary ligaments) | **More mobile** → **submeniscal arthrotomy** |

**Alignment** — **posterior slope 6–10°** · **3° varus** to tibial mechanical axis

**Kinematics** — 0–140° (walking 0–70°) · concave medial pivot + convex lateral → **femoral rollback**, screw-home (tibia ER in extension)

**Insertions** — ACL between spines · PCL posterior sulcus · superficial MCL deep to pes · **LCL anterolateral fibular head** · ITB **Gerdy's tubercle** · pes anteromedial

**Nerves** — **common peroneal around fibular neck (commonest nerve injury)** · tibial with popliteal artery

### Associated meniscal & ligament injuries

| Injury | Association |
|---|---|
| **Lateral meniscus** | **Commoner than medial** · **Schatzker II** · **> 10 mm depression + > 6 mm widening** (Orthobullets; OB) · **≥ 6 mm depression + 5–6 mm widening** (OB) — read the options |
| **Medial meniscus** | **Schatzker IV** |
| **ACL** | **Types IV + VI (25%)** (Orthobullets; OB) · **types V + VI** (TO; OB) — read the options |
| Neurovascular | **Schatzker IV fracture-dislocation**; hyperextension bicondylar |
| Compartment syndrome | High energy |

**Meniscal tear type** — **peripheral / meniscocapsular detachment** (vascular zone, heals) · meniscus stays with femur as plateau widens\
✗ Wrong options: horizontal cleavage · radial · root · bucket-handle medial

**Rates** — meniscal tear \~30% at surgery, **up to 91% on MRI** · ligament injury > 75% on MRI · LM injury > 75% of displaced Schatzker II

**Lateral meniscal tear risk** — **> 10 mm depression**, Schatzker II > I, **age < 48, male** (✗ Wrong options: > 50 · female · calcaneus fracture) · **6 mm widening** suggests LM tear (✗ Wrong options: 3 mm depression · spine fracture (ACL))

**Management** — **submeniscal arthrotomy dividing meniscotibial ligaments**, repair at fixation (longitudinal → repair · radial → partial resection)\
✗ Wrong options: meniscofemoral · leave untreated

**Repaired LM tear — 1-yr outcome** — **similar function + QoL**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ TKA · ↓ ROM · ↑ OA grade · ↑ re-arthroscopy

**ACL + PLC with plateau fracture** — grade 3 Lachman + varus laxity 0° + 30° + ER asymmetry → **ACL + PLC reconstruction (LCL + popliteofibular)**, LM repair · missed PLC → ACL graft fails

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — rule out **open** wound · **compartment syndrome** · varus / valgus stress in **full extension: laxity > 10° = instability** · pulses → **ABI if asymmetric; < 0.9 → arteriogram** · tibial + common peroneal nerves

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| AP, lateral, **oblique** (depression) | Depressed surface, **sclerotic band**, alignment · lateral: **posteromedial fracture lines**, slope |
| **Plateau view** | **10° caudal tilt** (matches posterior slope) |
| **CT** | Depression, comminution, **posterior coronal split** (axial + sagittal) · **after ex-fix** if staged |
| MRI | Meniscus + ligaments, occult fracture |

### Lipohaemarthrosis — occult fracture

**Lipohaemarthrosis** — **intra-articular fracture**: **tibial plateau commonest** (TO) · **occult fracture** (OB)\
✗ Wrong options: meniscus tear · MPFL · tendon rupture · ligament injury · arthritis

**Layers** — **fat (top), serum, RBCs (bottom)** · separation up to **3 h** · CT / MRI as sensitive as arthrocentesis

### Classification (Schatzker, Moore, hyperextension varus)

| Schatzker | Pattern | Note | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Lateral split (wedge)** | **Young, strong subchondral bone** | ![Schatzker I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1044-schatzker-1.jpg) |
| **II** | **Lateral split-depression** | **Commonest** · average age > 50 (TO) · **LM tear** | ![Schatzker II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1044-schatzker-2-2.jpg) |
| **III** | **Lateral pure depression** | **Elderly osteoporotic**, uncommon | ![Schatzker III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1044-schatzker-3.jpg) |
| **IV** | **Medial plateau** | **Fracture-dislocation, NV + ligament injury, MM tear** | ![Schatzker IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1044-schaztker-4.jpg) |
| **V** | **Bicondylar** | **Spines continuous with shaft** | ![Schatzker V](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1044-schatzker-5.jpg) |
| **VI** | **Metaphyseal-diaphyseal dissociation** | Severe soft tissue | ![Schatzker VI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1044-schatzker-6.jpg) |

**FALSE (TO)** — "**type V = bicondylar wedges with metaphyseal-diaphyseal discontinuity**" — that is **VI**

| Hohl + Moore (fracture-dislocations; 10% don't fit Schatzker) | Pattern |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Coronal split** (medial, displaces distally; doesn't cross eminence) |
| **II** | **Entire condyle** (≈ Schatzker IV) |
| III | Rim avulsion, lateral plateau |
| IV | Rim compression |
| V | Four-part |

**3-column concept** — **medial, lateral, posterior** (CT) · captures **posterior** fractures Schatzker misses · guides approach

**Hyperextension varus bicondylar (HEVBTP)** — **loss / reversal of posterior slope** · posterior cortex tension failure, anterior compression, coronal varus · **popliteal artery 12%** (vs 1%), **peroneal nerve 16%**, compartment syndrome 12%, **PLC injury** · fix: **anteromedial buttress + graft anterior defect**\
✗ Wrong options: posteromedial corner · ↓ peroneal risk · posterior tibial artery

**Posterior column + varus** — **poor function + sports limitation**\
✗ Wrong options: low energy · highest meniscal injury · high satisfaction · Schatzker-classifiable

![Three-column concept](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1044-tibial-plateau-column-concept.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Tibial plateau fracture
├─ Minimally displaced, stable to varus / valgus → hinged brace, NWB / PWB 8–12 wk, early ROM
├─ Unicondylar lateral (I–III) → lateral approach, elevate + graft, lateral buttress plate / rafts
├─ Medial (IV) → ABI → posteromedial / medial buttress plate
├─ Bicondylar / VI, swollen or polytrauma → spanning ex-fix → CT → dual-incision dual plating
└─ > 65, osteoporotic, non-reconstructable → acute TKA
```

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **Articular depression / step-off** | **> 5–10 mm** (Orthobullets) · **> 3 mm** step-off (OB) — read the options |
| **Condylar widening** | **> 5 mm** |
| **Varus / valgus instability** | **> 10°** (in extension) |
| **Medial plateau fracture** | All |
| **Bicondylar** | All |

**Decision for minimally displaced fracture** — **physical exam (varus / valgus stability)** · may decide up to 2 wk\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · age · compensation

**Nonoperative** — minimally displaced split / depression, stable, non-ambulatory, unfit · PTB cast / immobiliser / **hinged brace** · **early passive ROM**

### Spanning external fixation — indications & effects

**Indication** — **axially / length-unstable** (**Schatzker VI with shortening**, bicondylar) or **knee subluxation** · significant swelling / **blisters**, polytrauma, open\
✗ Wrong options: I–III · minimally displaced IV

**Bicondylar VI + blisters next day** — **knee-spanning ex-fix**\
✗ Wrong options: splint / cast · ORIF

**Polytrauma, open femur + plateau, head + abdominal injury** — **ex-fix femur + plateau**, splint ankle + forearm\
✗ Wrong options: acute nailing / ORIF

**Technique** — **two 5 mm half-pins in femur + two in tibia**, outside future incisions · **ligamentotaxis** · lock in **slight flexion** · **wires > 14 mm from joint** (capsule / septic arthritis)

**Expected** — **transient ↑ compartment pressure** during placement · (✗ Wrong options: ↓ pulses · pulmonary injury · blocks wound access · ↑ deep infection / malunion)\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ compartment syndrome

**Downside** — **arthrofibrosis \~15% → return to OR** (MUA, quadricepsplasty, lysis) · **each day in frame ↑ odds 10%** · **CPM ↓ risk up to 70%**

**Posterior shear fracture, swollen, no skin wrinkles** — **ex-fix + realign now**, delayed posteromedial ORIF\
✗ Wrong options: immobiliser · fasciotomy without signs · prompt ORIF

### Lateral plateau fixation — buttress principle & constructs

**Partial articular (Schatzker II)** — **lateral plate + metaphyseal graft** · rafting subchondral screws\
✗ Wrong options: medial plate

**Good bone** — **undercontoured lateral nonlocking plate (buttress)** ± graft substitute · **osteopenic (63-yr, 15 mm depression) → still lateral nonlocking**\
✗ Wrong options: locking (no buttress in pure locking mode, + cost) · ex-fix · screws alone · dual plates

**Osteoporotic screw order** — **3.5 mm nonlocking (lag) first → then 3.5 mm locking** (compress / narrow rim, then hold elevation)\
✗ Wrong options: locking first · 6.5 mm screws

**Buttress technique** — **undercontoured plate + nonlocked screw near apex** of vertical fracture · resists axial load at **90° to deformity** · best for **simple partial articular / Schatzker I** (not depression, not metadiaphyseal)

**Split-depression (skiing)** — **ORIF with absolute stability**\
✗ Wrong options: relative · IMN · ex-fix · cast

| Plate function | Stability |
|---|---|
| **Buttress** | Resists axial / shear |
| Compression | Absolute |
| Neutralisation (with lag screw) | Absolute |
| Bridge | Relative |

![Buttress plate](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1044-buttress-moved.jpg)

### Bone void fillers — calcium phosphate cement

**Least subsidence + highest compressive strength** — **calcium phosphate cement** · osteoconductive, porous, biodegradable\
✗ Wrong options: ICBG · cancellous allograft · reamings · calcium sulfate (no structural strength) · tricalcium phosphate · hydroxyapatite · rhBMP-7

**Depression technique** — "open the book" or **cortical window + tamps** → fill void → rafts

### Bicondylar fractures — dual plating & posteromedial fragment

**Posteromedial fragment** — common; **lateral locking plate can't capture it** → **lateral + posteromedial plates via two incisions**\
✗ Wrong options: single lateral / polyaxial plate · single anterior approach (infection) · hybrid frame · IMN

**Bicondylar + blisters** — **ex-fix → delayed dual-incision dual plating**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral locked plate · single-incision dual plating · splint

**Posterolateral fragment** — must be **fixed separately** or fails\
✗ Wrong options: single plate · rafting screws · cement

**Hybrid ex-fix definitive (V / VI)** — **unacceptably high malunion / nonunion** · shorter surgery, ↑ superficial infection, faster early return, shorter stay

### Posteromedial approach — interval, positioning & buttress plating

**Interval (Lobenhoffer)** — **pes anserinus (sartorius, gracilis, semitendinosus) ↔ medial head of gastrocnemius** · semimembranosus ↔ medial gastrocnemius (OB) — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: superficial MCL · POL · soleus · popliteus

**Position** — supine figure-4 or **prone** (extensile posterior / posterolateral shear) · elevate popliteus subperiosteally · knee **extension + valgus** aids reduction

**At risk** — **saphenous nerve + vein**, **posterior tibial recurrent artery** (distal / lateral)

**Schatzker IV / medial condyle** — **medial (posteromedial) buttress plate, absolute stability**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral locking (shear failure) · medial bridge · lag screws alone (leg lever arm) · IMN · ex-fix

**Posteromedial shear (fall on flexed knee)** — **posteromedial approach + buttress plate**\
✗ Wrong options: direct medial · lateral · anteromedial · hybrid

**Posterior fragment only** — **posterior buttress plate**

![Posteromedial approach](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1044-posteromedial-approach.jpg)

### Other approaches

| Approach | Interval / fact |
|---|---|
| **Anterolateral** (commonest) | Hockey-stick over **Gerdy's**, elevate anterior compartment + ITB, **submeniscal arthrotomy** |
| **Posterolateral** | **Gastrocnemius-soleus ↔ peroneals** (TO; OB) · lateral gastrocnemius ↔ biceps, **CPN in plane** (OB) |
| Direct posterior | Medial ↔ lateral gastrocnemius — popliteal artery, small saphenous vein, CPN |
| Fibular neck osteotomy | Rare — NV risk |
| **Midline** | **Avoid** (stripping) |

### Elderly — acute arthroplasty

**Primary TKA > 65–70 yr, osteoporotic** — **earlier full weight bearing**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ implant failure (that's ORIF) · fewer complications than TKA for OA · worse than TKA after failed ORIF

### Infection risk & fixation timing

**Infection after ORIF** — **male, smoking, pulmonary disease, bicondylar, operative time > 3 h** · vancomycin powder ↓ deep infection\
✗ Wrong options: age · unicondylar · prior injury · mechanism

**Fixation vs fasciotomy closure timing** — **does not alter infection risk**

### Articular cartilage healing — early motion

**Best for surviving chondrocytes** — **gentle compressive loading via early ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: immobilisation · shear · distraction · glucosamine

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Rate / fact |
|---|---|
| **Post-traumatic arthritis** | **25–35%** · meniscectomy, malalignment > 5°, instability |
| **Conversion to TKA** | **3–7% at 10+ yr** (Orthobullets) · **5–10% at 10 yr** (OB) · **\~5× general population** (3–7×) — read the options |
| **Compartment syndrome** | **7–20%** · **young age**, high energy, **Schatzker IV** (Orthobullets) / **VI**, **V + VI** (OB), fibula fracture, plateau-shaft |
| Infection | **2–11%** (Orthobullets) · 6–12% (OB) |
| Nonunion / malunion | 2–4% · **Schatzker VI**, comminution |
| **Stiffness / arthrofibrosis** | **10–25%** · **\~15% after temporary ex-fix = commonest early complication** · MUA + lysis if < 90° flexion at 4 wk |
| Loss of reduction | 5–30% · e.g. posteromedial coronal fragment missed by lateral plate |
| DVT | Nonop 9%, op 6% |

### Vascular injury — medial / Schatzker IV patterns & ABI

**Schatzker IV** — **fracture-dislocation variant**: femur follows the **medial** fragment · **popliteal artery + peroneal nerve stretch** → **ABI** · ABI < 0.9 → CTA / MRA\
✗ Wrong options: compartment pressures · ex-fix before vascular check

**Highest vascular risk** — **Schatzker IV**\
✗ Wrong options: II · V · tibial shaft · pilon · subtrochanteric

**Schatzker IV — true** — **medial meniscus + ACL injuries common**\
✗ Wrong options: femur follows lateral plateau · lateral plate · bicondylar plating · vascular injury rare

### Outcomes & arthritis — alignment, stability, age, conversion to TKA

**Strongest predictor of long-term outcome** — **restoration of joint stability**\
✗ Wrong options: meniscal repair · surgery < 48 h · depression correction · diastasis

**Most ↑ arthritis** — **alteration of mechanical axis (> 5°)** · mild incongruity tolerated\
✗ Wrong options: step-off · type · male · age

**Age > 50** — **worse outcomes even in low-energy fractures**; only 35% satisfied · complication rates **don't differ** with age\
✗ Wrong options: older → more infection · younger → more nonunion

**Prognosis** — 1-yr mortality 5% · return to work 70–90% · ROM 10–145°

## X vs Y

| | **Schatzker II** | **Schatzker IV** |
|---|---|---|
| Side | Lateral split-depression | **Medial** |
| Meniscus | **Lateral** | **Medial** |
| Danger | — | **Popliteal artery, peroneal nerve, ACL** |
| Fixation | **Lateral buttress + graft** | **Medial / posteromedial buttress** |

| | **Lateral locking plate** | **Nonlocking buttress plate** |
|---|---|---|
| Buttress effect | **None** (pure locking) | **Yes** |
| Use | Comminution, osteoporosis (after lag screws) | **Partial articular, good bone** |

## How it's asked

**"Least subsidence filler?"** → **calcium phosphate cement**

**"Highest compressive strength filler?"** → **calcium phosphate**

**"Medial meniscus tear — which fracture?"** → **Schatzker IV**

**"Soft-tissue injury with lateral split-depression?"** → **peripheral lateral meniscus tear**

**"Radiographic sign of LM injury?"** → **6 mm joint widening**

**"Grade 3 Lachman + varus laxity + ER asymmetry — repair?"** → **LCL (PLC)**

**"Schatzker II — commonest associated injury?"** → **lateral meniscal tear**

**"Biggest LM tear risk factor?"** → **> 10 mm depression**

**"Meniscus tears — true?"** → **lateral, Schatzker II, divide meniscotibial ligaments**

**"ACL injured most in?"** → **Schatzker IV / VI**

**"Schatzker IV — true?"** → **medial meniscus + ACL injuries**

**"Repaired LM tear — 1-yr outcome?"** → **similar function**

**"Skiing — commonest injury pattern?"** → **peripheral lateral meniscus tear**

**"Schatzker FALSE (TO)?"** → **type V with metaphyseal-diaphyseal discontinuity**

**"69-yr pure lateral depression?"** → **Schatzker III**

**"Entire condyle fracture-dislocation?"** → **Moore II**

**"Hyperextension varus bicondylar — hallmark?"** → **loss / reversal of tibial slope**

**"Schatzker VI after ex-fix, good soft tissue?"** → **medial + lateral plates, two approaches**

**"Posteromedial fragment — why it matters?"** → **may need dual plating**

**"Most stable for bicondylar?"** → **lateral + posteromedial plating**

**"Bicondylar + blisters?"** → **ex-fix → delayed dual-incision dual plating**

**"Medial condylar split — construct?"** → **posteromedial buttress plate, absolute stability**

**"Posterior fragment only?"** → **posterior buttress plate**

**"Medial plateau fracture fixation?"** → **medial buttress plate**

**"Posteromedial interval?"** → **pes anserinus ↔ medial head of gastrocnemius**

**"Prone position fracture?"** → **posteromedial / posterolateral shear**

**"Buttress plating best for?"** → **Schatzker I / simple partial articular**

**"Schatzker II surgical plan?"** → **lateral plate + metaphyseal graft**

**"Schatzker II, 40-yr?"** → **lateral nonlocking plate ± graft substitute**

**"Osteoporotic screw sequence?"** → **nonlocking then locking 3.5 mm**

**"Buttress technique?"** → **undercontoured plate + nonlocked screw at apex**

**"Osteopenic Schatzker II, 15 mm depression?"** → **lateral nonlocking construct**

**"Split-depression treatment principle?"** → **ORIF with absolute stability**

**"Plate functioning as buttress?"** → **force at 90° to deformity axis**

**"Initial treatment, high-energy bicondylar?"** → **spanning ex-fix**

**"Polytrauma open plateau + femur?"** → **ex-fix femur + plateau**

**"Most benefit from temporary ex-fix?"** → **Schatzker VI with shortening**

**"Ex-fix sequela?"** → **transient compartment pressure rise**

**"Ex-fix duration ↑ risk of?"** → **return to OR (arthrofibrosis)**

**"Hybrid ex-fix definitive?"** → **high malunion / nonunion**

**"Vascular complications commonest?"** → **Schatzker IV**

**"ABI most indicated?"** → **Schatzker IV**

**"Schatzker IV + foot numbness after reduction?"** → **ABI**

**"Strongest long-term predictor?"** → **joint stability**

**"Most ↑ arthritis?"** → **mechanical axis alteration**

**"Age > 50 outcomes?"** → **worse in low-energy fractures**

**"Surgery decision, minimally displaced?"** → **physical exam**

**"Commonest complication within 6 months?"** → **arthrofibrosis**

**"Future TKA risk?"** → **\~5× general population**

**"Counselling — TKA at 10 yr?"** → **5–10%**

**"Posterior column varus fracture?"** → **poor function + sports limitation**

**"Elderly acute TKA advantage?"** → **earlier full weight bearing**

**"Failed dual plating — missed?"** → **posterolateral fragment**

**"Fixation timing vs fasciotomy closure?"** → **doesn't alter infection risk**

**"Infection risk factor?"** → **operative time > 3 h**

**"Young age associated with?"** → **compartment syndrome**

**"Help reconstructed chondrocytes?"** → **gentle compression via early ROM**

**"Medial plateau shape + position?"** → **concave, larger, more distal**

**"Lipohaemarthrosis (TO)?"** → **tibial plateau fracture**

**"Lipohaemarthrosis means?"** → **occult fracture**

**"Posterolateral approach plane (TO)?"** → **gastrocnemius-soleus ↔ peroneals**

# Tibial Shaft Fractures

*Trauma · 65 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest long-bone fracture** — **17% of lower-limb fractures** · 4% of Medicare fractures · M > F, bimodal · proximal ⅓ = 5–10% of shaft fractures · open \~20%

| Mechanism | Pattern | Fibula | Soft tissue |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Low energy, torsion** | **Spiral** | **Different level** | Less · **posterior malleolus** with spiral distal ⅓ |
| **High energy, direct** | **Wedge / short oblique**, comminuted | **Same level** | Severe (Tscherne II / III, open) |

**Amputation predictor** — **severity of muscle / soft-tissue injury**

### Anatomy

**Shaft** — triangular · **proximal canal centred laterally** (start point) · subcutaneous anteromedial crest · tubercle \~3 cm below joint · **tibia carries 80–85% of load**

| Compartment | Contents |
|---|---|
| Anterior | Tibialis anterior, EDL, EHL · deep peroneal n. |
| Lateral | Peroneus longus + brevis · superficial peroneal n. |
| Superficial posterior | Gastrocnemius, soleus, plantaris |
| Deep posterior | Popliteus, tibialis posterior, FDL, FHL · tibial n. |

![Compartments of the leg](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1045-compartments-of-leg.jpg)

### Associated injuries (posterior malleolus in spiral distal-third fractures)

**Spiral distal ⅓ (twisting)** — **posterior malleolus (Volkmann) fracture** — 25–60% · **CT ankle** (or ankle X-rays) before nailing · nail can displace it → **AP lag screw first** · may affect syndesmosis\
✗ Wrong options: MRI knee / hip · CT knee · calcaneal view · CT chest · arterial duplex

**Not associated** — vertical medial malleolus shear, Chaput, talus, Tillaux, knee dislocation · **PITFL intact** with posterior malleolus fracture

**Compartment syndrome** — **diaphyseal 8.1%** (proximal 1.6%, distal 1.4%) · **occurs in open fractures too** · check all 4

**Floating knee** — antegrade tibial nail + retrograde femoral nail

![Posterior malleolus on axial CT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1045-ct-axial-posterior-malleolus.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — deformity, blisters, wounds · **compartments** · DPN, SPN, sural, tibial, saphenous · DP + PT pulses (Doppler → CTA if absent)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — full-length AP + lateral tibia · **knee + ankle** AP / lateral / oblique · repeat after splinting

**CT** — plateau / plafond extension · **distal ⅓ or spiral → exclude posterior malleolus** · nonunion

### Classifications

| OTA 42 | Pattern |
|---|---|
| A | Simple |
| B | Wedge |
| C | Complex / comminuted |

| Tscherne (closed soft tissue) | Injury |
|---|---|
| 0 | Indirect, negligible |
| I | Superficial abrasion, simple fracture |
| II | Deep abrasion, contusion, **impending compartment syndrome** |
| III | Crush, degloving, muscle destruction, **compartment syndrome, vessel / nerve rupture** |

| Gustilo-Anderson | Wound / soft tissue |
|---|---|
| **I** | **< 1 cm, clean**, simple |
| **II** | **> 1 cm**, no extensive damage |
| **IIIA** | High energy / comminuted / segmental; usually > 5 cm; **coverage adequate** · **1 cm wound with high-energy comminution = IIIA** |
| **IIIB** | **Flap needed** (STSG doesn't count) · proximal ⅓ **gastrocnemius**, middle ⅓ **soleus**, **distal ⅓ free flap** |
| **IIIC** | **Vascular injury needing repair** |
| Always III | Barnyard, shotgun / high-velocity GSW, > 24 h to treatment |

**Exposed bone, dopplerable pulses, peroneal numbness** — **IIIB → spanning ex-fix, delayed fixation + coverage**\
✗ Wrong options: immediate plating (infection up to 44%)

![Gustilo IIIB](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1045-grade3b-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Tibial shaft fracture
├─ Closed, low energy, acceptable alignment → long leg cast → PTB brace ~4 wk
├─ Most → reamed, statically locked IM nail
│      ├─ proximal ⅓ → lateral start, semi-extended / suprapatellar, blocking screws
│      └─ distal ⅓ → fibular plating, center-center guidewire
├─ Open → antibiotics < 3 h, I&D < 12–24 h, nail or ex-fix
└─ Polytrauma / contaminated → ex-fix → nail when resuscitated (ideally < 7 d)
```

### Nonoperative management — deformity & contraindications

| Acceptable alignment (cast / functional brace) | Limit |
|---|---|
| Varus-valgus | **< 5°** |
| Anterior-posterior | **< 10°** |
| Cortical apposition | **> 50%** |
| **Shortening** | **< 1 cm** |
| Rotation | **< 10°** |

**Contraindication to brace** — **shortening > 10 mm** (24 mm comminuted) · open, intra-articular, non-compliant\
✗ Wrong options: same-level fibula · transverse · 9° sagittal · 7° rotation

**Cast risk** — **shortening hardest to control** (oblique / comminuted; mean 4 mm) · angulation + rotation held well · nonunion \~1% (Sarmiento 1.1%) · union \~18 wk

**Intact fibula** — **varus malunion (\~25%)** — fibula = strut · relative contraindication to bracing

**Nonop most common deformity** — **varus**\
✗ Wrong options: valgus + procurvatum (proximal ⅓ nailing)

**IM nail vs cast EXCEPT** — "**↓ compartment syndrome**" — nailing may ↑ it (11%, Finkemeier) · nail: faster union, ↓ malunion, ↓ shortening, quicker return to work

### Operative criteria

| Option | Indications |
|---|---|
| **IM nail** | Unacceptable closed alignment, soft tissue won't tolerate cast, **segmental, comminuted, floating knee, polytrauma, bilateral, morbid obesity** · union > 80% closed |
| **Ex-fix** | **Damage control**, contaminated / soft-tissue defect, metaphyseal |
| **Plate** | Proximal / distal with poor nail fixation, **adjacent implant (TKA)** |
| **Amputation** (relative) | **Severe soft tissue, warm ischaemia > 6 h, severe ipsilateral foot trauma** |

### Open fractures — classification, antibiotics & infection

**Antibiotics** — **within 3 h** · **cefazolin for 24 h after definitive surgery (I, II, IIIA)** · **+ aminoglycoside IIIB** (Orthobullets) / **type III** (OB) — read the options · + **penicillin** farm (Clostridium) · **tetanus**

**Type III regimen** — **1st-gen cephalosporin (cell wall) + aminoglycoside (30S, bactericidal)**\
✗ Wrong options: 50S macrolide / clindamycin · tetracycline (bacteriostatic 30S) · fluoroquinolone alone (water contamination) · vancomycin alone

**Most important to ↓ infection** — **early antibiotic administration**\
✗ Wrong options: debridement < 6 h · immediate internal fixation · antibiotic irrigation · high-pressure lavage · reamed nail · VAC · tetanus · 9 L irrigation

**Second (after antibiotics, LEAP)** — **time to transfer to definitive trauma centre**\
✗ Wrong options: ex-fix · debridement < 6 h · coverage < 48 h

**I&D** — within **12–24 h** · remove all dead tissue + cortical bone · primary closure if clean, no tension · NPWT doesn't ↓ infection

**Persistent infection with retained implants** — strongest predictor **open fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: smoking · Pseudomonas

### Limb salvage vs amputation (LEAP)

**Most important in salvage vs amputation** — **severity of soft-tissue injury**\
✗ Wrong options: plantar sensation · bone loss · education · contralateral injury

**Insensate foot** — **not** an indication · **55% normal plantar sensation at 2 yr** in both groups

**LEAP** — **amputation = salvage** at 2 + 7 yr (function, psychosocial, return to work) · **depression at 3 months → worse outcome**\
✗ Wrong options: plantar sensation · nail · free vs rotational flap · number of debridements

**METALS (military)** — **amputation better function** than salvage · civilians: equal or slight benefit to amputation (OUTLET) · disability common either way

### Soft-tissue coverage — flaps

| Zone | Flap | Supply |
|---|---|---|
| **Proximal ⅓** | **Medial gastrocnemius** rotation (larger, no CPN risk) | **Medial sural artery** (popliteal branch) |
| **Middle ⅓** | **Soleus** rotation | — |
| **Distal ⅓** | **Free flap** (latissimus, rectus, gracilis) or reverse sural | — |

**Staged ORIF + free flap** — **IIIB intra-articular distal tibia**\
✗ Wrong options: midshaft · proximal Schatzker I · IIIC

### Reamed vs unreamed nailing & reaming hazards

| | **Closed** | **Open** |
|---|---|---|
| **Reamed** | **↓ time to union, ↓ reoperation** (SPRINT) · larger nail | **Equivalent** to unreamed |
| Unreamed | ↑ grafting, dynamisation, exchange · ↑ **locking screw breakage** | Same union, infection, reoperation |

**Unreamed in open** — **no difference** · not an adverse factor · surgery maybe shorter · pulmonary benefit unproven\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ nonunion · ↑ / ↓ infection

**Adverse outcome (open tibia nail)** — **high energy, stainless steel nail, gap > 1 cm, smoking**\
✗ Wrong options: unreamed nail

**Biology** — **mineral apposition similar** reamed vs unreamed · **reamed → most new bone** · **limited reaming → lowest porosity** · **tight nails ↓ cortical reperfusion** · over-ream **1–1.5 mm**

**Massive necrosis after reaming** — **over-reaming**\
✗ Wrong options: tourniquet · SLE · steroids · hyperflexion

**Tourniquet during reaming** — **no thermal necrosis** · with reamed femoral nailing in polytrauma → **↑ pulmonary morbidity** (reperfusion + emboli) · ↑ post-op pain

### Nail start point & malalignment (proximal & distal fractures)

**Start point** — **medial border of lateral tibial eminence** (AP) · just anterior to articular margin (lateral) · proximal ⅓ → **more lateral + proximal** (on lateral eminence) · **lateral knee X-ray** checks nail not proud

**Proximal ⅓ deformity** — **valgus + procurvatum (apex anterior)** — **patellar tendon extends proximal fragment**, **pes anserinus → valgus**, hamstrings / gastrocnemius flex distal · **medial start point → valgus** · malunion up to 50%

**Prevent** — **semi-extended / suprapatellar** (↓ procurvatum), **lateral start**, **blocking (Poller) screws in concavity: posterior in proximal fragment + lateral in metaphysis** (+25% stability), unicortical plate, lateral parapatellar

**Distal ⅓** — **valgus** malalignment with infrapatellar nail vs plate · guidewire **center-center** at physeal scar · nail tip slightly lateral to talar centre · aids: **fibular plating**, clamps, Poller screw, small plate\
✗ Wrong options: **syndesmotic fixation**

**Distal ⅓ extra-articular + fibula fracture** — **reamed IMN + fibular plating**\
✗ Wrong options: tibial + fibular plates (wounds) · nail without fibula (valgus, late loss)

**Malrotation** — **highest after nailing distal ⅓** · check: perfect lateral knee, rotate C-arm **105–110°** → ankle mortise

![Posterior blocking screw](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1045-posterior-blocking-screw.jpg)

### Anterior knee pain — infrapatellar vs suprapatellar nailing

**Commonest complication of nailing (TO)** — **anterior knee pain** — **57%** (TO) · **50–75%** (> 70% at 14 yr) (OB) · > 30–50% (Orthobullets) · \~20% (OB) — read the options · activity-related, kneeling · only 10% can't work

**Tendon-splitting vs paratendinous** — Keating 77% vs 50% · **Toivanen: no clinically relevant difference** (67% vs 71%)

| | **Suprapatellar (semi-extended)** | **Infrapatellar** |
|---|---|---|
| **Alignment** | **Better**, **↓ procurvatum + valgus** | More flexion → procurvatum |
| Anterior knee pain | **Less** (OB) · no difference (OB) | **More** |
| Septic knee, nail size, ROM, quads, PF disease, op time | **No difference** | No difference |
| Fluoroscopy | Easier | — |
| Use | **Proximal / distal ⅓** | Midshaft |

### Percutaneous plating — SPN risk & radiation

**LISS / long lateral plate** — **superficial peroneal nerve** at **holes 11–13** (13-hole) → **dorsal foot / midfoot numbness**, motor normal · SPN exits fascia **\~8 cm above lateral malleolus**\
✗ Wrong options: CPN · sural · tibial · compartment syndrome

**Plate vs IMN (distal ⅓)** — plate: **↑ radiation**, **↓ angular deformity**, larger incision, harder removal · nail: **↑ pain**, shorter surgery · same union, infection, wound breakdown, prominent hardware (OB)

### Biological adjuncts — BMP-2 / BMP-7

| BMP | Approval / fact |
|---|---|
| **rhBMP-2** | **FDA: acute open tibial shaft fracture + IM nail** · **↓ secondary bone grafting** (IIIA / IIIB), faster healing, ↓ hardware failure, ↓ infection (controversial) · not for nonunion |
| **rhBMP-7 (OP-1)** | **= autograft for tibial nonunion** (RCT) · **HDE** for recalcitrant long-bone nonunion |
| BMP-3 | **No osteoinductive activity** |

### Timing of definitive fixation in polytrauma

**Convert ex-fix → nail** — **as soon as resuscitated: lactate < 4.0 mmol/L + base excess > −5.5** (day 3, lactate 2.0, BE −2.5 ✓) · ex-fix **> 14 d** (> 15 d, Orthobullets) → **↑ infection** · ideally **< 7 d** (> 8 d ↑ infection, OB)

### External fixator pins — capsular reflection & septic arthritis

**Knee capsule** — up to **14 mm below subchondral line (posterolateral)** → pins **> 14 mm** · too proximal → **septic arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: foot drop · anterior tibial artery · contracture

**Frame stiffness** — **larger pins, more pins each side, rods closer to bone, multiplanar**

**Definitive ex-fix (type III)** — **slower union, worse function, more malalignment** than nail · pin infection

### Ballistic tibia fractures

**GSW with exposed bone** — **treat as open** · infection + soft-tissue reconstruction **similar to blunt open** · **↑ compartment syndrome + reoperation vs blunt closed**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Rate / fact |
|---|---|
| **Anterior knee pain** | **Commonest after nailing** · proud nail (lateral X-ray) · nail removal unpredictable |
| Malunion | 8–10% · **proximal ⅓ up to 50%** · nonop → varus |
| **Nonunion** | **2–10%** · **no healing at 9 months** · open, < 50% cortical contact, transverse, gap |
| Malrotation | Distal ⅓ nailing → ankle arthrosis |
| Compartment syndrome | 1–9% · closed + open |
| Infection | \~5% · open, contamination, delayed cover |
| Nerve | SPN (LISS), saphenous (locking screws), **transient peroneal palsy** after nailing, DPN (posterolateral ex-fix pins) |

### Peroneal nerve palsy after nailing

**Transient peroneal neurapraxia (up to 5%)** — **↓ EHL strength** + 1st web numbness (DPN) · improves by 3 months\
✗ Wrong options: lateral hindfoot (sural) · Achilles reflex (S1) · peroneus longus (SPN) · plantar (tibial)

### Nonunion — risk factors, exchange nailing & fibular ostectomy

**Greatest nonunion risk after nailing** — **postoperative fracture gap**\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs · fibula fracture · smoking · mechanism

**Delayed union** — 20–26 wk · **nonunion** — > 9 months or no progress for 3 consecutive months · **4 months with healing signs → observe ≥ 6 months**\
✗ Wrong options: exchange now

**First step** — **rule out infection (ESR, CRP)** — then CT / vit D\
✗ Wrong options: CT · vit D · stimulator · exchange

**Reamed exchange nailing** — **aseptic diaphyseal tibial nonunion** · **hypertrophic** (85–90%) · **< 30% cortical bone loss** (76–96%) · **oblique** best · bent nail, no bone loss → exchange\
✗ Wrong options: circular frame first · ICBG alone · cast / brace

**Exchange nailing poor for** — **infection**, **segmental bone loss / 4 cm defect**, **atrophic** (needs graft / BMP), **metadiaphyseal** (plate), comminuted (45–80%), humerus (30–40%), distal femur (50–60%)

**Mid-diaphyseal nonunion after reamed 10 mm nail (10 months)** — **exchange nailing**\
✗ Wrong options: adjunct plate (metaphyseal / hypertrophic) · nail removal + plate (metadiaphyseal) · fibulectomy + cast · rhBMP-2

**Intact / healed fibula holding tibia apart** — **fibular osteotomy / partial ostectomy**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · solid nail same size · BMP-3 · Masquelet

**After ex-fix (IIIB, near stiff TKA), oligotrophic** — **compression plating** (92–96%)\
✗ Wrong options: MUA · cast + stimulator · ex-fix · IMN

**Bone loss** — posterolateral graft · defects **up to 5–6 cm** graftable

### Malunion & deformity correction (CORA, ankle effects)

**Distal ⅓ varus 25°** — **ipsilateral ankle pain + stiffness**\
✗ Wrong options: spine · hip · knee

**CORA** — **intersection of proximal + distal mechanical (≈ anatomical) axes** in tibia\
✗ Wrong options: concave / convex cortex point

**Osteotomy away from apex** — **secondary translation** deformity

**Early malalignment after nailing** — return to OR: remove nail, reduce, renail · late → osteotomy

### Late equinus & claw toes

**Checkrein deformity (TO)** — after **distal ⅓ tibia fracture** · **FHL adheres to callus** → **hallux flexes with ankle dorsiflexion**, extends with plantarflexion\
✗ Wrong options: FHL tear · IP fracture · tethering at MTP

**Claw toes flexible in PF, rigid in DF + equinus not relieved by knee flexion** — **TA lengthening + release / retromalleolar lengthening of FDL + FHL**\
✗ Wrong options: PT · wait · tibialis posterior transfer (normal heel rise) · IP fusion

![Proximal-third malunion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1045-proximal-third-tibia-malunion.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Proximal ⅓** | **Midshaft** | **Distal ⅓** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Nail deformity | **Valgus + procurvatum** | Rare | **Valgus**, malrotation |
| Trick | Lateral start, semi-extended, Poller | Standard | Fibular plate, center-center wire |
| Flap | Gastrocnemius | Soleus | Free |
| Associated | Plateau extension | — | **Posterior malleolus** (spiral) |

| | **Hypertrophic nonunion** | **Atrophic nonunion** |
|---|---|---|
| Problem | **Mechanical** | **Biological** |
| Treatment | **Exchange nailing** / stability | **Graft / BMP** + stability |

## How it's asked

**"Commonest complication of reamed nailing (TO)?"** → **knee pain**

**"Most common complication, closed nailing?"** → **anterior knee pain**

**"Knee pain at union after reamed nail?"** → **50–75%**

**"Suprapatellar technique advantage?"** → **improved alignment**

**"Infrapatellar vs suprapatellar — true?"** → **more anterior knee pain with infrapatellar**

**"Suprapatellar effect?"** → **↓ procurvatum**

**"Semi-extended prevents?"** → **procurvatum**

**"Distal ⅓ nail vs plate — malalignment?"** → **valgus**

**"Check nail not proud?"** → **lateral knee X-ray**

**"Medial start point, proximal ⅓?"** → **valgus malunion**

**"Standard start in proximal ⅓?"** → **valgus, apex anterior**

**"Distal tibia reduction aids EXCEPT?"** → **syndesmotic fixation**

**"Plate vs IMN advantage?"** → **less angular deformity**

**"Start point?"** → **medial border of lateral tibial eminence**

**"Reaming — true?"** → **similar mineral apposition**

**"Reamed vs unreamed closed?"** → **decreased time to union**

**"Tourniquet + reamed femur nail?"** → **↑ pulmonary morbidity**

**"Open fracture reamed vs unreamed?"** → **equivalent**

**"Unreamed in open fracture?"** → **no difference**

**"NOT an adverse factor?"** → **unreamed nail**

**"Unreamed vs reamed?"** → **similar union in open fractures**

**"Massive necrosis after reaming?"** → **over-reaming**

**"Greatest nonunion risk?"** → **post-op fracture gap**

**"Best for reamed exchange nailing?"** → **diaphyseal tibia, < 30% bone loss**

**"Oligotrophic nonunion after ex-fix + flap?"** → **compression plating**

**"Exchange nailing without graft?"** → **hypertrophic tibial shaft nonunion**

**"Isolated exchange nailing?"** → **hypertrophic diaphyseal nonunion**

**"Highest union with exchange nailing?"** → **oblique tibial shaft**

**"4 months, partial healing?"** → **observe**

**"10-month diaphyseal nonunion, normal labs?"** → **exchange nailing**

**"Bent nail, nonunion?"** → **exchange nailing**

**"24 wk, intact fibula?"** → **fibular osteotomy**

**"10-month pain, hypertrophic callus — first?"** → **inflammatory markers**

**"Equivalent to autograft for nonunion?"** → **BMP-7**

**"rhBMP-2 at fixation?"** → **↓ subsequent bone grafting**

**"Adjunct improving outcome with nail?"** → **rhBMP-2**

**"rhBMP-2 FDA indication?"** → **acute open tibia + IM nail**

**"FDA adjunct for open tibia?"** → **rhBMP-2**

**"Decreases infection?"** → **immediate antibiotics**

**"Classification + treatment, exposed bone?"** → **IIIB, ex-fix, delayed fixation + cover**

**"1 cm wound, high-energy comminution?"** → **IIIA**

**"Greatest effect on infection (transfer case)?"** → **time to definitive trauma centre**

**"Reduces infection MOST?"** → **early IV antibiotics**

**"Persistent infection predictor?"** → **open fracture**

**"Type III antibiotics?"** → **cephalosporin + aminoglycoside (bactericidal)**

**"Pin too proximal → risk?"** → **septic arthritis**

**"Capsular reflection?"** → **14 mm**

**"Salvage vs amputation decider?"** → **soft-tissue injury severity**

**"Worse long-term outcome predictor?"** → **depression at 3 months**

**"IIIB with insensate foot — true?"** → **soft-tissue severity predicts amputation**

**"Military vs civilian amputation?"** → **military better after amputation**

**"Nail vs cast EXCEPT?"** → **↓ compartment syndrome**

**"Oblique distal ⅓ in cast — risk?"** → **shortening**

**"Varus malunion in cast?"** → **intact fibula**

**"Nonop commonest deformity?"** → **varus**

**"Bracing contraindication?"** → **shortening 24 mm**

**"Spiral distal ⅓ — associated?"** → **posterior malleolus fracture**

**"Twisting injury — order before nailing?"** → **ankle radiographs**

**"Twisting distal tibia — imaging?"** → **CT ankle**

**"High-energy twisting — associated fragment?"** → **Volkmann**

**"Dorsal foot numbness after MIPO?"** → **superficial peroneal nerve**

**"Long lateral plate → numbness?"** → **dorsal midfoot**

**"MIPO pin location injuring nerve?"** → **distal plate holes (11–13)**

**"Plate vs IMN?"** → **more radiation**

**"Distal varus 25° → risk?"** → **ankle pain + stiffness**

**"CORA?"** → **proximal + distal mechanical axes meet**

**"Osteotomy away from apex?"** → **translation**

**"Transient peroneal palsy after nailing?"** → **↓ EHL strength**

**"Staged ORIF + free flap?"** → **IIIB intra-articular distal tibia**

**"Proximal ⅓ gastrocnemius flap supply?"** → **medial sural artery**

**"Distal ⅓ + fibula fracture?"** → **reamed IMN + fibular plating**

**"Comminuted C2 distal tibia, diabetic?"** → **ankle-spanning ex-fix**

**"When to convert ex-fix?"** → **day 3, lactate 2.0, BE −2.5**

**"Ballistic tibia — true?"** → **treat as open if bone exposed**

**"Checkrein deformity (TO)?"** → **distal third tibia fracture**

**"Claw toes + equinus after distal tibia?"** → **TAL + FDL / FHL release**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Trauma Scoring Systems

*Trauma · 66 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Purpose & types

**Uses** — triage, outcome prediction / counselling, quality assurance, research, reimbursement

| Type | Scores |
|---|---|
| **Physiological** | **RTS**, APACHE, SOFA, **SIRS**, Emergency Trauma Score |
| **Anatomical** | **AIS**, **ISS**, **NISS**, Anatomic Profile, PATI, ICISS, TMPM-ICD9 |
| **Combined** | **TRISS**, ASCOT, ICISS, **Ganga Hospital Open Injury Score** |

![Classification of trauma scores](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1055-classifications-of-trauma-scores.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Glasgow Coma Scale

| Score | **Motor (6)** | **Verbal (5)** | **Eyes (4)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6 | Obeys | — | — |
| 5 | Localises pain | Oriented | — |
| 4 | Normal flexion (withdraws) | Confused | Spontaneous |
| 3 | **Abnormal flexion (decorticate)** | Inappropriate words | To speech |
| 2 | **Extension (decerebrate)** | Incomprehensible sounds | To pain |
| 1 | None | None | None |

**Brain injury** — **severe < 9 (≤ 8)** · moderate 9–12 · minor ≥ 13 · range **3–15**

**Limits** — ignores focal / lateralising signs, metabolic processes, intoxication

### Revised trauma score

| Code | GCS | Systolic BP | Resp. rate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **4** | **13–15** | **> 90** | **10–29** |
| 3 | 9–12 | 76–89 | **> 30** |
| 2 | 6–8 | 50–75 | 6–9 |
| 1 | 4–5 | 1–49 | 1–5 |
| 0 | 3 | 0 | 0 |

**RTS** — **GCS + SBP + RR** codes · **most widely used pre-hospital triage tool** · **lower score = more severe** · **RTS < 4 → transfer to trauma centre**

**RTS — true (TO)** — **improves appropriateness of treatment**\
✗ Wrong options: improves diagnostic / investigation error rate (no difference) · higher score = more severe · includes respiratory effort + capillary refill (those were in the old Trauma Score, removed in RTS)

**RTS limits** — **underestimates single-system injury** (may delay referral) · best for predicting survival in **head injury** (TO)

![Revised trauma score](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1055-revised-trauma-score.jpg)

### Injury Severity Score calculation (ISS, MISS)

| AIS grade | Severity |
|---|---|
| 1 | Minor (laceration, contusion) |
| 2 | Moderate (**large joint dislocation**) |
| 3 | Severe, not life-threatening (**femoral shaft, open pelvic fracture**) |
| 4 | Severe, life-threatening, survival probable |
| 5 | Critical, survival uncertain (**crushed larynx**) |
| 6 | **Maximal / unsurvivable** (crushed head) |

**ISS** — **sum of squares of the highest AIS in the 3 most severely injured body regions** (A² + B² + C²) · range **1–75** · **any AIS 6 → 75** · first anatomical scoring system

**Body regions** — **6** (TO; OB: head, face, chest, abdomen, extremities + pelvis, external) · **9 anatomic regions** (Orthobullets) — read the options

**Only the highest AIS per region** — **bilateral femur fractures count once** → NISS fixes this

**Polytrauma threshold** — **ISS > 15** (major trauma; **10% mortality**) (Orthobullets, TO, OB) · **ISS ≥ 18** polytrauma / transfer (OB) — read the options

| Worked example | ISS |
|---|---|
| **Chest 4, liver 4, open tibia 3**, radius 2, scalp 1 (TO) | 16 + 16 + 9 = **41** |
| Open chest 4, colon 4, femur 3, shoulder 2, thyroid 1 | **41** |
| Subdural 4, spleen 4, pelvis / femur 3, nose 2, ribs 2 | **41** |
| **Bilateral femur 3 + abdomen 3 + face 2** + lung 2 | 9 + 9 + 4 = **22** |

**NISS** — **3 highest AIS regardless of region** → better for **severe single-region injury**; **more predictive of complications + mortality**

**ISS limits** — hard to calculate in resus · misses multiple injuries in one region

**MISS (paediatric)** — **5 regions** (neurological, face / neck, chest, abdomen / pelvic contents, extremities / pelvic girdle) · graded 1–5 · same sum-of-squares · **MISS < 40 → no deaths**; > 40 → 50%; > 50 → 75%

**MISS example** — chest 3 + abdomen 2 + face / neck 4 (GCS 14 → neuro grade 1, excluded) → 9 + 4 + 16 = **29**

### Mangled Extremity Severity Score

| MESS component | Grade |
|---|---|
| **Skeletal / soft-tissue injury** | **1–4** (low → very high energy) |
| **Limb ischaemia** | **1–3** (reduced pulse / pulseless / cool, paralysed, insensate) — **doubled if > 6 h** |
| **Shock** | **0–2** (normotensive / transient / persistent hypotension) |
| **Age** | **0–2** (< 30 / 30–50 / > 50) |

**NOT in MESS** — **neurological status** (TO) · **time from admission to surgery** (OB)

**Cut-off** — **≥ 7** (TO; original designers) · **> 7** (Orthobullets; OB) predicts amputation — read the options

**Accuracy** — **high specificity, low sensitivity** (lower scores may still need amputation) · not the sole criterion · MESS more accurate than LSI · doesn't predict function after salvage

### SIRS

| Criterion (1 point each; **≥ 2 = SIRS**) | Threshold |
|---|---|
| Heart rate | **> 90** |
| WBC | **< 4,000 or > 12,000** |
| Resp. rate | **> 20** or PaCO₂ < 32 mmHg |
| Temperature | **< 36 or > 38 °C** |

**SIRS** — ↑ cytokines, complement, hormones · likely genetic predisposition · → **DIC, ARDS, renal failure, MOF, shock**

## 6 Complications

### Reconstruction vs amputation outcomes

**Reconstruction vs amputation at 2 yr (LEAP)** — **reconstruction → higher rehospitalisation**\
✗ Wrong options: better return to work · better SIP · psychosocial SIP improves · better if no high school

**Sickness Impact Profile** — **12 categories** (sleep, eating, work, home, recreation; physical: ambulation, body care, movement; psychosocial: social, alertness, emotion, communication) · **psychosocial subscale doesn't improve with time**

### Sex differences in polytrauma outcomes

**Men vs premenopausal women, ≥ 10 yr after polytrauma** — **men: higher QoL scores** · **women: ↑ PTSD, ↑ sick-leave days** · screen for **depression**\
✗ Wrong options: men more absentee days · CRPS · psychiatric care · amputation

## X vs Y

| | **ISS** | **NISS** | **MISS** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Regions | 6 (or 9) | Any | **5 (paediatric)** |
| Scores used | **Highest per region**, top 3 regions | **Top 3 AIS anywhere** | Top 3 regions |
| Strength | Standard | **Severe single-region injury** | Children |

| | **RTS** | **ISS** |
|---|---|---|
| Type | **Physiological** | **Anatomical** |
| Use | **Field triage** | Research, outcome |
| Direction | **Low = severe** | **High = severe** |

## How it's asked

**"ISS: chest 4, liver 4, tibia 3, radius 2, scalp 1 (TO)?"** → **41**

**"Child MISS: chest 3, abdomen 2, face / neck 4?"** → **29**

**"ISS: chest 4, colon 4, femur 3, shoulder 2, thyroid 1?"** → **41**

**"ISS: subdural 4, spleen 4, femur 3, pelvis 3…?"** → **41**

**"ISS: bilateral femurs 3, abdomen 3, face 2, lung 2?"** → **22**

**"Not in MESS (TO)?"** → **neurological status**

**"MESS EXCEPT?"** → **time from admission to surgery**

**"MESS 9 — specificity / sensitivity?"** → **high specificity, low sensitivity**

**"Reconstruction vs amputation at 2 yr?"** → **higher rehospitalisation**

**"Men vs women after polytrauma?"** → **men higher QoL**

**"RTS — true (TO)?"** → **improves appropriateness of treatment**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Wound & Hardware Infection

*Trauma · 67 / 67*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest complication of orthopaedic trauma surgery** — **up to 16%** after fracture (Orthobullets) · ≥ 10% after severe injury, **< 2% after closed elective surgery** (TO) · SSI 11.8 / 100 patients (TO)

**Risk factors** — **host immunodeficiency**, extremes of age, diabetes, obesity, alcohol, **tobacco**, steroids, malnutrition, radiation, vascular insufficiency

### Biofilm — glycocalyx & quorum sensing

**Seeding** — soft-tissue, vessel + periosteal disruption lets bacteria evade defences · direct seeding of implant · haematogenous

**Biofilm** — depends on **exopolysaccharide glycocalyx** · forms **within \~4 weeks** · resists antibiotics → chronic (> 4 wk) → **explant + antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: osteoclast differentiation (RANK-RANKL) · metastasis · endochondral / intramembranous bone

| Biofilm stage | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **1 Adhesion** | Individual bacteria attach to substrate (adhesins) |
| **2 Maturation** | **Quorum sensing** (cell-to-cell signalling) → **activates virulence-factor genes**, ↑ resistance (✗ Wrong options: creates planktonic bacteria · mediates adhesion · ↓ resistance) |

**Planktonic bacteria** — individual free-floating

### Shoulder surgery infection — C. acnes

**Indolent infection after arthroscopic acromioplasty + mini-open cuff repair** — **Propionibacterium (Cutibacterium) acnes** · arthroscopy fluid may dilute skin prep before mini-open\
✗ Wrong options: E. coli · Strep. viridans · Enterococcus

## 3 Presentation

**History** — soft-tissue + bone injury, hardware, previous surgery / infection at site

**Symptoms** — **pain at old fracture site** (infected nonunion) ± fever, chills, night sweats

**Exam** — erythema, **drainage / purulence**, tenderness · **gross motion = nonunion**

### Surgical site infection — definition & time window (TO)

**SSI (TO)** — infection **within 30 days** of surgery, or **within 1 year with an implant**, at incision or deep tissue

**NOT an SSI (TO)** — **no instrumentation, 3 months**\
✗ Wrong options: implant at 3 or 6 months · no implant at 1 month (all are SSIs)

| SSI type (TO) | Features |
|---|---|
| **Superficial incisional** | Pus from superficial incision; pain, swelling, redness, heat |
| **Deep incisional / organ-space** | **30 d (1 yr with implant)** · pus from deep incision, **spontaneous dehiscence**, fever > 38 °C |

**HAI (TO)** — not present / incubating on admission; most appear **> 48 h** after admission · up to 7% (developed) / 10% (developing) of inpatients

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** (biplanar ± 45° obliques) | **Normal early** · subacute / chronic: **peri-implant lucency**, **involucrum** (reactive new bone), **sequestrum** (dead infected bone) |
| CT | Pre-operative planning |
| **MRI** | Extent, abscess · **98% sensitive, 78% specific** · metal artefact |
| **WBC-labelled scan** | **Infection around hardware** (MRI artefact) · distinguishes mimics |

![Involucrum](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1053-involucrum.jpg)

![Sequestrum](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob1053-sequestrum.jpg)

### Labs & cultures

**WBC** — may be normal (chronic, indolent)

**ESR** — stays ↑ for **months** after injury / surgery without infection · normalises **slowly** — poor marker of cure · normal: men < 50 yr 0–15, women 0–20 mm/h · ↑ pregnancy, renal disease, cancer, anaemia · ↓ sickle cell, leukaemia, CHF, hyperviscosity

**Cultures** — **in-office swabs / sinus-tract aspirates unreliable** · **intra-operative deep cultures (multiple sites) most reliable**

### CRP / ESR kinetics after surgery & infection

**CRP after fracture fixation** — **peaks by 48 h (day 2), then falls** · **rising / not falling after 48 h → infection or haematoma** · **most predictive marker in first post-op week** (> erythema, serous drainage, serial ESR)\
✗ Wrong options: 24 h · 96 h · 7 d

**Cure after treated wound infection** — **normal CRP (< 3 mg/L)** even if ESR still raised (CRP 1, ESR 25 ✓) · CRP tracks clinical response

## 5 Management

```
Infection after fixation
├─ Early (< ~4 wk), deep, fracture unhealed, implant stable → I&D + retain implant + culture-specific antibiotics until union
├─ Unfit for surgery / unhealed, high surgical risk → antibiotic suppression (32% fail)
├─ Chronic (> 4 wk biofilm) or healed fracture → debride + remove hardware + antibiotics
└─ Loose / failed implant → remove, stabilise (ex-fix), staged revision
```

### Early postoperative infection — debridement with implant retention

**Purulent drainage probing to plate, 4 wk after ankle ORIF** — **I&D + keep fixation + culture-specific antibiotics until union**\
✗ Wrong options: oral / IV suppression without surgery · remove implants + cast (instability, malunion / nonunion) · office culture + suppression

**Success** — **71%** for early acute post-op infection with debridement + antibiotics · **failure risk: IM nail, open fracture**

**Debridement** — any active infection · keep hardware if removal threatens stability · thorough excision · deep bone samples for **culture + histology**

**Irrigation** — **low-pressure normal saline** may be best during debridement (Orthobullets) · **povidone-iodine before closure; saline not recommended** for SSI prevention (TO, WHO) — read the options

**Suppression** — when surgical risk > benefit (immunosuppressed, elderly) or **infected unhealed fracture** · monitor ESR + CRP · **32% persist / worsen**

### SSI prevention (TO, WHO)

| Measure | Recommendation |
|---|---|
| Skin prep | **Chlorhexidine-alcohol**, concentric circles outward |
| Hair | **Don't remove**; if needed **clippers**, never shave |
| **Antibiotic prophylaxis** | **Within 120 min before incision (ideally 30–60 min)** |
| Temperature | **Warm** — hypothermia < 36 °C; start ≥ 30 min before induction (NICE) |
| Glucose / fluids | Tight perioperative glucose · goal-directed normovolaemia |
| Drapes | Woven or non-woven; **plastic adhesive incise drapes not recommended** |
| **Laminar flow** | **Not** recommended for arthroplasty SSI prevention |
| Wound | Sterile dressing **48 h**; advanced dressings not better · drains out when indicated |
| Elective surgery | **Postpone** if remote-site infection |

## X vs Y

| | **CRP** | **ESR** |
|---|---|---|
| Post-op | **Peaks 48 h, then falls** | Raised for **months** |
| Early infection | **Most predictive (week 1)** | Poor |
| Cure | **Normalises with response** | Lags, less specific |

| | **Involucrum** | **Sequestrum** |
|---|---|---|
| What | **Reactive new bone** around infection | **Dead infected bone** (nidus) |
| Treatment | Preserve | **Excise** |

## How it's asked

**"When should CRP start to fall after ORIF?"** → **48 hours**

**"Infection cured — which labs?"** → **CRP 1 mg/L, ESR 25 mm/h**

**"Exopolysaccharide glycocalyx?"** → **biofilm creation**

**"Quorum sensing role?"** → **activates virulence-factor genes**

**"Deep infection 4 wk after ankle ORIF?"** → **I&D, retain fixation, culture-specific antibiotics until union**

**"Indolent infection after cuff repair?"** → **Propionibacterium acnes**

**"Not an SSI (TO)?"** → **no instrumentation, 3 months**
